- A scenario changed in `hosts\spt-oracle\scenarios` changes it for every kept recording made with the old one: put the old file in that recording's own `scenarios` folder first (the thirteenth's `61-legs-dry` was overwritten and had to be written back from the session's own reading of it).

**The inventory** (the spec has the rules; these are the traps).
- The whole of `Assembly-CSharp.dll` is under `local\decomp\src` in namespace folders; the top level holds only the types with no namespace. `EFT\InventoryLogic` and `EFT\UI` hold the inventory.
- In the database a slot's `_mergeSlotWithChildren: false` does not mean "does not count toward the size": false is "as the item says" and true is "always" (`SlotSerializer`). Read the other way round, a rifle comes out one cell by one.
- The pockets' template says 20 by 20 cells for the item itself: it is never put in a grid, so the figure means nothing.
- The "Tasks" tab is `Notes` in the code's list of tabs; there is an Achievements tab between it and Prestige.
- The starting characters are in `templates\profiles.json` by edition and side (nine editions in this install). The Standard USEC carries an M4A1 and 14.563 kg, the BEAR an AK-74M and 16.531 kg; neither starts with an AKM, which is the app's weapon in hand.
- Item names for people are in `locales\global\en.json` under `<template id> ShortName` and `<template id> Name`.
- No recording carries the inventory or the game's own total weight; the header's inertia is the only trace of it (`replay` finds the carried weight from that).

**The environment.**
- Git Bash rewrites an argument that begins with a slash into a Windows path (`/Standard/usec` became `C:/Program Files/Git/Standard/usec`): set `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` for a command that takes such an argument.
- In `awk`'s `gsub` an `&` in the replacement is the matched text. Three source lines were mangled that way this session. Change source with the editor's exact replacement, not with `awk` or `sed`.
- A here-document in the Bash tool failed on a long Rust file with quotes in it. Files go through the Write tool, as `AGENTS.md` already said.

## Added 2026-10-11, later (the fourteenth recording, moving things, the game's screens)

**The fourteenth recording.**
- The body's lag in height is stepped by the player's own clock (`_bodyTime`, what `ManualUpdate` was given), not the engine's frame time. It only shows where the two differ: by under half a millisecond on an even frame (1 to 4 mm of eye in a jump), and outright on a hitch (frame 953 of the recording: the engine's frame 8.3 ms, the player's clock 75 ms, the eye 13.9 cm out for one frame). A hitch in a recording is worth looking at rather than cutting out: it separates the two clocks.
- Lying down the game feeds nothing into that lag.
- With that lag by the player's clock the climb's eye at the high wall is 2.9 mm out root mean square where it was 4.2, and 49 mm at its worst frame where it was 40: the worst frame is the wall's placement from the trace, not the lag.
- The game's `ActiveSlot` (the slot the hands last drew from) is why a rifle put on the sling of a character with empty hands is not taken in hand: nothing has been drawn from that slot yet.

**The game's screens.**
- They are in no bundle. The build's scenes are `EscapeFromTarkov_Data\level<N>` (558 of them, 2.6 GB), listed by path at the start of the build settings object in `globalgamemanagers` (class 141: a count and that many strings). `level44` is `Assets/Scenes/UI/CommonUIScene.unity` and holds the inventory screen, the scavenger's and the transfer screen; `level48` is the menu's, `level49` the preloader's (which has a window for looking at another character's equipment, with the vest, backpack and container in the same panel), `level45` and five more the "environment" scenes the menu's character stands in.
- A scene file has no type trees, but three kinds of object have the engine's fixed layout and are enough for where things sit: an object (its components, layer, name, tag, whether it is on), a transform (its object, turn, place, scale, children, parent) and a screen transform (the same and then the two anchors, the offset, the size and the pivot, two numbers each). A script begins with its object, an enabled flag, its script and a name; what follows is the class's own fields in the order of the class, parents first, and a pointer to another object is twelve bytes (a file number and a number in that file). A script's class is the name of the script object it points to, in `globalgamemanagers.assets` (which is 358 MB: read it for a look, not at every start).
- The names in `globalgamemanagers.assets` that look like screen objects (`EquipmentTab`, `ItemsPanel`) are the scripts, not the objects: each appears twice, 36 to 44 bytes apart (the name, then the class's name).
- `EquipmentTab`'s fields are the slot views in the order of the class, but not from the first byte: a run of eleven pointers is looked for whose first is a script on one of the panel's own children.
- The panel in the inventory screen itself (`.../LeftSide/Left Panel/Gear Panel`) has no size in the file (a layout script gives it one); the template beside it has (494 by 650).
- The largest loot containers in the item database are five by five cells, bar the airdrop crates and `box_preset1` at ten by twelve. A rifle (5 by 2), the BEAR's starting vest (4 by 3) and backpack (4 by 5) are 42 cells.
- `CantRemoveFromSlotsDuringRaid` is on the pockets and the secured containers only; `CantPutIntoDuringRaid` is true on nothing in SPT's database.

**Traps of this session.**
- A session that is cut off (a usage limit) leaves no notes. Its transcript is `%USERPROFILE%\.claude\projects\e--stlkreft\<session>.jsonl`, one JSON object a line, and every edit and command of it can be read back out (`ConvertFrom-Json` a line at a time in PowerShell). The session before this one stopped in the middle of the inventory screen with the app not building; what it had found in the fourteenth recording was only in its transcript.
- Editing a Rust file with `perl -pe 's|...|...|'` goes wrong as soon as the text holds a `|` (every closure does): the delimiter ends early and the replacement lands somewhere else, twice in one day in a file's first lines. A long here-document with quotes in its text failed to parse in the Bash tool twice (the shell looked for a closing quote; why was not found, and short ones pass). What works: a small Perl file written with the Write tool that reads the whole file and swaps one exact string for another with `index` and `substr`, and dies if the string is not there; and for text to append, a file written with the Write tool and `cat` onto the end.
- An empty sum of `f32` is minus zero, and prints as `-0.00`.
- `probe item <part of a name>` prints the details only when three items or fewer match; more, and it lists the names.

## Added 2026-10-11, afternoon (the hands and looting)

- The hands' animator (the weapon's) has the states for an open inventory on its hands layer: `OPEN INVENTORY`, `IN INVENTORY`, `CLOSE INVENTORY`, with the clips `akm_hands_out`, `akm_hands_off` and `akm_hands_in`. The left hand's own layer (`LActions`, weight 0 until used, 29 of its 64 masked bones moved) has the gestures: doors by their hinge, `Take Loot` (`hand_take`), `hand_drop_stuff`, the trunk, the gestures to other players.
- `FirearmsAnimator.SetInteract` is empty in this version: an interaction reaches the hands another way (`Player.OnAnimatedInteraction`), not followed.
- `LootPlayerState` and the body animator's `Loot` flag are dead: nothing enters the one or sets the other.
- The body animator's `WeaponType` and `WeaponTypeFloat` (rifle 0, pistol 1, thrown 2, knife 3, empty hands 4, launcher 5) only choose between rifle, pistol and launcher variants in additive layers (30 blend trees). They do not give an unarmed upper body: that comes from the empty hands' own rig, which the arms are solved onto like a weapon's.
- Standing, the game's reach (1.3 m from the eye) ends 0.2 m over the ground straight down, so a thing at the feet is only just within it; the app counts a thing as looked at within 0.25 m of the line (an estimate) and puts things down 0.4 m ahead for that reason.
- Bevy: `Window::set_cursor_position` and `ButtonInput::press` in an `Update` system are enough to drive the screen's own picking (`RelativeCursorPosition`) a frame later, which is how the scripted drags and presses work; the real pointer moves with it.

## Added 2026-10-11, evening (what an open screen does to the keys, and the hands' held clip)

- **The game's input is a chain of nodes, and a screen takes it.** `InputNode.TranslateInput` asks a node's children first (the newest first), then lets the node take commands out of the frame's list and take the axes away. `UIScreen.TranslateAxes` sets the axes to nothing, and `InputNode.GetDefaultBlockResult` blocks every command but five (`MakeScreenshot`, `ShowConsole`, `ToggleInventory`, `ToggleTalk`, `StopTalk`). The player's node is added with low priority (`AddWithLowPriority`), so with any screen up it is handed no axes (its `TranslateAxes` is not called at all) and none of its keys' commands, releases included. `inventory.md`, "What an open screen does to the keys".
- **The keys are commands, the movement is an axis.** Sprint, lean, step aside, vault, aim, the trigger, crouch, prone and jump each come as a command when the key goes down and another when it comes up; a command a screen blocks is gone. Movement, the mouse and the slow lean are axes, read afresh every frame.
- **`MovePlayerState._moveCalled` is never true.** `Move` stores `false` into it (bytecode: `ldc.i4.0; stfld _moveCalled`, at the method's first instruction), the update stores `false` again, and nothing stores `true`. So `_timeWithOutExternalMoveCall` only ever grows, and from 0.2 s into the state's life every update ends with `Direction = zero`. It looks like a slip (the method named for the call clears the flag that says it was called) and it is the game's: with the flag working, a walk would go on for a fifth of a second after a screen opened. `movement.md`, "A frame with no input".
- **How that was checked.** `ilspycmd -il -t <type>` writes the bytecode, but for this assembly it wrote the whole of it (166 MB) whatever type was named; searched for the field's name and deleted again. Nothing of it is kept.
- **The dropped input changes nothing in the recordings.** With every walking update ending by dropping its input, and every sprinting update too, all fourteen reports are byte for byte what they were (`after23` is `after22`): no recorded frame has a second update of either state that the input would have mattered to.
- **A weapon's clip that does not loop is held on its last frame.** Of the stock AKM's clips whose names have an `a` (85 of them), one is marked as looping (`hand_take`). The app's clip player started its resting clip again from the beginning whenever it ran out. For the resting clip that shows as 0.1 mm at the left hand, which is why nobody saw it; for `akm_hands_off`, 33 ms long and with 22 mm of travel in it, it was the shake the user reported with the inventory open.
- **`akm_hands_off` is the end of the way down, not a pose.** Its state plays it at a tenth of its speed (0.333 s for 33 ms of clip) and is entered half way through `akm_hands_out` with a blend of 0.25 s, so the two together are the hands' way off the weapon; the held pose is its last frame. The weapon is not out of view in it: it is turned aside in the right hand with the left hand off, as a capture shows (`local\shots\inv_walk.png`). The afternoon's note that it "goes down out of view" was a guess from the clips' names.
- **The body's `ProneReload` flag has two writers.** `MovementState.OnReload` and `OnInventory` both set it, each from its own call, so it is whatever was said last: a reload that ends with the inventory open clears it. Every hands controller calls `OnInventory`, empty hands included.
- **`Player.Vaulting` is one call for the key going down and coming up** (the recorder calls it on each change of its `vault` key, as the game's two commands do), and it turns a switch over. `OnStartInventoryOpen`, which turns it off, is raised by `InventoryOpenRaiseAction` for the shutting as well as the opening.
- **Not followed:** what the idling weapon does with a press of the trigger while the hands' animator is still in `CLOSE INVENTORY` (it has no way into the fire state from there); and the body animator's own `Inventory` and `InventoryOperation` flags, which are the pose others see.
- Bevy: two chains of systems in `Update` with nothing between them are run in no settled order relative to each other. The inventory screen's chain now runs `.after(controller::step).before(animation::play_clips)`: a key that opens the screen is seen by the simulation's frames from the next drawn frame, and what the weapon is told plays that same frame.

## Added 2026-10-11, late evening (empty hands)

- **The empty hands' bundle is a weapon's in every way that matters to the reader.** `assets/content/weapons/empty hands/weapon_empty_hands_container.bundle` has a `WeaponPrefab` on its root whose `_weaponObject` is `weapon_empty_hands.generated`: 62 bones, both arms under a weapon rig's own names, `Camera_animated`, and `Weapon_root/Weapon_root_anim/weapon` with the hand markers, the elbows' goals, `aim_camera`, `fireport`, `shellport` and two `prop` bones. No renderer anywhere. Its animator controller is read by the same code as a weapon's (38 clips; `empty_hands_*`, the left hand's gestures, three for the compass). The game names the bundle in code (`EmptyHandsTemplate`); no database entry has it.
- **44 of those 62 bones have the stock AKM's own paths** (everything but the weapon bone's subtree), which is what lets the app give both rigs one set of arm bones.
- **The empty hands' animator has no state for an open inventory**, and its resting state is a blend tree over `Aim_angle` with `empty_hands_idle` at 0 and `empty_hands_idle_down` at 90. `FirearmsAnimator.SetAimAngle` gives it the player's pitch every frame, for any hands whose animator has the parameter; the AKM's resting state goes by `LauncherID` and is not affected.
- **`EmptyHandsController.Remove` finishes at once:** it tells the body's animator the hands are busy and calls its own completion in the same call. `IDLE TO OUT` and its `WeapOut` mark are there in the animator and nothing waits for them.
- **`ProceduralWeaponAnimation` is one object across hands.** `ClearPreviousWeapon` forgets the weapon data and sets `_swayStrength` and `_displacementStr` to 0.5; `InitTransforms` ties it to the new rig and resets the springs. `HandsContainer.RotationCenter`, the position spring's damping (`Aiming.RecoilHandDamping`, set only in `InitWeaponData`) and `_ergonomicWeight` are left as the last weapon set them, and `UpdateSwayFactors`, which `OnAimOrPoseChanged` and the weight's update both call, works the two strengths out again from that stale weight. On the player prefab itself `PlayerSpring.RotationCenter` is (0, -0.17, 0) and the position spring's damping 0.5.
- **The stock AKM's draw has a way into the inventory's states:** `OUT TO IDLE` goes to `IDLE` at 0.98 of itself unless the inventory flag is up, and to `OPEN INVENTORY` at 0.78 with a blend of 0.22 s if it is. Its `SPAWN` state chooses between `OUT TO IDLE` and `OUT TO IDLE ARM` (which racks the bolt, 1.47 s) by whether a round is chambered.
- **Clips found by the end of their name are a trap with more than one rig:** the empty hands have both `empty_hands_out_to_idle` and `compass_out_to_idle`. The app now takes the draw and put-away clips from the animator's states of those names (`trp_sim::state_clip`).
- **In a sprint the empty-handed arms need nothing new:** the body's own animation takes the arms there, as it does with a rifle, and with no weapon in them they run (`local\shots\eh_sprint_side.png`). What the body's animator would do differently when told the hands are empty is not known: it is not told.
- Bevy: the four resources of "what is in the hands" are swapped with a fifth that holds the other set (`std::mem::swap` through `ResMut`), so no system had to learn that there are two rigs.

## Added 2026-10-11, the session after the evening's (the kind of thing in hand, the weapon's animator, the lean)

**The weapon's animator.**
- **It is the engine's own, updated after every script, like the body's.** The local player's arms are made with the update mode `Auto` (`BaseLocalGame`), and only `UseHandsFastAnimator` in a config this install lacks makes it the game's own "fast" animator stepped by hand. So `Player.ArmsUpdate` runs the controller's operation and nothing else, the engine then updates the animator, and the marks are delivered from `ArmsLateUpdate`, which `ComplexLateUpdate` calls at the very end of `Player.LateUpdate`, after `VisualPass` and so after the firearm controller's own late update has given the recoil its shot.
- **That order is the frame between a single shot's kick and an automatic one's.** A single shot is made by a mark's handler (the end of frame N), so the recoil gets it in frame N+1's late update and the springs feel it from N+2; automatic fire's first shot is made by the command (frame N's update) and the springs feel it from N+1. It had been recorded as "2 or 3 frames, never 1" against "1 or 2" and built as a one-step delay; the reason is this.
- **A mark fires when the state is next looked at, not at its time.** Each state keeps the point it had reached; a mark fires if it lies at or after that point and before the state's time now. Three things follow that the fixed sequence did not have: a state being left fires *all* its remaining marks on the first update of the transition out (`MagHide` at 0.779 of `RELOAD OUT`, not 0.850); a mark at exactly 1 (`WeapIn` in the draw) fires only as the state is left, so `IdleStart` comes first and it is `IdleStart` that tells the game the weapon has appeared (25 ms before `WeapIn` for the AKM); and a state entered at exactly 0 fires nothing until it has moved.
- **Marks carry conditions, tested when delivered.** Kinds: 0 a whole number, 1 a float, 2 a flag; modes: 0 equal, 1 not equal, 2 greater, 3 less, 4 at least, 5 at most. A float has tests only for greater and less; a float condition in any other mode would throw in the game. The reader's own comment had the first two kinds the wrong way round.
- **An exit point is a crossing, every cycle, loop or not.** `DRY FIRE` does not loop, and its way out "at 1 once the trigger is up" is taken at 2 when the trigger came up at 1.44. The game's own animator code tests exactly that (`AnimatorControllerTransition.IsTimeForTransition`). The engine here had "passed once, passed for good" for a state that does not loop; the body's fourteen recordings replay the same line for line either way, so nothing in them had ever asked.
- **The game asks the animator for a state outright in three places that are built:** `FIRE` at 0.2 (a single shot, and automatic fire's start), and `IDLE` at 0.1 when automatic fire hands back to idling. Everything else is parameters and the animator's own transitions: a reload is one trigger (`Reload`) and three flags, and `FIRE` is entered again between automatic shots by `IDLE`'s own way to it while `Fire` is up.
- **The fire mode changes at the key**, before the animator is told (`Idling.ChangeFireMode`), and the weapon is idling through the switch: by the code the trigger works during it.
- **`CanReload` is set true at the first reload and never back** by anything built here.
- **The weapon's first layer has no states at all** (`Base Layer`, weight 0, "starts in -"). The engine gives the first layer full weight whatever is stored, and indexed its first state: it now lets a layer with none be.
- **How it was found to be right:** not by looking. The recorder has written the animator's state, time and transition flag since 0.2.0, and nothing had used the last two. Run beside the logic and set beside them, the state machine was the game's on every frame of 18 of the 19 weapon scenarios at the first try, and the one that differed (the dry fire held past a cycle) was the engine's exit rule, not the weapon's calls.
- **A comparison script can be one line out.** The simulation's trace has no header line and the game's has one: a first look had the simulation "a frame early" everywhere, reloads included, because the script dropped a line from both. Check a known-good scenario with a new script before reading anything into what it shows.

**The kind of thing in hand.**
- **Three clips of one name.** `sprint_slow_0` is three different clips in `character_animations.bundle` (14, 15 and 16 curves): the legs' and a long firearm's; one with `Hand_Left` at 1; one with both hands' curves at 1 and the fingers closed into fists. The states reader keeps each tree's own clip by reference, so nothing was mixed up, but a look by name (`probe bodyclips sprint_slow_0`) shows three and does not say which tree has which. To find out, run the animator with each value and read the curves off it.
- **Telling the animator the kind changed nothing visible,** because the empty hands already told it a width of one cell, and a firearm one cell wide gets the same clip as empty hands, a pistol and a thing thrown. Measure before and after a change that "obviously" matters; this one was built before the test that showed it.
- **The right hand's fingers are the rig's whatever the body's clips say.** `Player.UpdateFirstPersonGrip` sets the right hand's poser to full weight at every change of hands and nothing else writes it; only the left's follows `1 - Hand_Left * First_Person_Curve_Weight`. So in an empty-handed sprint the left hand is the body's fist and the right keeps the empty hands' own resting curl. From the code; the recorder writes no finger bones.
- `WeaponTypeFloat` is in no transition's condition; 41 blend-tree nodes read it: the sprint's arms (a clip per kind), 31 launcher variants (a clip at 4 and one at 5) and 9 pistol variants (0 and 1), of which only blind fire is reachable.

**The lean.**
- The crawl does not straighten a lean as it begins. `ProneMovePlayerState.SetTilt` turns any *request* into one for no lean, and nothing else touches it: a lean held lying still goes on into the crawl until a lean key is next pressed or let go. The simulation had put it to zero on every frame of a crawl, which was the held keys' way of looking at it.

**The crate.**
- The BEAR's starting vest holds three AK-74 magazines of 5.45 PS and a medical kit; the backpack two lots of 5.45 (PS and another), water and a ration. The AKM's stock magazine (`59d625f086f774661516605d`, short name "AK55") is the same two cells, so the swap moves nothing. The rounds' short name is "PS" in both calibres: the screen's picture and caption do not show the change, the weight does (12.32 kg against 10.775).

**Traps of this session.**
- A here-document for the Bash tool fails on a Perl or Rust text with quotes in it (again). What worked all session: a Perl file written with the Write tool that swaps exact strings and dies if one is missing or found twice, with the old and new text as `<<'A'` and `<<'B'` blocks inside it, each `chomp`ed so that a block may end in the middle of a line.
- A struct built with every field named (the app's simulation input) does not compile when a field is added, which is how a missed call site showed. One built with `..Default::default()` hides it.

## Added 2026-10-11, night (the weapon goes by its animator)

- **A reload has its own way into the inventory's hold.** The AKM's `Hands.RELOAD INVENTORY END` (`akm_hands_out_half_2`, 0.333 s) is entered from `RELOAD IN` (0.94, with a round chambered or an empty magazine going in) and from `RELOAD CHARGE END` (0.98) while the animator's `Inventory` flag is up, and gives way to `IN INVENTORY` a quarter of the way through. `IN INVENTORY` in turn has ways into `RELOAD IN`, `INV TO RELOAD`, `LOAD ONE`, `MOD SETUP`, `DISCHARGE` (the weapon worked on in the screen) and `IDLE TO OUT` (put away from the hold). A fixed sequence of states cannot know any of that; the controller's own transitions do. `probe states <weapon> INVENTORY`, or `local\akm_hands_states.txt` for every state of the hands' layer.
- **What a command does is the operation's answer, and most operations have none.** The base class of the firearm's operations logs every call as prohibited and does nothing; each operation overrides the few it answers. So "can the trigger be pulled in a reload" is read off whether the reload operation overrides the call, not off any flag. A look at the weapon (`UtilityOperation`) is a subclass of idling with answers taken away, which is why the fire mode can be switched in one.
- **Idling is in hand far more of the time than the animation suggests:** from a reload's handing over (the magazine in) to the end of its animation, through a fire-mode switch, while the hands are down for the inventory. In all of those the fire mode, the look at the magazine, the reload key and (but for a reload's own flag) the trigger are the idling operation's.
- **A shot plans one thing** (`FireOperationBase._plannedAction`): a reload or a put-away asked for during it. The reload key is not dropped in a shot.
- **A draw does not hear of the inventory**, and the firearm's draw, unlike the knife's, does not read the player's flag either. By the code a weapon equipped in the open screen is held at rest behind it. Not on record.
- **The inventory's flag is kept in two places:** the controller's (`InventoryOpened`, set by idling and by a reload) and a shot's own (`_openInventory`). A shot ends by telling the animator its own; a reload tells it the controller's. Kept apart in `Firearm` for that reason.
- **`WeapIn` sits at the very end of the draw's state** (1.000), after the point the state is left at (0.98). It is delivered because a state being left gives up its remaining marks; the game's draw also ends at `IdleStart`, which is the fallback if it were not.
- **Where the old drawing was wrong, measured:** the clips played by name had the left hand's marker up to 22.5 mm and 55 degrees from the recorded one during blends (and the weapon's bone 5.8 mm), in every recording. Two causes: a blend was laid as "the new clip over the old by its weight", bone by bone from whatever the rig held, and the resting clip was begun again each time it ran out, where the animator holds a clip that does not loop on its last frame. With the animator's pose all three are within 0.05 mm.
- **A bug the rewrite removed:** the logic told the animator of the inventory in a reload only while the magazine was out (`magazine.is_none()`); the game tells it from the magazine's coming out to the reload's end, which matters exactly in the chambering case.
- **The app read the rig a drawn frame late.** The simulation took the weapon's root, the right hand's marker, the aim bone and the camera bone off the drawn transforms, which were those of the last drawn frame's clips. It now reads the animator's pose of the same simulated frame, after the animator's update, as the game's late update does.
- **Trap:** a here-document of some 140 lines in the Bash tool failed with "unexpected EOF" (the limit `AGENTS.md` warns of). Long edits go into a file with the Write tool; `local\apply_edits.pl <file> <spec>` replaces exact pieces of text from a spec file without any quoting.

## Added 2026-10-12 (the first frames, the fire-mode switch, the hands on the world)

- **Why a thing fails to draw the first time it is seen.** Bevy builds a render pipeline (shaders compiled for one mesh layout and one sort of material) the first time something needing it is queued for a view, on its background task pool, and skips the thing until the pipeline is ready. On DirectX 12 each opaque mesh pipeline takes over a second (four build side by side; a fifth waits for a free thread and adds a second). A hidden entity is never queued, so its pipelines are not asked for until it is shown: the app's weapon, hidden for a bare start, was missing again at its first draw. `synchronous_pipeline_compilation` cures the missing but builds one after another: 3.6 s and two more of a second for the first three frames. What works: have everything in view behind a curtain and wait for `PipelineCache::waiting_pipelines()` to stay empty (read in the render world, handed over in an atomic). Vulkan builds the same set in 0.45 s against 1.8 s.
- **A capture taken in the first second and a half was never written:** the screenshot's own pipeline was still building when the run ended 40 frames after asking for it. Captures now end when their file exists.
- **Scripted runs count from the run's beginning, not the window's.** `--tap`, `--drag`, `--screenshot-frame` and the simulation all wait for the warm-up, so frame numbers in older notes still mean what they meant.
- **The left hand's gestures are the body's, not the weapon's.** The weapon's controller has a layer for them (`LActions`: hinges, keys, `Take Loot`, `hand_drop_stuff`) and the firearm's operations still forward `Interact`, `Pickup` and `Loot` to the weapon's animator; but `FirearmsAnimator.SetInteract` and `SetPickup` are empty and nothing calls `SetLooting`. What plays is `InteractionsLayer` of the body's animator: `probe bodystates - Player_anim_controller` (the layer's machine is 17; `local\body_states_all.txt`).
- **The interaction clips carry the arm's hand-over curves.** `tp_item_take`, `tp_hinge_...`, `tp_inventory` and the rest have `First_Person_Curve_Weight`, `Hand_Left`, `Elbow_Left`, `Shoulder_Left` rising to 1 and falling back (`probe bodyclips <clip> tracks x` prints a clip's curves at five points). So the first-person arm follows them through the machinery measured for a crawl and a climb.
- **`LeftHandProgress` is a parameter of the hands' animator**, and the game's state for a door waits on it (over 0.99 to have the thing answer, under 0 to end). A firearm's animator plays nothing for an interaction, so by the code the figure stays at 0 and both waits fall through to their clocks: 1.6 s and 3 s. If a recording shows it moving, the reading is wrong.
- **Dead in this version:** the gesture for a thing thrown out of the inventory (`DropItem`, `ThrowInventory`: in the list, asked for nowhere); the layer's `Inventory Start` state (a later way out of `Idle` with the same conditions as the one into `Inventory`).
- **A pick-up is only taken out of the standing state** (`IdlePlayerState.Pickup`; the base class's does nothing), and the pick-up state turns no view and takes no stance.
- **Nothing in the game's input path refuses the trigger in a fire-mode switch** (`FirearmHandsInputTranslator` passes it; the idling operation refuses only by the movement state and the reload's flag; `FireOperation.Start` asks the animator for `FIRE` outright). The user says Tarkov does refuse it. One of the two is wrong and `scenarios-hands\64` says which; until then the user's word is built.
- **The recorder cannot read `MovementContext.StateLocksInventory`** (no get accessor); left out of 0.6.0.
- **Trap:** a function name used twice in one file (`let Some(out) = out.as_mut() ...` in both loggers) put an inserted line in the wrong function; anchor an insertion on text that occurs once.

## Added 2026-10-12, later (the fifteenth recording and the recordings made by hand)

- **The game fires in the middle of a fire-mode switch** (`64-fire-in-firemode-switch`): the mode is the new one from the key on, and a press 0.3 s into `FIREMODE` begins a shot. The reading of the code had it so; a refusal built for a day on the user's word is out, and the user took the word back.
- **A reload asked for while the last one's animation is still playing out is taken** (`69-reload-in-reload-tail`): the operation is in hand from the key, the animator plays the first out, shows one frame of `IDLE`, and begins `RELOAD START`. The same one frame of `IDLE` stands between a reload's tail and a look at the magazine, and between a fire-mode switch and a reload.
- **Two ways to a thing of the world, and the common one holds nothing.** A plain open, close or search is `Player.ExecuteInteraction` (the base movement state's `ExecuteDoorInteraction`): the thing is told, the gesture asked, no state taken. `Player.StartInteraction`, with the approach and the door state of 1.6 or 3 s, is for a key, a card and a kick only (`InteractionContextHelper` shows which menu entry calls which). The first reading took the second for the first. **The check that would have caught it is the one `AGENTS.md` already asks for: who calls it.**
- **(Corrected that afternoon: a container waits the install's 0.55 s and a door half a second of its own; see below.)** **A thing does its part half a second after it is told** (`WorldInteractiveObject.Interact` and `LootableContainer.Interact` both wait `DelayToOpenContainer`), doors and containers alike, opening and shutting alike. A searched container's screen is asked for after the same wait, by a timer of the player's.
- **(Read on to the 195th field that afternoon.)** **The settings object is read only as far as its 165th field** (`RollCheckGroundCastLength`; `hard_settings_layout.txt`). `DelayToOpenContainer` lies some twenty-five fields further on, past arrays of the game's own classes (`ColliderPresets`, `StaticIcons`, two arrays of quaternions) that the layout has no kinds for. Its figure here is the class's own default, 0.5, and the recordings agree with it.
- **(Explained that afternoon: the install's wait is 0.55 s.)** **The screen of a searched container was open 0.550 to 0.553 s after the key** in five recordings: the half second, then six frames (at 120 a second) in which the game asks the container for its contents and builds the screen.
- **The inventory opening stops the gestures** (`Player.SetInventoryOpened` calls the model's forced stop): recorded, the flag falls and the layer is back in `Idle` at time zero on the frame the screen opens.
- **A gesture's flag falls on the frame its state is left**, not a frame later (recorded in the layer itself: `local/gesture_layer.sh`). The simulation took the state's end a frame late; it now takes it straight after the body's update.
- **A container's gesture begins a frame after a door's:** the layer is put to rest first (`Idle` at time zero on the frame of the key) and blends into the gesture from the next, as the model's instruction for it says. Recorded for 29 and 30.
- **What has just appeared in the hands is told how the screen stands.** `Player.SpawnController` raises the hands-changed event as the draw ends; `PlayerOwner.RefreshHandsController` answers; `GamePlayerOwner.SetHandsController` calls `Player.SetInventoryOpened` with whether the inventory screen is showing. Recorded for the empty hands (told on the frame they had appeared, 0.055 s after their controller showed). So a weapon drawn under an open screen goes down when it is up. The night's reading had searched the operations and the player for a caller and not the owner.
- **A controller made at a mark's delivery has its first update in the next frame.** The empty hands that follow a put-away show `SPAWN` at time zero on the frame the weapon went, and leave it in the next; a weapon put on its slot by the screen (made before the frame's update of the animators) is in its draw state on its first frame. The app does the same by its own order of things: the swap after the step, the first step the next frame.
- **The inventory opening reaches the weapon's animator before that frame's update**: the game's animator is already blending into `OPEN INVENTORY` on the frame the screen is first open.
- **In one of two recorded draws under the open screen the game's weapon was idling on the first frame its controller showed**, its animator told of the screen from that frame, the body's hands-busy figure already down: the draw's operation had been cut short before the controller was the player's (it had been running three frames by its animator's time). The animator played `OUT TO IDLE ARM` in full all the same. What cut it short is not in the recording. Not built.
- **The game's own figure for the carried weight does not follow rounds leaving a weapon at once.** In two of fifteen headers it was that of an earlier moment (thirty rounds taken out of the rifle's magazine; one round fired), while the equipment list and the elite-Strength figure, which the recorder has the game work out afresh, were current.
- **A chambered round is an item in a slot of the weapon** named `patron_in_weapon`, kept by the database in a list of its own (`Chambers`) beside `Slots`.
- **(Explained that afternoon: it was shutting, as every screen's container does.)** **A container shut by its screen was still open 0.6 s and 1.7 s later** (the key on it then asked for its closing gesture, 30): its shutting waits the same half second, and what follows was not looked into.
- **A recording made by hand runs at uneven frame times** where the screen opens (one frame of 0.06 s, several of 0.02): anything timed from it should use the frame's own `dt`, as `bin\byhand` does. `local/changes.sh` rounds the values it compares to two places; the time it prints is exact.
- **Trap:** `cargo test --test <name>` rebuilds the package's binaries, so `target\debug\replay.exe` is fresh after it; `cargo test -p trp-app` still does not rebuild `trp.exe`.
- **The game's own numbers for the hands' gestures are not the animator's.** `EInteraction` has the doors' six at 23 to 28 and the containers' at 30 to 37; the model in front of the animator turns them into the layer's 16 to 21 and 23 to 30 through a table of its own. A number written in the game's code (`Door.CalculateInteractionIndex` returns 23 to 28) is the first kind; a number in a recording (`p_InteractionIndex`) or in a state's name is the second. `trp-sim\src\interactions.rs` goes by the names and gives the animator's.
- **(Corrected that afternoon: a door runs the curve at its own pace, 2.5 s; containers and trunks at 1.5.)** **A door's swing is one curve for every door** (`DoorCurve` of the settings object, which the reader has: `probe hardsettings door`), run at 1.5 times its speed and begun half a second after the key: 1.67 s, more than half the turn in the first 0.38 s. Doors are reached from 1 m (`DOOR_RAYCAST_DISTANCE` in the install; the class's default says 0.75), containers from 1.3 m.


## Added 2026-10-12, afternoon (fourteen recordings made by hand with recorder 0.6.1: doors, containers, a trunk)

- **Corrected: a door swings for 2.5 s, not 1.67.** `SetDoorState` starts the routine at 1.5 times the curve's pace, but `Door` overrides the routine with a half-second wait of its own and then calls the base one without passing the speed on, so it runs at 1. A container (its own routine) and a trunk (the base one, not overridden) do run at 1.5. Recorded: a door at its new state 3.005 and 3.009 s after the key; a container or a trunk 2.220 to 2.225 s after.
- **Corrected: a container answers 0.55 s after the key, not 0.5.** The settings object's `DelayToOpenContainer` is 0.55 in the install; 0.5 is only the class's default. This is the whole of the search's "six frames" that the app had as an estimate (`SCREEN_COMES_UP`, gone). A door does not use it: its half second is written in its own routine.
- **The settings object is now read to its 195th field** (`AnimatorCullDistance`), through `ColliderPresets` (a list of the game's `BallisticPreset`: a name, a material number, a list of floats, a flag and a curve each), two references to other objects (`StaticIcons`, `ChatSpecialIconSettings` are scriptable objects, so 12 bytes each in the settings) and two lists of quaternions. The values on either side of the new fields are plausible (`DOOR_BREACH_THRESHOLD` 0.8, `GrenadeFullSyncDelay` 5, `AnimatorCullDistance` 10), which is what says the layout holds.
- **Corrected: a container whose screen shuts is always told to shut** (the screen's closing action, `TryInteractionCallback`). The morning's note that one "was still open 0.6 s and 1.7 s later" was the shutting under way: it stays `Open` through its 0.55 s wait and its 1.67 s swing. A key pressed on it meanwhile makes the gesture and holds it as being worked, and the order is refused (its last order was for the same state); it is shut when the screen's order has run.
- **What a thing does on the key, in order** (`MovementState.ExecuteDoorInteraction`): the gesture is worked out from the thing as it stands (before it is held), the thing is held as being worked (`LockForInteraction`, which notes what it was), it is told (`Interact`), and the gesture is asked. Being worked, nothing is offered for it (`GetAvailableActions` returns none), and a door's gesture comes out 28 (recorded on every frame of a swing).
- **The routines' frame order.** A swing's first frame stands where the curve begins and moves the curve's time on by that frame; the frame that finds the time past the end sets the new angle and state. A wait (a timer, or a door's `WaitForSeconds`) is over on the first frame at least its length after the frame it began on, that frame not counting. Coroutines run after the player's update, so a host steps the things after the character. All of it gives the recorded angle to 0.000 degrees.
- **A door's gesture rule reproduces the game's own number** on every recorded frame, with the door's forward axis its local y; the other three axes square to its hinge fail on some recording. The recorder writes the door's rotation, axis and place, not its forward axis.
- **Whether anything is looked at at all** is two flags: the movement state's (`CanInteract` on the body animator's state scripts, read with `StateSensitivity`; standing, walking, lying still, crawling and blind fire have it) and the hands' (`FirearmController.CanInteract`: idling or firing). The install's reaches: 1.3 m for loot, 1 m for doors (the class default is 0.75), each from 0.15 m behind the eye.
- **Doors are solid to the character and lift its speed cap.** Every door's colliders on the player's static collision layers go to the player's controller as colliders "with no speed limit" (`CharacterControllerSpawner`, `LocationScene.DoorsCollisionColliders`): `CheckSpeedLimit` does nothing for a move whose capsule overlaps one. `DisableDoorColliderOnInteraction` (on in the install) is read by nothing.
- **The recorded things named their gestures as their prefabs set them**: a technical supply crate and a wooden crate 36/37 (the animator's 29/30, with lids of 4.78 and -30 degrees), a dead Scav 28/35 (a door's pull backward to open), a drawer 32/27 (a door's push forward to shut; it slides, its angles 0 and 0), a car's trunk 34/35 (a `Trunk`, opened and shut, no screen; 60 degrees). The game's numbers are the animator's plus seven for all of these.
- **Searching lying down gets the character up** at once (`Prone2Stand`, 0.834 s, stance 0 after), and the gesture plays meanwhile.
- **The install's loot containers by name** (`templates/items.json`, English names in `locales/global/en.json`): Drawer `578f87b7245977356274f2cd` (2 x 2), Dead Scav `5909e4b686f7747f5b744fa4` (4 x 4), Technical supply crate `5d6fd45b86f774317075ed43` (5 x 5), Wooden crate `578f87ad245977356274f2cc`; the four "Weapon box" sorts are `terraWBox...`. A car's trunk is no item.
- **Trap:** a long here-document cut off by the Bash tool again (an edit spec of some 180 lines); written with the Write tool instead, as `workbench.md` says. And `perl` is Git Bash's: from PowerShell it is not found.
- **Trap:** `powershell -File script.ps1 -Places a,b` passes the list as one string; call the script with `& .\script.ps1 -Places 'a','b'` instead.


## Added 2026-10-12, 15:00 (the inventory's clicks and keys, the install's key bindings, the M3 Super 90 as the install has it)

- **The install's key bindings are plain JSON** in `SPT_Runtime\user\sptSettings\Control.ini`, beside the sensitivities the app already read: a list `keyBindings`, each entry the game's name for the key (`EGameKey`), two `variants` of keys held together (the engine's names: `Alpha2`, `LeftAlt`, `Mouse0`) and a `pressType` (`Press`, `Release`, `Continuous`, `DoubleClick`). `probe keys <part of a name>` prints them. `InputManager` turns each into a command (`PrimaryWeaponFirst` into `SelectFirstPrimaryWeapon`, `Slot4` into `SelectFastSlot4`).
- **The sling's weapon is on `2`, the back's on `3`, the holster's on `1`** (`PrimaryWeaponFirst`, `PrimaryWeaponSecond`, `SecondaryWeapon`). The app had the sling on `3` "as remembered". `FastAccess` says the same from the code's side: its fixed cells are `ItemV` the sheath, `Item1` the holster, `Item2` the first primary weapon, `Item3` the second.
- **Loose end: two of the app's weapon keys are not the install's.** `ReloadWeapon` is `R` on `Release` (a `DoubleClick` of `R` is `QuickReloadWeapon`), and `CheckAmmo` is `T` with `LeftAlt` (`T` alone is `Tactical`). The app reloads as `R` goes down and looks at the magazine on `T`. How the game's input manager tells a single press from the first of a double one (and whether the single one waits for `DoubleClickTimeout`, 0.3 in the same file) is not read. Also in that file, unread until now: the look axes carry a `sensitivity` of 5 for `Mouse X` and `Mouse Y`, which bears on the app's one `ESTIMATE` for degrees of look per mouse count.
- **This install's knife key is `U`**, not the `V` the screen's own view is named for: the bar's captions come from the bindings.
- **A double click does not put a thing on.** In this version it opens a container in a window or inspects a thing (or uses food and medicine); Alt and a click is what puts a thing on. (The spec had "a double click to put it on" from memory.)
- **Ctrl and a click is not "to the first place found".** It goes target by target (`PrioritizeTargetsOrder`), and the character's equipment stands for only four of the things worn, in an order by the thing's kind; no equipment slot is tried (that is the pick-up's order alone). And the grid's search counts the thing's own cells as taken, so a thing with nowhere else to go hops to the next free place in its own container.
- **A pick-up is offered only if it would find a place** (`GetAvailableInteractionState` runs the same search as a trial).
- **In a raid a question window answers itself.** `ItemUiContext` shows no confirmation there unless forced, so the discard key puts a thing down at once.
- **With Ctrl held a drop is only ever part of a stack** (`partialTransferOnly`): a thing that is no stack is refused, where the app used to move it.
- **The window that asks how many comes up at half the most and one**, the most being all but one of the stack (or what the other stack has room for): 30 of 59 for a stack of 60. Its slider has at most 24 notches and the most at the end.
- **A bound thing is unbound by any move that takes it out of the vest or the pockets**, and those two are the only places things are bound in (`Inventory.FastAccessSlots`), though a thing may be taken in hand from four more (`BindAvailableSlotsExtended`: the two long weapons' slots, the holster, the sheath).
- **The weapon in hand, moved to another slot, is not followed there.** The item leaves its address before the hands are asked (`OutProcess`), so the "last weapon held" is not where it can be held from and the hands go to the first *other* thing in the slots' order, or empty; the active slot stays the one it was drawn from, so the weapon is taken in hand again only if it comes back there, or by its new slot's key. Read from the code; recording 7 of the README's list would show it.
- **`Process.Proceed` does nothing for the thing already in hand**, and a second process asked for while one is scheduled takes its place: a slot's key pressed twice draws once.

**The M3 Super 90 as the install has it** (looked at with the readers as they are; nothing of the game's code for it is read, nothing built):

- Item `6259b864ebedf17603599e88` (`Shotgun`, a `Weapon`), 3 x 1 cells before its parts, 12 gauge, `ReloadMode` `InternalMagazine`, `weapFireType` `single` and `semiauto` (the game's fire modes 1 and 5), `isBoltCatch` and `isChamberLoad` true, `BoltAction` false, `bFirerate` 60 and `SingleFireRate` 300, one chamber, `CompactHandling` true, `Ergonomics` 42, `RecoilForceUp` 336 and `RecoilForceBack` 595, default round the 12/70 7 mm buckshot (`560d5e524bdc2d25448b4571`, twenty to a stack). Seven slots, five of them required: barrel, forend, magazine (five kinds: the stock preset's is the five-shell cap; a seven-shell one and extensions to nine, eleven and thirteen), stock (two kinds; the telescoping one carries the pistol grip), charging handle; a mount and a rear sight besides.
- **It assembles and its animator reads**: `probe weapon`, `probe states` and `probe states <id> parameters` all work on it (167 nodes; 74 parameters, among them `FireMode`, `AmmoInMag` and `AmmoInChamber` as floats, `BoltCatch`, `StockFolded`, `Rechamber`, `Malfunction`). Seven layers: the hands', the left hand's, `Hammer`, `Catch`, `Stock`, `Malfunction`.
- **Its states come in two families**, chosen by the `FireMode` parameter: pump action (`FireMode` between 0 and 2: `PA ...`) and self-loading (between 4 and 6: `SA ...`). `FIRE` is the pumped shot (`m3s90_pa_fire_fast`, 0.667 s, with the shell thrown out 0.45 of the way through and the pump's two sounds), `SA FIRE` the self-loading one (a 1 s clip at five times speed). `TO SA` and `TO PA` change the mode (1.167 s each). The tube is filled a shell a loop: `RELOAD START` (0.667 s at 1.2), `RELOAD IN` (`m3s90_ammo_in_loop`, 1.167 s at 1.2, looping), `RELOAD END`. From empty the first shell goes in by `PA RELOAD FIRST` (2.333 s, three variants) or, self-loading, `SA AMMO IN FIRST CATCH` (2.4 s) or `NO CATCH` (3.233 s). There are states for a fistful of shells and for two and four at a time (`AMMO IN FISTFUL`, `TWIN LOADING`, `QUAD LOADING`), for the chamber emptied from the inventory (`PA DISCHARGE`, `SA DISCHARGE`), for the chamber looked at in each mode, for rechambering, for the stock folded and unfolded, and a set for each malfunction in each mode. Drawn in 1.0 s (`OUT TO IDLE`), or with the action worked when the chamber is empty and the tube is not (`PA OUT TO IDLE ARM`, `SA OUT TO IDLE ARM`, 1.5 s); put away in 0.833 s; the inventory's three states as the AKM has them.
- **What it needs that the AKM did not** (to be read from the game's code before any of it is built): a reload that is a loop of single rounds and can be cut short; a shot that is followed by an action of the hands (the pump) before the next; two modes with their own states and a change between them that takes time; a chamber loaded apart from the magazine; the bolt held open on empty in one mode; a stock that folds; shot with several pellets (`ShotgunDispersion`, `shotgunDispersion`).
- **Traps:** an app the user has open cannot be told from one that hangs by cargo's error alone: `Get-Process trp` shows when it was started. And a capture run begun then would take the keyboard's focus and, with `--drag`, move the real mouse pointer: none was started until the build went through.

## Added 2026-10-12, 17:00 (the M3 Super 90 built, the sixteenth recording, sixteen more made by hand)

- **Following the game's code is enough to build from, now.** The M3 (a weapon class with nothing in common with the AKM's reload) and the inventory's clicks were each written from the code and the install, with no recording looked at first, and thirty-five recordings then changed nothing in either. What makes that so: the weapon's own animator and its marks run as the engine runs them, so which state follows which, when and with what blend is the install's data and not something read or guessed; the operations are short and were each checked for which weapon gets which class; and the order of a frame was settled by the earlier recordings. Where recordings did correct a reading before (the frame a shot is made on, the clip a state picks, a draw under an open screen, the trigger in a fire-mode switch), it was the *engine's* behaviour or a second class that takes part, and those are now built in. **A recording still earns its four minutes as a check of a new kind of system**, and it found two faults this time, both in the checking and none in the model: the replay's count of shells within reach, and two scripts that did not reach the case they were written for.
- **A script has to be read against the state the weapon is in when it runs.** `86-m3-reload-cut` and `88-m3-mode-in-reload` each replayed with every frame the game's and still showed nothing of what their names say (a filling that ended full on the frame the cut would have ended it; a reload refused on a full tube). A scenario that matches on every frame says the model agrees with the game *in what happened*, which has to be read off the recording before it is claimed.
- **Which operation a weapon gets is decided in one place** (`FirearmController.GetOperationFactoryDelegates`): the fire operation by rocket launcher, flare gun, one-off, `OnlyBarrel`, revolver, `BoltAction`, else the plain one; the tube's filling by `MustBoltBeOpennedForInternalReload`. The M3 gets the plain `FireOperation` and `ReloadInternalMagOperation`. The classes `ClientPlayerReloadInternalMag...` are the online raid's.
- **A pump is not an operation.** The pumped mode's `FIRE` state is one 0.667 s clip with the shot, the shell out and the next shell in as marks; the logic is the AKM's single shot with nothing added. `SetBoltActionReload`, which holds a bolt action's cycle back while the trigger is held, is the bolt action's own operation's and the M3's animator has no such parameter: holding the trigger does not hold the pump (recorded: `72`).
- **Marks are handed out one at a time, each tested when its turn comes** (`AnimationEventsEmitter.EmitEvents`). The simulation tested a frame's marks all at once before any handler ran; the AKM never told the difference (the regression is unchanged), the M3's bolt catch would have.
- **A full tube is not filled, whatever the chamber** (`Idling.ReloadWithAmmo` asks only whether the tube has room): with the tube full and the chamber empty the reload key does nothing, and the sights have gone down and the trigger been let go all the same. Recorded with a shell chambered (`88`); with the chamber empty it is from the code.
- **A tube's count is the weapon's only when the filling ends** (`Commit`), where a magazine's is the weapon's from the reload's first frame; the animator's `AmmoInMag` goes up shell by shell meanwhile. An empty chamber, though, has its round from the key's frame (`Prepare`). The recording shows both.
- **The aim key does nothing at all in a tube's filling**, whatever the mastering: only the magazine's reload answers it. And the filling does not raise the flag by which a magazine's reload refuses the trigger in its tail.
- **`CanAbort`'s setter cannot do what it is written to do:** it stores the new value and then tests whether the old one was false and the new true using the field it has just overwritten, so the inventory's state is never passed to the animator at that moment. A screen opened before the first shell shows is only noted. Kept as read.
- **The fire mode is refused while the bolt is caught** on a weapon with `NoFiremodeOnBoltcatch` (the M3 has it). After the last shell fired self-loading the mode cannot be changed until a shell has been loaded.
- **Within reach is the pockets and the vest, the pockets first** (`Inventory.FastAccessSlots`), and only what lies directly in them: the same two places a thing may be bound in.
- **The recorder's `ammo` in a trace's first line is empty for an empty weapon.** What a weapon is fed with has to come from its chamber's own filter.
- **A kick is a movement state of its own** (`BreachDoor`, after `Approach`), not the unlocking state: the interactions spec had the two together, from a reading of `Player.StartInteraction` that stopped short of which state the movement context goes to for a breach. The kick lands 0.45 of the way through the state (`BreachDoorState.KickTime`).
- **The engine's approach state lasted 0.261 s both times**, for a thing that snaps the character nowhere; the class's field starts at 0.33 s and `Enter` only rewrites it for a thing that snaps. Not looked into (what ends the state is the body animator's `Approached`).
- **A door's leaf moves a standing character at up to 1.83 m/s** with its movement state the standing one: the "no speed limit" colliders as read. Its path does not fit a flat leaf through the hinge.
- **The weapon in hand moved to another slot: the move itself waits for the hands.** The list of what is carried has it in the new slot only after the put-away (0.84 s) and a twentieth of a second more.
- **A draw under an open screen ends in `IDLE` for exactly one frame**, the animator told of the screen on that frame.
- **The quick-use bar's row is centred in its panel:** the views' places as the scene has them, centred, put the view for `5` at 1025.6 of 1920, and the one recorded drop onto it was at 1026.
- **No stock preset has a round in its chamber** (one `patron_in_weapon` among 2,994 preset entries in `globals.json`, 27 `cartridges`): a weapon made from its preset is as the user says a found one is.
- **`ActiveSlot` is set by a draw and by nothing else** (`DetectActiveSlot`, called from the hands' process), and `TrySetInHands` goes by it alone: a character that has never held a weapon does not take one in hand by putting it on. Whether the game does otherwise by a path not found is the one open point of the session.
- **Traps.** `Bash`'s working directory follows a `cd` into the next call: give paths from the workspace's root. The replay's reports gained lines (`hp_...`, `fire_mode`), which threw `metricdiff.sh`'s line-by-line pairing off; it pairs by name now. A test that taps the trigger and then "runs" the weapon has already stepped once inside the tap: the fire state is entered there, and events raised by a call come out with the next step.

## Added 2026-10-12, evening (a kick, the door's menu, a lock; the Five-seveN, the Model 700, the M3's stock)

- **A kick opens a door only from the side it swings away from.** `Door.BreachSuccessRoll` rolls nothing: the door has to be one that can be breached, and the way from the kick's place to the door's aiming point has to run with the sum of the leaf's shut and open lines. The body's animator is told the answer at the key and plays `Stand_Kick` or `Stand_Kick_Fail` by it.
- **A kick is made from a set place**, 1.3 m square off the leaf and half a metre along it from the hinge (`GetBreakInPosition`), and the answer above goes by that place, not by where the character stands.
- **Whether the character is walked there first is the door's own** (`Snap`, a set of the door's states). The two recordings differ: the shut door did (0.209 m in 0.110 s, the view levelled from 9.8 degrees down), the locked one did not. The first reading of the afternoon took "0.261 s both times" for the approach's own length; it is the quarter of a second of the animator's blend into the approach and a frame, and the walk happens inside it.
- **The approach's time is the longest of three:** the walk at 2.5 m/s, the view's turn and its levelling at 90 degrees a second each. Recorded 0.110 s for a levelling of 9.8 degrees (0.109 by the rule).
- **The kick's curve is the class's own** (`KickCurve`, from nothing to all in half a second at a slope of one at both ends): the recorded door's angle is that curve's to 0.000 degrees on every frame, so the prefab did not change it.
- **A kick costs the legs a jump** (`Physical.OnBreach` calls `OnJump`): recorded, 11.2 off the legs on the frame it lands.
- **The kick's motion is remembered for standing's carry-over.** The base movement state notes each frame's motion before it moves (`LastBlendMotionDelta`); with that the body comes to rest 0.650 m on for a recorded 0.649, and without it 0.630.
- **The menu has lines that can never be chosen** (`Bang & clear`, `Flash & clear`, `Move in`: `Disabled = true` in the code), and they are drawn.
- **While a menu is up the wheel is the menu's**, even for one line: the panel blocks both scroll commands, so the walking speed cannot be changed while anything is offered.
- **The install's texts are found whatever the case of the name:** the code asks for `OpenDoor` and the file has `OPENDOOR`.
- **A key's lock id is the key's own template id** (`KeyTemplate.KeyId` is `_id`).
- **Which operation a weapon gets, again in one place:** `BoltAction` gets `BoltActionFireOperation`; `MustBoltBeOpennedForInternalReload` gets the filling with the bolt open. The Model 700's template says `InternalMagazine`, and its stock preset's magazine is the one that stays in: it is filled a round at a time, not by a change of magazine.
- **A bolt action's bolt waits for the trigger**, by one parameter of the animator (`BoltActionReload`), which the operation sets from the trigger only when the trigger changes: the shot's own mark lets the controller's trigger go and does not touch it.
- **Filling with the bolt open throws the chambered round on the ground** (`Prepare` takes it out of the weapon at the key; the chamber's mark throws it), and the bolt takes the first of the rounds loaded when it is shut. A rifle at four and one, topped up, loses a round and is at four and one again.
- **A pistol's slide is caught by the fire state's own mark**, let go by the reload (once), and **always forward after a draw**: the controller clears the catch as the weapon is made.
- **A folded stock is held closer only if the fold ended with the sights down.** `CheckShouldMoveWeaponCloser` asks for the sights and is called from `UpdateWeaponVariables` alone, which aiming does not call.
- **`Weapon.StockRecoilDelta` is used by nothing** read; the fold's effect on recoil is `RecoilDelta` leaving the stock's share out.
- **The stock M3 is five cells wide**, four folded (the spec had guessed four and three).
- **Loading three more of the body's states changed no figure** of any of the sixteen kept recordings (`after33` against `after32`): the animator never enters a state nothing asks for.
- **Traps.** A kick's test with the wrong hinge axis "opens" a door the recording has holding: which way a recorded door's leaf lies is only known from frames where it was looked at shut or open (its gesture by side). `fall_and_move` is not the game's base path for a state's motion: `apply_motion` is, and the difference is the carry-over. The recorder's `working_snap` is the game's word for the interaction in hand; `thing_snap` is asked of the door while it is being worked, and is always false then.

## Added 2026-10-12, night (the sights, a part on its bone, the skill's speeds)

- **The eye is aimed by the sight's bone, not the weapon's.** `FindAimTransforms` takes each sight's `mod_aim_camera` (turned no more than 55 degrees from a half turn), then any on the weapon, and only then the weapon's own `aim_camera`. On the AKM the two are a millimetre apart, which is why the recordings of it never showed the fault; on the M3 13.3 mm, on the Five-seveN 12.0.
- **The iron sights' line is measured each time the sights come up** (`method_14`), from the deepest `mod_align_rear` and `mod_align_front` under the weapon, and cleared as they go down. It gives the eye a shift (up, and along the barrel) and the weapon a turn about the muzzle that is eased in at five a second. The lines of the four weapons are within a fortieth of a degree of the barrel's way, so the turn is nothing to see; the shift is the whole effect.
- **A weapon with no sight is aimed by its own bone**, and the Model 700's lies 7.8 mm left of the bore. Whether the game shows it so is not known here: no recording, no screenshot.
- **A part is put on its bone a quarter turn about x from the bone, or by its own `ModPlacer`** (`ContainerCollectionView.SlotView.InsertItem`). The rule used since the first days (the part's root turned to match the weapon's root) is the same thing for a part on one of the weapon's own bones, and wrong below a part that carries a `ModPlacer`. Of the four weapons' twenty-five parts two carry one: the M3's stock and its forend.
- **The weapon's clips are bound to a part by its path, the part's own name in it**, and place its root every frame where they have curves for it. That hid the old rule's quarter turn on the M3's stock and forend, and left the pistol grip, which no clip touches, standing wrong.
- **A check worth keeping for any new weapon:** `probe clips <id> idle check` says how many bones the idle clip turns and how many rest within a degree of it. All of them should (81 of 81, 79 of 79, 68 of 68, 74 of 74 for the four); under the old rule the M3's stock and forend roots rested a quarter turn from it.
- **The animator's `SpeedReload` and `SpeedDraw` come from the weapon class's skill** (0.004 and 0.008 a level, on top of 1), are told to the body's animator too, and are both 1 with an arm damaged. A holstered pistol's fast draw sets the second from the settings object instead.
- **The kept recordings were made at level 0 of each weapon's skill:** they replay at a speed of 1 with the game's time through every state on every frame, and a single level would put a reload a frame and a half out.
- **Traps.** A capture of the side view at its own size hides a part a quarter turn out; cut the picture and enlarge it (`local\crop.ps1`). "Looks right from the front" proved nothing about the M3: the stock and the forend were right by the clips, not by the rule. A menu's first faint line is the app's own caption, not a line of the game's. From the range's start, the two doors' leaves meet where the view points: the start's side looks at the left-hand (unlocked) door and the yard's at the right-hand (locked) one.


## Added 2026-10-12, late night (the game's input manager, the install's bindings, the options tab)

- **The game hears its keys through one small machine, and it is all readable** (`EFT.InputSystem`, some 3,400 lines): a combination for each variant of each binding, run once a frame in an order (more keys first, double clicks first among equals), each taking its keys from the ones after it. What a hand-written key check gets wrong is never the plain case: it is `T` alone firing with `Left Alt` held, the crouch on a key just used with the wheel, a reload on a key that also has a double click. `docs/spec/settings.md` has the rules; `trp_sim::bindings` is them.
- **This install binds the sprint and the aim as a press, not as held.** A binding that holds has two commands (given, taken back); bound as a `Press` it only ever gives the first, which the player's code treats as "the other of on and off". The app had both held, as the recorder's scripts hold them. **A script that holds a key says nothing about how the key is bound.**
- **A double click on a key delays the key's plain press by the double click's time** (0.3 s in the install, from the first press): the double click's combination runs first, has the key, and only when its time is out gives the key back, as just pressed. The install has one on `R` (the fast reload), on `B` (forced automatic) and on `1` (the pistol's fast draw), so by the code the reload, the fire mode and the pistol's draw are each 0.3 s late in the game. Read from the code and run on the port (a tapped `R` begins its reload 0.32 s after the key went down in the app); no recording was looked at for it.
- **The install's stance is `C` with the wheel**, a variant that ends in an axis (`"isAxis": true, "axisName": "Mouse Scroll"`). The reader had kept only a variant's `keyCode`, so it read the stance's binding as plain `C`: a list of keys is not the whole of a variant.
- **`Jump` and `Vaulting` are two bindings on one key** (`Release` and `Continuous`), which is the whole of "two moves, one key": the vault is given as Space goes down and taken back as it comes up, and both of those only turn the vault's try over.
- **The turn axes carry a sensitivity of their own.** `Control.ini` binds `TurnX` and `TurnY` to the engine's `Mouse X` and `Mouse Y` with `"sensitivity": 5.0`, which the manager multiplies in before the player's own mouse sensitivity. The app's degrees of look for a count of the mouse (0.1, an `ESTIMATE` in `controller.rs`) take no account of it. **Not looked into further**, and not changed: what the engine's own axis gives for a count is still unread, and no recording has the mouse's counts beside the view's turn.
- **The game's words for its bindings are in the install's texts by the binding's own name** (`Shoot` is "Fire", `Duck` "Crouch", `LeanLockRight` "Lean right"); the ways of being pressed are not there under their own names.
- **The display's mode is kept as a figure** (`FullScreenMode`: 0 the screen taken over, 1 borderless, 2 a window), and the field of view is on the game's Game page, not its Graphics page.
- **Traps.**
  - A scripted click put straight into the engine's record of the mouse's buttons is seen by the app's own systems and **not by a button of the screen**: the screen reads a click before the app's systems run, and the record's "just pressed" is cleared at the start of each frame. A click made as the engine's own event for it reaches both (`scripted_drags`). So no capture before this one had clicked a tab or a window's cross, whatever it seemed to.
  - A capture run did not hear the character's keys at all (it holds its own): a run of `--tap` presses with no `--hold` now does, through the bindings, which is the only way the bindings' own path is tried unattended.
  - The engine takes at most twenty systems in one chained group; one more is nested.
  - A sprint that stops by itself 4.4 m ahead of the start has met the crate against the low wall. Turn first (`--yaw 90`).
  - **Look at a scratch file before writing one of its name.** `local\crop.ps1` was overwritten this session by a script of the same name and written again from the workbench's account of it; `local\` is not under git.

## Added 2026-10-12, night into the 13th (the rest of the keys, the mouse, the pointer)

- **The game's reload does begin late, by the double click's time.** Recorded (`local\traces\byhand4`): `R` down on frame 48, up on 55, the reload's operation on frame 85, 0.312 s after the key. The plain press waits for `QuickReloadWeapon`'s double click to fail (0.3 s from the first press) and comes a frame after that, when the key the manager gave back "as just pressed" is seen to be up. Moving the double click off the key ends the wait.
- **The input manager as written here gives a binding on the game's own frame**: the reload once and the inventory's key four times, of five that a recording can show (`bin\byhand`, `keys_heard`). A recording made by hand since recorder 0.6.1 has every key going down and coming up, which is all the manager needs besides each frame's length; the wheel is not on record.
- **Commands come before axes in a frame** (`InputNode.TranslateInput`), and the slow lean is the last of the axes: so "the lean was made slowly" is, to a lean key, what the frame before did.
- **The slow lean goes by frames, not by time** (a tenth of a degree a frame), and the body follows it by an older rule than the lean keys' (`LegacySmoothTilt`, the settings object's `TILT_CHANGING_SPEED`, 10). It stays where it is left.
- **The walk key is the speed wheel put to none**, with what it was put aside; the pace at none is the floor under a walk (0.82 m/s unburdened).
- **A backpack leaves its slot by a gesture of the body's**, state 10 of the interactions' layer, with a mark of its own (`OnBackpackDrop`, a quarter of the way through `tp_backpack`); the body's states carry marks as the weapon's do (`trp_data::states` reads both; `probe bodystates` now prints them). It is the one gesture asked for whatever the weapon's animator is doing, and the one the hands carry out as an operation of their own, which only the resting operation begins.
- **The look all over is the one look the trigger ends**, and the one the sights refuse; the look at the selector puts them down. Both are the same utility operation as the looks at the magazine and into the chamber, each with its own trigger of the animator (`Look`, `FiremodeCheck`).
- **The AKM's look at its magazine lasts 4.33 s**; the look all over 3.33 s; the look at the selector 0.87 s.
- **The mouse's turn for a count is three figures and only two are the install's**: the build's own for `Mouse X` (0.05, in `globalgamemanagers`: the input settings are object class 13, an array of axes each seven strings, three floats, two flags and three whole numbers), the binding's `sensitivity` (5, `Control.ini`), and the engine's halving of a raw count on Windows (0.5), which is the engine's code and nobody's setting. 0.125 degrees a count before the player's sensitivity; the community's converters list the same.
- **One notch of the wheel reads exactly one** (`Mouse Scroll`: the mouse's third axis at one a count), and the manager takes the wheel for pressed only on a frame that reads exactly one: two notches in a frame are none.
- **The head's free look takes the mouse from the turn through the same manager** (`InputAxisCombination` with `Nidnod` as its modifier): while that binding's keys are held the look axes have the mouse's two and the turn has none, and the first frame of each burst of movement gives neither anything. Its key coming up asks for the way back (`ResetLookDirection`). The camera takes the head's turn added to its rotation spring's figure. Read, not built.
- **The game's settings screen is in `level44`** (`Common UI/SettingsScreen/Control Settings`), and the install's texts have its words: the four bars', the two switches', the four ways of being pressed (`EPressType/...`: the late night's note that these are not in the texts was wrong), `Exit`.
- **The engine applies a hold on the pointer only when what is asked changes, and the system drops one whenever another window comes to the front**: a window that asked once is hidden-but-loose after Alt+Tab. Asking from what stands every frame, and for none while the window is not in front, takes it again on coming back. A lock is put where the pointer is, so the pointer is put in the window's middle first.
- **Windows takes a key pressed with Alt held for a letter of the window's menu** (`WM_SYSCHAR`, then `WM_SYSCOMMAND` with `SC_KEYMENU`), and shows the pointer for the moment it looks: once a key, repeats included. Answering those two messages before the window's own procedure does ends it; Alt+F4 is another command and still shuts the window.
- **Traps.**
  - `Process.MainWindowHandle` is not the app's window: it gave a hidden one of 16 by 16. `FindWindowW` by the title (`EFT PC Runtime` then; `TRP (Tushonka Runtime Project)` since 2026-10-14), with a true null for the class (`[NullString]::Value` in PowerShell), is.
  - Whether the pointer shows cannot be found by looking every few milliseconds: a flash is shorter. `SetWinEventHook` for the pointer's showing and hiding (`OBJID_CURSOR`) counts each.
  - `perl` is not on PowerShell's path: `local\apply_edits.pl` is run from Git Bash.
  - A here-document with the spec of an edit fails in the Bash tool as the workbench says; the Write tool, then `apply_edits.pl`.
  - The engine's own `Process` for the app, started by a script, reads and may write `local\options.json` (it is a run someone is at the keys of).

## Added 2026-10-13, early (Alt and the window; the guide)

- **The engine's account of a key going down waits for the letter the system makes of it** (the window layer holds the key until its `WM_CHAR` or `WM_SYSCHAR` has come, to give the press its text). A window procedure set before it that answers `WM_SYSCHAR` therefore swallows every key pressed with Alt held: nothing is heard of it at all. Only `WM_SYSCOMMAND` with `SC_KEYMENU` may be answered; the system sends that to the window after the window's own procedure has had the key and its letter.
- **A key's way through the window is not tried by a capture.** `--tap` puts a press straight into the engine's record of the keys, so everything before that (the window, the system's own handling of Alt) is passed by. What touches the window is tried on the window: `local\window_keys_test.ps1` sends keys as a keyboard does and reads the app's own log for what the character did.
- **A measurement that watches one thing proves one thing.** The night's test counted the pointer's showings and found the fix good; the same fix had stopped the keys. The test now reads both.
- **A borderless window does not flicker under Alt** with or without the fix: it has no menu for the system to look for. The fault shows in a window with a title bar.
- **The app run from a folder that has only `local\eft_dir.txt`** finds the install and has no options file: its first window, the install's bindings, nothing read from or written to the workspace's `local\options.json`. The grid's shader is compiled in, so nothing else is looked for beside the working folder.
- **Traps.**
  - A press put in by a capture is seen for one frame by whatever runs after it in that frame and by nothing that ran before: a system that asks whether a key was just pressed is put after the capture's presses (the guide is, after the weapon's clips).
  - `sed` with a Windows path in its replacement eats the backslashes; the Write tool and `apply_edits.pl` again.

## Added 2026-10-13, a new chat (a weapon that is not the game's: the StG 44)

- **Which hand works the action is the thing a donor decides, and it is not what a weapon's looks suggest.** Run as weapons with no catch (`bin\donor --no-catch`), 57 of the install's 91 rifles, carbines and guns are worked by the right hand in a draw and 34 by the left. The whole Kalashnikov family is worked by the right hand, which leaves the grip for it. The AR-15 family is worked by the right in a draw and by the left after a reload, from behind. On the left side of a rifle with its magazine ahead of the trigger there are only the SCAR, the SA-58, and near them the UMP and the G36.
- **A weapon's trigger mesh is no place to measure from**: it carries the sear's arms, and its middle is 2 to 4 cm over the blade. The hand is: the forefinger's last joint, as the resting clip has the rig's own hand, is on the trigger.
- **With the right hand on the grip, the weapons of one kind are much alike behind the trigger and differ ahead of it**: the right palm's marker is 12 to 15 cm behind the trigger's mesh and 4 to 8 cm under it in the seven looked at; the magazine begins 4 to 9 cm ahead of it, the handle anywhere.
- **The SCAR-L's rig** (`6184055050224f204c1da540`): its bare prefab has the bolt (`weapon_bolt`, with `mod_charge` under it, so the handle goes with the bolt at every shot), `mod_trigger_group` with the hammer, the selector and the trigger, `mod_catch`, `mod_release`, `mod_magazine`, the markers; **its muzzle and its sights are on its parts** (the barrel, the two sights), so a weapon built on the bare prefab has to bring its own. Its handle travels 10.6 cm; its selector turns 45 degrees about the axis across the weapon; a reload from empty with no catch is `RELOAD START`, `RELOAD OUT`, `RELOAD IN`, `RELOAD CHARGE TO IDLE`, 4.5 s.
- **The same animator with and without a catch**: of the left-handed, the SCAR, the SA-58, the UMP and the G36 work the action after a reload from empty when nothing has caught the bolt; the MP5 and the Aug A1 lock it open first all the same.
