- **A pose can be edited where a clip cannot.** A rule on the pose the animator gives out (a fixed shift of a bone; a shift of the left palm's marker that goes by what the palm is near) is the same as an edit to every clip at once, and holds through blends, because a blend of positions is a straight one. It leaves the animator's states, times and marks alone, and with them everything the simulation takes from the weapon.
- **In the simulation the selector's layer follows no switch of fire mode** (found while testing the StG 44's selector; the AKM's is the same: its `weapon_selector` is turned alike in both modes after any number of switches). The layer's two states change on the trigger `FiremodeSwitch`, the hands' layer changes on the same trigger, and the animator here clears a trigger for the transition that uses it, so the layer evaluated second never sees it. The install's controllers are built to have both layers go on one trigger, which suggests the engine lets every layer of an update see it (not looked up, not recorded). **Not changed**: it is the simulation's animator, and outside what was asked. What is drawn of a lever after a switch is the mode it was drawn in; the hands' layer, which the recordings compare, is not touched by it.
- **`Animator::describe` stops on a layer with no states** (index out of bounds): the SCAR-L's first layer has none. Not changed.
- **A binary FBX file** (version 7400): records of a name, typed values and records below, offsets of four bytes (eight from 7500), arrays that may be deflated; the scene in `Objects` and `Connections`. A polygon's last corner is stored as its complement. Normals and texture coordinates are laid on the corners (`ByPolygonVertex`), directly or through an index. This model: centimetres, y up, the barrel along -z, every model at the scene's root with its mesh already in the scene's axes.
- **The model, measured** (its own centimetres): bore y 14.14; gas tube y 16.46; muzzle z -46.42; butt z 46.5; the trigger's face z 11.0 at y 6.6, its pin at (9.44, 11.09); the magazine z -3.55 to 2.22 at the well's mouth (y 7.98), its top y 13.0; the handle's knob at z 1.45, y 16.46, x -0.9 to -2.7, in a slot of the receiver's tube from z 0.43 to 18.1; the handguard z -27.2 to -8.1, its underside about y 13 (a lug under it reaches 11.97); the notch's top (0.40, 19.94), the post's top (-44.03, 19.98); the selector's button about (y 11.33, z 15.58).
- **Pictures of 2048 and 4096 pixels a side, twenty of them, are read and made ready in 0.7 s** on a thread each (`custom::pictures_of`; the 4096 ones halved).
- **Traps.**
  - **A model mirrored into the rig's axes is inside out** unless two corners of every triangle change places. Reasoned the wrong way first ("the engine's front faces are clockwise, so the order stays"): by the numbers a front face has the same sign in both, and a mirroring flips it. The capture showed a white, hollow rifle; the first guess, that the metal was to blame, was wrong and was only seen to be wrong once the faces were right. `a_weapon_of_ones_own_is_built_on_its_donors_rig` now sets every triangle's face beside its normals.
  - **Most of the repository's files have CRLF line ends in the working tree, and `grep -c $'\r'` in Git Bash says they have none.** `file <path>` says. A `perl -pi` or a here-document that adds lines with bare line feeds leaves a file of both kinds. `local\apply_edits.pl` keeps a file's own.
  - `head` on a tool's output ends the tool at its next line: `donor --overlay ... | head -1` wrote no picture.
  - A figure that the game adds up over a build must not be given twice: a template set to a build's whole ergonomics, with its own magazine then added by the game's rule, came to 37 for the AKM's 40.

## Added 2026-10-13, midday (a character seen from outside)

The whole account is `docs/spec/third-person.md`. What is worth knowing without it:

- **There is one class for a character and a switch on it** (`Player.PointOfView`). A bot is that class made in third person (`LocalGame`, `EPointOfView.ThirdPerson`), and so would be another player of an offline raid. `EFT.NextObservedPlayer` (some two hundred classes) is the live game's own way of showing a player from the network's messages and is never made here: **a rule taken from it would be one this install never runs.**
- **`ChangePointOfView` (`Home` in this install) is a binding with no handler**: the method that flips the switch is in `PlayerInputTranslator` and nothing calls it. The switch is a public property, and the recorder may set it (0.7.0, the key `\`).
- **Every curve that hands the weapon or an arm to the body is multiplied by `First_Person_Curve_Weight` for the character's own eyes and by 1 for any other.** That one line of `VisualPass` is most of the difference: the body's weapon bone has the weapon whole at rest (`Weapon_3rd` is 1 in the standing, walking and sprinting clips), and both hands are still solved onto it.
- **The upper arms are not put on the weapon rig's from outside** (`SetShoulders` is in the first-person branch), and **the ribcage is turned to the view only as part of the first-person squeeze** (`FirstPersonStrategy.ApplyFovAdjustments`).
- **The weapon's clips tell the body what to do with it** by a mark, `ThirdAction <number>`, which sets the body animator's `FirstAction`: 2 in the put-away's states, 1 in the draw's (0.008 to 0.010 of the way in, on the stock AKM and on the empty hands). The body's layer 1 plays it; that layer's weight is 0 in first person.
- **The weapon's rig carries a marker for the vest** (`weapon_vest_IK_marker`, under the weapon's bone), and the body's reach for the vest is weighed by how near the left hand's marker comes to it (0.5 m to 0.2 m), from outside only.
- **The weapon's motion from outside is `ThirdPersonStrategy`**: the turn-away, an aim pose, the two springs and the recoil. No sway, breath, bob or reaction, and the recoil kicks the weapon back but does not turn it. **A bot's mask is the turn-away alone** (`BotOwner.Create`), so a bot's weapon does not kick at all; the prefab's mask is 125.
- **The prefab's turn-away has a second pair of figures for every component** (`AltIntensity`, `AltIntensity1`): a rifle is drawn back 0.27 m from outside where its own eyes see 0.40.
- **The player component is added in code** (`Player.Create`: `AddComponent`), so a public field of `Player` with a value in the code is that value: the prefab has no say (the reach's two distances and its fade).
- **Standing still, the pose from outside and the first-person pose looked at from outside nearly coincide** in a capture. That is the data's doing (the body's weapon bone rests where the first-person rig holds the rifle), not a sign that nothing changed: the differences are in motion (the breath, the lean into a walk, no sway) and in what the hands' work adds.
- **The app's "what is looked at" went by the camera**, which is only right while the camera is the eyes. It goes by an entity at the eyes now (`weapon::Eye`); in first person the two are the same transform.
- **Traps.**
  - **A here-document in the Bash tool fails on an apostrophe** in its text (`unexpected EOF while looking for matching`), quoted delimiter or not. Text with apostrophes goes into a file with the Write tool.
  - **The app's link can fail with "unresolved external symbol anon..." out of its own incremental build** after a run of small edits. `cargo clean -p trp-app` and a rebuild (a minute and a half) put it right; nothing in the code was at fault.
  - **A capture from `--view body` with a `--start` before a wall has the camera inside the wall**: the outside camera stands to the front and left of where the character faces.

## Added 2026-10-13, afternoon (the head's free look; every weapon counted)

The free look's whole account is `docs/spec/free-look.md`.

- **The look's two axes are the mouse's with a modifier** (`InputAxisCombination(EAxis.LookX, EGameKey.Nidnod)`): one pair of them for each variant of the binding that has keys. An axis takes the mouse's on a frame of travel with the keys held and **gives nothing on that frame**; a frame with no travel along it lets it go. So every run of travel along an axis loses its first frame, to the turn and to the look alike.
- **The look has no sensitivity**: `Player.Look` is handed the two axes as they come (0.125 degrees a count here), where the turn goes through the player's multiplier.
- **The mouse "has the head" only from a frame that moves it on both axes at once** (`_mouseLookControl`); until then the way back runs every frame against the travel.
- **`Player.Look`'s first argument is named `deltaLookY` and is the travel across**, and its second, `deltaLookX`, the travel down: the names are crossed in the code, the use is not.
- **What is shown of the turn down is worked out before the frame's way back**, so it trails the turn itself by a frame while the head returns.
- **`Nidnod` is "Free look" in the install's English texts**, bound to the middle mouse button and to its release (the release is the way back's command, `ResetLookDirection`).
- **The settings object's two-number fields are not printed by `probe hardsettings`** (it lists the plain numbers); `HardSettings::vec2` reads them. The free look's limits there are the code's own.
- **The install's weapons, counted** (`probe weapons`): 163 items, 149 with a stock preset, every one of which assembles and has its animator read; 97 distinct sets of clips, 91 of states. The fourteen without a preset are the stationary guns, the signal flares and two that the database has no build for. Families that share one set of clips: the AKM's six, the AK-101 to 105's five, the Desert Eagle's five, the four AR-15s. By `ReloadMode`, `BoltAction` and `weapClass`: the four logics built (magazine and self-loading, pistol, tube, bolt with a fixed magazine) are what 128 of the 148 go by; the other 20 need a bolt action whose magazine comes out (5), a revolver's cylinder (5, one of them a shotgun), a self-loader filled through its action (4), barrels loaded one by one (5), and a launcher's drum (1). That is the database's properties alone: it does not say how far a family's own states differ from the weapon its logic was written for.
- **Every weapon as an animation library: what was recommended to the user, not decided** (2026-10-13; weapons past the four and the StG 44 are not to be coded until told).
  - The install is the library already: a weapon's rig, animator, clips and marks are read from it on demand and nothing of a weapon is kept. All 149 read today. So "adding every weapon" is not a matter of animations.
  - What it is a matter of: the app builds every rig it knows at its start (five now; 149 would have to be built when first taken in hand); the 20 weapons whose kind of logic is not written; the differences inside the kinds that are (an open bolt, a belt, a slide that is cocked by the trigger), which only show when each family is run; and a recording for each kind before anything is called the game's.
  - What serves a weapon of one's own or another game's mesh is a **catalogue of donors**: for each of the 97 distinct animators, the measurements `bin\donor` takes (which hand works the action and on which side, where the handle, the magazine, the grips, the muzzle and the butt are), for every class and not only the 91 rifles, carbines and guns; written to `local\` when asked for, and a weapon of one's own choosing its donor by its own measurements.
  - The order put to the user: the catalogue; then rigs built as they are needed and any weapon of a kind already built to be had by `--weapon` and from the crate; then the kinds not built, one at a time, each with a short recording.
- **Traps.**
  - **`sed -i` in Git Bash rewrites a file with bare line feeds.** A file that had CRLF ends has none after it, and one edited in between by another tool has both. Edits go through `local\apply_edits.pl` or the Edit tool, which keep a file's own. `perl -e 'binmode STDIN; ...'` counts both kinds truly, and `git ls-files --eol <file>` says `w/mixed` for a file of both.
  - **`apply_edits.pl` writes nothing if any one block is not found**: a wrong word in the eighth block leaves the first seven unapplied too, and the run says only which block.
  - **A turn of nothing added to a figure can change its sign of zero**: where the free look adds to the camera's spring, it adds only while the head is turned, so that a run with no free look is the same to the last bit.

## Added 2026-10-13, evening (the five kinds; the donors' catalogue; rigs when needed)

- **Which logic a weapon runs on is three words of its template**: `ReloadMode` (`ExternalMagazine`, `InternalMagazine`, `OnlyBarrel`), `BoltAction`, and whether the item is of the class `Revolver`. The firing: `FirearmController.GetOperationFactoryDelegates`. The reload: the reload key's own test in `FirearmHandsInputTranslator`. A launcher with a drum is a `Revolver` to the game, with a `SpringDrivenCylinder` for its magazine. The full account: `docs/spec/more-weapons.md`.
- **Two of the five kinds needed no code at all.** The SV-98 (a bolt action with a magazine that comes out) and the SKS (a self-loader filled through its action) each ran right on its own animator the first time it was tried: what was written for the Model 700, the M3 and the AKM goes by the template's flags (`MustBoltBeOpennedForExternalReload`, `MustBoltBeOpennedForInternalReload`, `isBoltCatch`) and the animator's own states. That is nine more of the install's weapons (five and four) on logic already there.
- **A cylinder has no chamber of the weapon's.** A revolver's `Chambers` is empty; its rounds are in the slots `camora_000` onward of its magazine, a `CylinderMagazine`. `Firearm::magazine` counts the rounds in all of them and `chamber` is never set, so the display reads "6 + 0".
- **A double barrel's chambers are numbered**: `patron_in_weapon_000` and `_001`, where every other weapon's one chamber is `patron_in_weapon`. Anything that looked for the chamber by that exact name missed them (the loose rounds in reach did).
- **The game's inventory has the rounds in the barrels before the reload's hands begin.** `ReloadMultiBarrelResult.Run` moves each round to its chamber's address as the reload is asked for, and `ReloadMultiBarrelOperation.Start` then tells the animator `Weapon.ChamberAmmoCount`: the count after loading. The MP-43's animator needs that: its `IDLE` goes to `RELOAD START` with `AmmoInChamber > 0`, or with `ShellsInWeapon > 0`, and its third way, written for a weapon with neither, is `AmmoInChamber < 0 & ShellsInWeapon < 0`, which no count ever is (the MP-18's own is `< 1` and `< 1`). Told the rounds it had, an empty MP-43 never began a reload here; the game's would by the first way. The same may hold for other operations that run an inventory move first: **look at what the model holds when the animator is told, not at what it held when the key went down.**
- **Fired cases stay** in a cylinder and in barrels (`Weapon.ShellsInChambers`) until a reload's mark takes them out; a revolver's come out one at a time by the mark `RemoveShell` while a round is still in the cylinder, and all at once from an empty one (its animator has `RELOAD START ALL` for that).
- **A revolver's hammer is state of the weapon** (`CylinderHammerClosed`), set at the shot's mark `AddAmmoInChamber` by the fire mode, and it decides which chamber is next: the one under it while cocked, the one after while it is down. Double action from a hammer that is down plays `DOUBLE_ACTION_FIRE` outright, as bursts' and automatic fire's states are played.
- **The donors' catalogue ranks as a day of measuring did.** From six figures of the StG 44 (bore, muzzle, butt, magazine, handle ahead and up) and three words (a rifle, worked by the left hand, on the left), the first four are the SCAR-L, the G36, the SCAR-H and the SA-58. What a rank cannot see is why the G36 was passed over by hand (its handle is on top). The catalogue has 95 lines for 146 weapons; fourteen weapons have no stock preset and the TOZ-106's animator has no trigger the run can press.
- **A rig costs 0.4 to 0.8 s to build** (the MP-43 0.36 s, the M3 0.84 s), all of it in the one frame its weapon is hung on a slot. Ten weapons built before the first frame had been making every start longer; built when hung, a start is the run's own weapon and no more.
- **Counted by one barrel**: of the five weapons with `OnlyBarrel`, three have a single barrel (the MP-18, the FN40GL, the RShG-2) and the game reloads those by another operation (`ReloadSingleBarrelOperation`), which is not built; the rocket launcher has a firing of its own as well. With those two, every weapon of the install with a stock preset would have its logic.
- **Traps.**
  - **A weapon's name in the database is not unique by its family's word**: `--weapon rhino` is the Rhino 200DS, the first by name; `rhino_50ds` is the one in the crate.
  - **Pure perl takes five minutes to read `items.json`** (`JSON::PP` on 19 MB). For a few figures of a few items, `awk` from the item's `"_id"` line to the first line with the figure is instant; for anything more, a `probe` command.
  - **`local\donors\catalogue.tsv` is as old as the logic that made it**: made before the cylinder and the barrels were written, it had those weapons reloaded as tubes. Make it again after a change to the weapon's logic.
  - **`cd` in a Bash call moves every later call**, the tools' own included: a call that began `cd /e/stlkreft` left the next ones a folder up. Paths from `TRP` are given whole, or the call begins by going there.

## Added 2026-10-13, night (a body from the other game: the Sunrise suit)

- **Where the other game keeps things.** GAMMA is a Mod Organizer folder (`E:\GAMMAEFT`): 808 mods under `mods\`, each with a `gamedata` of loose files; `ModOrganizer.ini` names the profile (`selected_profile=@ByteArray(G.A.M.M.A)`) and `profiles\<profile>\modlist.txt` lists every mod with `+` or `-`, **the one that wins first**. Anomaly itself (`E:\AnomalyEFT`) has little loose (`gamedata\configs`, `scripts`, `textures\ui`): its models and pictures are in packed archives under `db\` (`meshes\meshes_actors.db0`, `textures\textures_actors.db0` and `.db1`, half a gigabyte each).
- **An outfit's body is named by its item**: the section's `actor_visual` (and `npc_visual`), a path under `meshes`; `player_hud_section` names the arms the player sees, another model. The Sunrise suit is `stalker_outfit` and `actors\stalker_neutral\stalker_neutral_2.ogf`.
- **The model's format is in the engine's published source** and reads as that says the first time: see `docs/spec/outfits.md`. Its space is Unity's own kind (left-handed, y up, z ahead), so nothing is mirrored between the two games; Tarkov's clothing meshes are in a third arrangement of the same hand (z up, facing along minus y).
- **Tarkov's clothing is made with the arms forward**, not out to the sides: the upper arm from the shoulder out, forward and down, the forearm straight ahead, the palm half turned inward (the knuckles run from the first finger, in and up, to the little finger, out and down): the pose of holding a rifle. A body made with its arms out has to be brought to that, and its forearm turned along its length.
- **The two skeletons, set side by side** (`probe ogf`, `probe skin`; metres, each in its own space):

  | | The other game's | Tarkov's |
  |---|---|---|
  | head's joint over the ground | 1.656 | 1.658 |
  | hips apart | 0.240 | 0.182 |
  | shoulders apart | 0.468 | 0.325 (arms forward) |
  | shoulders under the head's joint | 0.118 | 0.182 |
  | thigh, calf | 0.397, 0.471 | 0.455, 0.461 |
  | upper arm, forearm | 0.283, 0.250 | 0.297, 0.252 |
  | ankle's joint over the sole | 0.133 | 0.063 |
  | bones of the spine | pelvis, three, neck (the chest all one bone, 0.4 m of it) | pelvis, three, neck (the chest two) |
  | fingers | three, of three joints | five, of three joints |
  | along a forearm, a thigh | one bone | three, two |

  The same height and nearly the same limbs, but a figure a quarter broader, with its shoulders higher under its head. **Laying bone on bone would have halved the chest or doubled the belly, and tipped the boots 24 degrees** (the line from ankle to toe slopes 40 degrees on one and 16 on the other, the soles being flat on both).
- **What the fit does about each**: `docs/spec/outfits.md`, "The fit" and "The rig". **Two ways were built, and the second kept.** The first moved the mesh onto Tarkov's joints: every bone stretched to Tarkov's length, the trunk narrowed and re-spaced to Tarkov's hips, shoulders and head. Everything met and nothing tore, and it was the wrong figure: slim, long in the thigh, its fingers half as long again. **A mesh fitted to another skeleton's joints takes that skeleton's build; to keep its own, the skeleton it is drawn on has to have its joints.** So the second way leaves the mesh alone but for its pose, and puts the animation on the mesh's own lengths: the same turn for every bone, the three things that must be where the simulation has them (hips, hands, feet) taken as they are, and the two-bone limbs between solved again. That is a matter of drawing only: the simulation gives the same result.
- **What has to stay where** decides the rig: a hand is on the weapon and a foot on the ground, so those are Tarkov's own bones and the limb gives; the hips carry everything, so those are Tarkov's too and the trunk hangs from them at its own heights. An elbow and a knee are then nowhere fixed, and go where the lengths put them, bent to Tarkov's side.
- **A limb's side to bend to is its middle joint off its own line** (from where it hangs to where it ends), not its first bone's direction: taken the second way, a nearly straight limb bends sideways.
- **In first person the arms are squeezed with the weapon** (the weapon rig's root is scaled along the view), so the bones under that root do not have their true lengths as the engine has them. The rig is posed with the squeeze taken out (the root's place and turn, times the inverse of the root as it is) and its squeezed bones are hung, through two entities, from that root again.
- **The other game's packed archives** (looked at only then; read since 2026-10-14, night, "Added 2026-10-14, night" below): a file of chunks like a model's. Chunk 666 is a short text header; chunk 0 the files' bytes; chunk 1 the table of files, **packed** (its number has the top bit set, its body begins with the unpacked length: 94,129 for `textures_actors.db0`), by the engine's own LZH (`_decompressLZ`), and each file may be packed by LZO (`rtc_decompress`). The table's entries: a length, then real size, packed size, a checksum, the name, an offset (`LocatorAPI.cpp`, `ProcessArchive`). Two decoders to write, both published; Anomaly ships a tool that unpacks them (`tools\db_unpacker.bat`), which writes into its own folder and is the user's to run.
- **An armour of the install's cannot be put on without its soft inserts** (`missing_vital`: its slots `Soft_armor_front` and the rest are required). An item made of an armour's template with empty slots is refused with "no free slot"; the outfit's template has no slots.
- **Bevy takes at most twenty systems in one tuple**: the app's chain of the frame was at twenty; a twenty-first goes in as a pair with its neighbour.
- **Traps.**
  - **A model's "up" is not hips to head.** A head two centimetres behind the hips tips an "up" taken that way by 1.6 degrees, and the feet with it. Up and right are the axes of the model's own space nearest those directions.
  - **A scripted drop goes by the item's first cell**, not its middle: a thing of three cells by three let go with the mouse at the middle of a free area lands a cell too far right and down ("the place is taken"). Let it go on the area's first cell.
  - **A capture's frame numbers are not seconds at any one rate.** `--tap` and `--screenshot-frame` count drawn frames, and how much time a drawn frame is changes through a run (a tapped `R` at frame 320 had its reload in frames 333 to 443, a 3.3 s reload in 110 frames; at the start a frame is 6 ms). To catch a moment, run once with `--log-frames <file>` and read which frames the state wanted is on (`awk '{print $5}' file | uniq -c`), then ask for one of those.

## Added 2026-10-13, late night (the suit's own arms for first person)

- **The other game has a second model for the player's own arms.** An outfit's `player_hud_section` names a section (in `gamedata\configs\creatures\actor_hud_hand_animations.ltx` of a hands mod) whose `visual` is the arms: for the Sunrise suit `anomaly_weapons\hands\wpn_hand_loner_sunrise`, loose in `23- THAP Rework` with its two pictures. Two arms from a root, 42 bones, five fingers of three joints, a bone more near the wrist for the forearm's turn; the thumb is `l_finger0`, the other fingers `bip01_l_finger1` to `4`.
- **Its hand is a bare hand's size and Tarkov's skeleton is a gloved hand's.** Measured (`probe ogf`, which now says how far each bone's vertices reach along it and across): the knuckles 5.9 cm across for Tarkov's 7.5; a finger's first joint 4.2 cm for 5.9; the hand's joint two centimetres inside the palm, 7.0 cm from the middle knuckle, for Tarkov's wrist 10.9 cm from it. The arms themselves are of a size (forearm 26.6 cm for 25.1, upper arm 27.1 for 29.7).
- **A hand is put on another's by its knuckles**, not its wrist: `docs/spec/outfits.md`, "Why knuckle on knuckle". With the middle knuckles together, the other three knuckles of this hand are within 1.1 cm of Tarkov's.
- **Arms for one's own eyes go back from the hand; arms on a body come down from the shoulder.** The body's arm hangs at the body's shoulder and is bent anew to reach the hand, because the shoulder is seen. The first-person arm was first hung the same way from Tarkov's shoulder and came out 2 cm too short to reach its own wrist at rest (a shorter arm, and a wrist further on for the knuckles): it would have been straight in every pose. Held at the wrist and turned as Tarkov's forearm instead, it comes into the picture exactly as Tarkov's arm does, and what is left over is at the shoulder, which nobody sees.
- **What looked like a thumb out of place was the glove's strap.** In first person a dark knob shows beside the left wrist; from the side, with the upper body left out, it is a tab of the glove's own model hanging under the wrist, and the thumb is forward along the handguard as Tarkov's is. Looking from a second side settled in a minute what the numbers had not.
- **A model for the player's own eyes has no size in the world.** The other game draws its first-person arms in a space and a field of view of their own, and their file's size is whatever suited that. On Tarkov's weapons at that size the hands were too small and nowhere Tarkov's hands are (the user saw it at once in two screenshots side by side). The size that fits is measured, not chosen: the breadth of the hand at the knuckles on the two skeletons, 7.5 cm over 5.9, 1.266. At that size every finger's joints fall within a centimetre of Tarkov's, which no laying of the smaller hand could do. **The test for arms in first person is the same frame of the same animation with Tarkov's own arms**, one capture beside the other; the joint-by-joint figures said "1.4 to 2.6 cm short" all along, and it took the pictures to read that as wrong and not as a property.
- **"Keep its proportions" was said of the body.** It was carried over to the arms' model as a reason to leave its size alone, which was not what had been asked.

## Added 2026-10-13, late (the look's stutter; a page drawn every frame; a hand's size)

- **The look at a weapon stutters 30 times a second, and it is the clip.** `akm_look` keeps 267 of its 837 curves as samples at 30 a second (the engine's dense curves), the weapon's own place and turn and each hand's marker among them, and such samples are joined by straight lines (`Clip::sample`, as the engine's published rule for dense curves has it). Each curve alone is then a path of straight pieces; but a hand's place in the world is the weapon's turn applied to the marker's place on the weapon, two straight pieces multiplied, which is right at each sample and bowed between them. The weapon's turn is kept as three Euler angles, and through the look's fast turns two of them run at 450 to 940 degrees a second against each other with the third near 80 degrees, where they all but cancel: the turn that is left is 80 to 200 degrees a second, and its error between samples is the larger for it. Followed from the clip alone (`probe clips 59d6088586f774275f37482f akm_look path weapon_L_IK_marker 2.55 2.85 0.004`), the marker's speed along one axis runs from 0 to 0.8 m/s inside one thirtieth of a second and snaps back at the next sample, five samples running; the hand is up to 3 mm off a smooth path, 30 times a second. **The app draws exactly that**: the drawn left palm's travel a frame, at 165 frames a second, climbs for five or six frames and snaps back. All ten weapons the app carries show it in their own looks. **Not compared with the game**: there is no recording of a look. The reloads, which were compared frame for frame, are made of the same kind of curves. (Later the same day, at the user's word, the app draws the look all over on curves through the samples; the simulation still has the straight lines. Three bullets at this part's end, and `docs/spec/firing.md`.)
- **How it was found, and what was ruled out first.** The complaint came after the suit's arms were made larger, so the suit's rig was measured first: every joint of it and of the install's written out on every frame of a look, and the two sets' second differences set side by side. They are the same to the hundredth of a millimetre a frame. Then the frame times (6.06 ms, 0.3 ms either way, none over 12) and the camera's turn (smooth to the log's last figure). Only then the palm's own path, where the period (5.5 frames at 6.05 ms) named the cause before the clip was opened.
- **A resource that is written is a resource that has changed.** `std::mem::take(&mut carried.pack_asked)` on a `ResMut` marks the whole resource changed on every frame, flag set or not; so does any `&mut` through it. The inventory's page is drawn afresh whenever `Carried` has changed, and was on every frame from the night the backpack's key went in. Nothing looked wrong: the page was the same page. What broke was whatever asks a part of the page whether the mouse is over it after the page is drawn (a part just made does not know), which was the crate's wheel and its bar. **Look before writing** (`if carried.pack_asked { carried.pack_asked = false }`), and count: the page now warns if it is drawn on 120 frames running. A capture did not catch it because `--wheel` set the scroll outright; it sends the engine's own wheel events now.
- **A hand's size is on its skin, not in its joints.** Two skeletons put the knuckles' joints at other places inside the hand: the other game's arms' model has them 5.9 cm apart in a hand as broad as Tarkov's, which has them 7.5 cm apart. Sized so that the joints were as far apart (1.27 times), the hand was 8 to 13% broader than Tarkov's own glove. Sized by where its skin is (the furthest point on the little finger's side in slices behind the knuckles; the thumb's side is no measure, a hand reaches there as far as its thumb lies): 1.12. With Tarkov's bare hands beside it in the same pose the first was plainly too large, which the joint-by-joint figures had not said.
- **Fingers want the other skeleton's joints, not its angles alone.** A finger turned by another skeleton's angles about its own joints ends where that skeleton's finger ends only if the joints are at the same places. The model's thumb began 2.3 cm from Tarkov's with a first bone a third longer, and was within a centimetre of Tarkov's at rest and two centimetres off about a grip. Put joint on joint (each piece made as long as Tarkov's along its bone), it is on Tarkov's in every pose.
- **Tarkov's waist** is the top of its mesh of the legs: 1.082 m at rest on the default legs, 12.0 cm over the thighs' joints (the mesh goes on up under the top, which is why the character's own eyes do not see into it). The two skeletons have the bone they call the pelvis 3 cm apart in height with the thighs' joints 2.5 cm apart: measure a waist from the thighs.
- **Which of the install's hands are bare.** Their bundles' names do not say. Their pictures do (`probe texstats <bundle> <word> <folder>`): the BEAR's default are Mechanix gloves; the Telnik's, the Hawaiian shirts' and the scavs' are skin.
- **`--play <clip> --at <seconds>` holds a clip still**, the arms solved onto it: two runs with two sets of arms are then the same pose to the frame, which a look started with a key in two runs is not.
- **Samples come in two kinds of keeping.** A clip's curves are kept as cubics from key to key, as samples at a steady rate, or as one figure (`probe clips <weapon> <clip> path <bone>` says which, bone by bone). **A curve kept as cubics may be samples all the same**: a key at every thirtieth of a second, each span a straight line (its two upper terms nothing). The Five-seveN's look is all of that kind where the AKM's is samples, and it breaks at every key just as the AKM's breaks at every sample. Anything done for the one kind has to be done for the other; the first cut of the smooth drawing was not, and only running all ten weapons showed it.
- **A pose for drawing beside the pose.** The animator can work its pose out twice from the same states at the same moments, once as the engine samples a clip and once on curves through the samples, and give the second to whoever draws. The first is still what every rule reads. It costs the look a second sampling of its clips and nothing at any other time. (The body's animator has had `drawn_bones` since the body was drawn; this is the same idea for the weapon's.)
- **Second differences of a place want even frames.** A hand's travel a frame, less its travel the frame before, is a fair measure of a stutter only if the two frames are as long: one frame of 8.6 ms and the next of 3.5 make 4.7 mm of it out of nothing. Divide by the frames' lengths, or measure on the animator alone at a fixed step (`tests\look.rs`).

## Added 2026-10-13, night (a hand's size again; where a wrist bends; one switch)

- **"A hand's size is on its skin, not in its joints" (the part before this) was half right, and its measure was wrong.** The measure took one edge of the hand: how far the skin reaches on the little finger's side from the middle of the knuckles' line. The suit's arms' model has its palm most of a centimetre to that side of the install's when the two are laid knuckle on knuckle (at 1.27: 0.4 cm short of the install's glove on the first finger's side, 1.2 cm over on the little finger's). So one edge said "8 to 13% too broad" of a hand that from edge to edge is 9% broader, and the size that made that one edge agree, 1.12, left the whole hand a twelfth narrower than it had been reckoned. **A breadth wants both edges** (`outfit::palm_box`).
- **And a hand whose fingers are another skeleton's has a size already.** The fingers of these arms are made as long as the install's, joint on joint, whatever the size. Any other size for the palm and the wrist then makes a hand out of proportion to its own fingers: at 1.12 they were a ninth too small for them, which the user saw as "wrists too small, palms not wide enough" on every pose. Three measures of the size agree within three in a hundred: the knuckles' joints as far apart as the install's (1.266); the palm's skin over the band behind the knuckles, from the furthest point of one edge to the furthest of the other (1.26); the hand from where its own skin bends to its knuckle as long as the install's from wrist to knuckle (1.24).
- **The reduction to 1.12 had been this work's own judgement, written down as the user's.** The spec said "two sizes before this one were wrong, each said by the user"; the log has the user's words for the first only (the file's size: "too small"). Of 1.27 the user said nothing about size; "plainly too bulky" was a reading of a capture. A reading of one's own goes into the documents as that.
- **A model's wrist joint is not where its wrist bends.** These arms have the hand's joint 2.0 cm nearer the knuckles than the install's skeleton has its own (7.0 cm from the middle knuckle in the file for the install's 10.9; a finger's first joint 4.2 for 5.9: the ratio of the one to the other says so, 1.67 for the install's 1.85), and the skin's own change from the hand's bone to the forearm's is 1.8 cm behind that joint: at the joints' size, where the install's wrist is. **A forearm turned about the wrong point leaves the other skeleton's forearm by twice the sine of half the bend times how far off the point is**: 3 cm off, bent thirty degrees, 1.5 cm; sixty, 3 cm. At rest it is nothing, and at rest is where everything had been measured. The thin wrist in the user's captures was a hand holding a magazine out, its wrist well bent.
- **A measure made at rest says nothing of a pose.** Every figure of the fit (joints, skin, the rig that moves nothing at rest) was right while the forearm stood a centimetre and a half out of place in the pictures. Ask of each bone of a rig what it is turned about, and whether the other skeleton turns its own about the same point.
- **Two skins compared want the same weights, or none.** "What goes mostly with the palm's bone" is one stretch of skin on the install's hand and another on the other game's (the ball of the thumb and the web go with the palm on one and with the thumb's first bone on the other). A box about it is good for the breadth behind the knuckles, where neither has a thumb; through the hand the two boxes were of different things, and a palm moved to fill the install's box (built, and taken out the same night) came out a centimetre and a half too far forward with a step at the thumb.
- **Strip by strip says what a box cannot.** The palm's two faces, in strips two centimetres wide across the hand: at the joints' size the suit's hand is as thick as the install's glove everywhere, has its back a centimetre or two further out at the little finger and its face half a centimetre short there. It is a flatter hand with a fuller edge, not a smaller or a displaced one. Left as it is.
- **Two sets of arms in one picture.** `EFT_OUTFIT_ARMS=both` draws the outfit's arms and the install's own at once. Where each shows is where it is the outer skin. One capture of that said in a minute what three tables of slices had not: which side of the hand was short.
- **The pose that shows a hand is not the pose it was fitted by.** The look all over the rifle at 1.0 and 2.0 s (the part before this) shows the fingers on the weapon, which the joints settle. The open palm towards the eye (1.5 s) shows its breadth; the look at the magazine (`akm_check_0`, 2.6 s) shows the hand from the thumb's side with its wrist bent, which is its thickness and its wrist.
- **A switch that something draws by is a resource of its own.** The options tab's state is written on every frame its screen is open (it is borrowed to write), so a thing that asked "has it changed?" of that state would run every frame. The switch is kept twice: in the tab's state, for the file, and as `Features`, written only when it is thrown.
- **A long here-document fails in this shell** (again): a spec of 130 lines for `apply_edits.pl` given as one ended the command with "unexpected EOF". The Write tool, then the script.

## Added 2026-10-14 (the hands' animations drawn smooth, all or none)

- **The reloads stutter as the look does, and a mean does not show it.** A clip's samples joined by straight lines give a hand a velocity that is constant from one sample to the next and steps at each: frame by frame through the AKM's reload, the left palm's speed falls by 0.10 to 0.16 m/s every five or six frames at 165 a second and by next to nothing between. Drawn on curves through the samples, it falls by 0.02 to 0.05 every frame. The mean change of velocity a frame is the same for both (0.39 and 0.38 mm): a staircase down and a ramp down add up to the same fall. **Measure a stutter as what a frame's change is off the changes about it** (`local\palm_jerk_s46.awk`: less the mean over seven frames), and read its middle and its ninth tenth: a few large changes that both have (a hold taken over from one marker to another, 8 to 9 mm) rule a mean or a root mean square.
- **A drawing that asks the animator for more costs a sampling of its clips more**: the simulation's share of a drawn frame went from 0.06 to 0.07 ms with every animation of the hands drawn smooth.
- **A switch the simulation is told every frame survives an animator made afresh.** `WeaponAnimator::reset` makes a new `Animator` (for every draw), which starts as the engine has it; the weapon passes the host's choice on at every step, so nothing is lost at a draw.

## Added 2026-10-14, later (a weapon drawn invisible; what a rig costs; pictures re-packed)

- **A thing shown in the frame it is made is hidden by its own making.** `RigBuilder::build` spawned a rig's entities and inserted `Visibility::Hidden` on its weapon bone through `Commands`, which take effect at the frame's end; the inventory, in the same system and frame, took the weapon in hand and set the bone visible through a query, which found no entity and did nothing. Then the commands ran: hidden. Floating hands, until the weapon was put away and drawn again. It happens when a weapon is hung on the slot the hands last drew from while they are empty: it is taken at once (reproduced with the M3 on the sling, `--loot --equip` and drags). **A rig is taken from the frame after it is in the world** (`Stowed::fresh`), whatever makes it.
- **A rig's cost was its pictures, and not their reading.** Timed piece by piece: the M3 0.83 s, of which assembling 0.06, its logic and animator 0.07, its meshes 0.01, reading its pictures 0.02, its colour pictures 0.01 (they go to the graphics card compressed as they are), and **its normal and gloss pictures 0.70**: each decoded from DXT to four bytes a pixel, every level, on the CPU, and repacked to two channels. 48 to 52 ms a 2048-pixel picture. The start decoded the body's, the arms' and the first weapon's: 1.6 s.
- **DXT re-packs as BC5 without pixels.** A DXT5's alpha block is a BC4 block; a channel of a DXT colour block is four values on a line between two endpoints, and BC4 holds four values on a line exactly, on its steps 0, 2, 4 and 6 of eight, with its second endpoint a sixth of the range past the line's far end. Where that endpoint would pass 0 or 255, the line on steps 1 to 7, or the six-step kind with 0 and 255 of its own; then the nearest. A DXT1 block of three colours and black has its middle colour within a tenth of its range. A 2048-pixel normal or gloss picture: 4 ms (`texture::two_channels`). Bevy 0.16 reads a BC5 normal map as two-channel (`TWO_COMPONENT_NORMAL_MAP`) and a BC5 roughness picture's green as roughness.
- **The first cut was slow for its allocation**: a candidate list built on the heap for every block, and a search through it, made it 100 ns a block; a direct path for a line of four (nearly every block) made it 12.
- **A rig made away from the frame**: the thread has its own reader of the install and its own database (made in 0.09 s), and returns meshes, pictures and materials as the engine's own values; the frame puts them into the stores and spawns the tree in 0.1 to 0.3 ms. The crate's nine rigs take some 2.1 s of that thread.
- **What a rig brings to the graphics card can stop a frame on its own.** The StG 44's pictures are uncompressed (its own PNGs, four bytes a pixel): its arrival stood a frame still for 100 to 137 ms with the frame's own work under a millisecond. The upload budget (`RenderAssetBytesPerFrame`, a picture or so a frame) took it to 105 ms, so it was not the copying alone; the curtain waiting for the crate's rigs puts all of it before play.
- **Tarkov's own icon cache on this machine has no items** (`%TEMP%\Battlestate Games\EscapeFromTarkov\Icon Cache\live`: clothing and player icons only), so the inventory's pictures cannot be taken from it.

## Added 2026-10-14, evening (the inventory's pictures, the game's way)

- **The game draws its item pictures itself, from the items' models** (`IconCreatorBase`, `ItemIconCreator`): never shipped as pictures. Its size is 63 pixels a cell and one (`ItemViewFactory.GetCellPixelSize`), which is the app's own cell already; drawn at twice that with eight samples, then halved.
- **The pose is on each prefab** (`PreviewPivot`, a behaviour with type trees in the bundle): a weapon's on its `_container` object, which the weapon and its parts hang from; every weapon and magazine looked at has the same turn (0, 0.8434, 0, -0.5373) and a bounds' scale of 0.9. `probe icon <item>`.
- **`resources.assets` has no type trees, and the engine's own layouts read it**: a game object (its components, its layer, its name), a transform (turn, place, scale, children, parent), a light (on, kind, shape, colour, intensity first), a behaviour (its script, its name, then its fields). The `IconCamera`: at (0, 0, -1), orthographic, cleared to nothing, drawing only the weapons' preview layer; three directional children, `Key Light` 1.24, `Fill Light` 0.39, `Hair Light` 0.91 light blue; an `IconShadow` of 10/255 grey with no offset (its script's name read from `globalgamemanagers.assets`). `probe resources <word>`, `probe studio`.
- **A mesh with bones and nothing to move them stops Bevy's renderer** (wgpu: the mesh's bind group is not the skinned pipeline's). An item's loot model may be a skinned mesh: drawn for a picture, its bones are taken off it and it lies in its rest pose.
- **The same frame again**: the studio's camera was spawned in the frame the first model arrived, and aiming it in that frame found nothing; the first picture (the AKM's, a run's first item) was read from a target nothing had drawn to, and kept empty. The studio now waits a frame after it is made, and an empty picture is never kept.
- **A camera's own `AmbientLight` makes Bevy 0.16 warn once** of an entity with a camera and no render graph: the light requires a camera, and is copied into the render world, where it gets one. That entity is the render world's; the studio's camera is drawn as it should be. (Taken off, the warning went, and the studio would have had the range's ambient light.)
- **Pictures drawn in play cost frames**: each new model's first frame in the studio took 20 to 90 ms (its pictures and meshes going to the card, the studio's pipelines). The curtain waits for the pictures not yet in the cache, so a first run starts at 8.3 s and every later one at 2.9 to 3.5 s.
- **What a picture costs to keep**: 77 PNGs, 960 KB.

## Added 2026-10-14, night (the other game's archives read; its suits' models of the item; restart required)

- **Anomaly's packed archives are read** (`trp_data::xray_archive`; the format as found earlier in this file, "the other game's packed archives"). All 68 under `E:\AnomalyEFT\db` (patches, addons and the rest; `levels`, `sounds` and the 500 MB textures' among them): 47,842 files, every table read whole in 0.05 s together. A table's entry is as read before: a length, the size, the packed size, a CRC-32 of the file's bytes, its name under `gamedata` (chunk 666 says `entry_point = $fs_root$\gamedata\`), the offset; folders are listed too, ending in `\`. A file whose packed size is not its size is LZO1X; **every file read so far matched its checksum** (the suits' two models, their pictures).
- **The engine's LZHUF is not quite the 1988 program's**: it rebuilds its tree when the root's count reaches **0x4000**, where the published `LZHUF.C` has 0x8000. With 0x8000 the 56 smaller tables decoded and the twelve longer ones went wrong where the tree is first rebuilt; with 0x4000 all 68 decode whole. Read from the archives themselves, not from the engine's source.
- **Which archive wins where two have one file** is not read (`PROVISIONAL`: `patches` and `addons` first, then a later name before an earlier); the files read here are in one each. Loose files win over all of them, as the game's own `fsgame.ltx` mounts `gamedata` over the archives.
- **The suits' models of the item**: the item's `visual` in that game's outfit configs (`configs\items\outfits\o_sunrise.ltx`, `o_seva.ltx`, loose in a GAMMA mod; `actor_visual` is the worn body): `dynamics\outfit\stalker_outfit` (the Sunrise; also the autumn and bear variants') and `scientific_outfit` (the SEVA). Neither is loose in any GAMMA mod (`31- Fixed Vanilla Models and Textures` has other suits' there); both are in `meshes\meshes_dynamics.db0`. Each is a skinned model of two bones (`link`, `joint1`, for the physics of a thing on the ground) with every vertex on `link`, read as the bodies are. **They lie flat, face up, the head towards minus z** (the SEVA's visor glass, a mesh of its own with a picture of 32 bits a point, lies at minus z and on top): 66 by 79 cm across and 27 high (the Sunrise), 71 by 81 and 34 (the SEVA). Their pictures are the worn bodies' own (`act\act_stalker_neutral_2`, `_3`), loose in GAMMA.
- **What "restart required" saves, measured or read**: the smooth switch on works the hands' pose out twice a step (the engine's, which the simulation goes by, and the smooth one to draw: `Animator::step`), 0.06 to 0.07 ms a frame as measured before; fixed for the run it costs nothing to look at, and off it is once. Custom hands off as a run begins leaves both suits' arms unread and unfitted. The pictures off leave the icon thread and studio unmade.
- **PowerShell 5.1's `*>` writes UTF-16**: Git Bash's `grep` finds nothing in such a log (a build log looked clean that way). Read those with `Select-String`.

## Added 2026-10-14, late night (getting ready to publish)

- **What the repository holds, checked before publishing**: 11 commits, 216 files, every one text (Rust, Markdown, JSON, TOML, the recorder's C# and project files, a shader, `hard_settings_layout.txt`); no binary was ever committed (`git log --all --diff-filter=A`), the largest blobs are `history.md` and test files. The whitelist `.gitignore` is what keeps it so. **A fresh copy of exactly those files** outside the workspace, with only `local\eft_dir.txt` added, built in 3 min 7 s and ran: 75 pictures drawn on its first start, the StG 44 left out (its model is the user's alone), the suits drawing nothing without the other game's folders. Personal details in it: the commits' e-mail; the user's profile folder in `findings.md`, `workbench.md`, `AGENTS.md`.
- **How other re-implementations say what they are** (their READMEs, read 2026-10-14): OpenMW "You need to own the game"; OpenRCT2 "requires original files of RollerCoaster Tycoon 2"; the Super Mario 64 decompilation "does not include all assets ... A prior copy of the game is required"; OpenJKDF2 is frank about method ("a function-by-function reimplementation", "Hex-Rays can be used for decompilation") and "does *not* include any original game assets"; Ship of Harkinian "does not include any copyrighted assets" and "we do not condone piracy". None of the five carries more of a legal statement than its license. The README's "What was learned, and how" follows them.
- **Battlestate's own word on decompiling**, in its DMCA notice to GitHub of 2020-06-30 (`github/dmca`, `2020/06/2020-06-30-bsg.md`, against a repository of game clients): "any decoding, disassembling of program code is prohibited by the EULA (paragraph 4.2.5)" and "We consider any decompilation, deobfuscation and modification of code infringe our intellectual property rights."
- **SPT ended in August 2026** (out of date: it carried on under another name, "the small hours" below; reported by Insider Gaming, Neon Lights Media, MassivelyOP, inkl; not read from the team's own announcement): after a trademark notice from Battlestate over "Tarkov" in its name and domain, the team said on 2026-08-01 that development ends, 4.1 being "always the intended last version"; the site and patch hosting were to go dark by 2026-08-12; the GitHub organisation to be renamed and archived without the name, its code kept for anyone to carry on. So a new user of this needs an SPT 4.1 they already have; and a project named after the game is what drew that notice.

## Added 2026-10-14, the small hours (SPT carried on as Single Player Tushonka)

- **SPT did not end; it was renamed.** The user, 2026-10-14: "We are using 'The Forge' which is called Single Player Tushonka now". Read that night: the GitHub organisation is [SP-Tushonka](https://github.com/SP-Tushonka) ("Slide Papa (some) Tushonka", site `sp-tushonka.com`), with `server-csharp` ("A C# rewrite of the SPT server project", 4.1.6 as a container), `modules`, `launcher`, `installer`, `bento` ("Builds and packages a Single Player Tushonka release") and `forge` ("The greatest resource available for Single Player Tushonka modifications"); its server's own README calls itself "the Server project for the Single Player Tushonka mod for Escape from Tushonka", licensed CC BY-NC-SA 4.0. The acronym stays SPT, and so do the install's own names (`SPT_Runtime`, `spt-*.dll`). The old `forge.sp-tarkov.com` no longer answers. So the reading of "SPT ended in August 2026" above was the press's and is out of date: a new user gets SPT 4.1 from Single Player Tushonka.
- **How this project names it since**: the mod and the modded install are "Single Player Tushonka (SPT)" (the README, the manual, the first start's question, the install's messages); "Escape from Tarkov" is left where it names the game whose controller is rebuilt or the game one must own, and in the game's own file and class names, which are what they are.

## Added 2026-10-14, late (the quick reload, the switches, every weapon, pictures when first wanted)

- **The quick reload is the reload key's double click, and the plain press waits for it.** `QuickReloadWeapon` (`R`, `DoubleClick`) is heard only with the weapon's animator at rest; it raises `ReloadFast` where the plain reload raises `Reload`, and the AKM's `IDLE` answers it with `RELOAD OUT ALL` (1.0 s, the magazine out at 0.49 s) in place of `RELOAD START` and `RELOAD OUT`. The old magazine, given no place, is thrown into the world at `MagHide` from where the weapon's magazine is (`DropMod`). 134 of the install's 176 weapons allow it (`isFastReload`); on a weapon whose magazine stays in it (the Model 700, which allows it) the key does what `R` does.
- **The magazines within reach are those lying directly in the pockets' and the vest's grids** (`Inventory.FastAccessSlots`), never deeper and never in the backpack, and a reload takes the fullest the weapon's slot takes. The game sorts them with .NET's `List.Sort`, which keeps no order between two that hold the same: which of those is taken is not known.
- **The player's weapon-motion mask is 125**: the bit for the walk's bob is off, yet the game bobs the weapon in a walk (every walking recording has it). The first-person path does not go by the mask but for the shot's bit: a switch built on the mask's bits would have changed the game's results.
- **A weapon's class in the database is not its class as the game names it for skills**: by the class chain the M32A1 is a `Revolver`, and by its template's `weapClass` a `grenadeLauncher`; the MTs-255 is a shotgun by `weapClass` and a revolver by its chain. The crates of weapons go by `weapClass`.
- **The RShG-2 is not a one-off in this install** (`IsOneoff` false): a `RocketLauncher`, `OnlyBarrel` with one chamber, its controller without a single state to reload in. Its `FIRE TO OUT` (with a `DropWeapon` mark that would throw it away) is played by nothing in the game's code. It fires only from the sights: its `IsAimingFloat` is set to 1 and 0 by its clips' marks `AimReady` and `IdleReady` (`FirearmController.OnAimReady`, `OnIdleReady`), and its `IDLE` goes to `FIRE` only above 0.9. By the code, its trigger is refused from the hip only just after the sights came down (the controller's `bool_0`, lowered by `IdleStart`); otherwise a press from the hip starts `RocketLauncherFire`, which then waits on a shot its animator will not play.
- **The game tells every weapon's animator of the sights** (`SetAimingInAnimator`: `AimingIn`, `AimingOut`, `PrevIsAimingFloatState`, and `IsAimingFloat` but for a rocket launcher). None of the other weapons built has any of the four in its controller's ways or blends, which is why the recordings matched without them.
- **The game asks for an item's picture when the item's view is made** (`ItemView.UpdateStaticInfo`, `RefreshIcon`): nothing at a raid's start, and a view shows its loader until its picture is read or drawn. The app had drawn every picture of every container under the curtain.
- **A screen rebuilt whole is cheap; reading pictures in the frame was not** (measured the same night, putting right a first reading): with the crate of rifles open (80 rows, some 210 things) a rebuild forced every 20 frames costs about a millisecond of the update and no frame went past 8 ms; a move from the crate to the vest, 8.7 ms. The 57 to 65 ms frames were two other things. **Opening the crate read its 91 cached pictures in the frame** (41.7 ms of one update): they are read away from the frame now, as the game reads them, and the opening frame takes 1.5 ms of update. **A weapon first hung** (the M4A1 from the crate) has one frame of 50 to 60 ms once its rig is in the world, its update under a millisecond: not the uploads to the card (the same with 4 MB a frame for 24), and the same on Vulkan and with a render thread of its own; not yet traced. Every picture that came had also drawn the whole screen afresh, and the screen was drawn while shut: neither is done now (`inventory_view::fill_pictures`).
- **After Alt+Tab the pointer is taken again** in every way tried on the user's fullscreen window with a second screen: back by Alt+Tab, by a click on the window, with the pointer moved to the other screen meanwhile, with the inventory open throughout (`local\alttab2_test_s3f.ps1`). The user's report that it is not was not reproduced.

## Added 2026-10-14, later still (a part of a weapon drawn on its own bones)

- **Two of the install's weapons have a part drawn on bones of their own**: the AS VAL's and the VSS's hammer spring (`vss_hammer_spring_LOD0`), a skinned renderer on two bones of the weapon's own tree, `weapon_hammer_spring` and `weapon_hammer`, so that the spring is stretched as the hammer moves. No other weapon of the 149 with a stock preset has a skinned renderer (`tests\every_weapon.rs` lists them). The app drew that part as a plain mesh that kept its joints, and **Bevy's renderer stopped the first time it was drawn** (wgpu: the `mesh_layout` bind group is not the `skinned_mesh_layout` the prepass pipeline wants), the same trap as the pictures' of the evening above. The user's report: "ASVAL causes a crash."
- **Drawn now as the game draws it**: on its two bones, with its own bind poses, in both ways a rig is made (at the start, and made ready away from the frame when first hung). A skinned part whose bones are not all in its own tree would be drawn as it lies, its joints taken off, and so are the parts of a weapon of one's own and what is worn on the head, which hang on plain nodes.

## Added 2026-10-14, night (what goes with a thing, lit)

- **Tarkov lights other boxes only while a thing is carried** (`GridItemView.ShowAcceptHighlight`, from `CheckAcceptHandler`): green where it could go, yellow where it would join a stack or go into a container, at alpha 10/51. Under the mouse a box is white at 77/255 (`ItemView.DefaultSelectedColor`), which the app had not drawn. Nothing lights what goes with a thing pointed at: that is S.T.A.L.K.E.R.'s, and a departure the user asked for (`inventory.md`, "The box under the mouse, and what goes with it").
- **S.T.A.L.K.E.R.'s lighting is two scripts**: Anomaly's `itms_manager.ActorMenu_on_item_focus_receive` lights from a weapon only (its rounds, scopes, silencer, launcher, parts, repair kits, upgrade tools); GAMMA's Mags Redux adds a weapon's magazines, a magazine's rounds and rounds' magazines. Neither lights a weapon from its rounds; here it goes both ways, as the user asked ("and vice-versa").
- **A magazine's rounds go by its `Cartridges` filter**, which `Kind` had not kept (the rounds lie loose in it): kept now (`Kind::cartridges`). A weapon's `Chambers` and a cylinder's `camora_*` slots were already among a thing's slots.
- The orange (209, 153, 45) is the game's own: a thing with an active repair bonus has it as its background, at 32/255 (`GridItemView`).

## Added 2026-10-14, night (the run's own weapon made when wanted; why a box blinks)

- **The run's own weapon was the one rig made at the start, whatever the run**: begun with nothing in hand, the AKM was still built under the curtain (167 nodes, 15 meshes), because every later rig hangs from the first and shares its arms' and camera's bones. **The empty hands have every bone a weapon's rig shares** (all 149 weapons; the Uzi and the MPX have a `Main_camera` under the camera bone that the empty hands lack, which draws nothing and is then their own), so they are the first rig when nothing is in hand, and the AKM is made when wanted as the rest are. A rig's every bone is written from its own pose each frame, so the shared bones' rest pose from another prefab changes nothing.
- **A slot's key for a weapon whose rig was still being made was lost**: the hands hear a key only for a weapon with a rig. The StG 44's rig takes 1.15 s from being hung (the AKM's 0.46, the M3's 0.50): `2` pressed 0.06 s too soon left the hands empty. The key now waits for the rig.
- **Why a box blinks in the game** (`ItemView.Blinking`: added, removed, examined, loading rounds): an inventory move's `Begin` and `Succeed` (`AbstractAsyncOperation`) bracket its owners' `OutProcess` and `InProcess`, which for the player wait on the hands (`Player.TrySetInHands`, `TryRemoveFromHands`); a weapon taken in hand for a move succeeds at `SpawnOperation.WeaponAppeared`, its draw's `IdleStart`. Nothing else waits in the single-player game, and loot's models are loaded before the raid (`BaseLocalGame`: "Loading loot..."): the blink is the hands' time, not a model's loading. The fade: alpha to 0 over 0.5 s and back over 0.5 s (`ItemViewAnimation`, DOTween's default ease, out-quadratic).

## Added 2026-10-14, night (the freeze when a weapon is first taken, found and taken away)

- **What it was**, measured with each of the AKM, the StG 44, the M3 and the Five-seveN taken from the crate at a bare start (`local\freeze_s3k.ps1`): one frame of 53, 73, 56 and 48 ms as the rig came into the world, the app's own update under a millisecond. **Not the renderer's schedule** (every stage of it fast in that frame, `TRP_RENDER_TIMING`), and no pipeline being built: **two things on the main thread**, about half each (the StG 44's: 37.5 ms in the main world's schedules, then 36.3 ms handing over to the renderer).
- **The thread that makes rigs let go of its reader of the install in the frame its last rig came**: its loaded bundles and its whole database, freed on the main thread (40.6 ms, timed). Now freed on a thread of its own.
- **Bevy copies, on the main thread, every asset the main world keeps as well** as it hands it to the renderer (`extract_render_asset`: an asset marked `RenderAssetUsages::RENDER_WORLD` alone is moved, any other cloned), so a rig's every mesh and picture were copied in the one frame. A rig's are now for the renderer alone (`PreparedLooks::add_with_pictures`); nothing of the main world reads them after.
- **What is left is the upload to the graphics card**, spread over frames by Bevy's budget (`RenderAssetBytesPerFrame`): at 24 MB a frame, a frame of 8 to 10 ms; at 8 MB (`UPLOAD_BUDGET`), at most 2 or 3 ms. **A rig is taken in hand only once all its meshes and pictures are on the card** (`weapon::Uploads`, answered by the renderer), so that a smaller budget cannot have it drawn without them: 4 to 13 of the renderer's frames after it comes, which the waiting key and the blink cover.
- **After**: the worst frame after the put-on 7.2 to 10.3 ms against the frames' 6.05 ms (165 a second), where it had been 47.5 to 73.4 ms. The weapons' first draws set beside a draw of the same weapon long on the card: the same at the same moments (the StG 44's arm draw has the hands up before the rifle comes into view, both times).
- **Read for it**, and not the cause here: Godot's account of the stutter of a pipeline compiled when a thing is first drawn ([docs.godotengine.org](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/tutorials/performance/pipeline_compilations.html)), Bevy's mesh allocator, which copies its buffers as they grow ([`MeshAllocatorSettings`](https://doc.qu1x.dev/bevy_trackball/bevy/render/mesh/allocator/struct.MeshAllocatorSettings.html)), Bevy 0.16's notes ([bevy.org](https://bevy.org/news/bevy-0-16/)). The cause was found by measuring; Bevy's own source (0.16.1) says how its extraction copies.

## Added 2026-10-14, night (what a rig costs; pictures of one's own compressed; a move's slow frame)

- **What every weapon's rig costs**, measured by making each ready as for the hands and not putting it into the world (`--measure-rigs all`): the 149 together 20.7 s away from the frame, **8.4 GB of pictures and 311 MB of meshes**, 23,372 nodes; a rig's median 52.8 MB, the largest 215 MB (the StG 44's pictures as read, uncompressed). The crate of rifles alone (53 weapons): 3.5 GB of pictures and 140 MB of meshes, 9,129 nodes, 7 s; of pistols (29) 775 MB. **Making a crate's rigs before they are taken** would ask that much of the graphics card's memory at once, and keep every one of those nodes in the world; its uploads, 8 MB a frame, would not stop a frame. A rig is never let go once made: a run that hangs many weapons keeps them all. The install's pictures are kept as the install keeps them (BC1, BC3, BC7, and BC5 for the two channels the app re-packs); rigs that share a picture each have their own copy of it.
- **A weapon of one's own's pictures had gone to the card as they were read**, four bytes a pixel: now BC1 and BC5 (`custom-weapons.md`), a fifth of the size, and kept so in `local\picture_cache`.
- **The ~14 ms frame after a move was the reweighing, not the screen**: whatever changes what is carried makes the movement's settings again for the weight (`MovementSettings::reweighed`, as the game does), and that copied the root motion table each time, 9.4 ms of the 9.4. The table is shared now (`Arc`), and reweighing takes 0.01 ms. The screen drawn afresh after a move costs 0.3 ms of the update with the airdrop crate open (414 of the interface's nodes) and its stages (the text measured and laid out, the layout) 2 to 6 ms with the crate of rifles (1,126). Found with `TRP_RENDER_TIMING`, whose readings now take in the interface's stages, when each schedule was reached, and single systems that say so (`render_timing::Clock`). A frame's `dt` in a `--log-pacing` file is the time up to it: what was slow was the frame before.
- **The thread that draws the inventory's pictures let go of its reader in the frame too**, as the rigs' thread had: now away from it.

## Added 2026-10-14, night (the game's screens' character; rigs ahead, let go of, shared)

- **The game's screens show the character by the body's own controller** (`PlayerModelView.Show`, `PlayerModelLoader.Load`, `CreateWeapon`, `CreatePlayerBody`): the menu's player (`assets/content/commonprefabs/menuplayer.bundle`; `probe animator` names its controller, `Player_anim_controller`) at (0, -1, 5) of the screen's view, turned by `new Quaternion(0f, 180f, 0f, 0f)` (half round the vertical once the engine has made it whole), its layer 11 at 1, the kind of thing in hand in three parameters, and one update of `animatorDeltatime * 5`, which every caller gives as 0 (the matchmaker's, the profile's, the inventory's own window). The animator is not stopped after: in the game it plays on. The layer's states are in `docs/spec/body-animator.md`, "The stance of the game's screens".
- **Rigs alike share a third of their pictures**: the MDR 7.62 hung after the MDR 5.56 (one tan, one black) found 9 of its 21 pictures already on the card, 6.3 MB not sent again; the other way round the same 9 of 24. Marking a rig's pictures by their bytes costs nothing that shows in its making (0.03 to 0.04 s for its meshes and pictures together, as before). A rig let go of after 30 s took 15 of its 24 pictures off the card and left the 9 its fellow draws with; its 119 entities went in a frame of 7.3 ms (the run's mean 6.1).
- **A rig is made ahead in 0.29 s away from the frame**, and the hands then take it as soon as it is hung. A weapon taken up is asked for at once, one the mouse rests on after a quarter of a second.
- **The character model costs two frames when something changes and none otherwise**: the vest put on from the airdrop crate was drawn again with no frame over 6.9 ms; it was on the card a frame after it was made (the body's own meshes and pictures were there already).

## Added 2026-10-14, night (DirectX 12's 2,048 places for samplers; a picture's dropped reading; the game's magnified grids)

- **DirectX 12's 2,048 places**: the user's run stopped with `Unable to allocate descriptors: RangeAllocationError` and `Not enough memory left` in `create_bind_group` for a `StandardMaterial`. wgpu 24 on DirectX 12 keeps one heap of 2,048 places for the samplers of every bind group there is (`wgpu-hal` 24, `dx12::Device::new`: `capacity_samplers = 2_048`), and each bind group copies its samplers into it. **Bindless materials, which would share them, are off there** (the device lacks what Bevy asks for them: `samplers: bindless materials false`), so every material holds the standard material's six (`bevy_pbr` 0.16), the range's lined material six too, and each picture the interface draws one, for as long as the picture is kept. Measured (`TRP_RENDER_TIMING`, a line `samplers:` whenever the count moves): the run began at 527 places, **402 of them the range's**: a material for each of its 67 blocks and parts. Each rig made ahead of its being hung took 60 to 120 more, and those had no limit but the 30 s: seven rifles taken up one after another brought a run to 1,269, without the cameras' own bind groups, which the count leaves out. **Now**: the range's blocks share a material for each colour (32, 192 places; drawn the same, the lines going by where a point is), a run begins at 317; at most four rigs nobody carries are kept, and rigs are let go of while more than 1,536 places are held (`weapon::SamplerPlaces`, counted by the renderer every frame); the same seven rifles and the crate scrolled peaked at 893. The game keeps every picture in memory for the run, and so does this; over all eight crates that is a few hundred places, which the rigs make room for. Vulkan, whose pools grow, has no such limit, but hands this machine its frames in clumps of four (`Bevy 0.16 on Windows takes Vulkan` above).
- **A picture's reading back was dropped, and the studio waited on it for ever**: `Screenshot::image` of a target the renderer does not have yet is skipped ("Unknown image for screenshot, skipping") and never answered. The target had been made with data (zeros), which waits its turn in the upload budget; made with none (`Image::new_uninit`) it is there at once. The character model's target is made so too.
- **A new kind of material takes 1 to 2.5 s to build on DirectX 12** (`TRP_ICON_TRACE`, each waiting pipeline by label: `pbr_opaque_mesh_pipeline`, creating, for 180 to 400 frames). wgpu compiles DirectX 12's shaders with its older compiler unless it is given the newer (DXC: two libraries beside the program, or a feature of the build); not tried.
- **The game's container panel is magnified** (`docs/spec/icons.md`, "When a picture is asked for"): read from the install with `probe pointers`, which lists where a scene object's scripts point among the scene's own scripts.

## Added 2026-10-14, night (DXC against FXC; the game's own boxes for the tables of weapons)

- **DirectX 12's shaders by DXC, measured against FXC** (`local\dxc_compare_s3o.sh`, two runs each, the libraries of this machine's Windows SDK 10.0.26100 put on the path, `WGPU_DX12_COMPILER` set): what a run draws with built in 0.43 to 0.61 s against 1.59 to 2.04 s (the curtain's time); the crate of rifles' 22 pictures on an empty cache 4.0 s after the warm-up against 7.2; a walk with frames not held to the screen 4.6 ms a frame either way. Bevy 0.16 looks for `dxcompiler.dll` and `dxil.dll` in the folder it is run from alone, and without them compiles with FXC; the app now gives the engine the two beside `trp.exe` or in the newest Windows SDK that has them (`main::dxc_libraries`). The Bevy feature that links DXC in (`statically-linked-dxc`) would fetch a prebuilt library while building; not taken.
- **The game has a weapon box and an ammunition box of its own** (`LootContainer`s): `WeaponCrate` (`578f87ad245977356274f2cc`, a grid of 5 by 2, the same template as the range's wooden crate) and `AmmoBox` (`5909d45286f77465a8136dc6`, 3 by 3); the grenade box is 3 by 2. The tables' gun cases and magazine boxes are those, larger inside as the crates were.

## Added 2026-10-14, night (pictures short of parts)

- **A picture short of some of its parts** was read back before its model was all on the graphics card (`docs/spec/icons.md`, "A picture read back before all of its model"): the studio's wait was a guess of 24 MB a frame where the app sends 8 and shares them. Measured with the guess kept beside the true wait: 26 of 38 models of the rifles' case came on the card later than the guess. The studio waits on the card itself now (`weapon::UploadWatch`).

## Added 2026-10-14, night (a stock preset without a magazine)

- **One stock preset of a weapon fed from magazines has none**: the RPK-16's (`RPK16 nomag`, `5c0d1ec986f77439512a1a72`); the other 120 of SPT 4.1 have one in `mod_magazine` (`local\nomag_survey_s53.ps1` reads `globals.json` and `items.json`). One of the two traders that sell it sells that build too; the other builds they sell have the 95-round drum or a 45-round magazine.
- **`defMagType`, the magazine a weapon's template names as its default, is never read by the game's client** (`WeaponTemplate.defMagType`, declared and used nowhere in `local\decomp\src`). SPT's server reads it (`TemplateItemExtensions.GetWeaponsDefaultMagazineTpl`) where a weapon's build has no magazine, for the kind of the spare magazines it gives a bot (`BotWeaponGenerator.GetMagazineTemplateFromWeaponTemplate`, its only caller being `AddExtraMagazinesToInventory`; read in SPT's published source, and named in the server's own documentation in the install, `SPTarkov.Server.Core.xml`). The app puts that magazine into such a preset (`Database::fit_magazines`).
- **ConvertFrom-Json cannot read `items.json`** in PowerShell 5.1: some items have keys that differ only in case (`ExplDelay` and `explDelay`). `System.Web.Script.Serialization.JavaScriptSerializer`, with its `MaxJsonLength` raised, reads it in a few seconds.

## Added 2026-10-14, night (the health system read)

- **The player's health is `PlayerHealthController`** (`LocalPlayer.Create`), an `ActiveHealthController`; `OfflineHealthController` is the menus' between raids. Several of the controller's effects come only from what a raid has and the range has not (explosions, flashes, toxins, zones, events, zombies), and two of its parts no player of this install meets at all: the profile's starting buff is empty, and metabolism is never turned off.
- **The player reacts to an effect when it starts and when it goes residual**, not when it is added (`EffectStartedEvent`, `EffectResidualEvent`): a tremor's 15 s delay passes before the hands shake, and medicine's 2 s delay before the character is held still.
- **A fall is asked of health on every frame on the ground** (`CheckFlying` calls `HandleFall` with the height since last grounded, 0 but on a landing), measured to where the ground check calls the character grounded: off the 6 m ledge the fall came to 5.92 m.
- **Over-damage is spread by the other parts' maxima**, not by what they have left, and a destroyed part takes none: so a leg destroyed by a fall takes the rest's health down in proportion to their size, the head least.
- **A vault hurts only the left leg** (`ApplyDamageByVaulting` asks of the left leg alone for a vault; the climb's branch, which asks of both, is never called: only `VaultState` calls it).
- **The oxygen's capacity holds the common health as it was at the last change of energy** (`Oxygen.TotalCapacity` is kept, and marked stale by `OnEnergyChanged` alone).
- **The game names an effect for its screens by a table of its own** (`HealthHelper._effectNames`): a fracture is `BrokenBone` ("Fracture"); the install's `Fracture` key is an item's attribute ("INFLICTS FRACTURE"). Low health, medicine at work and hunger have no name and are not listed.
- **A destroyed part mended keeps that share of its maximum** (`RestoreBodyPart`: the new maximum is the old times the drawn share, in whole hundredths, the larger never drawn): the surgical kits' `healthPenaltyMin` and `Max` are what is kept, not what is lost (the CMS 25 to 45%, the Surv12 60 to 72%).
- **The jump's push off the ground goes by the carried weight** (`JumpPlayerState.Enter`: `lerp(1, 0.66, Overweight)`), which the simulation had left out; no recording was overweight, and bringing it in with health's damaged leg moved nothing in the regression.
- **A medicine's hands are a `UsableHandsPrefab`**, a `WeaponPrefab` with the bone the item hangs from besides (`item_position`), so the weapon reader takes them as they are: the IFAK's controller has a draw (`OUT TO USE`, 2.13 s), a looping use, a put-away and a break, driven by `Active`, `NextLimb` and `AnimationVariant`, with a second set for a syringe.
- **Barbed wire needs nothing of a map**: its damage is 2 a metre a hit collider moves inside it (`BodyPartCollider.ProceedBarb`), its slowing a constant of the class (0.15).

## Added 2026-10-15 (the death)

- **The player prefab carries its own ragdoll** (`RigidbodySpawner`, `CharacterJointSpawner`), with type trees, so `trp_data::ragdoll` reads it whole. Its colliders are the hit colliders (`BodyPartCollider`), switched off in the prefab: a body's own on its bone and those hung under it with no body of their own. The calf hangs from the second thigh bone, not the first its joint holds to.
- **`globalgamemanagers` gives the physics layers' names and matrix** (`probe layers`: the matrix found as 32 masks after their count that agree with one another): `Deadbody`, the layer a corpse is put on whole, meets itself, so a corpse's limbs strike one another.
- **The settings object's layout reaches the corpse's figures** (`hard_settings_layout.txt` to `DEBUG_CORPSE_PHYSICS`, past the shells' settings): `CorpseMaxDepenetrationVelocity` 4, `CorpseEnergyToSleep` 0.01 (the code's default 0.05: the install's differs).
- **The first-person ribcage is not a bone's frame**: the body's meshes hang their ribcage on the weapon rig's root, turned as the view; a corpse made from that pose has its upper half 131 degrees off the spine's, and is moved onto the body's own animated ribcage first (the arms keep their hold on the weapon).
