- **avian 0.3's spherical joint takes the same axis in both bodies' frames and limits the swing as a round cone** (gone with avian in 0.12.0: below, "the game's own engine"): the game's joint (an asymmetric twist, two swings of their own) is solved as an XPBD constraint of the app's own (`death::CharacterJoint`), with both bodies made turned as the world so that the limits count from the pose at death, as the engine's joints do.
- **A death with nothing hitting the body has no blur**: `FastBlur.Die` scales its curve by the last hit's power, which only a felt hit sets (`EffectsController.OnPlayerDamaged`; a bleeding is self-inflicted and sets none).

## Added 2026-10-15, later (the game's own engine; things let fall and thrown)

- **The game's physics is PhysX 4.1, and it can be run here**: Unity 2022.3 builds on PhysX 4.1; the Rust bindings `physx-sys` 0.8.2 are PhysX 4.1.1 (0.10 on are PhysX 5), and build from PhysX's source with Visual Studio's C++ Build Tools (five minutes the first time). avian (0.11.0's) fell back where the game falls forward; on PhysX, with the game's settings, the corpse falls forward as the recordings do.
- **The physics settings past the layer matrix sit 4 bytes later than a first reading had them** (Unity 2022.3's `PhysicsManager`): friction type at 252, enhanced determinism 256, improved patch friction 258, solver type 260, most spin 264; a test reads them from the install.
- **The game steps its physics once a drawn frame, by the average of 600 frames' times** (`PhysicsExtensions.Simulation.SmoothSimulate`, at most `MaxSmoothPhysicsDeltaTime`, 0.03), not on Unity's fixed step: a recorded corpse moves on every frame of 1/120 s.
- **A character joint's limits count from the skeleton's rest, not from the pose at death**: the recordings' elbows stop 25 degrees either side of the prefab's rest relation whatever the death's pose was (`bin/corpse --angles`). Laid on PhysX's six-way joint with the corpse's body first, the twist as it is, the first swing about the swing axis and the mass scales as the game sets them, the four deaths are followed to 1.5 to 3.5 cm on average over a quarter of a second (`--conventions` tries the rest).
- **PhysX allows one foundation a program**: a second world made while another stood failed; every world is a scene of one engine kept for the program (`trp_physics::engine`).
- **PhysX's default filter does not sweep**: a body asking for continuous detection is only swept against pairs whose filter says so (`eDETECT_CCD_CONTACT`); the layer filter here says so for every pair that touches.
- **A capsule no taller than its two caps makes PhysX fail** (the jaw's, 0.1 m with a radius of 0.05): Unity makes it a sphere, and so does this.
- **A weapon's colliders are switched off in its prefab** and switched on by the game when it is let fall (`DropItemDead`) or lies loose (`CreateLootPrefab`); a filter on "switched on" took none.
- **Loose things are boxes, but for backpacks and rounds** (`probe colliders`): a magazine has one box on its first level of detail, a can or a medkit one box of its own, a backpack or a round a convex mesh; most have a material of 0.4 friction, some none (the engine's default, 0.6).
- **A loose thing's model is moved back by its centre of mass** (`LootItem.SetShift`) and its object then put where it set off (`ThrowItem` does not make up for it; `SetupItem` does): what is drawn sits that much off the place it left, and the object's place is the centre of mass from then on, which is what a recording's `pos` of a thrown thing is.
- **`Loot` meets neither itself, nor a corpse, nor the character**: so a thing's fall is its own, and one whose model comes late can be stepped through what it missed with the same result.
- **Every loose thing is swept**: `LootVolumeForHighQuallityPhysicsClient` is 999 m³.
- **The quick reload's magazine sets off at 1 cm/s with none of the character's velocity** (`DropMod`), from where the weapon's magazine is, and the weapon draws none from then until the next appears (`RemoveMod` at `OnMagPuttedToRig`, `SetupMod` at `OnMagAppeared`); a throw from the inventory has half the character's velocity and is not drawn for 0.15 s.
- **Unity's `Mathf.Clamp` takes crossed bounds, Rust's `clamp` stops the program**: a sprint landed after a fall that caps the speed to nought gives the sprint's limit `Clamp(0, 0.1, 0)`, which the game answers with 0.1 (`SprintAcceleration`). Where the game's own clamp can cross, the simulation uses `math::unity_clamp`.
- **Every raise of the version strands a build's worth of our crates in `target`**: Cargo puts a crate's version in its identity (`-C metadata`), so a raise builds our crates' libraries, programs and incremental caches afresh under new names and never deletes the old. With a raise for every change the build folder had grown to 72 GB by 0.12.1 (46 GB of incremental caches in 651 folders, 7.8 GB of debug files, 1.7 GB of avian and the old `eft-*` names). `local\sweep_target.sh` keeps the live ones (it runs at the end of every regression), and the crates we depend on build without debug information since 0.12.2.
- **Things on the ground filled DirectX 12's heap of samplers again**: each brought its model's materials, a rifle a dozen at the standard material's six places each; 22 rifles thrown out of their case took the count to about 2,000 and the app stopped as on 2026-10-14. A material of the app's own with one sampler for all its pictures (`loose_material`) holds one place; the standard material's six are its six texture bindings' own samplers, whether it draws with them or not. Bevy's PBR functions can be imported by such a material: only what is used of `pbr_bindings` is put in the shader (as Bevy's `array_texture` example does).
- **A material of one sampler was not enough**: every gun of the range on the ground is some 2,000 materials, and with the rigs of those taken up the heap was full again ("fragmented_free_length: 32"). A material can hold none: Bevy's view bind group, bound for every draw, holds samplers of its own (the environment map's, linear with mipmaps, always in the layout), and a material's shader may read its pictures through one of them; a picture's own repeat is then done by hand (`fract` outside 0 to 1, with the place's own gradients). **wgpu 25 changed the heap**: one for the device, each distinct sampler once, referred to by index (`wgpu-hal` 25 `dx12/sampler.rs`; 24 has no such file), so the limit is 2,048 distinct samplers, not places: Bevy 0.17 (wgpu 26) and on. **The game draws with Direct3D 11** (the user's `Player.log`: "Direct3D 11.0 [level 11.1]"), where a draw's samplers are bound to it and no such heap exists.
- **The game keeps pictures to the user's texture quality** (and the app since 0.14.0: `docs/spec/streaming.md`): `GraphicsSettingsController.ChangeTexturesQuality` sets Unity's `globalTextureMipmapLimit` to `clamp(2 - TextureQuality, 0, 2)` (one more in its SD mode) unless mip streaming is on, when the streaming controller takes the same as its bias (`GraphicsSettingsGroup.TextureQualityToMipBias`). The user's `Graphics.ini` (SPT's settings) has `TextureQuality` 1 and `MipStreaming` false: every picture of their game loads without its largest level, a quarter of its bytes. The app loads every one whole: 22 rifles on the ground sent 971 MB of pictures (44 MB each, the largest 186 MB), every gun of the range some 6 to 8 GB, against a card of 10.

## Added 2026-10-15, night (Bevy 0.18; the pictures by their levels)

- **Bevy after 0.16, measured** (walking with the rifle and opening the inventory, frames 200 to 909, `--log-pacing`): 0.16, 0.17 and 0.18 all 164.8 frames a second by the mean (the spread 0.53 to 0.72 ms), their pictures alike (0.17 the same as 0.16 to the pixel over the tables; 0.18 within 0.02 to 0.21 of 765 of 0.17's); **0.19 (wgpu 29) 125.6 and 0.20 (wgpu 30) 111**, the renderer's "Prepare: phase buffers and flush" 3.6 and 5.2 ms a frame, the same without Bevy's `debug` feature. 0.18 kept (`TRP\local\trp_app_cargo_0*_s60.toml` and the lock files keep each one's manifest).
- **wgpu 26 and on, on DirectX 12, must check indirect draws**: the check writes each draw's first instance; switched off, every model was drawn from another's instance data (huge, torn). And **wgpu-core 26.0.1 asserts in a debug build** what does not hold of Bevy's indirect draws (`render.rs`, the output's offset against the input's stride: 320 against 308): the crates we depend on are built without debug assertions (`[profile.dev.package."*"]`).
- **Bevy 0.17's `RelativeCursorPosition::normalized` counts from the node's centre** (-0.5 to 0.5), not its corner as 0.16's did: the inventory's share of a box under the mouse is it plus a half (`inventory_view::share_of`). A transform on an interface node warns (B0004): `UiTransform`.
- **A picture the engine has been given must not be changed**: changing new pictures in `Last` (even taking one to change, `Assets::get_mut`, which marks it changed), the engine's own among them (its lighting's tables), drew every surface black, the range's plain ones too; taking the new materials to change did not. Streaming takes only the models' pictures, and a change of level is a new picture.
- **Unity streams only a texture imported for it** (`m_StreamingMipmaps`): every one of the AKM's 27 is (priority 0); 9 of the pictures the range starts with are not, and are drawn whole with streaming on, as the game's are.
- **A bundle's data is blocks compressed each on its own** (its block table, LZ4 here): a texture's bytes are read again from the bundle's file by decompressing only the blocks they lie in (`Bundle::read_range`), as Unity reads a streamed texture's levels. Keeping the pictures whole in memory instead cost 2.6 GB of private memory in the 48-rifle run; holding the bundles open to read from cost as much, since the threads' readers let theirs go when idle.
- **A run takes the install's texture quality unless told** (1 in the user's): with streaming off it halves every picture, so a comparison of streaming on against a run left at the options' quality measures the quality, not the streaming. Windows' "GPU Process Memory" dedicated figure is the allocator's reserved blocks, which are not given back as they empty; what is in use is the allocator's own report and the renderer's pictures (`TRP_GPU_MEMORY`).
- **The game's budget is the map's**: `LevelSettings.ApplySettings` sets each map's `MaxMapTextureMemory` (1,024 MB at every quality unless the map says otherwise) over the settings' 1,024; DLSS and FSR take 1.5 levels off the streaming's bias (`CameraManager`); the inventory's pictures are drawn from every texture whole (`ItemIconCreator`: `requestedMipmapLevel` 0).

## Added 2026-10-15, late (why Bevy 0.19 and 0.20 draw this app slower; the game's shadows)

- **The first comparison of Bevy's versions was held to the screen**: 0.16, 0.17 and 0.18 all came to 164.8 frames a second because the screen takes 165 with vsync on. Uncapped (`--present off`) the same run (`--equip --hold w --tap 500:tab`, frames 200 to 1509) takes 2.62 to 2.77 ms a frame on 0.18: compare versions uncapped.
- **Uncapped, 0.18 against 0.20** (untraced builds; the light run above, and the heavy one: 48 rifles thrown out of their case, 43 on the ground, the 330 frames after; `local\heavy_pace_s61.ps1`):

  | | light | heavy |
  |---|---|---|
  | 0.18, DirectX 12 | 2.69 ms | 4.64 ms |
  | 0.18, Vulkan | 2.10 ms | 2.93 ms |
  | 0.20, DirectX 12 | 8.85 ms | 74.0 ms |
  | 0.20, Vulkan | 4.27 ms | 5.45 ms |

- **Where 0.20's frame goes** (traced, `--features profile`): the renderer 8.3 ms of a light frame (0.18's: 2.0), of it 2.9 ms writing each shadow view's binned instance buffers (`write_binned_instance_buffers<Shadow, …>`), 1.1 ms preparing the preprocessing's bind groups (`prepare_preprocess_bind_groups`, 0.04 ms on 0.18) and 1.3 ms in twelve submissions to the queue a frame; among the 43 rifles 34.9, 15.0 and 12.6 ms of a 72 ms renderer. All grow with the distinct groups of draws (a mesh and a material each, in every view: the camera's and each shadow cascade's), and nearly every part of a weapon has a material of its own. Bevy's own account of the same: issue #24448, 0.19's `prepare_preprocess_bind_groups` from 33 µs to 1.03 ms after PR #23481 moved the unpacking of batches to the GPU, cured for its reporter by bindless materials. The work moved to the GPU pays for scenes of many objects of few materials; this one is few objects of many.
- **Bevy's materials are never bindless on DirectX 12**: the derived `bindless_supported` asks for `BUFFER_BINDING_ARRAY` and `TEXTURE_BINDING_ARRAY`, and wgpu's DirectX 12 backend (27 and 30) reports the second alone. On Vulkan they are, which is what Vulkan's figures above show, 0.18's included.
- **Vulkan paces evenly on 0.18**: with vsync at 165 Hz, 165.0 a second, the spread 0.56 ms as DirectX 12's (0.56), but 4.6% of frames more than a tenth off the median against 2.3%, and 2.7% of updates a quarter of a frame early or late against 1.1%.
- **0.20 reads shaders as WESL**: a material's own WGSL with `#import` fails to build ("expected global item … found '#'") and what draws with it is left out (the range's ground in a first 0.20 run, which then measured faster than with it); ported, its imports are `import bevy_pbr::render::{…};`, the material's group `@group(constants::MATERIAL_BIND_GROUP)`, and `alpha_discard` takes the flags, the cut-off and the colour. `Button` is in `bevy_ui_widgets` (which needs picking), `Interaction` a deprecated alias, `Assets::get_mut` gives an `AssetMut`, `GpuImage` keeps its size in its `texture_descriptor`.
- **A build with `trace_chrome` writes every span to `trace-<time>.json` in the working folder unless `TRACE_CHROME` names a file** (2 to 4 GB a run here: 19 GB were left before it was seen), costs about a millisecond a frame, and replaces `target\debug\trp.exe`: keep a traced build under another name (`docs/workbench.md`, "Profiling").
- **The game's shadows are its quality's preset** (`QualityLevelPreset`, `GraphicsSettingsGroup.GetMaxShadowVisibility`; `docs/spec/settings.md`, "The shadows"): at the install's medium two cascades to 40 m, where the app had Bevy's four to 150 m. As the game's: 2.42 against 2.69 ms a light frame, 4.00 against 4.64 among the rifles.

## Added 2026-10-15, later (Vulkan against DirectX 12 on 0.18, as played)

- **With vsync, three runs each** (`local\backend_compare_s62.ps1`; walking with the inventory opened, and walking while the view turns 90 degrees a second; frames 200 to 1509): the screen's 165 a second on either, the ripple of the motion over time the same (95th percentile 0.54 to 0.57%, 99th 0.78 to 0.87% turning); Vulkan's own clock a little less even, 6.9 to 8.2% of frames more than a tenth off the median against 2.2 to 3.9%, 1.5 to 3.5% of updates a quarter of a frame early or late against 0.6 to 0.9%, the 99th percentile 7.2 to 7.4 ms against 6.6 to 6.9. Its off frames come in pairs, one about a millisecond late and the next as much early (7.19 then 4.85 ms): no frame of the screen's missed (that would be some 12 ms).
- **Ready sooner on Vulkan**: what a run draws with was built 0.48 to 0.50 s into it against 0.62 to 0.77 on DirectX 12 (with DXC).
- **The same picture**: a still view at the range within 0.17 of 765 on average; the inventory's six pictures drawn from an empty cache on each within 0.2 to 0.9 of 1,020 (alpha counted). No warning on Vulkan.
- **A backend that finds no card**: wgpu's `Instance::enumerate_adapters` for one backend answers before the engine starts (`backend::has_card`); with Vulkan's loader pointed at no driver (`VK_ICD_FILENAMES`, `VK_DRIVER_FILES`) it found none and the run took DirectX 12.
- So Vulkan is the default since 0.16.0 (`docs/spec/settings.md`, "The renderer"): more room for many weapons, built sooner, as even on screen.

## Added 2026-10-15, later still (health set beside the medicine course)

- **The game's health is updated on the player's whole-millisecond clock** (the movement context's `LastDeltaTime`, the trace's `clock_dt`), not the engine's frame time: over a stew's 5 s of work the frames' `dt` came to 4.992 s and the stew had already ended; the whole-millisecond clock came to 5.005. With it every recorded transition lands on its frame. The app's health takes `Movement::player_dt` since 0.16.1.
- **An effect added in a frame is updated with that frame's time** (the game's order: what the frame did, then the health's update): a bleeding's 0.4 s delay ends at 0.402 s from a hurt in the first frame. In a take where the recorder's hurt or the player's key fell after the health's update, the same ends a frame later: the replay allows two frames.
- **The game's buffs are the skills' levels times fixed figures** (`SkillManager`, a level being progress over 100, at most 51): Stress Resistance's `StressPain` 0.01 a level, Health's `HealthEnergy` and `HealthHydration` 0.006 and `HealthBreakChanceRed` 0.012, Vitality's `VitalityBuffBleedChanceRed` 0.012, Metabolism's `MetabolismRatioPlus` 0.01, Immunity's `ImmunityMiscEffects` 0.01 and `ImmunityPainKiller` 0.006, Surgery's `SurgerySpeed` (0.2 at 50, 0.4 elite) and `SurgeryReducePenalty` (0.01, 1 elite). The user's profile has 40 in each (`Install::profile_skill_levels` reads the newest profile, never writes). Pain's residue on a hurt of 30 to a leg of 65: 15 × 30/65 × (1 - 0.4) = 4.15 s, as recorded. The recorded character's energy tops at 110.
- **Existence's hunger and thirst**: 3.2 energy and 2.6 hydration every 60 s, cut by the Health skill (2.432 and 1.976 at level 40), on a loop that began with the raid.
- **A light bleeding takes 0.8 from every part** on its loop and 0.5 energy, as the simulation had it.
- **Food and drink by a share**: the inventory's "Use" opens a dialog for how much (`ItemUiContext.ShowSplitMenuDialog`), "Use All" and a quick-use key take what is left. The course's water was drunk half and half: 1.5 s, 30 of 60, 42 hydration (30 × 1.4, Metabolism) each time, both ending as a use ends.
- **Sprinting cannot cut a use short**: the course's "break it off by sprinting" did nothing (the movement bars a sprint while an item is at work). How the game's cut-short is reached in play (the hands' `HideWeapon`, `Remove`: `CancelApplyingItem`, `RemoveMedEffect`, the medicine effect's forced residue) is read, not recorded.
- **A mended part's share is a draw** (`Random.Range(HealthPenaltyMin, HealthPenaltyMax)`, the larger never drawn), drawn toward all by the Surgery skill: the recorded CMS left the arm at 39 of 60.
- `local\health_timeline_s62\` (a scratch crate) prints a recording's hands, effects, parts and vitals where they change: a quick look before a replay.

## Added 2026-10-15, at night (the HEALTH tab read from the scene)

- **The HEALTH tab is the GEAR tab's page** (`ItemsPanel.UpdateEquipment`): on it the left panel shows the health panel in place of the equipment panel and its strip of figures (`Health Parameters`, off in the scene, which the GEAR tab shows: weight, health, hydration, energy); the containers' panel, the quick-use bar and the stash or loot beside them are shown on either tab.
- **The left panel's size is set by a layout while the game runs**, and the scene's numbers give it: the left side (`Items Panel/LeftSide`) is a `HorizontalLayoutGroup` (its fields: padding 0, 0, 0, 0, the upper left corner, 5 apart, its children's widths and heights set and grown, none scaled, not reversed) of `Left Panel` (an `Image` with no sprite, which asks for nothing), `Border` (its `LayoutElement` has `ignoreLayout`) and `Containers Panel` (nothing to lay it out); `Blocker` is off. So the left panel is half the left side's width less the 5: 604.16 by 902 on a 16:9 screen, 528 wide on 4:3, 699 on 21:9. (The 637 by 902 written on 2026-10-15 for it came from nowhere kept.)
- **The scene's shared files keep no type trees** (`sharedassets44.assets`: engine 2022.3.43f1, 533 sprites, 484 textures, 16 clips): they are read by the trees of the same classes in a bundle of the install's, which keeps them: a sprite's and a texture's in `assets/content/textures/gui/hideout/...` (each a texture and its sprite), a clip's in `assets/content/weapons/weapon_root_anim_fix.bundle` (24 KB; `simple_animations.bundle`, the obvious one, is 154 MB). `probe classfind <class>` lists the smallest bundles that keep a class's tree. A texture of these files keeps its levels in the `.resS` beside it, read by its range.
- **The engine's own fields of its UI scripts**, in the order the scene has them after a script's name (read off the scene and checked against what each must hold): a layout group's padding (four whole numbers), its corner, its spacing, then seven flags each padded to four bytes; a layout element's flag to ignore it, its least, preferred and flexible width and height, its priority; every graphic's material, colour, whether it takes the mouse and how far out, whether a mask cuts it and whom it tells (an empty list here); then an image's sprite, kind, keeping its shape, filling its middle, how and how much it fills, which way, from where, whether its shape is the sprite's own, and its pixels per unit; a TextMeshPro text's words, right to left, its font and materials, its colours, a gradient, its sprites and style sheet, then its size (at byte 260 after a five-letter text), weight, sizing to fit with its least and most, its style and how it lies across and up and down.
- **A transform's own list of its children is the order the screen draws them in**; the order of the transforms in the file is not.
- **The parts' views, the figures and the rest are named by the panel's scripts**, not by their names: `InventoryScreenHealthPanel` (the figures' panel, the silhouette, the head, chest, stomach, left arm, right arm, left leg and right leg, the legend), `BodyPartView` (figures, bar, border, the part's shape in the silhouette, cross, effects, damage, button), `HealthParametersPanel` (overall health, poisoning, radiation, blood pressure, energy, hydration, temperature; weight none on this panel), `HealthParameterPanel` (text, maximum, two glows, icon, warning colour, its other, the fewest digits, the noughts' colour; `BuffableHealthParameterPanel` then two arrows and the rate's text). On this panel the figures name no icon and no glow but the overall health's warning glow.
- **The views' glow is hidden standing**: the views' button's animator plays the scene's clip `Normal` (`BackGlowImage` at alpha 0) and `Highlighted` (0 to 1 over 0.333 s) when told (`HealthBarButton`: a thing that can be used on the part held over it). The rate's arrows play `BuffPaneActive` (1.5 s, looping: 2 units down to 2 up and back, alpha 0.5 to 1) while their figure is short of its maximum and `BuffPanePassive` (still, whole) otherwise. `probe sceneclips <file> <bundle with a clip> [name]` lists a file's clips with what each drives, sampled through its length; what a binding's numbers are for is found by hashing the panel's own paths and the usual properties (`m_Color.a`, `m_AnchoredPosition.y`).
- **The screen's rates are each effect's own** (`SetHealthRatesPerSecond`, a second, shown times 60): set as the effect starts, changed as it works, zeroed as it fades. A heavy bleeding shows −94.50 health a minute (0.9 from seven parts every 4 s), a light one −56.00; existence −3.20 energy and −2.60 hydration; a bleeding's own energy besides (0.5 each 6 s for a light one). A bleeding's health rate becomes what its last loop took from the whole (`BleedHealth`), which is less where a part had less left than the damage.
- **Blood pressure is never written**: the figure keeps the scene's `120/80` and its rate `0/0`; poisoning's and radiation's rates are the scene's `0.00`.
- **The game's rounding**: `Math.Round` and `Convert.ToByte` round a half to even, `MathUtil.Floor` and `Ceil` are the plain ones, `Positive()` is at least `float.Epsilon` and `Negative()` at most −1e-45.

## Added 2026-10-15, at night, later (the GEAR tab's strip; the game's fonts)

- **Where 637 came from**: the GEAR tab's strip of figures (`Health Parameters`) was saved laid out for a left panel 637 wide (four panels of 138.11 in a strip of 552.44); at run time its row (`HorizontalLayoutGroup`: no padding, no spacing, widths and heights set and grown; each panel's `LayoutElement` asks 208 by 128, sets no least) lays them out again, each from 0 towards 208 as far as the strip's width allows: 133.02 on 16:9. The scene's saved places of a laid-out object are the last layout's, not the running game's; `ScenePanel::placed` now lays out a row's children as Unity does.
- **The build keeps its fonts whole** in `resources.assets` (317 MB; its table read from its head and each object by its range): eleven `Font` objects with their files' bytes (Bender regular, bold, italic and bold italic, OpenType; Arial in four, TrueType; Meiryo, Noto Sans Korean, SimSun). A font's fields are few and in a fixed order (name, line spacing, material, size, texture, five small figures, its characters at 44 bytes each, its kerning at 8, its pixel scale, then its file's bytes), read so and checked by the file's signature. The panels' TextMeshPro texts name font assets in the same file, `Jovanny Lemonad - Bender Outline SDF` and `... Bender Normal SDF`, each named for the font it was made from.
- **The weight's figure** (`HealthParametersPanel`): written as the weight changes, to a place, against `UpperOverweightLimit` (`BaseOverweightLimits.y`), and coloured by `SetWarningColor`: its own up to `LowerOverweightLimit` (the least `x` of the walk's, base, sprint's and walk speed's overweight limits), its other colour (0.918, 0.710, 0.196) under the upper, its warning red over it; both times the skills' and the health's carrying modifiers (1 and 0 untrained).
- The left panel's foot (`Bottom Bar`) is a white image of a sprite tiled; it lies under both tabs.

## Added 2026-10-15, at night, latest (the game's GEAR tab: slots, things, containers)

- **The game's gear page has an outline of the character** behind the slots (`CharacterGear`, `character_pmc`, which `EquipmentTab` never hides): 0.19.0's "no figure, as in a raid" was wrong; the 3D model is what the game lacks there.
- **The build's Resources are found by the engine's own table**: `globalgamemanagers` holds one `ResourceManager` (class 147) whose container lists each path in lower case with its object (`resource_paths`; `probe resources-paths <part>`). The UI's prefabs are among them (`prefabs/ugui/layouts/slot_layout`, `grid_layout`, `ui/rig layouts/<name>`, 63 of those), and the slots' pictures (`slots/equipment/tacticalvest` and the like, sprites).
- **A prefab in `resources.assets` (317 MB) is read object by object** from its root game object (`Scene::load_prefab`): its table from the file's head (`SerializedFile::head_size`, `parse_head`), then each game object, its transform's children in their order, its scripts with their classes (named in `globalgamemanagers.assets`), by their ranges. A template a scene points to by its script is read from that script's game object (`load_prefab_of_script`).
- **The views of a thing**: their ground is the item's database colour (`BackgroundColor`) at 0.302 over whatever is under it; the game's table of those colours is in its code (`TaxonomyColorExtension`). A magazine's count is not the same thing as a stack's: it hangs on whether the magazine was checked and on the skill (`Magazine.GetAmmoCountByLevel`).
- **The containers' column** is a column of rows (120 down, 45 apart), each a row of a 125 by 125 slot panel and the container's grids 7 to its right; a container's grids are at the places of its own layout prefab where its template names one (`RigLayoutName` for vests, `GridLayoutName` for backpacks), else in a row 6 apart (`GeneratedGridsView`, a template in `sharedassets44.assets` the scene points to).
- **A sliced picture's middle may be left out** (`Image.fillCenter`): the grids' `border_shadow` frame is one, which drawn whole hid the cells under a light grey; Bevy's slicer always fills the middle, so the app draws sliced pictures in nine pieces. **`PixelPerfectSpriteScaler`** keeps a sliced border a texture pixel to a screen pixel at any size of the screen.
- **A pointer read off a script's fields by scanning can be misread four bytes off**, as a huge object number (a pointer's file and the next one's low half): object numbers here are below 2^40.
- `probe scenepanel <file> <path end> [w h]`, `probe prefab <path or file:script>`, `probe containers [layout]` print what the app reads, with each object's layout group and element and each picture's texture (size, format and its pixels' ranges).

## Added 2026-10-15, late at night (the inventory screen's places, a scroll view)

- **The inventory screen's own places** at 1920 by 1080: its bar of tabs across the top, 43 high; the left side at 11.6 across and 48 down, 1213.3 by 902; the right's panel (`Stash Panel`) at 1228 across and 75 down, 680 by 873; the quick-use bar at 935 down. The left side's top region is not under the tabs: the equipment panel lies 100 below its top, the containers' first row 120.
- **A screen's scripts point to places by their transforms** (`RectTransform`): a scroll view's content, view and handle are such pointers, which the panels did not resolve (they knew game objects and scripts). `ScenePanel::transform_at` resolves them; the pointer scans that look for runs of scripts (`pointers_from`) keep to scripts and objects, so that a transform among the fields does not move a run.
- **The containers' column is a scroll view of its own** (`ScrollRectNoDrag`: dragging the content does nothing): 63 units a notch, clamped, no inertia, its bar hidden while all shows; its view is its own rectangle, which starts 37 in from the column's left, so the wheel does nothing over the strip left of it nor over its bar. Its bar's handle is tinted, not the bar (`m_TargetGraphic`). Nothing puts it back to its top when the screen opens (`AutoZeroing` off).
- **`Scrollbar.ClickRepeat` steps twice on the frame the bar is pressed**: started in the press, it runs at once and again at that frame's end (`WaitForEndOfFrame`), then once a frame.
- **A frame of a pixel stretched over a panel needs point sampling**: the left side's frame (`Allscreens_Borders_Default`, 3 by 3, its middle pixel clear, `fillCenter` on) blended between its pixels lays a pale wash over both panels; its texture is set to point filtering (`m_TextureSettings.m_FilterMode` 0), now read (`TextureData::point`) and kept by the app's sampler.
- **In Bevy 0.18 a plain UI node lets the mouse through**: `Node` requires a `FocusPolicy`, which is `Pass` unless set; a `Button` brings `Block`. (Written wrongly at 0.21.0 from `focus.rs`'s `unwrap_or(&FocusPolicy::Block)`, which no node reaches.) Of two buttons that overlap, the one drawn later takes the mouse, as Unity's raycast gives it to the graphic on top.
- `probe sprite <file> <id>` prints a sprite's cut and, for a texture 16 by 16 or less, its pixels; `probe containers` prints the column's scroll view and bar.

## Added 2026-10-15, later at night (the bar of tabs, TextMeshPro's widths)

- **TextMeshPro lays a text out by its font asset, not its font file**: the asset (`TMP_FontAsset`, a script's object in `resources.assets`, read by its fields' order: the object's head and name, a hash, its material, its version and source font, how its atlas is filled, its face, 52 bytes a glyph, 16 a letter) keeps each glyph's advance in whole pixels at the size it was sampled at (48 for Bender), and adds `normalSpacingOffset` (2 for Bender's regular asset: hundredths of the size) after every letter but the last. A text's preferred width is that, with its margins, rounded up to a hundredth: the same to the hundredth as 53 of the 115 widths the scene keeps for texts sized to their words (`probe textwidths level44`); most of the rest are short by whole letters' spacing, as if kept from before the asset had it. The font file's own advances run some 0.3 short a letter at 16. The asset's last fields are its weight table (ten pairs of typefaces), an empty old list, `normalStyle`, `normalSpacingOffset`, `boldStyle`, `boldSpacing` (2, 2, 0.5 for Bender), `italicStyle`, `tabSize`; its bold (700) has a typeface of its own (Bender Bold SDF).
- **A TextMeshPro text's fields after its spacing** (476 bytes for a plain one): the spacings of words, lines and paragraphs, the narrowing, wrapping and its ratio, the overflow, two linked texts, kerning (`m_enableKerning`, off on the tabs' words), two paddings, rich text, control characters, orthographic, culling, two mappings, the line offset, the sorting, the static scale, the buffer, the descender, the page, the margins, then three flags, a material and a mask's offset.
- **A script built on Odin's `SerializedMonoBehaviour`** (the game's `UIElement` and all its screens' elements) keeps 40 bytes before its own fields when it serializes nothing of Odin's (`SerializationData`: a format, bytes, objects, a string, a prefab, two lists and its nodes); read past (`skip_odin`), not assumed.
- **The bar of tabs' widths** are all the widest tab's (`TabSizeController`), each tab's its words' sample plus 85: in English ACHIEVEMENTS (113.86) makes every tab 198.86, 172.86 apart (the gap −26). The tabs' keys are the install's words' (`HEALTH_TAB` for HEALTH; `Prestige` shown in capitals by its text's style). The prestige tab's `NewState` is the "new level" mark, off unless a level can be finished.
- **A Unity mask** (`Mask`) draws what is under it only where its own picture is not clear (its stencil written with alpha clipping), and shows its picture only if told (`showMaskGraphic`, off for the tabs). Bevy's screens have nothing like it: the app cuts the tabs' fills to their masks itself.
- `probe tabs` (the bar as read, each tab's words and width), `probe textwidths <scene>` (every text sized to its words: its kept width beside TextMeshPro's), `probe raw <file> <id> [from] [words]` (an object's bytes as words).

## Added 2026-10-15, latest at night (the panel for a container opened, Unity's layout)

- **A raid shows a looted container in the stash panel's simple panel** (`ItemsPanel.Show`: `SimpleStashPanel` for any loot, `ComplexStashPanel` for a corpse's gear) and nothing on the right while none is open. The simple panel's script keeps Odin's data and the input node's field (44 bytes) before its pointers: the header, its grids' view, the grid's name text, the filters, the money, the name's box and its text, the lobby's list (the bottom left panel: sort, pin, lock, priority), the sort, the sorting table's tab, the search's tab, the scroll view, the outside mark, the pin, the lock, the priority.
- **Its header in a raid is the toggle-styled one, in its disabled look**, saying the install's `LOOT`; its row (`TopPanel`) is sized to it and stands above the panel's top (`ContentSizeFitter`, pivot at its lower left, 2 above the panel).
- **The filter column shows for a container** (each grid view shows the filter panel it is given), its tabs 32 high and 2 apart; a tab of a kind the grids hold nothing of has its picture and icon at 0.15 (`FilterTab.SetInteractable`). Its 18 rules are the game's item classes in this order: all, weapon, magazine, ammo and ammo box, meds, food and drink, knife, mod, throwable, barter, vest (the icon of a rig), visors, searchable and simple containers, armor, info, key, special, found in raid.
- **A loot container's name** is its short name in the install's words in capitals (`ItemController.ContainerName` over `LootItem.CreateLootContainer`'s `item.ShortName.Localized()`).
- **The panel's scroll view is elastic with no elasticity** (`MovementType.Elastic`, `m_Elasticity` 0): Unity's `SmoothDamp` takes its least time (a ten-thousandth of a second), so a view let past an end is back within a hundredth of a unit on the same frame; the containers' column's is clamped. A scroll view with no viewport shows its own rectangle.
- **TextMeshPro's height of a line** is its asset's ascent to descent at the size over the sampling size, rounded up to a hundredth: 17.61 for Bender at 16, which with the padding of the corpse panel's box of a name is the 27.61 the scene keeps.
- **Unity's layout, more of it**: a `VerticalLayoutGroup` lays out as the horizontal one along the other axis (`ScenePanel::in_row`); what an object asks of a layout (`LayoutUtility`: least, preferred, flexible) comes from its own group's children (their sum with the spacing along it, their largest across, and the padding) and is overridden by its `LayoutElement` (`ScenePanel::asks`); a group with a `ContentSizeFitter` takes that size about its pivot. A picture's and a text's own preferred sizes are not had (none). Laying the scene's panels out with a script's on and off states matters: the header's row fits only once the header is on.
- `probe stash` (the panel as read), `probe points <scene> <path> <script>` (each pointer of a script into its panel, objects, scripts and places).

Its views are off in the scene and shown by its script; laid out on, they are 70 apart with 10 more between the two groups.
