## Added 2026-10-15, latest at night (the item's own hands)

- **An item's use rig holds the item at scale nought**: the AI-2's model hangs under `bone_item_medkit`, which rests at scale (0, 0, 0), and the clips scale it up as it is drawn; an animator that drops scale tracks draws the hands alone. The animator now keeps a bone's scale where its rig asks (`WeaponAnimatorData::load_scaled`, the items' rigs). **A weapon's clips carry scale tracks on nearly every bone** (the M4A1's and the AKM's: the arms' bones, the camera's, every part of the weapon), not taken: with them taken the M4A1 at rest looked the same. What showed under the M4A1 of the starting loadout at rest, with or without them, is a little tag on the glove (the user, 2026-10-15: "you might be mistaking the 'bayonet' for a little tag coming from the glove"), not a blade.
- **The item's hands' animator is made a ten-thousandth of a second on** (`MedsController.Setup`: `SkipTime(0.0001)`), before its variant and `Active` are set: the recordings' first frame finds the Salewa's resting state at 0.253 of its 0.033 s clip, (0.0001 + 0.00833) over it.
- **`UseTimeMultiplier` takes the Surgery skill's buff for a percentage** (`GoNextBodyPart`: `SurgerySpeed.Value / 100`, the buff being 0.2 at level 50): 0.0016 at the user's level 40, where health's own working time takes the 0.16 whole. The fifth more goes by the list's head: the common body is the seven parts' sum (`GetBodyPartHealth(Common)`). The CMS's recorded speed, 1.2016, has the fifth: its use named the arm (its own priority of parts, "Use"'s way), which lacked 3.4.
- **Which parts an item is put to depends on how it was used**: a quick-use key and "Use All" name the common body; the inventory's "Use" the common body unless the item has its own priority of parts; a drop on the HEALTH tab its part (`health.md`, "The parts' list by the way of use"). With the common body a part done always ends the use. Read, not seen: a drug named a part stays on the list (`TryGetBodyPartToApply` lets a drug go to any part named), so a counted drug dropped on a part would seem to go on to its next dose there.
- **The next hands after an item come one frame after its put-away's mark in eleven uses of twelve, three frames in one**: the game makes them asynchronously (`SetControllerInsteadRemovedOne`); the simulation gives the hands up at the mark.
- **The seven items' animators differ in shape and agree in their marks** (`health.md`, "The hands"): some rest before their draw, some begin in it, some in their use; each has `OutUse`, `WeapIn`, `WeapOut` and `ThirdAction` where the controller looks for them, and the controller's rules hold them all.
- `probe states` prints each state's hash as a recording keeps it (`#<24 bits>`); `probe props <id> [names]` an item's properties; `probe tree <bundle>` a rig's tree.

## Added 2026-10-15, latest at night (the near clip plane; the shadow of a body drawn as a shadow alone)

The user, after 0.25.0: "All models now clip when too close to the first person camera, check mk7 mutant and akm. The first person shadow is not showing the third person body as it should. (Only half the player is visible). Contact shadows are missing too".

- **Bevy 0.18 clips by a near plane of its own** (`PerspectiveProjection::near_clip_plane`, `bevy_camera` 0.18's published source): it defaults to 0.1 m whatever `near` is, and a camera made with `near: 0.03, ..default()` cut everything nearer than 10 cm (the projection made oblique to put the near plane there). The AKM's receiver cover and rear sight and the MK47's charging handle and sight, aimed, were cut open (`local\shots\near_akm_before_s72.png`, `near_akm_after_s72.png`, `near_mk47_after_s72.png`). Since the move to Bevy 0.18 (0.13.0). Now the plane is put at the game's 0.03 m (`main.rs`, `NEAR_PLANE`), and at the character-model camera's own 0.5 m.
- **Bevy 0.17 on lets a camera's shadow maps take only what is on that camera's render layers** (`bevy_pbr`'s `queue_shadows`: `camera_layers.intersects(mesh_layers)`; 0.16 had no such test). The first-person upper body and the corpse's head were on a layer the sun saw and the camera did not, Unity's "shadows only" as the app had made it, so in first person the body's shadow was its legs and hips alone, ending 0.45 m from the feet where the head's should reach 1.1 m with the sun at 58 degrees (`shadow_hi_down_s72.png`; from overhead, where the upper body is drawn, it was whole). Not the cascades (the same at medium and high, on Vulkan and DirectX 12, with the depth clamp native or emulated) nor culling (every body piece is exempt from it). Now such a mesh stays on the camera's layer and is drawn with a twin of its standard material whose fragment shader keeps no pixel (`shadow_only.rs`): the camera's pass draws nothing of it and the shadow pass writes its depth (`shadow_hi_down_fixed_s72.png`, `shadow_hi_ahead_fixed_s72.png`). It holds while the main camera has no depth prepass: one would take the mesh's depth and hide what is behind it.
- **The body's shadow falls on the first-person arms** with the sun behind, as a body drawn "shadows only" does: the game's first-person body is so (`skin_Body` `ShadowsOnly`), its own hands off.
- **No contact shadows were ever drawn**: Bevy 0.18 has no such effect, and the app has had no ambient occlusion. The game's is its `Ssao` setting (`HighQuality` in the user's `Graphics.ini`); not read, not built (asked).


## Added 2026-10-16 (feet over the ground after a short drop; ambient occlusion, DLSS and FSR researched)

The user: "sometimes when walking off a ledge, the characters legs don't actually touch the ground, and we can see space between the feet and shadow", then: "Go 3 steps up the first stair case, then walk off towards the main spawn. Take a look at the feet then".

- **Reproduced** (`trp.exe --start stairs --tap 100:w:170 --tap 330:d:200 --view watch --log-pacing ...`, `local\pace_st2_s73.txt`): up to the third step, then stepped off the flight's side toward the spawn, the character comes to rest with its place 0.0556 m over the ground, 2.3 m clear of the stairs; its ankles at 0.119 and toes at 0.075 where standing anywhere else they are at 0.063 and 0.020. A walk off the 6 m ledge lands on the ground exactly; stopped short of that ledge's edge, the capsule rests on its corner with the feet 1 to 18 cm below the top (`pace_ledge30_s73.txt` to `pace_ledge44_s73.txt`).
- **It is the game's own rule, measured already**: on the ground means the controller touched ground in its last move, or a ray from the capsule's middle meets ground within half its height, its skin and the check distance (0.07 to 0.15 m by state; `MovementContext.UpdateGroundCollision`); on the ground on the flat gravity adds nothing (`ApplyGravity`). A drop whose last frame ends within the check stays there. The eighth recording's starting spot stood 7.62 cm over the terrain (`ground.txt`; "The terrain is not where the feet are" above).
- **The game's drawn body rides the capsule too**: the player prefab has FinalIK's foot grounder (`GrounderFBBIK`, switched by `GrounderSetActive` on a jump and a landing), but in the game's build its `Update` is empty and nothing calls its `Initiate`, so it never joins the full-body solver; `Player.HeightInterpolation` only eases the body after a change of height (the app has it, `body-animator.md`). Read, not seen: the recordings keep the capsule, not the game's foot bones.
- **Ambient occlusion in Bevy 0.18** (`bevy_pbr`'s `ssao`): it requires `Msaa::Off` (with MSAA it logs an error and draws nothing), brings a depth and a normal prepass with it, and is advised with temporal anti-aliasing against its noise. The game's choices: `ESSAOMode` Off, FastestPerformance, FastPerformance, HighQuality, HighestQuality, ColoredHighestQuality (the user's `HighQuality`).
- **The game's anti-aliasing and upscalers**: `EAntialiasingMode` None, FXAA, TAA_Low, TAA_High (the user's None); DLSS (`EDLSSMode` Quality, Balanced, Performance, UltraPerformance, with a preset: the user's Quality, preset M, on), FSR 2 and FSR 3 (the same four modes; the user's off), supersampling, Reflex, a sharpening of 0.6. The app draws with Bevy's 4x MSAA, which is none of these.
- **DLSS in Bevy 0.18**: the optional `dlss` feature of `bevy_anti_alias` (the crate `dlss_wgpu` 2): an RTX card, Vulkan only, a DLSS project id given before the renderer starts; the camera in HDR with a depth prepass, motion vectors and jitter, MSAA off. To build: NVIDIA's DLSS SDK (not redistributable by the crate), the Vulkan SDK and clang, `DLSS_SDK` and `VULKAN_SDK` set ([dlss_wgpu](https://github.com/bevyengine/dlss_wgpu)); to run: `nvngx_dlss.dll` beside the exe, and the SDK's licence text shipped with it. As a cargo feature off by default, others could still build without the SDK.
- **FSR**: nothing in Bevy or for wgpu; FSR 2's Rust crates are unsafe bindings to AMD's C library on raw Vulkan (no DirectX 12, no wgpu: [fsr2-rs](https://github.com/notapenguin0/fsr2-rs), [fsr2-sys](https://www.lib.rs/crates/fsr2-sys)), and FSR 3 has none. AMD's FidelityFX is open source, so a port to wgpu is possible and large.
- **What the camera would need for any of them**: MSAA off, a depth prepass, normals or motion vectors. The first-person body drawn as its shadow alone (`shadow_only.rs`) would then need its prepass to draw nothing for the camera's view while still drawing for the sun's.
- **Drawn down, the feet meet the ground** (0.26.0, `GROUNDED FEET`): the body's root lowered by the gap between the character's underside and the ground that holds it, while on the ground within the check, puts the drawn ankles and toes where they stand on level ground (0.063 and 0.020 m), and leaves a character resting on an edge's corner as it is (the gap there is metres; nothing is done).
- **The game's foot grounder** (0.27.0, `GROUNDED FEET` as foot IK; `body-animator.md`, "The feet on the ground"): the player prefab's `GrounderFBBIK` (on `IKController`, `m_Enabled` 0) has weight 1, step 0.35, foot speed 0.9, prediction 0.3, pelvis speed 20, lower and lift pelvis weights 1 and 0.1, its quality 1 (simple) on the prefab and set to the fastest for one's own character by `GamePlayerOwner`. Run on the drawn body: standing sideways on the stairs, each ankle and toe at 0.063 and 0.020 m over its own step, the pelvis a step's height down and the higher leg's knee bent up; walking up the flight the toes go into the steps by at most 5.1 cm where they went 10.5 (one ray at the ankle cannot see a riser ahead of the toe).
- **The foot IK taken back** (0.28.0; the user: "The ik stuff doesn't work while sprinting, and when legs spread too much the start twitching/bending backwards"): a two-bone solve bends the knee in the plane the animated knee makes with the hip and the goal, which is ill defined for a leg near straight and can flip from frame to frame; and a ray at the ankle, looking ahead by the prediction, reads a sprint's long strides and the steps' risers poorly. Not looked into further. What did hold, for any drop of the drawn body: the shoulders and ribcage hang from the weapon rig, so in first person the drop must stop at the waist and from outside the rig must come down with the body.

## Added 2026-10-16 (the inventory set beside the user's own captures)

- **The game composes its screens in gamma space.** In the user's own capture of the GEAR tab at 1080 lines, the left side's frame (`Allscreens_Borders_Default`, white at 0.149) over a ground of 18 comes to 51: blended by the stored, gamma-encoded numbers that is 53, in linear light 110. A panel's black at 0.498 (the containers' column's `Scrollview Parent`) leaves the world behind it at 9 to 10 where the world beside it is 16 to 22, half of it (linear light would leave 0.73). Bevy blends its screens in linear light, which draws everything half clear lighter: the left panel's foot (`bottom_bar_tile`, white at 0 to 15 of 255) came out a pale band from 27 to 72. The app now composes the screens in gamma space with `TARKOV UI` on (`gamma_ui.rs`): the frame encoded before the screens, Bevy's two screen shaders patched as they load to encode their inputs, the frame decoded after.
- **The game's blur behind the inventory** (`InventoryBlur` on `Cam2`): its fields read from `resources.assets` by the engine's order (value, material, 256, 128, 8); its material `FastBlur` names a shader in `globalgamemanagers.assets` (object 284) whose compiled programs are one LZ4 block (710 bytes to 1464, the shader's `compressedBlob`; `probe unpack`); the fragment program's DXBC reads, by hand: two samples, `add r0, t1 - t0`, `mad o0, cb0[2].x, r0, t0`: the sharp picture and the blurred square mixed by `_Value`, nothing else. The darkness behind the user's screen is their raid's own.
- **Bevy finds a font's face by its family and weight** (`bevy_text`'s `get_attrs`: `Family::Name` and `TextFont::weight`, normal unless told): two fonts of one family (Bender Regular and Bender Bold) loaded as two `Font` assets, a text given the bold one's handle at the normal weight is drawn in the regular face. A bold text has to ask for `FontWeight::BOLD`. Every bold text of the game's panels was drawn regular until 0.29.0.
- **A font asset's name need not begin with its font's**: the bold typeface of `Bender Normal SDF` is the asset `DON'T USE! Jovanny Lemonad - Bender Bold SDF`; its face (`m_FaceInfo`) says Bender Bold, which finds the font `Jovanny Lemonad - Bender Bold` in `resources.assets` (`screen::font_by_face`).
- **The scene reader skipped two things the screens use**: objects' turns (the sheath's empty picture is turned 45 degrees: `Layout::turn`, from the transform's rotation about z) and the engine's own parts that scripts point to (a tab's `CanvasGroup`: `Scene::components`).
- **The install's starting characters have no dogtag**: SPT's server gives a profile its own as it is made. The Standard edition's characters have their pockets (`627a4e6b255f7527fb05a0f6`: four cells, three special slots).
- **A component Bevy stops extracting is taken off; what was made from it is not** (0.30.0, the user: "strange artifacting occuring when opening, then closing the inventory and walking around. stuff gets blurry, flashes blue"). `ExtractComponentPlugin` removes the render world's copy when `extract_component` gives none, but `UniformComponentPlugin`'s `DynamicUniformIndex` and anything a prepare system put on the view stay: the blur's pass kept running after the screen shut, on a stale offset into its uniforms and on squares the texture cache had already let go. A view node's query has to name the extracted component itself, and what was put on beside it has to be taken off (`screen_blur::prepare`).
- **A page built by commands is drawn a frame before an unordered system fills it**: the health panel's filling (`fill_panel`) ran unordered with the inventory's rebuild, so a page built on a frame showed the scene's own colours on it (the parts' shapes in the scene are red, at full alpha): "the little shilloute flashes red". Ordered after the screen's systems (`inventory_view::ScreenDrawn`), the commands are applied between and the page is filled on the frame it is built.
- **The parts turn red as they are hurt** with the HEALTH tab open, as the game's do every frame (`BodyPartView.Update`: the shape red at `1 - share / 0.8` under 0.8 of the part), checked in a capture; the whole's figure follows once a second, as the game's (`HealthParametersPanel.Update`, a second between).

## Added 2026-10-16 (the battle screen's panels; a font's rim; the scenes' switched-off scripts)

- **A scene's script may be switched off, and the reader had read it as on** (`Behaviour.enabled`, the byte after the script's object): the battle screen's slider handles each have their own picture switched off and an arrow under them that draws. `Scene` now keeps each script's `enabled`, and a panel's image or text switched off draws nothing. The inventory's captures were the same after.
- **A slider's handle is not where the scene keeps it** (`Slider.UpdateVisuals` runs as the value is set, not as the scene is saved): the speed's handle stands at 0 in the scene with 0.575 kept as its value, the stance's at 0.83 with 1 kept. Placed by the anchors the slider gives it (along the slider at the share, across over the whole), each lands on the notch of its value (standing on the top mark, crouched on the seventh, lying on the last).
- **What the stance panel's events carry**: `OnSmoothedPoseLevelChange` gives the stance's target as it is set (`SetPoseLevel`: `_poseLevel`), the smoothed one only as lying begins or ends; `Stamina.OnChanged` comes as a drain is begun or ended and as a cost is taken off at once (`Consume`), never as a pool refills: the panel is not held up by a refill; `OnSpeedChangedEvent` comes on every frame of the sprint's run-up.
- **The battle screen's part colours lose their alpha** (`ColorUtilities.Saturation` makes a new colour from the three channels halved, its alpha whole): white to red's alpha (`0.8 − 0.6h²`) never shows, and a healthy part is (0.5, 0.5, 0.5).
- **TextMeshPro's rims and shadows are in the font asset's material**, not in the text: `Bender Outline SDF` (the HEALTH tab's headers, the things' figures) has an outline of 0.1 and an underlay of 0.6 unmoved, black, a dark rim; `Bender Shadowed SDF` (`BACK`, the version's line) an underlay moved 0.3 right and down, black at 0.33, a drop shadow. Read with `probe fontmaterial`.

## Added 2026-10-16, later (the battle screen's quick bar; the game's screens' list)

- **The game's screens stand in a list**: showing one shuts the one shown before (`ScreenController.PrepareShow`: `CloseCurrentScreen`), and shutting it shows the one before again from the start (`ShowScreenAsPrevious`: `InitScreen`, `DisplayScreen`, `Screen.Show`). So the inventory opening shuts the battle screen (its panels go at once: `BattleUIScreen.Close`), and shutting it shows the battle screen afresh (`UpdatePanelsVisibility(false)`: a panel always shown fades in, one that autohides stays out until raised), as the user describes their game.
- **The battle screen's quick bar is the inventory's script** (`InventoryScreenQuickAccessPanel`), but its input node lists fifteen children before the views (the inventory's lists none): the reader assumed an empty list and ran off its fields. It now skips the list by its count. Its views are a weapon's view with an under-barrel weapon's beside it (`BoundSlotView._subWeaponView`, off for a weapon without one), and each names the ground lit for the thing in the hands (`Background`), which the inventory's views do not.
- **A view's fields after the input node's** (`QuickSlotView`): its install place, key's text, caption, border, ground and selector's arrow, in that order.
- **The first weapon is in the hands before the battle screen shows**: in the app the hands take it a few frames into the run, after the HUD is up, which raised the quick bar as a change of the hands; it is not counted now.
- **The game's own word for the stamina's setting is misspelt in its texts** (`Ui/Settings/StanimaVisibility`, and `HeathVisibility` for the health's): the keys are as the install has them.

## Added 2026-10-16, latest (an item stopped and let go on a part; the text's rims)

- **An item in use is stopped by the shoot, aim and scope keys** (`MedsInputTranslator.TranslateCommand`: `ToggleShooting`, `ToggleAlternativeShooting`, `ChangeScope` call `Remove`), 0.6 s later (`RemoveWithDelay`), by emptying the parts' list and calling `CancelApplyingItem`, which the local player's health (`PlayerHealthController`) makes `RemoveMedEffect`; the client's sends a packet, the offline one throws. The aim key's command is `ToggleAlternativeShooting` (`InputManager`: `ToggleInputKeyCombination(EGameKey.Aim, …)`).
- **A drop on a part of the HEALTH tab** goes to `HealthController.ApplyItem(item, part)` (`HealthBarButton.OnDrop`, a script on each part's view), which on the local player checks `CanApplyItem` for the part and takes the item in hand with that part its list, as a quick-use key does with the common body; food and drink go in hand with their share, the part not passed on. The view's glow (`Highlighted`, 0.333 s, the glow's alpha 0 to 1) is lit as a thing that can be used enters (`OnPointerEnter`), only then.
- **TextMeshPro's outline and underlay widths are in the distance field's measure**, not in the atlas's pixels: taken as `_GradientScale` atlas pixels to the whole (0.31.0), Bender Outline's rim came to half a pixel at 11 and could not be seen; the field's whole spans twice the padding (`_GradientScale` less one), which makes it about a pixel, as the game's captions show it. An underlay's offset is in the atlas's pixels times the gradient's scale (the shader's own sum), unchanged.
- **The texts of the things' views and the HEALTH tab use their font asset's own material** (`m_sharedMaterial` read now: `TextLook::material`; `probe textmats`), none a preset of its own.

## Added 2026-10-16, night (the screen's sounds; the window that shows a thing; the tabs' order; a text's face)

- **The sound settings are notches, not levels**: `Sound.ini` keeps `OverallVolume` and `InterfaceVolume` as 0 to 10, each an index into `SoundSettingsGroup._realSoundValues` (−80 to 0 dB), set on the mixer's `MasterVolume` and `UIVolume`. The user's install has both at 1: −40 dB each, −80 for an interface sound, which cannot be heard. The app keeps the install's, and the options' SOUND page changes them.
- **An audio clip's samples are an FSB5 bank of one sample** beside its file (`m_Resource`). Its IMA ADPCM (codec 7) is FMOD's Xbox layout: in each 36-bytes-a-channel block all the channels' four-byte headers come first, then the codes four bytes a channel in turns. Read as bytes alternating between channels, a stereo clip came out as noise peaking at full scale; set right by comparing each reading's roughness with the clips kept as PCM (`probe sounds`). Thirteen of the things' clips are Vorbis, not read.
- **The interface's sounds are a list on one object of the Resources** (`audio/uisoundswrapper`, `UISoundsWrapper`): after the script's header and its name, a count and sixteen bytes a pair (the kind as a 32-bit number, the clip's pointer).
- **Where a moved thing went decides its sound** (`ItemUiContext.PlayOperationSound`): only a slot's or a magazine's address names a holder; a grid's cell names none, so a thing put into any grid sounds its `drop`, the equipment's own slots and a magazine its `use`, a part into a weapon's slot the interface's installing by its class (`GetModClass`, functional first, then gear, vital).
- **The inventory screen's `_playBackpackSounds`** could not be read from the scene with the fields the reader knows (the word before its back button's pointer is not it): taken as on.
- **The tabs are drawn in the reverse of their row** (`TabGroup.SelectTab` with `setAsLastSibling`, which the inventory's group is made with): every tab is set last among its siblings from the last to the first, then the chosen one, so each tab lies over the one after it and the chosen over all. The row's order (`OrderNumber`) is where each lies, not which is on top. Drawn the other way, the chosen tab's words were cut by the next tab's slanted edge (the user's capture).
- **TextMeshPro's face dilation widens the letters themselves** (`_FaceDilate`, 0.15 in Bender's materials: the shader's weight `_FaceDilate * _ScaleRatioA * 0.5` in the distance field's measure, 0.42 pixels at a size of 11 at 1440 lines), and the rim's edge lies that much further out; every edge fades over the shader's ramp, twice the padding over the gradient's scale in pixels (1.8) at any size. Left out, the app's words were thinner and their rims harder than the user's capture.
- **The window that shows a thing is found through the preloader's item context**: its prefab is not in the inventory's scene but in a shared file, pointed to by `ItemUiContext._infoWindowTemplate` on `UIContext` in the preloader's scene (`sharedassets49.assets` in the user's install). Its prefab keeps the panel of a weapon's parts on; laid out with it on, everything under it sits 26 units low for a thing without slots, as the game turns it off (`_modsPanel.SetActive`).
- **A window opens beside the one opened last, not the one on top** (`ItemUiContext.OpenWindow`: the window with the highest `WindowData.Index`, a count of windows opened; bringing one to the top does not change it), 40 units right and down; the first in the middle of the screen. A fifth window of a kind shuts the oldest of the kind, a ninth the oldest of all.

## Added 2026-10-16, late (the user's recording of both games: the sounds, the letters)

- **rodio 0.20.1's queue garbles the start of a sound appended to a new sink** (Bevy 0.18's `AudioPlayer` makes a sink for each sound and appends at once): an empty queue stands as one channel at 48 kHz (`Empty`, then `Zero` of one channel at 44.1 kHz in stretches of 512 samples), the output's mixer reads a source's format again only at a stretch's end, so up to 512 samples of a new sound are read in the stretch's format: a stereo clip's start is its two channels read as one (both sides alike, a third quieter), a mono clip's start played at 48 kHz unconverted (fast). Seen in the user's recording (6 to 12 ms of every sound) and made again away from the app through rodio's own sink and mixer. One endless source of one format mixed by the app avoids it.
- **Unity plays a mono clip at equal power in the middle**: the game's mono clips came out 3.0 to 3.4 dB under their level in the recording (its stereo ones at it).
- **Out of a raid a thing's sound plays whole** on the interface's group (`GetCommonSoundsVolume`: 0.15 only in a raid): a recording at the CHARACTER screen is no measure of a raid's.
- **The screen recording keeps a limited range**: the game's and the app's black at about 14 to 16 of 255, the app's white of 216 at about 201. Dark rims measure lighter in it than in a capture.
- **TextMeshPro, as the game has it**: Bender Outline SDF has no bold typeface (a bold caption is made bold by the shader, `_WeightBold`, and spaced by `boldSpacing` 8), its spacing after every letter is 10 hundredths of the size (Bender Normal SDF's, 2); both materials use the full `TextMeshPro/Distance Field` shader (its outline always drawn); `_FaceDilate` is 0.04 and 0.15, `_Sharpness` 0 and 0.3. The spacing is a share of the text's own size (`m_fontSize`), not of a `<size>` run's. A line is aligned by where its last letter that shows ends (a trailing space not counted). The atlas's field rises a twenty-second of its whole for each texel inward (a half at the edge, eleven texels to each half), as the gradient's scale says. A font asset's glyph advances are its font file's at the sampling size to a half percent.

## Added 2026-10-16, evening (the sounds of moving)

- **A footstep is the body animator's `FootStep` curve changing sign**, the same moment the weapon's bob is retuned at: the game's `Run` and `Sprint` loops do both in one place. Two of the sounds' own rules set the time of the last footstep the bob measures from (a footstep played on leaving `Run` with none heard in it, a crawl's mark), so the sounds' rules run in the simulation, not the host.
- **SPT's `settings.json` names two of the player's figures as the game does not read them**: `MinStepSoundVolumeMult` 0.2 and `MinStepSoundRolloffMult` 0.5, where `ClientAudioSettings.PlayerAudioSettings` has `MinSpeedVolumeMult` and `MinSpeedRolloffMult` (no attribute maps them); the class's own 0.15 and 0.8 hold. Its `BaseMaxMovementRolloff` 90 is for others' hearing: in first person the game writes 70 and it cancels.
- **A turn on the spot to the left is silent**: the turn sound's share is of the average of the last ten frames' yaw change, signed, and the larger of it and 0 is taken (`GetAverageRotationSpeed`), so only a turn to the right is heard. The code's, kept.
- **A bank with two clips at once plays them at their shares only when both are there**: a list left empty gives no clip, and the one clip then plays whole (`SimpleSource.Play`). Concrete's run bank has its first list empty, so a slow footstep plays a walking clip whole rather than faded.
- **The bank's shuffle never moves the last place first** (`Random.Range(0, n - 1)` for whole numbers leaves its top out), and is kept for the bank, not the list: two clips simply alternate.
- **The steps' source in first person is the engine's 3D sound, not Meta's**: the `Character` group's spatial blend is 1, its source prefab rolls off logarithmically from 1 m, and the listener is the camera, so a standing step comes at about two thirds of its clip's level and a crouched or prone one whole. Meta's plug-in is only the room's reverberation of it then (`ReverbSimpleSource`).
- **The movement's 1,272 clips are all PCM or IMA ADPCM** (none is Vorbis), 1,133 of them mono at 44.1 kHz, 88 stereo, twelve at 39 to 40 kHz: 102 MB of 16-bit samples for all twenty surfaces.
- **The crawl's sounds are the clips' animation events** (`m_Events`: `PlaySoundBank` with `Prone`, at 0.40 s and 1.18 s of `Prone_0`'s 1.6 s), handed on by `GenericEventTranslator`; nothing in the player's code times them.

## Added 2026-10-16, evening, later (course 2: falls and barbed wire)

- **A recording's position is after the frame's move; the game's ground check reads before it.** A fall's landing height is the frame before's recorded height (6.6 cm higher than where the feet came to rest in a 4.3 m walk off a ledge), and with it the recorded leg damage comes out to the fourth decimal (12.436). The simulation's own movement already reads it so; only a replay from recorded positions has to take the frame before's.
- **Barbed wire's cut is the whole tally**: `ProceedBarb` does `_barbDamage` once it is 1 or more, then keeps `_barbDamage % 1`; the 67 recorded cuts are tallies of 1.000 to 1.064 (times the part's `EnvironmentDamageMultiplier`: limbs 0.5, stomach 0.17, chest 0.25, head 0.125).
- **A sprint into barbed wire ends when a hit collider is in it**, not when the capsule touches: the speed limit is the trigger's `AddPenalty` (12.111 s), the sprint's end is in `ProceedDamage`, which runs for the body's hit colliders (12.194 s, ten frames later).
- **The game's wire cut the stomach as often as a leg** (24 and 22 cuts) and never the chest or the head: its box reached the hips. Ours, 0.7 m high, is under the pelvis.

## Added 2026-10-16, the very last (the game's look drawn as its engine draws it)

- **Bevy rounds each UI node to whole pixels on its own**, so pieces that meet in the game's units do not meet on the screen at a scale that is not whole: at 1440 high (a third over 1080) a slot header's three pictures, measured after the layout, lay at 330 to 333, 332 to 501 and 502 to 505 where they are 330.00 to 332.67 to 502.00 to 504.67: the middle over the left end by a pixel and a pixel short of the right, the ground showing (a column of 79 in a plate of 29). `LayoutConfig { use_rounding: false }` lays a node out exactly; the GPU then fills shared edges without gaps.
- **Bevy softens every UI node's edge over a pixel** (`UiAntiAlias`, on unless the camera says off): two see-through pieces exactly side by side each fade at the shared edge, and the pixel between is lighter than either (a 95 between two grounds of 69). Unity's interface is not softened.
- **The game's textures say how they are sampled at their edges** (`m_TextureSettings.m_WrapU`, `m_WrapV`; 1 clamp): the app had built every texture repeated, as the world's materials want, so an interface picture's edge took in its opposite edge.
- **`PixelPerfectSpriteScaler` moves a picture as well as scaling its border**: its offsets from its parent's edges are divided by the object's scale, so its border lies so many screen pixels out at any resolution.
- **A `ContentSizeFitter` sizes an object inside a layout group on the axes the group does not control** (`childControlWidth` off): the quick-use header, kept at a saved 100 units, is 128.
- **A tiled image with a sprite border keeps the border whole** (as sliced) and tiles only the rest.
- **Bevy clips a node's children inside its border** (`OverflowClipMargin` defaults to the padding box): a border drawn as the node's own takes a pixel of room that the game's border picture does not.
- **The user's two recordings** (2026-10-16, `E:\stlkreft\SPT level 69.mp4`, 139.6 s, and `Real tarkov level 15.mp4`, 99.8 s, both 2560 by 1440 at 30 a second; `workbench.md`): SPT's gear tab at 30 s and its health tab at 105 s, the quick-use bar in play, the inventory with things dragged and dropped; the real game's inventory at 42 to 72 s (its interface larger than SPT's at the same size). Neither shows the menu of a right click or the window that shows a thing.

## Added 2026-10-16, later still (the shots' sounds; the game's mixer)

- **The sights lowered by the body make no sound; by the aim's call, the kit rattles.** The controller's `SetAim` plays the kit at 0.2 and, coming up, the weapon's aiming clip whenever it changes the sights: the key, an obstacle (through `ToggleAim`), a long fall, a vault, a climb or mounting (`ToggleFirearmAimByBodyAction`). A sprint switched on and a movement state that forbids aiming go through `OnAimingDisabled` to the operation in hand, which sets the sights down without `SetAim`, and are silent. The aiming's banks go by the weapon's kind (`ESoundWeaponType`): rifles, shotguns and grenade launchers share one bank, pistols and every other weapon another; the M32A1 sounds as a pistol (a revolver by its classes), the RShG-2 as a special weapon. Nearly every rifle aims at the volume's top (its ergonomics under 55), most pistols at its floor (over 68).
- **An automatic weapon's fire is one recorded loop, not a sound a shot.** The AKM's body bank is a 1.6 s loop of sixteen shots at its 600 a minute (`akm_close_loop`, `akm_distant_loop`, `akm_far_loop`), and its tail bank what follows the last shot (4 s close). The game starts the loop at the first shot, lets further shots only nudge its pitch, and ends it on the next sixteenth after the trigger is let go, where the tail begins (`WeaponSoundPlayer.FireBullet`, `StopFiringLoop`; `sound.md`, "The shots"). A single shot from the AKM is therefore the loop's first beat and the tail. Every bolt action, shotgun, pistol and revolver read (the SV-98, M3, MP-43, SKS, Rhino, Five-seveN) is `Non_auto`: each shot's clip whole, cut by the next.
- **A list of two clips is dealt in turn.** A bank's deck is shuffled by swapping each place with one drawn by an integer `Random.Range(0, n - 1)`, which never draws its upper bound: with two clips it always draws 0, so the deck is always [1, 0] and deals 0, 1, 0, 1 (the MP-43's two close clips alternate).
- **The game's mixer is in the install and reads whole** (`assets/content/audio/mastermixer.bundle`, type trees kept; `probe mixer`): 78 groups, 156 effects, three snapshots, the Outdoor one at the start. On the gunshots' way to the master every group is at 0 dB; the player's own movement's group is at -3.5 dB with two equalisers (the steps played 3.5 dB too loud from 0.36.0 to 0.39.1, as the spec had it "not read"); the master has a compressor at -1 dB, 10 ms attack, 20 ms release, no make-up, which keeps the game's full-scale gun clips from clipping. The `Guns` group sends three ways to the returns (`TarkovReverb` at -3.1 dB outdoors, `TarkovDelay` -30, `Reverb` muted): the reverb a shot gets in a raid is the mixer's, not the clip's. The effects' kinds fit FMOD's own numbering (16 compressor, 17 reverb, 3 and 5 low and high pass, 8 distortion, 10 equaliser on the groups that would have them; the compressor's attack and release at FMOD's least, 10 and 20 ms): my reading, not stated anywhere read.
- **The gun clips are mastered at full scale.** The AKM's close loop has 14,346 of its 141,119 samples at full scale and an RMS of 14,583 (7 dB under); its silenced loop is 6 dB quieter in RMS. A mix with nothing between the clips and the output clips them; with the master compressor, 4% of the samples of a magazine's fire reach full scale (the transients inside its 10 ms attack).
- **Which group the hands' sounds go to was not settled** (settled 2026-10-17, night: `Guns/Instrumental`, below): their sources are of the kind `Weaponry` (`BetterAudio.AudioSourceGroupType`), which is not a group of the mixer; the group a kind of source is sent to is set on the `BetterAudio` prefab, not read.

## Added 2026-10-16, the last of the night (what a frame costs)

- **What the work since 0.35.0 cost a frame** (the user: "Can we check to see how much performance we've lost/gained and if there are any areas we can improve on?"). 0.35.0 (built anew in `local\perf_base_s86`) against 0.39.0, uncapped (`--present off`) on Vulkan, three rounds in turns (`local\perf_compare_s86.ps1`): walking with the rifle, the inventory opened at frame 500, 2.21 ms a frame against 2.28 (+3%); the same with `TARKOV UI`, 2.91 against 2.94 (+1%); 48 rifles thrown from their case, median 2.63 against 2.68 ms (+2%; the 99th percentile 6.5 to 9.7 ms in both builds). The simulation costs about 0.05 ms a frame in both. `MOVEMENT SOUNDS` and `HANDS SOUNDS` off change the frame by less than the rounds differ, 0.02 to 0.03 ms (`local\perf_features_s86.ps1`). The largest working set of a run goes from 2.1 to 3.0 GB from one run of the same build to the next, too unsteady to put a difference on; the start is the same within 0.06 s.
- **Bevy's UI walks every node there is each frame, shown or not.** Its layout (`ui_layout_system`) goes down every root's whole tree to update the children and again to place each node, and its stacking (`ui_stack_system`) the same, whatever has changed; a node hidden by `Visibility` or by `Display::None` is walked as a shown one is. Taking out the F1 panel and the shut inventory screen (454 of the 929 nodes with `TARKOV UI`) saved 0.16 ms a frame, about 0.3 µs a node. Kept hidden until 0.39.1: the game's tab bar inside the shut inventory (257 nodes), the F1 panel's guide to the keys (147), the quick bar faded out or under the open inventory (255). Since 0.39.1 each is drawn while it is shown and not kept otherwise, as the gear page already was; the HUD's meters are written only when they change and the F1 panel's figures only while it shows (a node written is laid out again, a text written measured again). The quick bar with the install's `QuickSlotsVisibility` at `Always`, as the user's is, stays in play. Nodes: 354 to 207 in the app's look, 929 to 525 with `TARKOV UI`, 2099 to 1696 with its inventory open. The frame, three rounds in turns (`local\perf_ab_s86.ps1`): in the app's look 2.06 to 2.01 ms (-2.4%) shut and 2.36 to 2.25 (-4.7%) open; with `TARKOV UI` 2.40 to 2.26 (-5.7%) shut and 3.08 to 2.95 (-4.4%) open. The captures of the F1 panel, the inventory opened a second time and the quick bar are as before (`local\perf_shots_s86.ps1`); the quick bar raised by a change of hands under `Autohide` was not run.
- **What is left, by size**: half the frame is Bevy's drawing, and its own scans of every entity (the materials' `check_entities_needing_specialization`, about 0.14 ms a frame in the traced build, and the visibility's) are the engine's; the game's inventory open is still 1,700 nodes (its layout took 0.33 ms a frame in the traced build with 2,100); the sounds of moving are kept decoded, about 100 MB, where the install's ADPCM could be decoded as a surface is first stepped on; the thirteen Vorbis clips of the things are decoded at the start though nothing plays them yet (searching is not built); each rig decodes its own copy of a clip another rig has.

## Added 2026-10-16, last at night (Vorbis; the hands' sounds)

- **FMOD's Vorbis setup headers are in the game's engine**: `UnityPlayer.dll` holds 33, each starting with 5 and `vorbis`; a bank names its own by the header's CRC32 (zlib's), in its `VORBISDATA` chunk. The game's thirteen Vorbis clips name two. Read from the install, nothing of FMOD's need be kept.
- **FSB Vorbis decodes with block sizes 256 and 2048** in the identification header made again: each of the thirteen clips came out at exactly its own count of frames.
- **The using of medicine sounds in a raid from the item's hands, not from the things' bank**: the bank's `med_medkit_use` and the rest play when a thing goes into an equipment slot, or out of a raid (`offline_use`); in a raid the `UsePrefab`'s `BaseSoundPlayer` plays a clip for each `Sound` mark of its animator (the AI-2: Draw, Open, Take, CapRemove, Inject, Discard, Holster).
- **A rig's sound player is on the prefab's root**, above the animated rig the hands are built from (`item_medkit_container` over `item_medkit_simple.generated`): look there, not on the rig's objects.
- **`MainSounds` never play**: only `EventReceiver` plays them, and nothing in the build calls it; the handling sounds are all `AdditionalSounds` named by `Sound` marks (`Snd` before the mark's string).
- **The looting sounds play only during a search item by item**, from a source in front of the player, looping while the search lasts.

## Added 2026-10-16, latest at night (the menu of a right click)

- **In a raid a medkit's menu has no "Heal all"**: `HealAvailable` is health and not `Gameplay`. Only "Use" (and the double click) reach it.
- **Any button let go shuts the menu** (`SimpleContextMenu.Update`), and nothing covers the screen behind it: a press elsewhere goes to what is there.
- **The menu's line under the mouse is a dark red** (`_selectedBackgroundColor` 0.204, 0, 0 on an ordinary line and on Discard's; the wish list's a lighter blue), on the sliced `subbutton` plate; a line that cannot be done is at 0.3 (`SetUnlockStatus`), its reason in the simple tooltip.
- **The lines' captions are the locale's keys of the lines' names, whatever their case** ("TURNON" is "FLIP DOWN", "TURNOFF" "FLIP UP"); the reasons are keys of their own ("You can't disassemble equipped item" says "You can't disassemble an equipped item").
- **A capture's mouse buttons are heard a frame after they are written**: a right click tapped on the frame a scripted drag moves the mouse is heard where it moved to. Tap it two frames before.

## Added 2026-10-16, later at night (a double click on medicine, food or drink)

- **The game's window for a share of food or drink cannot go below what is gone**: its slider's track is the whole (1 to `MaxResource`), the knob may stand only from `(int)(whole - left + 1)`, and the figure it reports is the knob's place less that, plus one: so the figure runs from 1 to what is left, and the window opens at all of it (`IntSlider.CurrentValue`, `Delta` 1).
- **A double click on medicine never inspects it**: "Use" failing (nothing to help) falls to "Use All", which medicine never offers, and the click ends there with the game's warning.
- **"Use" is offered for any medicine**: only an item with a medkit's part (points, `MaxHpResource` above 0) is asked whether it has at least one; any other falls through to the cases that are always offered (`goto case EItemInfoButton.Reload`).
- **The surgical kits' own part comes only from "Use"**: a quick-use key names the common body, and so does "Use" for any item without a priority of parts or of the medkits' class (`BodyPartsPriority`, continuous healing off).

## Added 2026-10-16, late at night (the character as others see it, set beside the game)

- **A character seen from outside breathes with its chest**: `Additive_FullBody`'s `Breath`, played only with the switch thrown, moves the ribcage some 2 cm and the arms with it. A replay that starts it at nothing is 1 to 1.7 degrees off in the arms on every frame; started where the recording has it (the game's was 0.89 of the way through its cycle), the body's weapon bone is the game's to 0.000 degrees. **Start every layer from the recording**, not only the base one: a recording's scenarios run back to back, and the additive layers are somewhere in their play. In first person it changes nothing (the sixteen recordings byte for byte).
- **What the recorder writes of the layers is enough to take them up**: four numbers a layer, the low 24 bits of the hash of the state's own name (`shortNameHash`), how far through it, its weight, the state it is blending into or -1; the weight of the third (`UtilityOperation`) is the reach for the vest.

## Added 2026-10-16, night (courses 5 to 8: the AKM's keys, the world, from outside, the free look)

- **The free look is the code as read, to the frame.** The user's course 8 (38 s, ten presses, every limit reached, 990 frames of it aimed) is followed on all 4,552 frames by the input manager and `Player.Look` as written on 2026-10-13: the head's two figures to a ten-thousandth of a degree, the mouse's hold and the looking flag. **The first frame of each run of travel along an axis goes to neither the look nor the turn**: 235 of 1,951 frames' travel with the key held, a slow hand's travel lost as the code says. The look's axis up and down takes the engine's mouse axis the other way about (`LookY`'s pair in `Control.ini`), so up is negative.
- **The game's own left hand breaks 30 times a second through the look all over** (course 5, two recordings): at 120 frames a second its velocity holds for four frames and steps on the fifth (2.49, 2.46, 2.45, 2.42, then 2.22, 2.19, 2.18, 2.15, then 1.90 mm a frame), and through the fast turns, where the weapon's turn and the marker's place multiply, one frame in four still has the largest change. The simulation's path is the game's in kind; `SMOOTH FIRST PERSON ANIMATIONS` is a departure from it, as its spec says.
- **The state for working a thing with the hands times by the real clock** (`DoorInteractState`, `PickUpState`: `Time.realtimeSinceStartup` since they were entered), which runs with the player's whole-millisecond stopwatch, not with the engine's frame time: a lock opened with its key on the engine's frame time came a frame after the game's. The unlock's 1.6 s are counted from the frame after the state is entered.
- **A door may not be breachable at all.** Course 6's first door held a kick from the side it swings away from, where the code's rule opens it: so that door's `CanBeBreached` is off, which no recording writes. Kicked from the other side it held, as the rule says; a second door kicked from the side it swings away from opened, as the rule says.
- **A recording made from outside is posed by the outside rig**: course 7 has `point_of_view: ThirdPerson`, and its camera, weapon and arms are where the third person's are, 40 to 46 degrees from the first-person arms. Its animators' states are the game's on every frame as replayed.
- **A container walked away from as it is searched is told to shut**: course 6 walked 1.24 m further off within the 0.55 s; the lid and its state the game's on every frame.

## Added 2026-10-16, evening, latest (courses 3 and 4: the five kinds and the pistol)

- **The weapon's operations run on the player's whole-millisecond clock, its animator on the engine's.** `Player.UpdateTick` hands the player's `DeltaTime` to `ComplexUpdate` and so to `ArmsUpdate`, which updates the hands' controller and its operation; the animator updates itself. Seen in a pistol's queued shot (its 0.133 s cooldown out after 16 frames or 17 as their milliseconds came to 133 or 134), and then in the scripted recordings' automatic fire, whose round count it puts on the game's on every frame.

A recording by hand has the keys; the `Shoot` binding they give is the press. A click on an open screen is the same key and is not the weapon's.
- **A cocked revolver's shot starts at its fire state's 0.2**, as every single shot (`FireOperation.StartFireAnimation`), not by the animator's transition.
- **The recorded fire modes include `doubleaction` and `doublet`**, the revolver's and the barrels'.
- **A weapon switch keeps the controller's name** (`FirearmController` again, a new one): the operation goes `Remove`, then `SpawnOperation` for the other weapon.

## Added 2026-10-17 (recorder 0.10.0 run: `courses2`)

The user ran every course of recorder 0.10.0 on Factory (`local\traces\courses2`, 1.9 GB; read with `local\c2_s87`). What the run itself showed, beside what each course is for:

- **The recorder works as built.** Every line of every trace is whole JSON; every patch went on (BepInEx's log names none); every part of a frame was written where it applies; the check's three shots are three bullets with their hits.
- **The sound read off the listener is the whole mix.** Every sound file has sound in every 50 ms of it, the shots, the steps and the inventory alike (48 kHz, two channels, 32-bit floats). **It goes over full scale**: the AKM's automatic fire peaks at +1.5 dBFS (`sound.md`).
- **A death ends what there is to see in about a second** (`health.md`, "How soon the raid ends"): the game blacks the screen out over 1.05 s and lets the raid's world go. The recorder cannot follow the corpse further, and neither can the player.
- **`G` is the grenade's own quick slot, not a throw** (`QuickUseSelectorUtil`: `ThrowGrenade` chooses `EBoundItem.ItemG` as `PressSlot4` chooses slot 4, `PressThrowGrenade`, the key let go, takes it into the hands). The quick-throw controller (`QuickGrenadeThrowHandsController`) is made only for a player driven over the network (`ClientPlayer`); the local player's code never asks for it. Recorded so (`2-grenade-quick`): the grenade comes into the hands. The guide's line for it was wrong (recorder 0.10.1 says so).
- **A grenade's fragments are bullets of the thrower's**: each blast put 30 shots into the ballistics under the player's own profile (`bullets_new`, 60 for two grenades), each followed until the game let it go (51 of the 60 met something, at 45 to 86 m/s).
- **A throw's `since_safety` was 0 for all six recorded** (the time since the safety lever came off, which the grenade's fuse is shortened by); none was held before letting go (`2-grenade-kept` has a throw begun with both buttons and given up).
- **The optic's zoom is a figure of its own**: a variable scope changes the optic camera's field of view (29.5 to 7.375 degrees) through `SightComponent.ScopeZoomValue`, its mode and `GetCurrentOpticZoom` staying the same.
- **Recorder 0.10.0 watched too few keys** (`keys_down`): not the movement keys, `L`, `N`, `H` or `Page Up` and `Page Down`; what they did shows only in the state they changed (the helmet's toggles, the sight's range). 0.10.1 watches them.

## Added 2026-10-17, later (grenades and the knife built: 0.43.0)

- **A mesh marked skinned may have no bone weights**: the F-1's rig has two skinned renderers whose bones match the mesh's bind poses but whose mesh carries no weights; given a `SkinnedMesh`, Bevy's renderer meets a skinned bind group on an unskinned pipeline and the frame fails (wgpu: "`skinned_mesh_layout` ... is not compatible with ... `mesh_layout`"). A rig's mesh now goes on bones only where it has joints (`weapon.rs`, both paths). The AS VAL's and the VSS's hammer spring were the opposite case (joints and no bones: `render_data::unskin`).
- **A prefab may hide a part by its bones' scale**: the F-1's tripwire kit (`item_spec_mining_kit_peg`, `_wire`) is skinned to bones under `wire_root`, which the prefab scales to 0.01; the engine draws it a hundredth of its size. Drawn as it lies, unskinned, it stood beside the grenade at full size in its picture. A picture's and a thing on the ground's skinned mesh are now posed on their bones (`render_data::pose_on_bones`).
- **A mesh on one bone has no weights**: the F-1's tripwire peg keeps a bone's index for each vertex, one bind pose and no weight channel (each weight is the whole). Read with no weights, it was drawn as it lay, the rod beside every grenade's picture, which looked like the grenade's parts lying apart; they do not (the body's node lies 39 cm from its bone, the throw's `GrenadeItself` offset, but its mesh is drawn back at the bone). Now read with a weight of one (0.43.1).
- **Some meshes are quads**: the RGO's body, pin, ring and lever, and the tripwire's peg and wire in its prefab, have submeshes of topology 2 (`MeshTopology.Quads`), which the reader left out (a picture of a lever and a pin). Now two triangles each (0.43.1). Any other item drawn short for this has not been looked for.
- **A grenade's and a knife's container is empty**: their item prefab's `PreviewPivot` object has no renderer; the model is the weapon prefab's `Hierarchy`, as for a weapon (`ObjectsFactory.CreateItemAsync`). Before 0.43.0 neither had a picture ("nothing of it is drawn").
- **A press while the grenade is drawn is lost, in the game too**: the draw's operation does nothing with the fire input, and the key gives its command once as it goes down. A capture that pressed at frame 450, the button held through the draw's end, threw nothing; pressed at 520, it threw.
- **`G` goes by the equipment's top priority grenade**, which the raid screen's grenade view sets to the first by kind as it is shown; the random choice in `PlayerInputTranslator` is only for a player without it.

## Added 2026-10-17, evening (a magazine's rounds: course 3)

- **A magazine's round waits in whole milliseconds and moves on the next frame** (`Task.Delay(CeilToInt(time × 1000))`, continued on the main thread): at 120 frames a second an unload of 0.228 s a round comes every 28 frames (0.2334 s) and a load of 0.646 s every 78 (0.650 s), exactly as course 3 recorded. The first round of each came one or two frames later than its wait: the round is put in by an inventory operation the game completes a frame or two on, while the next wait has already begun.
- **The recorded character's skills are in the SPT profile** (`E:\spt\SPT_Runtime\user\profiles\<id>.json`, `Skills.Common`: `Progress` a hundred a level): Mag Drills at 4000, level 40. The traces' header has no skills; any figure a skill changes is to be read with the profile beside it (recorder: a header field for the skills would do).
- **The game's choice among a list of interface clips never takes the last** (`UIClipSounds.GetRandomClip`: `Random.Range(0, count - 1)`, an integer range that leaves its top out): of the seven load sounds and seven unload sounds, `ammo_load7` and `ammo_unload7` never play.
- **An unloaded round goes onto a stack of its round with room anywhere in the equipment before a free cell** (`UnloadAmmo`, 0xF: transfer first): with the vest's stack full, course 3's 30 rounds made a new stack in the vest; in the app's kit they top up the backpack's stack of 30.
- **A move of the weapon in hand's parts goes to its hands, a swap does not** (`FirearmController.CanExecute`, `CanExecuteWithoutAnimation`): one thing moved out of or into the weapon (its magazine, a round to its chamber, a part) is the controller's to animate and is refused unless its logic idles; a swap of two things touching it is made at once, with no operation of the hands to tell the animator. The menu's Reload on the weapon in hand is such a swap: no animation. Course 3 recorded the menu's Unload on the rifle in hand as `PullOutMagOperation` (the operation 0.058 s after the click, the magazine out of the slot at the hand's `MagHide`, 1.435 s after it began) and on the rifle on the back as a move made in 0.067 s.
- **The menu's Reload finds the old magazine's place before the swap**: the new one still lies where it was, so with the vest's first grid holding it the old one goes to the next grid of that size, not back to where the new one was.

## Added 2026-10-17, night (a magazine checked, a fold, the size rule: 0.47.0)

- **A thing's size: a place's own merge word does not make a thing look into it.** `CompoundItem.CalculateExtraSize` looks into a thing's places only if the thing itself merges (`MergesWithChildren`), and then into each place that merges (`ContainerExtensions.MergesWithChildren`: a place's `_mergeSlotWithChildren` true, or else the thing's word; `patron_in_weapon` never, `SlotSerializer`). The spec had said a place counted "if it says so itself, or else if its item does", which counts a place that says so under a thing that does not (90 such items in the install, all armour and helmets with plates: none adds a cell, so nothing showed).
- **Forced extra cells add up** (`ExtraSize.Merge`: `Left` and the rest by the largest, `ForcedLeft` and the rest summed; `ExtraSizeForceAdd` puts a part's cells in the forced ones: 120 items, most silencers left 1, some stock tubes right 1). The model had taken them as any other part's. **Measured**: the Model 700 with an AAC SDN-6 silencer on its flash hider, 6 cells in every `courses2` list that carried it (66) and 5 in the model; 6 now.
- **A fold**: the folded part's `SizeReduceRight` off its right extra (or, for the thing itself, a forced cell off), and the folded thing's `FoldedSlot` left out of the walk. Only a `Weapon` (`Foldable` or `Retractable`) and a `Stock` (`Foldable`) are made with a `FoldableComponent`; the app had taken `Retractable` on a stock too.
- **The fold is the item's, and one operation** for the key and the menu (`FoldOperation`: `Execute` turns the item's fold and resizes it where it lies, then `InProcess` asks the hands; refused there, the operation fails and rolls back). The menu adds the interface's `MenuStock` sound; the key does not.
- **A magazine check waits on `WaitForSeconds`** (a coroutine begun on the click's frame), not on `Task.Delay`'s whole milliseconds as a load's rounds; it ends any process running first, and shows as a load (`StartLoading(time, 1)`). The magazine also becomes known whenever an add or a removal leaves it empty or full (`CheckMagazineAmmoDepend` from `RaiseAddEvent` and `RaiseInOutProcessEvents`), and unknown after a shot that leaves it neither (`UncheckOnShot`, true in the install's globals; not built).
- **Recordings do not say a fold** (recorder up to 0.10.1): only a folded stock's own listed size gives it away (the M3's stock 1 by 2 for its 2 by 2). Recorder 0.10.2 writes `fold`.
- **The inventory's page was drawn afresh on every frame while open, from 0.44.0 to 0.46.0**: `work_magazine` took `carried.magazine_work.as_mut()` through Bevy's `ResMut` on every frame, which marks the resource changed even when there is no work, and 0.46.0's `settle` took the switches' list the same way. The page's own warning said so in every capture since (`the inventory's page has been drawn afresh on 120 frames running`); the cost was not measured. Both now look before they write.
- **A fold's view on a model not animated** (`FoldableStockView`, an `IDress` set up by `ObjectsFactory.CreateItem` with the model's own item): turns its named bones (breadth first below itself, a missing one passed over) to its folded or unfolded turn by that item's fold, at once on binding. 42 weapons of the install have such bones (`every_weapon_that_folds_has_its_fold_bones`). The M3's view is on its weapon, which has no fold (its stock has): it does nothing. The SR-3M's unfolded turn is 3.4 degrees off its model as made; the 9A-91's is written as all noughts. The SCAR-L's view names both colours' stock bones.
- **The SCAR-L's black build is 6 cells unfolded by the size rule** (its receiver and barrel each force 2 cells to the left; the stock 1 to the right) and 5 folded; the FDE build is 5. Not in any recording.
- **The tag window** (`EditTagWindow`, the item context's `_editTagWindowTemplate`, found by its script as the item window is): its field's most, 16, is TextMeshPro's `m_CharacterLimit`, stored after the selectable's trigger names (`Disabled` last), the interactable flag, seven pointers, the scroll sensitivity, eight settings, the regular expression (a string) and the point size (24); its validation `Everything` (-1, with the flags against a leading or trailing space set, which only flash an error); its caption's key `ADD/EDIT CONTAINER TAG`. The seven colour cells are made from a template (`ColorCellTemplate`) into a grid of 25 by 25, 15 apart (`GridLayoutGroup`: one row in its 360 width); the prefab also keeps seven cells of the same colours, off. The field's text is top left with margins 10 and 6.
- **A tag's plate** (`GridItemView.ResizeTag`): one frame after it is shown, the plate is the view's width with no name where the view's width less the caption's drawn width and 2 is under 40, else the name's preferred width and 12, from 40 to that room: so a one-cell magazine's tag is a strip of its colour with no name. The item window's (`InfoWindow.ResizeTag`) is the name's drawn width and 12, at least 40.
- **A round into the chamber** (`CompoundItem.Apply`, `Weapon.AllSlots` ending with `Chambers`, `SlotView`/`GridView.AcceptItem`): a round let go on a weapon that takes no rounds into a magazine of its own goes into its chamber as any thing let go on a thing; the weapon in hand's hands then load it (`Idling.Execute` → `LoadCartridgeToChamber`) or refuse it (a detachable magazine in, the chamber not loaded by hand: `isChamberLoad`, false for 12 weapons), the move undone. The AKM's animator plays `LOAD ONE` (`akm_ammo_in`, 3.767 s) on `LoadOne` from its inventory pose or idle, the round in at `AddAmmoInChamber` (0.885).
- **A weapon's view counts its magazine only** (`GridItemView.UpdateInfo`: never the chamber; with no magazine nothing, but a weapon loaded by its barrels, `OnlyBarrel`, which counts its chambers). The app had counted chambers for any weapon without a magazine.
- **A context menu line's icon box** (`probe contextmenu`): `Button Template`'s `Icon` is 16 by 16 in its 18 by 18 `IconPlaceholder`, `Wishlist Template`'s and `Dispose Template`'s 28 wide by 16 high, each `preserveAspect`; `ContextMenuButton.Show` sets only the sprite.

## Added 2026-10-17, night (a helmet's parts and its hinge: 0.50.0)

- **A worn thing's parts** (`ObjectsFactory.AttachMods`): its slots' objects are found by name below the model with the case of their letters ignored (`FindTransformRecursive(..., ignoreCase: true)`; the weapon's own part rule here, `weapon::attach_part`, finds them by the exact name, which every weapon so far has matched); a slot with no object says nothing only where it is an armour slot; an armour plate is never made. The ZSh-1-2M's armour plates' bundles (`armor_plates/helmet_top.bundle` and the rest) hold an empty object with a `PreviewPivot` and no mesh.
- **The ZSh-1-2M's `HelmetDress`**: `HingeTransform` is its `mod_equipment` (whose turn in the model, (-0.707, 0, 0, 0.707), is its `OnRotation`: the model is stored with the shield down); `OffRotation` (-0.948, 0, 0, 0.317). Its `HysteresisFilter`'s curves have infinite tangents, which the engine steps: forward 0 to 0.6 s then a straight line to 1 at 1 s; backward up to 1 at 0.4 s and flat after. Read with `probe dump <bundle> <path id>` on the two scripts (`probe scripts <bundle>` lists them).
- **Trap: `Assets::name_of`** reads the name only of an object whose first field is `m_Name`, which is most assets but not a GameObject (its first is `m_Component`): it gives nothing for one. A GameObject's name is read from the object (`read(...).get("m_Name")`).

## Added 2026-10-17, night (a grenade's blast on the character: 0.52.0)

- **The player prefab's hit colliders** (`BodyPartCollider`, `probe ragdoll`): 27, each with its part (`BodyPartType`, the game's numbers head 0 to right leg 6): seven on the head (eyes, parietal, back of the head, the head bone's sphere, both ears, the jaw), six on the chest (the top of the chest, both sides' tops, the neck before and behind, the third spine bone), six on the stomach (the second spine bone, the chest's lower sides and middle, the pelvis and its back), and one on each upper arm, forearm, thigh and calf. A blast's line goes to each collider's object, not its shape's middle.
- **The F-1** (`5710c24ad2720bc3458b45a3`): greatest distance 7, least 3, strength 80, contusion (1.5, 4, 15), armour damage (1, 4, 25), 90 fragments, no blindness, `explDelay` 3.5. **The Zarya** (`5a0c27731526d80618476ac4`): strength 0, both distances 0, contusion (1.5, 4, 20), blindness (10, 20, 40). `WallContusionAbsorption` (0.4, 0.01) in the globals.
- **By the rule as read, an F-1 kills within about 5.25 m** (where the head's colliders take 35, its maximum): blast damage is not reduced by armour (`ApplyEnvironmentalDamage`), only armour's durability is hurt.
- **Trap: a thrown grenade's flight in the app is keyed by its model's name** (`thrown grenade <template>`), not its item's template: `GrenadeBody::item` keeps the template for the blast.
- **The blast's effect** (`Effects`, in `assets/systems/effects/particlesystems/effects.bundle`, `probe dump <bundle> <its id>`): 35 named entries (`EffectsArray`); the F-1's `Grenade_new` has a particle mediator, a deferred decal and a sound bank in another bundle (`Sound`, file 2), no first-person bank, no light, no flash, no wind. `Effect.PlaySound`: a `Physical` bank plays distant (`PlayAtPointDistant`), else `LimitedPlay` or `PlayAtPoint` (`OneShotFullOcclusion`); a bank is not played past its `Rolloff`, its clip picked by the distance (`PickClips`), its volume and pitch drawn (`RandomVolume`, `RandomPitch`), the source's `maxDistance` its `Rolloff`.
- **Which mixer group each kind of source goes to** (`BetterAudio.method_5`, `FindMixerGroup`; this settles the item above on the hands' sounds): `Gunshots` to `Guns/Gunshots` (a `ReverbSuperSource`); **`Weaponry` to `Guns/Instrumental`**; `Grenades` to `Guns/Gunshots` too (a `SuperSourceDistant`); `Impacts`, `Character`, `Environment`, `Collisions`, `Speech`, `Distant` and the rest to `Main/Environment/CommonSounds`, but `Voip`, `NonspatialBypass` and `VehicleInSpeech` to their own. Each kind's preset is a Resource (`audio/audiogrouppresets/<kind>`, an `AudioGroupPreset`: `OverallVolume`, `SpatialBlend`, `SoundRolloff`, `DefaultMaxDistance`, the occlusion's settings; note "Weaponary" so spelled in the code's load). The groups made by `BetterSourceFactory` keep their prefab's own rolloff (only the non-spatial mixers set the preset's curve as a custom rolloff); the character's steps found the prefab's logarithmic one from 1 m (`footsteps.md`). Not read: the `SuperSourceDistant` prefab (its two sources and how it blends them by distance), which a grenade's sound plays from.
