## Added 2026-10-17, night (spent cases: 0.54.0)

- **A case is the round's own model** with its `shell` objects on and its `full` ones off (`AmmoPoolObject.SetUsed`). In the prefab the `shell` objects are off, and the app's model reader (`icon::model_from`) leaves off objects out, so the model came with nothing to draw: a case is read under its own key (`loose::CASE_MODEL`, `Worker::case_model`) with them switched on.
- **The AKM's shell port's forward points up** (the port at about 0.46 m down its parent, as the weapon's axes run), so the throw's second force (1 to 2) goes up: with `velocityMult` 3 and the flight's 1.5 times gravity a case rises about 1.2 m over the port and first lands 2.5 to 3.8 m from the eye. Read from the code and the install only.
- **`WeaponSounds` also has** the grenades' bounces (`GrenadeDropSoundBank`, the stun and smoke grenades' collisions), the loose things' impacts (books, cardboard, bottles, cans and more) and the vertical mount's sound, for later steps. The twenty shell banks all play from `Collisions`, sent to `Main/Environment/CommonSounds` (0 dB with every group above it).
- **A shot before the round's model is read throws nothing**; the model is asked for when the weapon is taken, so in a run the first shot or two may have none.

## Added 2026-10-18 (a magazine's rounds: 0.55.0)

- **A magazine's model has empty `patron_` points** (`probe tree <magazine's bundle>`): the 6L10 five at its lips (`patron_001` to `005`) and one at its foot (`patron_030`); the AKM's own magazine the same with `patron_029`. They are found breadth first, active or not; a point's round is the one as many from the top as its place in that list.
- **No spring moves**: `SpringMagazineVisual` keeps a spring animation it is never given (only `Reset` sets it, to nothing), so its `RefreshVisualParts` does nothing; `MagVisualController`, a stationary weapon's, is the one that sets a spring's clip by the count.
- **A picture's key goes by a visible figure** (`Magazine.GetMaxVisibleAmmo` over `VisibleAmmoRangesString`, 1 to 2 by default): the 6L10's `1-3` makes three rounds and thirty one picture, drawn at the count first asked for; a loaded AK magazine's picture differs from an empty one's only by a line of brass at its lips.
- **The weapon in hand's magazine item loses its rounds at rest** (`inventory_view`'s catch-up when the firearm can move a magazine), where the game's loses each at its shot: the points go by the logic's count.

## Added 2026-10-18 (a grenade's blast heard and felt: 0.56.0)

- **A grenade's sound from afar has its spatial blend at 0.9 always**: `Effect.PlaySound` passes `Mathf.Clamp(0.2f, 0.9f, distance / 30f)`, the value 0.2 first, which the engine raises to its least, 0.9. And it arrives late: `PlayAtPointDistant` delays it by the distance over 340 m/s.
- **The distant source** (`Audio/AudioSourceSuperSpatialBuiltinEQPrefab` in `resources.assets`, `probe resources AudioSourceSuperSpatialBuiltinEQPrefab`): two audio sources, logarithmic rolloff (`rolloffMode` 0) from 1 m, its 500 m replaced by the bank's rolloff. The groups' presets with their overall volumes are in SPT's `SPT_Runtime\SPT_Data\database\settings.json` (`AudioGroupPresets`: `Grenades` 1).
- **The force reaction's "hands position" is the camera's position**: `ForceEffector.Initialize` hands its values the camera's rotation, the camera's position and the hands' rotation for the targets it names camera rotation, hands position, hands rotation. The prefab's three values push the force into the camera's position, which a kick moves by about a third of a millimetre; the felt shake is the kick's turn of the camera's rotation spring (2.5, 2.5 and 5 times the power at most).
- **Effects' names**: the install's grenades name five effects; `cargo test -p trp-data --test install the_grenades_blasts_sounds_are_read -- --nocapture` lists each with its bank.

## Added 2026-10-18 (a contusion's tinnitus: 0.57.0)

- **The mixer's exposed parameters are kept by the CRC32 of their names** (`exposedParameterNames`, beside `exposedParameterIndices`, the value each moves): `probe mixer` lists what the game's names (`AudioMixerDataContainer`) move. `GunsVolume`, `MainVolume`, `InGame`, `WorldVolume`, `WorldLowpassFreq`, `OutEnvironmentVolume`, `MasterVolume` and `UIVolume` are there; **`Tinnitus1`, `Tinnitus2` and `ReverbVolume` are not**, so the tinnitus' sends and the reverb's volume the game sets do nothing in this install.
- **The tinnitus' curves** (hard settings): `TinnitusSound` up to 0.3 by 5% of the time, held to 25%, to nothing at the end; `MainChannelLevel` (read at 2.5 times the share) from 0 dB to −80 dB by about 18% and back by 40%; the clip 12 s of stereo, looped.
- **The Standard USEC loadout wears headphones** (`--loadout Standard/usec`): a contusion makes no tinnitus with it, as in the game.

## Added 2026-10-18 (a contusion's screen effects: 0.58.0)

- **The game's shaders can be read**: the `shaders` bundle holds 555 (`probe shaders [part of a name]`); `probe shaders <part> <folder>` writes a shader's decompressed blob and its DirectX 11 programs, each cut at its `DXBC` header, and the Windows SDK's `fxc.exe /dumpbin` (`C:\Program Files (x86)\Windows Kits\10\bin\10.0.26100.0\x64\`) disassembles them. The constants' names are not in the programs: each program's parameters in the parsed form (`m_CommonParameters`: a vector's name index into `m_NameIndices` and its byte place in the buffer) say which is which.
- **The camera's effects prefab** (`EffectsPrefab`, game object 4519 of `resources.assets`, named by `EffectsController._effectsPrefab` on `Cam2`): `probe resources "#4519"` (new: a game object by its number) prints each component's stored figures. The double vision 1.88 by 0.22 pixels, amount 1; the wiggle's timer 0, speed 1, scale 12, strength 1; the motion blur 0.8, no extra blur.
- **Unity's Standard Assets image effects are in `Assembly-CSharp-firstpass.dll`**, not the decompiled `Assembly-CSharp.dll`: `ilspycmd -t UnityStandardAssets.ImageEffects.MotionBlur -r <Managed> <firstpass dll>` decompiles one.

## Added 2026-10-18 (a flash grenade's stun and flash: 0.59.0)

- **What reads the flash's blindness**: `_BlindnessCoef`, set by the flash for every shader, is read only by `Hidden/VisorEffect` (the face shield's mask) among the `shaders` bundle's (`probe shaders --uses <property>`, new). The flash's own picture is `Hidden/WhiteFlash`, `c + coef × (1 − c)`; the rest of its look is the Prism tonemapping's values lowered by its strength (the app uses Bevy's tonemapping) and the eyes' burn.
- **The flash's curve** (`Cam2`'s `GrenadeFlashScreenEffect.WhiteBlack`, raw in `probe resources Cam2`): 0, 1 at 0.05 s, 0.215 at 0.172 s, 0 at 0.35 s, none of its keys weighted.
- **`Flash` drives nothing on the screen**: only the bots read it; the eyes' burn comes from `DoBurnEyes`'s event, and the white flash, the double vision and the tonemapping from the `Stun`.

## Added 2026-10-18 (the eyes' burn: 0.60.0)

- **A material of the build's own files can be read typed**: the screen module's reader of `resources.assets` takes a class's tree borrowed from any bundle that has the class (`tree_of(install, key, 21)` for a material) and reads the object by it (`ScenePictures::read_by`); its pictures by `ScenePictures::texture`. `probe raw resources.assets <number>` shows any object's words.
- **An empty texture slot is the shader's default picture**, by the property's default name in the parsed form (`m_DefaultName`): the eye burn's ramp is the engine's built-in `grayscaleRamp`.
- **The burn's spots combine by `Max`** (`BlendOp` 4 in the pass's state), so they do not add up where they overlap.

## Added 2026-10-18 (a hit and health's other screen effects: 0.61.0)

- **A fall is not a hit to the screen**: `HandleFall` gives its damage to health directly (`ActiveHealthController.ApplyDamage`), so it never reaches `Player.ReceiveDamage` and `OnPlayerDamaged`: no push, no blur. Barbed wire and a grenade's fragments do (`ApplyEnvironmentalDamage` → `ApplyDamageInfo`, whose `ReceiveDamage` is for any kind outside `WeaponDamage`), and a shot by `ApplyShot`.
- **The blends `EffectsController.Init` drives are copies it adds** (`AddComponentCopy` of the effects prefab's two `CC_Blend`), so they come after every effect the camera already has; the two blends on `Cam2` itself are never turned on.
- **The effects prefab** (`EffectsPrefab`, game object 4519) is where the accumulators' figures come from (`CC_RadialBlur.amount` 0.035, `CC_FastVignette` 74.9, the flicker of `EffectsCurveData`); `Cam2`'s own components keep other figures (its radial blur's amount 0), which `Init` does not copy.
- **Some of the build's pictures are crunched** (`TextureFormat` 28, `DXT1Crunched`: near death's `screen_neardeath_MULT`): one piece for all its levels, decoded by `texture2ddecoder::decode_unity_crunch` to colour (its pixels packed blue, green, red, alpha), its rows from the bottom as any of the build's.
- **A program's place in a shader's blob** is by its entry in the blob's table (a count, then an offset, a length and a segment for each), which the passes name: the programs taken in order did not match the passes (`probe shaders` writes `<name>_b<entry>.dxbc` since 0.61.0).

## Added 2026-10-18 (blood on the screen; gamma space: 0.62.0)

- **The game's project is in gamma space**: `PlayerSettings` (`globalgamemanagers`, object 1, read raw) has `m_ActiveColorSpace` 0 after `m_StereoRenderingPath` 0, placed by the fields round it (the default screen's 1024 by 768 twice before; `unsupportedMSAAFallback` 0 and `m_SpriteBatchVertexThreshold` 300 after). What the screens' blending showed (2026-10-16) holds for the whole picture: the camera's effects work in gamma-encoded numbers, a picture flagged sRGB is read as stored, a render texture keeps what is written.
- **Some of the camera's shaders are in `globalgamemanagers.assets`**, not the shaders bundle (blood's four): read by the shader's tree borrowed from the bundle (`probe shaders --build globalgamemanagers.assets <number> <folder> [depth]`). `probe build-objects <file> <class>` lists a build file's objects of a class; `probe typed <file> <number> <class> [bundle]` reads one by a borrowed tree.
- **Blood's `Start` mixes up its fields**: the drops' scales are set from the spread's (`StartXdistribution`), so every drop is the least size, 0.15; and the drawing's height is the screen's shape times the place's, which leaves many of a bleeding's drops (they start at the foot's edge) below the picture.
- **`Color.black` clears to an alpha of 1** (the drops' picture is cleared so before its drops are added).
- **Git Bash turns `/nologo` into a path**: run `fxc` with `MSYS2_ARG_CONV_EXCL="*"`.

## Added 2026-10-18 (the muzzle flash: 0.63.0)

- **A weapon's `MuzzleManager` is on its container**, the bundle's root, above the object the weapon's tree is read from (`WeaponPrefab._weaponObject`): read from the weapon's own bundle's scripts. Its jets' material is in a bundle of the weapon's dependencies; the jets' shader is `Custom/Additive` in the shaders bundle (`Custom/MuzzleFlash` is another).
- **The flash is no particle system**: a mesh of four corners at one point per particle, the shader turning them to face the camera at a size in the world (the projection's scales added to the clip place) and cutting the picture by a window that `MoveCurve` sweeps. Its `Chance` goes into the colour's alpha, which the shader never reads.
- **An additive effect added in Bevy's linear frame is far dimmer than the game's over a bright ground** (the game adds its numbers in gamma space): the flash at ten times its brightness looked as the game's. Drawn on the frame encoded for the camera's effects instead. The camera's depth is multisampled, so a pass after the main one tests against a copy of its first sample; the camera's `depth_texture_usages` must let it be read.
- **`Controller::shots_made` is taken by the inventory every frame** (its magazine's count): anything else counting shots reads `shots_fired`.
- **A capture run fires with `--hold auto+fire`** (the trigger two seconds in); with `--hold` given, a `--tap` of a mouse button is not heard as the trigger.

## Added 2026-10-18 (the gun smoke: 0.64.0)

- **Automatic fire keeps beginning the gun smoke again**: each shot adds 0.4 to a factor that falls only while the trail is on (0.5 a second), and at 1 the trail is cleared and begun anew; the trail grows only once the shooting stops, and by the muzzle's moving (a point a centimetre). Seen still, it is a sliver at the muzzle; turned, a wisp.
- **The trail's head starts at the world's origin** the first time (its place is never set before), and eases toward the muzzle by an eighth a frame.
- **`FastRndom.Int(input, start, range)` is a pure hash** (shift left 7 and xor, right 3 and xor, size modulo the range): the turbulence's draws are the game's own.

## Added 2026-10-18 (firing crashed with 17 weapons: 0.64.1)

- **17 of the 149 weapons' muzzles have no jets** (`MuzzleJet`), among them those built suppressed (the VAL, the VSS, the SR-3M, the VSK-94, the PB, the PP-91-01); whether the others' presets carry a suppressor was not looked at. The RShG-2, the StG 44 and the PD-20 have no smoke either (the list: `firing.md`, "The muzzle flash").
- **Trap: wgpu panics on an empty buffer's slice** (`buffer slices can not be empty`, from `bevy_render`'s `buffer.rs` in the render system), which ends the app, the window's surface failing after it. A buffer is never to be made or drawn from without something in it.
- **Trap: a change to firing checked with the AKM alone is not checked.** 0.63.0 and 0.64.0 were fired with the AKM only, and 17 weapons crashed the app on their first shot. `local\fire_every_s92.sh` fires every weapon in a capture run (`workbench.md`, 0.64.1); `every_weapon` makes each one's jets' mesh.

## Added 2026-10-18 (the StG 44's muzzle and magazine: 0.65.0)

- **Of the install's 7.62x39 weapons, only the SKS (both) and the RPD have their flash on the bare barrel**; the rest on a muzzle device (`probe muzzles 762x39`: every weapon's effect objects, their jets and smokes, and where each is from the fireport in the weapon bone's axes, in centimetres; a part of a calibre's name narrows it). In those axes x is across, -y along the barrel and z up.
- **A weapon of one's own's magazine was drawn as its donor's on the ground and in its picture**: a thing's model is made from its template's prefab, and the StG 44's magazine's template is made from the SCAR-L's (`custom-weapons.md`, "The magazine's object").
- **Trap: a node's parent must come before it** in a hierarchy: the app spawns a tree in order (`weapon::spawn_tree`), and the pictures' and loose things' code works out places in order. A node can be moved below another only if that one comes first: the StG's magazine's prefab is hung before the model's parts are made.
- **The run's first rig shared none of its pictures** (0.65.1 put it right): it was made on the frame's thread through the old way (`Converted`), where every later rig is made ready away from the frame, its pictures marked and shared (`SharedPictures`). So anything let go that draws with the first weapon's pictures sent them again: the StG 44's magazine 11.2 MB (its one material; its four had been 44.7 MB until `magazine_model` kept only what its part is drawn with), the AKM's too. The first rig is now made as the later ones are (`weapon::looks_of`, `looks_into_world`), on the frame's thread still: the StG 44's magazine let go finds its 3 pictures on the card and sends none. The weapons look as they did (captures of the AKM and the StG set beside those before).

## Added 2026-10-18 (the particle system and the muzzles' fumes: 0.66.0)

- **Where each muzzle effect is drawn** (`MuzzleManager.Shot`): fumes and heat haze are emitted, a particle at a time with the game's own values, into Unity particle systems all the weapons share (`Effects.MuzzleEffect.Fume`, `.HeatHaze`); sparks into the game's own renderer (`SimpleSparksRenderer.EmitSeg`); `MuzzleParticlePivot`s play whole particle prefabs at their object (`MuzzleEffect.CommonSystems`). A blast's effect (`Effects.Effect`) has its own particles (`ParticleSys` through the game's `Emitter`) and a `BasicParticleSystemMediator`, both only for a point of view in the third person (`pov == ThirdPerson`).
- **A particle system's every module is in the bundles with its type tree**: `probe dump assets/systems/effects/particlesystems/effects.bundle <its id> 8` prints them all; the fume's is 8852489164886100943, its game object "Effect Muzzle Smoke", its renderer -1846643144422863921, its material "Muzzle Smoke" 6284085635792115993. The `Effects` object is 1837122313713601487.
- **A particle material's shader is in the shaders' bundle**, which loading the effects' bundle does not load: load `shaders` first, or its name reads empty.
- **The fume shader's colour channels are figures** (`Particles/Smoke Distorted`): red the light's share, green the distortion's, alpha the alpha; the system's colour over life (from (1, 0.247, 1) to white) is a distortion growing with age, not a tint.
- **Its second pass and the `MBOIT_PARTICLES_ON` variants are for particles behind windows** (`WindowsManager`, `MBOITParticlesManager`), on levels that have them; soft particles are on at every quality (`QualityLevelPreset._softParticles`). The program types in a shader's parsed form: 16 a vertex program (SM 5.0), 18 a pixel one, 20 a geometry one (the fume's only passes its triangle on).
- **Two of its values are a level's** (`Shader.SetGlobal`): `_DirectionLightShadow` (`LevelSettings.DirectionLightShadowForUnshadowedShaders`) and `_Density` (`TOD_Scattering`: the scattering fog's height fall-off, height and density). The range has neither; unset, they read 0.
- **`FastRndom`'s tables are filled from Unity's own random numbers** (`Random.InitState` with fixed seeds, then `Random.value`, `onUnitSphere`): the engine's generator is not published, so they cannot be made again.
- **The AKM fires four fumes a shot**: one puff of 0.4 for 0.2 s and a cloud of 2 for up to 5 s (one shot in two) from `muzzleflash_000`, three to seven of 0.5 from its brake's side port (`muzzleflash_001`), and three small slow ones from the ejection port (`extractor_smoke`). The AKM's install test (`the_akm_s_muzzle_flash_is_read`) prints them.
- **The game leaves out a launcher's objects when it chooses the muzzle's effects** (`UpdateJetsAndFumes`: those with fumes under `mod_launcher`); `trp_data::muzzle::effect_objects` does not yet, which matters only for a weapon with an underbarrel launcher.

## Added 2026-10-18 (the muzzles' sparks and heat haze: 0.67.0)

- **A shader variant's own figures are not in its parsed form**: `m_CommonParameters` has only those every variant shares; the rest are in the blob's parameter entries (`m_ParameterBlobIndices` names them for each variant: the entries without a `DXBC`). Each figure there is its name (its length, then its letters to a whole word), five words, then its byte place in the buffer. The sparks' `TURBULENCE` variant's: `_TurbulenceFrequency0` 32, `_TurbulenceAmplitude0` 36, `_TurbulenceFrequency1` 40, `_TurbulenceAmplitude1` 44, `_FadeOut` 52.
- **A shader's program types** in its parsed form, those seen: 15 a vertex program (shader model 4), 16 one of model 5, 17 a pixel one (4), 18 (5), 20 a geometry one (5).
- **A muzzle's sparks keep their tables** (`MuzzleSparks`' eight `Values`, 128 each, worked out in the editor and stored): read as they are, not worked out again from their curves. A shot's count can be below one (the AKM's `CountMin` −2 and −5), and then none is emitted.
- **The render queues settle the muzzle's order**: the fumes' material 3000, the jets' shader `Custom/Additive` `Transparent` (3000, its material's −1 taking the shader's), the sparks' material 3003; the trails are drawn after the camera's (`OnRenderObject`).
- **The haze system simulates in its own space** (`moveWithTransform` 0, its object at the effects' origin); emitted places are the world's only with the effects' object at the world's origin, as taken. Its emitters on the AKM: one with every shot (`Chance` 1), one with a quarter. A haze's velocity is its object's place from its parent times 10 (about 2 cm for the AKM's, so 0.2 m/s) plus a ball's 0.4: the puffs drift slowly and widely.
- **The distortion values can be looked at**: a build whose composite returns the values (red and green the moves, blue the blur) in place of the frame shows them (`local\shots\haze_values_diag_s92.png`); the shader as kept in `local\distort_camera_keep_s92.wgsl`.
- **A heat haze is no particle drawn on the frame**: `DistortRenderer` takes the haze system's renderer off the frame (its material swapped for `Hidden/ClipAll`) and `DistortCameraFX` on `Cam2` draws it with its own material into a texture of distortion values (cleared to 0.5, 0.5, 0, 0) before the image effects, then, among the image effects (after `BloodOnScreen` and `PostprocessGrayscale`), blurs the frame at half size and moves it by those values. Its shader `Hidden/DistortCameraFX` is object 237 of `globalgamemanagers.assets`; the haze particles' `Particles/DistortAndBlur`. `Cam2`'s `DistortCameraFX`: `Distortion` 8, one blur iteration.

## Added 2026-10-18 (a grenade's explosion, its first part: 0.68.0)

- **A grenade's effect is played by copies**: `Grenade_new`'s mediator keeps five (`Effect Grenade Expl New (1)` to `(5)`), `Grenade_new2`'s five (`Effect SmallGrenade Expl`), and moves the next to the blast with `Play(withChildren: true)`; `probe particles <bundle> <id>` lists a copy's systems with their modules (the copies list the same). The effects' list as stored: `probe script assets/systems/effects/particlesystems/effects.bundle Effects 5 400`.
- **The flashbang's effect is no mediator's**: seven `ParticleSystemAdapter` emitters (the kind the impacts on each surface use) and a pooled light of 35 m. The database's `Grenade_indoor` is named by the grenades' class (`ThrowWeap`) alone; the smoke grenades' effect is a system of its own (`grenade_smoke_m18_green`, not in the effects' list).
- **Every explosion shader tests the depth and writes none** (`zTest` 4, `zWrite` 0 in each pass's state; `probe dump shaders <id> 14 400 | grep -A2 zTest`); `Particles/Smoke Bumped` culls back faces (2), the others none. Drawn on the encoded frame, which has no depth attachment, a particle is hidden by nothing nearer unless its fragment tests itself against the scene's depth as copied; the soft fade alone hid only the shaders that have one (read from the code; not seen over the weapon in a capture).
- **A shader's own figures by name**: its parsed form's `m_NameIndices` (the names and their numbers) is what the blob's parameter entries refer to; Smoke Bumped's sun is times `1 − _DirectionLightShadow`, which a level sets (`LevelSettings.DirectionLightShadowForUnshadowedShaders`; the range has none).
- **`Play` on a system already playing does nothing** (the 2022.3 scripting reference, the install being of 2022.3.43f1): a grenade's copy played again while its long smoke lives (10 to 30 s) is only moved. Whether a system set off by another (a sub-emitter) is played with its parent's hierarchy the documentation does not say: taken as not (`drop_dust`, the concrete's, a single burst as a collision's sub-emitter must be).
- **The renderers' sort modes** (`m_SortMode`): most of the explosion's 1 (by distance), the ash's and the long smoke's 3 (the youngest in front), the concrete's and the sparks' 0; the fumes' 0.
- **A capture of a blast**: `--yaw 90 --pitch 42 --tap 100:g --tap 520:lmb:120` throws the F-1 6.5 m; it goes off near frame 1410 (the line `the explosion … on frame N`); `--mouse 900:40:0:-6` after the throw raises the view 30° to have it mid-screen (a turn before the throw lengthens the throw).

## Added 2026-10-18 (research for what comes next; nothing built)

- **Unity publishes the symbols of this install's engine**: `UnityPlayer.dll` names its PDB `UnityPlayer_Win64_player_mono_x64.pdb`, id `BD9605D7A8BC4F21AF766D4707334ADC1`; `https://symbolserver.unity3d.com/UnityPlayer_Win64_player_mono_x64.pdb/BD9605D7A8BC4F21AF766D4707334ADC1/UnityPlayer_Win64_player_mono_x64.pd_` answers (3.4 MB, compressed; the `.pdb` name does not). With it, Ghidra names the engine's own functions: the particle system's rules this work marks `PROVISIONAL` (collision response, sub-emitters and `Play`, sorting, stretched billboards, scaling, the sheet's cycles, the shapes' distributions) and the point light's falloff can be read there. Ghidra and a JDK are not installed (nor Node, for REA); only ILSpy is (`local\tools\ilspycmd`).
- **Every built-in class's layout for 2022.3.43f1** is published (`AssetRipper/TypeTreeDumps`, `InfoJson/2022.3.43f1.json`): a level's own file (no type trees) can be read whole with it, its `RenderSettings` (the ambient light), light probes and lights. The game's levels are loose (`level69` is Factory by day: 422 lights, one `RenderSettings`); a level's scripts (`LevelSettings`, the sky's) need their fields' layout from the assembly, as `EFTHardSettings` has.
- **The game's own lighting and decal shaders are in the install** (`shaders`): `Hidden/Internal-DeferredShading` (how a light lights a surface, its falloff), `Hidden/Internal-DeferredReflections`, and the deferred decals (`Decal/Deferred DecalShader Diffuse+Normals` and others).
- **No test range of the developers' ships**: the developers' map's preset names `develop_ballistic.unity` and Arena has `Arena_Ballistic.unity` (`level163`), but both are stubs of 792 bytes in this install.
- **The ballistics are all the game's C#** (`EFT.Ballistics`: `BallisticsCalculator`, `Shot` (1,108 lines), `TrajectoryCalculator` with the G1 drag table, `BallisticCollider`, `MaterialType`; `ArmorComponent`): nothing of the engine's but the colliders' ray casts. `physx-sys` 0.8.2 has `PxSceneQueryExt_raycastSingle_mut` and `sweepSingle`, which the particles' collisions also need.
- **The recorder already records shots** (0.10.0, `courses2` `5-bullets`, `5-pellets`, in Factory): each shot's start, velocity, seed and the ammo's figures (`bullets_new`), and each hit's point, normal, collider, material, result (`StopHit`...), speed, age and path (`bullet_hits`); 21 shots of the AKM into concrete at about 18 m. Nothing yet at range, through a thin material, or into a body.
- **Ghidra reads the engine with names** (once the tools were installed): the analysis applies Unity's public PDB ("PDB Universal"), 1,354 functions with `ParticleSystem` in their names (`local\ghidra_particle_functions_s92.txt`; listed by `local\ghidra_list_s92.bat <text> <out>`, decompiled by `local\ghidra_decompile_s92.bat <names joined by +> <out> [depth]`, both on `local\ghidra_scripts`; Ghidra's own `ListFunctions.java` shadows a script of that name). Read so far: `ParticleSystem::Play` (a system marked as a sub-emitter is only marked playing; another is reset, its time to nothing and its start delay drawn, only from its stopped state), `MarkAllSubEmitters` (each sub-emitter's emission-stopped flag set, recursively), `IsPlaying`, `IsEmitting` (playing, the emission module on, and that flag clear). Field offsets' meanings are this work's reading of their use.
- **The explosion's next part, as stored**: the concrete's collision (`CollisionModule`: world, 3D, quality high, dampen 0.2, bounce 0, lifetime loss 0.05, least speed 0.17, radius scale 0.01, layers 0 and 12); its sub-emitter (`drop_dust` on collision, taking its duration); the `flash`'s lights module (every particle a copy of `Point Light` at 7.5 and 7 m, its intensity by the particle's alpha); the shock wave's shader takes the frame (`GrabPass` `_GrabTexture`) and has no `DistortRenderer` beside it.

## Added 2026-10-18 (the engine's checks: what Ghidra settled; nothing built)

The user: "do the checks" (rechecking what this work took from Unity's own engine, now readable with Ghidra and Unity's public symbols; `workbench.md` at its top; the decompiled text in `local\ghidra_*_s92.c`). Field offsets' meanings are this work's reading of their use.

- **The mouse's halving is the engine's** (`win::Input::Process`): the engine's mouse delta is the raw counts summed over the frame (`win::RawInput::OnMouse`) times 0.5 across and times −0.5 up (the vertical turned over), and a mouse axis is that delta times its sensitivity (`InputAxis::Update`). So the 0.125 degrees per count (0.05 × 5 × 0.5) stands on the engine's code, not on a post.
- **A transition cut short, its length** (`mecanim::statemachine::EvaluateStateMachine`, `EvaluateState`): while a blend is held, each frame adds to the new blend `(the overshoot past the exit + dt × speed ÷ L) ÷ duration`, held within 0 and 1, where L, for a duration that is a share, is the length of the state being entered (written by `EvaluateState` as it evaluates that state) over the speed of the state still reported (`ComputeStateSpeed`), and 1 for a fixed one. That is the body spec's provisional rule ("the share, of the state being entered, over the pace of the state held"), now the engine's. **A gap found**: the engine holds the blend on any cut (`AnimationStateMachineMixerPlayable::StartInterruptedTransition`, called for every interruption), where `trp_sim::animator` holds it only for a cut from the side of the state being entered and drops that state for a cut from the side being left (no recording has one).
- **A Direct blend tree** (`mecanim::animation::EvaluateBlendTree`, type 4): each child's weight is its own parameter's value (less than nothing taken as nothing), divided by their sum where the tree normalises its values and the sum is above 1, times the parent's weight. `trp_sim::animator` shares the weight equally: the body's controller (`Player_anim_controller`) has no Direct tree among its 1,131 (1,053 of one parameter, 39 simple directional, 9 freeform directional, 30 freeform Cartesian), so nothing drawn has gone by it.
- **The particle system** (`ClampVelocityModule::Update` and the drag and limit templates, `ParticleSystem::KillParticles`, `ParticleSystemRenderer::Sort`, `ShapeModule::Start`): the speed above the limit is brought toward it by `1 − (1 − dampen)^(30 × dt)` of the difference (the particle's velocity with its animated part), **as `trp_sim::particles` has it**. **The drag was not ours** (put right in 0.68.3): the speed less `drag × dt`, held at nothing, the drag times the particle's cross-section `π (size / 2)²` where multiplied by size and times the speed squared where multiplied by velocity; ours slows the speed by a share (`1 − drag × dt`) and multiplies by the size and the plain speed (no system drawn has a drag: the fumes' and every explosion system's are 0). **A dead particle's place is taken by the last** (so in this work too since 0.68.3; groups of four from the top, a group looked at again after any death), so a system with no sorting is drawn in that order, not oldest first as taken (the muzzle's fumes, alpha-blended, are such a system). Sorting by distance keys each particle by its squared distance from the camera in the system's space, by age by its life left; which way the sorted list is drawn is in the geometry's build, not yet read. The engine's own random numbers are a xorshift128, four lanes at once, from a system's seed (the recorder writes each system's seed and whether it is drawn anew). The shapes' code is long vector arithmetic: where a cone or a sphere puts a particle is left to recorder 0.11.0's particles, which measure it.
- **The sounds a spatializer places are Meta's** (`EscapeFromTarkov_Data\Plugins\x86_64\MetaXRAudioUnity.dll`, the Meta XR Audio spatializer, HRTF): a source the game marks `spatialize` (the recorder's sounds) is placed by it, not by Unity's or FMOD's pan; this work's equal-power pan is a stand-in for it. FMOD itself is in `UnityPlayer.dll` with its names (1,551 functions) for what is not spatialized.
- **Not yet checked**: the pictures' streaming formula and the shadow maps' sizes (`TextureStreamingManager`, the shadows' code), and the sort's drawing order.

The OVERALL tab's (`Camera_inventory`, level44's object 7805): 28 degrees, 0.3 and 25 m, off in the scene (`CameraImage` turns it on), culling layer 19, deferred. **Trap**: no bundle of the install's has a camera's type tree (the scopes' cameras are made while the game runs); read it by the layout (`screen::model_camera`).

The lights under it (eight objects) are not read: the model is lit by the gear page's `ESTIMATE`.

## Added 2026-10-19 (Factory's light and the camera's exposure: 0.70.0)

- **Recorder 0.11.0 writes everything it was built to** (`local\traces\courses3`, courses 0 to 4; course 5 not run): `shot_hits` and `body_hits` in the traces, a scene beside every trace, sound where asked, every key of the world's file; BepInEx's log names no patch that failed. The scene file is written once a step, so course 0's turns and corners give one scene each, not the light on each frame.
- **Factory by day is the rework** (`Factory_Rework_Day_Scripts.unity`, `level525` here: its weather, sun, level settings and environment manager); `Factory_Day` (`level69`) is the old map.
- **A level's camera is its own prefab where it names one** (`LevelSettings.CameraPrefab`, `CameraManager.SetCameraFromSettings`): Factory's (`Cam2_obshaga_zalupa4_DistantShadows_OFF`) has PRISM's exposure on and another filmic curve than `Cam2`'s, which has the exposure off; Customs' (`bigmap`) names `Cam2_obshaga_zalupa4`. The camera's effects read from `Cam2` so far may differ in a level's copy (not read).
- **The game's ambient is its own harmonics** (`TODSkySimple.CalculateSphericalHarmonics`, `AmbientLight.SetSH`), and `_EFT_Ambient` is them facing straight up; the engine's own (`unity_SHAr`) hold only the level's flat ambient made linear (0.03 / 12.92). Worked out from the install by the game's rule they are the recorded ones within two millionths.
- **The engine's harmonics** (`SphericalHarmonicsL2::AddDirectionalLight`, Ghidra with Unity's symbols): the colour times 16π/17 (the managed side has doubled it times the intensity), times each coefficient's basis and normalisation; the normalisation is data, and the bands' rule (basis × 1, 2/3, 1/4) is confirmed by the comparison. `AddAmbientLight` adds the colour to the constant as it is.
- **`glstate_lightmodel_ambient` is half the sky colour** (`RenderSettings::SetupAmbient`), kept as stored in a gamma project; the old vertex-lit shaders double it.
- **A surface takes the game's ambient taken to gamma** (`Hidden/WriteScreenAmbient`, the screen pass `AmbientLight` adds after the lighting): the harmonics' sum at the normal through Unity's `LinearToGammaSpace`, times the surface's colour. Taken as gamma figures as they are (0.70.0) the faces in shade were three to ten times too dim; the effects' `_EFT_Ambient` is the sum as it is.
- **Customs' light is of the same size as Factory's** (`0-light-outside`, `bigmap`, 09:37): the sun weaker (0.8), the harmonics from above about the same, sideways twice; its sky is Time of Day's (`TOD_Sky`: the `Time of Day/Atmosphere` shader on `Weather/Sky Dome`, a sun, a moon, space), whose shader's own figures the recorder did not write.
- **PRISM's kept brightness is eight bits** (`ARGB32`): held to one, in 255ths, so its 2.5% a frame stops within about 0.08 of the frame's brightness; and its blend is a share of each frame, not of a second. Its exposure is held to at most 0.9 (Factory's camera): on the range, whose frame is about 0.18 in the game's numbers, it never moves off that limit.
- **A product in gamma numbers through linear light**: encoding Bevy's linear frame by the sRGB curve gives a 0.32 surface under a 0.1 light 0.011 where the game's gamma product is 0.032 (the curve's straight part near black); a plain 2.2 power both ways keeps a product exact. The screen's own decoding stays sRGB.
- **The dome's cubemap's faces are stored top row first** (each face as the card samples it), so they go to the card as they are; Bevy reads skyboxes and environment maps by the world's way with z turned round, which is the game's way.

## Added 2026-10-19 (Customs' time of day: 0.71.0)

- **The build keeps a class's fields in an order of its own**: `TOD_Sky`'s auto-properties' fields (the cycle, the world, the atmosphere) come among the first, after its five enums, not where the decompiled class shows them (the sizes add up to the script's 2,340 bytes in Customs); the weather controller's wind, rain and cloud controllers are components (pointers), its clouds' remap an asset (`lighting.md`, "Customs by its time of day").
- **Curves may be weighted** (`ToDController`'s): a segment is then a cubic Bézier in time and value whose handles are each key's weight of the span along its slope (a third where a key does not weigh that side, which is the plain Hermite segment); read with each key's weights and mode (`trp_data::tod::WeightedCurve`).
- **The weather's cloudiness is not a field, but the recorded contrast's top gives it**: `WeatherController` sets `ContrastMinMax.y` to 1.5 less half the cloudiness (held between −1 and 0.4) on every frame, so the recorded 1.5974 is a cloudiness of −0.1949; the job's cloudiness is the weather's turned round.
- **The sky's shaders draw about the camera whatever the dome's place**: `Time of Day/Atmosphere`'s vertex shader takes no matrix of the object's, only the view's turn and the projection with its depth at the far end; the sun's adds the camera's place to its own and does the same. Customs' dome stays at the world's middle (nothing moves it: the player's camera has no `TOD_Camera`), which the light's turn confirms (`Quaternion.LookRotation(dome − sun)` with the sun's place one from the middle matches the recorded light to 5e-5).
- **A mesh of the build's own files is read without a type tree** by the engine's order of a mesh's fields (`trp_data::screen::build_mesh`: the name, the parts with their bounds, the blend shapes, the bones, four flags, the index format and buffer, the vertex count, the channels four bytes each, the vertex data). Customs' sky's `Quad` spans −1 to 1 (the sun's disc is twice its scale across), its `Atmosphere` an `Icosphere` of radius one, 2,562 vertices and 5,120 triangles, positions, normals, tangents and one set of coordinates 48 bytes a vertex.
- **The recorder keeps none of the sky's shader figures** but `TOD_LightDirection` (its globals are a list): the ground colour, the sun's mesh's colour, contrast and brightness, the moon's halo's power are from the code alone. Worth adding to the next recorder, with a capture of the screen.
- **Customs' run-time render settings**: the flat ambient black and the engine's harmonics nought, as its level settings have them; the fog on, exponential squared at 0.01, its colour the game's (0.101, 0.110, 0.102) where the level settings have (0.525, 0.412, 0.322). The sun's light carries a cookie, `Cloud Shadows`, 500 m across (the clouds' shadows).

## Added 2026-10-19 (the camera's colour filter: 0.72.0)

- **"Jason" is the camera's own filter, not the player's**: `CC_Vintage` is switched on in Customs' camera prefab, Factory by day's and `Cam2`, the filter "Jason" (10) at 0.498, its table `Resources/Instagram/Jason` (`resources.assets` 1374, RGB24, 512 square, repeated, bilinear); nothing in the game's code touches the component. The player's Post-FX colour filter is another (`PostFxSettingsGroup.ColorFilterType`, default `None`, under `EnablePostFx`, default off), drawn by `GradingPostFX`, off in every shipped camera and in the recording. The user's `PostFx.ini` (`SPT_Runtime\user\sptSettings`) names "Jason" at 50 with Post-FX off. No graphics mod is installed (`BepInEx\plugins`: SPT's and the recorder).
- **A lookup filter's table is read at a turned-round second coordinate** (`Hidden/CC_LookupFilter`: `v = −(row/8 + …)`), which a repeated texture makes one less it: the tiles' rows count from the table's top.
- **A higher sun is a dimmer one on Customs** (`ToDController.LightColor` by the sun's height): the light's colour times its intensity falls from (0.73, 0.63, 0.40) at 09:37 to (0.53, 0.48, 0.32) at 14:00, the same warmth (lighting spec, "On the range").
- **The sky's scattering over the scene** (`TOD_Scattering`, on in the recorded camera, an opaque image effect): its density 0.0041, height falloff 0.025 and zero level −1.6 (the level settings'; its own 0.029 and 15), its glow 0.9755; the weather sets its density only in its debug mode (`WeatherController.ControlScattering`). **Its MBOIT switch**: shipped off, turned on by the weather as the camera is made where the level has fog remap data (`OnCameraChanged`), which hands the fog to `MBOIT_Scattering` only while `WindowsManager` is active: in the raid recorded `TOD_Scattering` ran and `MBOIT_Scattering` was off. **`_StencilShadow`** (`AmbientLight.DrawStencil`) is cleared to nought each frame and drawn into by the indoor volumes (`StencilShadow`): outdoors it leaves the scattering whole. Its dithering (`bayer_matrix`) is `Alpha8`, which `decode_rgba` does not read (its bytes are the alpha).
- **The scene's depth was copied only while an effect showed** (`muzzle_view::prepare`'s views: a muzzle's jets, smoke, fumes, sparks, haze or an explosion): a pass that reads it at rest needs its view among them (`ScatteringScreen` since 0.73.0).

## Added 2026-10-19 (a brighter range: 0.75.0)

- **Under the camera's exposure a lighter surface darkens the rest**: the exposure is the middle grey over the kept brightness, at most 0.9, so a frame measured above about 0.256 is turned down. The range's start view measured 0.25 with its olive ground and 0.44 with a white one; the brightest picture is the one whose frame sits just under that point (lighting spec, "On the range").
- **The camera's kept brightness can be read off a capture**: drawn, for the test alone, into a corner by PRISM's main pass (the brightness, and the multiplier beside it), each pixel over 255 is the game's number (the frame goes back by the sRGB curve the screen encodes it by). Eight bits: within about 0.08.
- **An eight-bit kept brightness makes the exposure depend on the first frame**: blended by 2.5% a frame it stops where a step would move it less than half of a 255th, up to 0.08 short; the app's first frame, drawn while the range was still being made, gave the same view an exposure of 0.89 in one run and about 0.7 in another. Copied through the warm-up since 0.75.0.
- **The built-in inserts are armour plates in the class tree** (`BuiltInInserts` under `ArmorPlate`): a test of `ArmorPlate` takes 386 items, of which 39 are plates.

## Added 2026-10-19 (the range hall: 0.76.0)

- **Indoors, the game takes the sky's ambient away by volumes drawn on the screen** (`AmbientLight.DrawStencil`, `Hidden/StencilShadow`): each indoor volume's mesh writes (nought, its `FogAttenuation`, nought, its alpha) into `_StencilShadow`, and the screen's ambient passes (`WriteScreenAmbient`, `AmbientHighlihgt`, so spelled) keep one less the alpha of the sky's ambient and of its reflection. The volume's colour is not used. Customs' concrete warehouse (`sklad_concrete`) has an alpha of 1 and a fog attenuation of nought: all the sky's light gone inside, the scattering left whole.
- **Customs' indoor triggers** (`EnvironmentManager`'s, 79 of them): 71 share a middle grey of 0.14, a speed of 4 and a fade of 1 s, the figures Factory's main hall was recorded with; `SetTriggerForPlayer` sets the targets and, where the environment changes, puts the smoothing's velocities to nought.
- **Bevy 0.18 counts a reflection probe in each cluster it reaches but never writes its index there**: the probe is not carried into the render world, and only point and spot lights and decals are given an index among the clusters' objects (`GlobalClusterableObjectMeta::entity_to_index`, read by `prepare_clusters`), so the cluster's count of probes says one where its list holds none. A fragment reads the index after its cluster's own: the next cluster's first light's, or nothing (nought). With no lights in view it read nought and found the first probe; with the hall's lamps it read a lamp's index and found an empty probe, whose zero matrix holds every place and whose intensity of nought took all the ambient away: the closed doors black from the yard, the wall outside lit in screen tiles, half the hall's floor wrongly lit. Several probes behaved as one (only the first was ever found), which is why the hall was made one box. **Mended in the app** (`lighting::HallProbePlugin`): the probe is marked to be carried into the render world and given its index (its place in the view's list of probes, the first) after `prepare_lights` empties the table each frame. The cluster settings tried first (a far end to 52 m) made no difference and were taken out.
- **Bevy 0.18 clusters a probe by its sphere but lists it for a view by its box** (the same session, 0.76.1; the user's capture from the yard: "an orange glow that appears around the world"): `assign_objects_to_clusters` takes a reflection probe's bounding sphere (the half diagonal of its scale: 25 m about the hall's middle), the gathering of a view's probes its box against the frustum (`LightProbeInfo::frustum_cull`). Looking away from the hall from within the sphere, the clusters named a probe the view's list had not, and the empty entry read in its place took every outdoor face's sky ambient away: the faces in shade black, the ground lit by the warm sun alone. Mended in `lighting::HallProbePlugin` (`clear_hall_probe`: the probe taken out of the clusters of a view whose frustum misses its box, by Bevy's own test, after the clusters are assigned). Seen: `local\shots\glow_yard_away_before_s99.png` (the user's picture) and `glow_yard_away_s99.png` (mended).
- **Trap: the doors' leaves lie along the door wall's inside face**, so a volume taken a little into the walls swallows them, and their face toward the yard lost the sky's light; the hall's volume goes only 2 cm past that face.
- **The range's labels showed through walls**: drawn by distance alone (6 m), the storage's showed from the stairs outside; since 0.76.0 a label shows only on its own side of the hall's walls.

## Added 2026-10-19 (the range in Blender: 0.77.0)

- **Blender 5.2's glTF round trip keeps what the level file needs** (run headless, `--factory-startup -b`, `local\blender_roundtrip_s99.py`): every node comes in as an object of its name (a mesh, or an empty for a node without one), the hierarchy kept, the axes turned from glTF's y-up to Blender's z-up and back (glTF's (x, y, z) is Blender's (x, −z, y)); on export each mesh's positions carry their `min` and `max` (glTF requires them), so a box's bounds are read from the JSON alone; materials keep their names and base colours (linear); a copy is named with `.001`. Its export writes `glTF Binary` by default; the "embedded" `.gltf` is gone from its choices.
- **Blender's headless runs touch none of the user's settings** with `--factory-startup` (and `read_factory_settings(use_empty=True)` for an empty scene); its own Python runs the script, none of the system's.
- **In Blender one moves what one sees** (the user's first level, 0.77.1): the sampler's squares were moved and stretched one by one, and a start moved by its arrow, not its empty; the props with many parts (the barbed wire) were moved by their empties. Since 0.77.1 the squares and the arrows are read as they lie; a prop's other parts still are not.
- **A cube added in Blender has no material**: its primitive names none, and glTF's default material is white.
- **Two of Blender's wall pieces were edited in Edit mode** (44 and 52 vertices): extruded, solid, read rightly by their bounds; a hole cut in a mesh would not be (its box is whole).
- **Trap: `cargo test` does not build the app's `trp.exe`**: a capture after a test run alone runs the binary of the last `cargo build` (a lamp-matching fix seemed not to work).
- **Trap: a group's name can begin as a kind's does** (`lamps` and `lamp`): the kinds are matched by a whole first word.

## Added 2026-10-19 (ambient occlusion, anti-aliasing and the upscalers read again; nothing built)

The user: "What about adding ambient occlusion and FSR/DLSS? Can we do that now?" (after the research of 2026-10-16, above).

- **The user's `Graphics.ini` has changed since 2026-10-16** (read, never written): `Ssao` `FastestPerformance`, `AntiAliasing` `TAA_Low`; `DLSSMode`, `FSR2Mode`, `FSR3Mode` and `SuperSampling` off; `Sharpen` 0.6, `SSR` `Low`. On 2026-10-16 it said `HighQuality`, no anti-aliasing, DLSS Quality.
- **The level's camera carries all of them** (`probe fields sharedassets525.assets ""`, Customs' camera prefab): `HBAO`, Unity's cinematic `AmbientOcclusion`, `SSAOMask`, `ContactShadows`, `SSAA` with its two propagators, `Antialiasing` and the post-processing layer.
- **The SSAO setting is HBAO, with a second effect from `HighQuality` up** (`CameraManager.SetSSAO`): `HBAO` is on for every setting but Off, with the preset of the same name (`HBAO_Core.ApplyPreset`: `FastestPerformance` the lowest quality, radius 0.5, at most 64 pixels, the extra-wide blur at half size; `FastPerformance` low, 1, 64, wide, half size; `HighQuality` high, 1.46, the default 128, narrow; `HighestQuality` the highest, 1.46, 256, narrow; `ColoredHighestQuality` the same with colour bleeding). From `HighQuality` up, Unity's cinematic `AmbientOcclusion` is on too, at its low sample count. What every preset leaves at the default: after the lighting, no deinterleaving, full resolution, bias 0.05, intensity 0.75, normals from the G-buffer. Not read: what each quality's pass does (its directions and steps), the blur's kernels and how the result is laid over the picture, all in the compiled shader `Hidden/HBAO`; the prefab's own HBAO fields.
- **`ContactShadows` is Keijiro Takahashi's published effect** (its fields are his: rejection depth, sample count, temporal filter, contrast, downsample, noise textures; it draws into a light's screen-space shadow mask). No code of the game names it, so whether it draws rests on the prefab: the component on, and a light in its `_light`. Not read. The entry of 2026-10-15 above took the game's contact shadows to be its `Ssao`; that may not hold.
- **TAA is the post-processing layer's** (`CameraManager.SetAntiAliasing`): `TAA_Low` and `TAA_High` turn on the layer's temporal anti-aliasing at BSG's low or high quality (`SetLowQuality`, `SetHighQuality`) and the camera's motion vectors; `FXAA` the layer's FXAA. DLSS, FSR 2 and FSR 3 each run through the same TAA with its resolve pass off and the camera jittered (`SSAA.UseJitter`); DLSS also sets `SSAA`'s resampling to its mode's (`ComputeOptimalResamplingFactor`).
- **The upscalers are in BSG's own build of Unity's post-processing** (`Unity.Postprocessing.Runtime.dll`, not decompiled yet): `SSAA`, `SSAAImpl`, `DLSSWrapper` (through `DLSSImporter.dll` and `nvngx_dlss.dll` in `Plugins\x86_64`), `FSRWrapper`, `FSR2Wrapper` and `FSR3Wrapper`. FSR runs as compute shaders of the game's own (`FSR3AccumulateShader`, `FSR3LockShader`, `FSR3DepthClipShader`, `FSR3ReconstructPreviousDepthShader`, `FSR3ComputeLuminancePyramidShader`, `FSR3AutogenReactiveShader`, `FSR3TCRAutogenShader`, `FSR3RCAS2Shader`): AMD's FidelityFX passes, whose source AMD publishes under the MIT licence. The game also has FSR 1 (`CameraManager.SetFSR`: ultra quality, quality, balanced, performance), which the user's file does not name.
- **This machine's card is an RTX 3080** (`nvidia-smi`), and the app draws through Vulkan by default: the two things Bevy's DLSS needs to run.

## Added 2026-10-19 (the game's ambient occlusion: 0.79.0)

The user: "Can we just do ssao and then just do performance passes?" (`decisions.md`). Built as `docs/spec/ambient-occlusion.md` says.

- **A shader's programs can lie in a later segment of its blob.** `Hidden/HBAO`'s first segment is its table alone (1,674 entries, each a place, a length and a segment), and every entry names the second; `probe shaders` unpacked the first only and wrote no program. It now unpacks every segment and writes each entry whole (`<name>_b<n>.entry`), a program's also as its `.dxbc`. **A variant's own parameters** (its textures and the camera's buffers: `_CameraDepthTexture`, `_NoiseTex`, `_CameraGBufferTexture2`, `_ProjectionParams`, `_ZBufferParams`, `_WorldToCameraMatrix`) are an entry of their own, named by the program's `m_ParameterBlobIndices` at the variant's place in `m_PlayerSubPrograms`; the parameters every variant shares are in the parsed form's `m_CommonParameters`, their names by the pass's `m_NameIndices` (`local\blob_params_s92.pl <entry>` lists either).
- **The recorded camera ran `HBAO` at the user's preset** (recorder 0.11.0, `courses3\0-light-outside`): `FastestPerformance`, every field as `ApplyPreset` leaves it (`trp-sim/tests/hbao.rs`); `_HBAOTex` the screen's size (2,560 by 1,440); the camera deferred, HDR, no MSAA, near 0.03, far 10,000, its depth modes `Depth, DepthNormals, MotionVectors` (the last for the user's `TAA_Low`). The cinematic `AmbientOcclusion` off (intensity 3.32, radius 0.02, `cutOffDistance` 10, which Unity's published effect has not: BSG's), `ContactShadows` off with no light and no samples: **the game draws no contact shadows**, whatever the entry of 2026-10-15 above took its `Ssao` to be.
- **The noise is of bytes**: its texels hold a turn's cosine and sine as they are set, so a negative cosine is kept as nought. With three directions (the user's preset) one texel of the sixteen is so (the ninth number, 0.778313, a turn of 93.4°); with four and more, none.
- **The depth in two bytes against a far plane of 10,000 m**: one step of the stored depth is 15.4 cm, which the blur at sharpness 8 weighs at `2^−(0.154 × 8)²` = 0.35. The blur's taps between two steps of a surface's depth are weighed down so: the game's own, kept by keeping its textures' bytes.
- **Bevy 0.18's prepass and a material drawn as its shadow alone** (`bevy_pbr` 0.18.1, `prepass/mod.rs`, `fragment_required`): a material's own prepass fragment shader runs in the camera's prepass whenever it writes normals, and in a light's shadow pass only for a masked material or where the depth clamp is emulated. `shadow_only_prepass.wgsl` keeps no pixel under `NORMAL_PREPASS` and does Bevy's own otherwise: the first-person body leaves nothing in the occlusion and its whole shadow on the ground (`local\shots\ao_shadowy_t90_s102.png`).
- **Bevy's normal prepass is the game's G-buffer normal's kind**: world normals with their normal maps, `n × 0.5 + 0.5` in ten bits a channel (`Rgb10a2Unorm`, Unity's `ARGB2101010`).
- **The game's words for the setting** are in the install's texts (`ESSAOMode/Off` "off" to `ESSAOMode/ColoredHighestQuality` "colored ultra", `SSAO:`); `Contact SSAO:` is there too, and no code of the game names it.

## Added 2026-10-19 (what a frame costs, again: 0.80.0)

The user, of DLSS and FSR: "im just looking for bonus performance gains. Can we just do ssao and then just do performance passes?" (`decisions.md`). The light run (`--start lanes --equip --hold w`, uncapped on Vulkan, frames 400 to 1509, three rounds in turns: `local\perf_variants_s102.ps1`) had gone from 2.2 ms a frame at 0.39.1 to 3.6 at 0.78.0 and 3.8 with the ambient occlusion.

- **The frame is the processor's, not the graphics card's**: the light run at 800 by 450, 1,600 by 900 and 2,560 by 1,440 takes 4.0, 4.2 and 3.8 ms (two rounds each). Neither more pixels nor the ambient occlusion's passes move it; what does is the engine's work for each entity and each pass.
- **Where it went** (a traced build, `--features profile`, summed by `chrome_sum`): with the renderer on the main thread, the frame is the main app's schedules (2.3 ms traced) and then the renderer's (2.8 ms); in the renderer, the passes' encoding (the prepass, the opaque pass and three shadow cascades, each 0.25 to 0.35 ms), the submission (0.4), the extraction; in the main app, transforms, visibility (`reset_view_visibility`, `check_visibility`, `check_dir_light_mesh_visibility`), bounds and the materials' checks, each over every mesh there is. The app's own systems are small: the simulation's step 0.22 ms, the rest under 0.03 each.
- **2,483 of the scene's 2,846 meshes were hidden and never drawn** (`TRP_CENSUS=<frame>`: the world's census at that frame): the body, its copy on the gear page and the corpse each carry a mesh for every piece of clothing and every thing the run could wear (835, 829, 829), which since the storage cabinets of 0.74.0 is one of every rig, armour, helmet, mask, headset and backpack in the game; the corpse's and the model's skeletons with them (5,991 entities placed in the world). The engine goes over each, shown or not, every frame. Despawning them at frame 300 (`TRP_DROP_HIDDEN`, an experiment) saved 0.40 ms a frame: the corpse's 0.16, the model's 0.16, the body's hidden pieces 0.20, by groups (`TRP_DROP_GROUP`).
- **Parked, not despawned** (`park.rs`): a hidden piece is disabled with Bevy's `Disabled`, with all under it, and enabled the frame it is shown. Meshes the engine goes over: 369 of 2,846; entities placed: 1,001 of 5,991. **The frame: 3.78 to 3.38 ms** (three rounds, `TRP_NO_PARK` for the other). What is drawn is the same: seven still scenes (the hall, gear seen from outside, the side view, the gear page's model, the OVERALL tab, a chosen suit, the corpse after `F8`) differ with it on no more than two runs without it differ from each other (`local\park_ab_s102.ps1`); the first-person body's whole shadow is on the ground.
- **What disabling takes in Bevy 0.18**: the transform's and the visibility's propagation skip a disabled child (`iter_many`, `get(...).ok()`), and no view lists a disabled mesh to draw, so it is gone the frame it is disabled. But change detection counts on the engine's systems seeing a change, and none sees a disabled entity: a mesh disabled before the engine first saw it was never readied for drawing (`entity_specialization_ticks` empty: the corpse's first frame panicked in `specialize_material_meshes`), and a material swapped while disabled would not reach the renderer. So whatever the engine takes is marked changed as an entity comes back (`park::Touched`), and every system that keeps a parked entity's state sees it (`Allow<Disabled>`): its visibility, its material (`shadow_only::apply`), its layers, a helmet's hinge, the copies that follow it.
- **Not parked: the gear page's model as a whole.** Its shown pieces count for the pictures' streaming (`streaming::stream` goes by what is shown), and in first person they are what keeps the upper body's pictures at their full size (the body's own upper half is drawn by a shadow-only material, which streaming does not count): parked, the model's next drawing could come from smaller pictures. Its hidden copies are parked.
- **The render thread, measured again on Bevy 0.18** (`local\pacing_s102.ps1`: the walk at 165 a second, `--log-pacing`, two runs each way): updates a quarter of a frame early or late, 2 and 0 of 1,110 without it, 2 and 2 with it; the ripple of when the update runs, 6.1% and 6.5%. Bevy 0.16's one in eight (`history.md`, 2026-10-09) is gone. **The frame: 3.37 to 2.05 ms with it** (−38%): the main app's work and the renderer's overlap. Each frame is drawn one update later, as the game's own engine draws it: this install's `boot.config` has `gfx-enable-gfx-jobs=1` and `gfx-enable-native-gfx-jobs=1`, Unity's graphics jobs, which render on threads of their own behind the main thread.
- **All of it, against 0.78.0**: 3.60 ms a frame; with the ambient occlusion 3.77; parked 3.37; with the render thread 2.05 (the light run, three rounds each, `local\perf_final_s102.txt`). At 165 frames a second the screen's 6.06 ms holds either way; the room under it is what changed.
- **Left as the engine's**: GPU preprocessing is fully supported here (the log), so the opaque and shadow passes are already drawn by a few indirect calls; recording the draws is 0.015 ms of a pass's 0.3, the rest is wgpu's own work at a pass's end. The range's 325 boxes share a material per colour already.

## Added 2026-10-19 (the frame rate limit and the multisampling: 0.81.0)

The user: "add an fps limiter option in-game", then "also allow me to change theamount of msaa we have. Like 2x 4x or off". Built as `docs/spec/settings.md` says ("The frame rate limit", "`MSAA`").

- **The game's limit in a raid holds only with VSync off** (`GraphicsSettingsController.ChangeFramerate`): with VSync on it sets `targetFrameRate` to -1 in a raid and leaves the pacing to the screen; with it off, the game limit, unless `DisableGameFramerateLimit` or NVIDIA Reflex. Its limits are 30 to 144 (the game's own, which the server's `FramerateLimit` in `SPT_Data/database/settings.json` may replace: this install's has the same). The user's `Graphics.ini`: VSync off, `GameFramerate` 120, the limit on, so their raids run at 120 a second.
- **Where to wait, with the render thread on**: Bevy stamps its frame clock in the renderer, right after it presents (`render_system`, the `TimeSender`). A wait at the end of the main loop held the frames to the rate on average (11.111 ms at 90) but let the render thread's frames come 2 and 20 ms apart now and then (sd 1.3 ms); a wait in the renderer just before it draws holds them evenly (sd 0.16 ms), with or without the render thread.
- **A change of multisampling remakes the prepass's textures within the frame**: the ambient occlusion's pass chose its pipeline in its `prepare` by the normals of the frame before and its bind group in the node by the new ones (a validation error: a multisampled layout and a single-sampled texture); it now readies both and chooses as it draws. 4x to off to 4x to 2x, live, with the render thread on and off: drawn, no error; a burst of fire at off and 2x, no error.

## Added 2026-10-19 (Customs' clouds and the moon: 0.82.0)

Built as `docs/spec/lighting.md` says ("Customs' clouds", "The moon").

- **Customs' clouds are not Time of Day's**: the level's weather carries `EFT.Rendering.Clouds.CloudController`, the game's port of HDRP's cloud layer (one layer; its controller's two command buffers, the shadows `BeforeGBuffer` and the draw `AfterForwardOpaque`). Its shaders are HDRP's own in shape; the game's differ in taking the planet's radius from the settings (6,704,000 m, `PlanetCenterRadius.w`, handed in where HDRP's bake had its upper-sky flag: the bake always does the upper sky), in the shadows' texel (one plus the tint's luminance less the opacity, where HDRP's lerps toward the tint's colour) and in a general way out of the shell (from inside, as every way here is, the same roots).
- **The pieces as kept**: the settings' asset keeps neither the map's four opacities, nor the steps, nor the resolutions, nor whether shadows are cast: the remap and the quality's preset set them each frame. The remap (`CloudRemapData_main`) is four curves of seven keys over the cloudiness from 0 to 1. The wind controller (`Weather/WindZone`) keeps its wires' figures inline (eleven floats), then the rain's, the clouds' (2) and the far trees' multipliers.
- **A script asset's fields** (`trp_data::clouds::asset_fields`): after the engine's head (its object, its switch, its script, its name), in its class's order of declaration, the fields marked not to be kept left out.
- **A directional light's cookie** is laid from the light's own place (`CalculateLightMatrix`, read with Ghidra: for a directional light with a cookie, a half plus the light's own coordinates over the cookie's size, across and up), repeated; Customs' sun light stands at (−22.24, 43.48, −50.74) under the dome (recorded, and the scene's own).
- **Bevy's light textures**: a directional light's texture is a clustered decal of its own transform (two of its units across, centred, its scale taken), read across the other way to the engine's cookie (`local.xy × (−0.5, 0.5) + 0.5`), only its red, multiplying the light as the engine's cookie does (with the shadow after). They need Bevy's `pbr_light_textures` and binding arrays (Vulkan; not wgpu's DirectX 12). A light's scale does not touch its shadows (the cascades take its turn alone) or its way. Its cost in this build: 0.06 ms a frame with no light textured (the light run, three runs each against a build without it).
- **A compute shader's textures in wgpu**: the cloud texture and the cookie are written as four half floats (`Rgba16Float`, which wgpu writes on every backend; the game's texture is two).
- **A sky's picture sampled as the game's**: the game's project is in gamma, so its pictures are sampled as stored (the moon's DXT1 is marked sRGB and read as it is); the app hands such a picture to its own shaders as `Unorm`, its rows as stored (the game's v is the stored row's).
- **The moon's shader takes no colour of its mesh**: `TOD_MoonMeshColor` is set by the sky and not read by `Time of Day/Moon`. Its sphere's coordinates: across, a half less the turn about up from the front over a whole turn; up, a half plus the height's angle over π.
- **The light run later the same day**: 2.26 to 2.29 ms a frame for a build of 0.82.0 with no light textured (with and without Bevy's light textures in it), where 0.80.0's runs that morning read 2.05; not explained (the machine's state or this version's other changes, the moon's sampling and the clouds' plugin idle), not measured apart.
- **At night by these rules** (`--hour 22`, not compared): the clouds come out a dull red from the harmonics' night colour; the range's frame at night is dark, PRISM's exposure at its top.

## Added 2026-10-19 (the ambient highlight, the hall's bounced light: 0.83.0)

Built as `docs/spec/lighting.md` says ("The ambient highlight"; "The range hall", the lamps).

- **The game lights the first-person hands a second time** (`AmbientHighlight`, on Customs' `ambientManager`): stencil-tested on the materials' own `_StencilType`, which the character shaders write into the stencil's lowest two bits and which the game's code never sets. The arms' and the weapons' materials carry 2 (hands); the shader's default is 0 (static). No `FakeCharacterGI` on Customs' camera (the recorded camera's components), though the class is in the code.
- **A trap: `probe dump` cuts a list at 12 items unless given a larger limit** (`probe dump <key> <id> <depth> <items>`): the arms' material has 35 floats, `_StencilType` the 13th or later; read without the limit it seemed to have none.
- **`probe shaders --stencil`** lists every shader's passes that write the stencil, by figure or by the property that sets it (`local\stencil_shaders_s102.txt`): the `p0/` character shaders by `_StencilType` (write mask 3), the windows' glass and blur 6, the optic sights 1, the lights' own volumes 65 to 191.
- **The hall's light inside is physics' at a mid-dark floor**: the lamps' bounce comes to 0.037 off the floor (0.35, 0.10 made linear) and about 0.01 off the walls in the frame's linear light: a surface in the hall taking light only from it shows about 0.15 to 0.22 of its colour in the game's numbers, before the camera's indoor exposure lifts the frame.

## Added 2026-10-19 (the light checked against the game's)

The user: "double check our light system with tarkovs". What the app takes from the game is the game's, compared where recorded (520 tests: the sun's way, colour and intensity, the ambient harmonics and `_EFT_Ambient`, the atmosphere, PRISM, the scattering, the clouds' figures, the highlight's setting). **How a surface turns them into its colour is not the game's**:

- **The game's deferred light** is its own shader, `Hidden/Internal-DeferredShadingEFT` (the build's graphics settings, `globalgamemanagers` 7: deferred mode custom, `globalgamemanagers.assets` 18; the sun's variant with the clouds' cookie and the screen's shadows disassembled, `local\shaders_deferred_s102`). It is Unity's standard light (Disney's diffuse without its 1/π, GGX with the joint Smith visibility, Schlick's Fresnel on the G-buffer's specular colour, the specular's square root as Unity takes it in a gamma project), worked on the G-buffer's gamma figures, the light's colour times the cookie's alpha times the shadow; each light added to the frame, the ambient after it (`Hidden/WriteScreenAmbient`). So every term is the game's number and they add as numbers.
- **The app** draws with Bevy's light: the same diffuse (Burley's) and nearly the same specular, but in linear light, each figure made linear by the 2.2 power and the sum taken back by it. A product of two figures comes out the game's; a sum or a slant does not. For a grey of 0.5 under 09:37's sun (0.728 red) and sky: **facing up in the sun, the game's 0.517, the app's 0.388 (a quarter darker)**; at half the sun's slant beside the sky's 0.16, the game's 0.262, the app's 0.274; in the shade the same. The app's light is flatter: the sun's side darker, the slant softer.
- **The game's surfaces' figures** come from their own shaders' G-buffer passes (`p0/Reflective/Bumped Specular SMap`, the arms' and weapons', its pass `DEFERRED` with light probes and HDR disassembled, `local\shaders_p0_s102\..._b10649`): the colour times a rim term by the view (`_Fresnel`), the specular colour `_SpecColor` times the picture's alpha and the rim, the smoothness the gloss picture times `_Glossness`, the normal map scaled, and in the emission a cubemap's reflection (`_Cube`, `_ReflectColor`) and the light probes' ambient taken to gamma. The app's materials take the colour, normal and gloss pictures into Bevy's (no metal, roughness from the gloss), its specular Bevy's own dielectric, and Bevy reflects the sky's ambient cube where the game reflects its own cubemaps.
- **The camera's effects not built** that shape the picture's light, all on in the recorded camera: `UltimateBloom`, `BloomAndFlares`, `ChromaticAberration`, `CC_Sharpen`, `Undithering`.

## Added 2026-10-20 (Tarkov's surface shading, its first part: 0.84.0)

Built as `docs/spec/lighting.md` says ("Tarkov's surface shading").

- **The engine's attenuation of a point or spot light is a texture it makes at the start** (`BuildAttenuationTexture`, `LightAttenuation`, read with Ghidra: `local\ghidra_atten_dec_s103.txt`): `UnityAttenuation`, 1,024 texels, 1/(1 + 25u) at the distance squared over the range squared, faded linearly to nought over u from 0.64 to 1. The deferred passes sample it at `dot(d, d) × _LightPos.w`. Until 0.84.0 the fade was not read (`PROVISIONAL`); it touches only the last fifth of a light's range.
- **The game's light passes treat a light of pure red apart**: where the light's colour's length equals its red, the specular is left out and the colour held to 0.4, and with the x of a global (the light pass's constant 13) under one, the light gives nothing. The global's name is not in the pass's own names; no light of Customs' or the app's is red.
- **The screen's ambient pass adds a term besides the harmonics**: the surface's colour's brightness squared times 0.05 (1 + max(1.5 × `TOD_Fogginess`, `_NightVisionOn`)) times one less the sun's height, indoors and out (its globals' names from the pass's own, `local\screenambient_parsed_s93.txt`; `_SunDirection` set by `RippleController` to the sun's way turned round, recorded on Customs). For a mid-grey on Customs' morning 0.0059; the spec had called it under 0.003 before it was worked out.
- **A trap: a local name the same as one of the module's imports makes naga_oil import a module that is not there.** Bevy's shader modules take their imports from what the code names, not only from the `#import` lines (`naga_oil::compose::get_preprocessor_data`): in `pbr_functions.wgsl`, which imports `bevy_pbr::{..., ambient, ...}`, a variable of the patch called `ambient` was taken as the item `ambient` of the module `bevy_pbr`, which no shader declares. Every pipeline that used the module then waited for that import without end, silently (`ShaderImportNotYetAvailable` is retried, not reported): the scene was not drawn, and the warm-up ran to its 30 s limit. Found by listing the pipeline cache's states from the render world. `shading.rs` now refuses a patch that would import anything the module did not.
- **Bevy's own shader modules can be replaced as they load**, as `gamma_ui` replaces the screens' top-level shaders: putting a changed `pbr_functions.wgsl` into the shader assets under its own id clears it and every module that imports it from the composer, and the pipelines built with it are built again (once, at the start: none had been built yet).

## Added 2026-10-20 (a grenade's explosion, its second part: 0.85.0)

Built as `docs/spec/grenades-and-knife.md` ("The blast's effect") and `particles.md` say.

- **The engine's own meshes are in the install, readable**: a bundle's pointer into its external `Library/unity default resources` (the effects' bundle's file 5) is the build's `EscapeFromTarkov_Data/Resources/unity default resources`, which keeps no type trees (format 22, engine 2022.3.34f1: 40 pictures, 13 meshes, 6 shaders among others); its meshes read by the tree of a mesh borrowed from any bundle that has one (`probe typed`, `trp_data::screen::build_object`). Object 10207 is the sphere (515 corners, 768 triangles, 0.5 out), as Unity's built-in numbering has it (10202 the cube).
- **A material's queue can put a transparent-looking shader among the opaque things**: the shock wave's material has its own queue of 1000 (its shader's tag says `Transparent`); in the deferred path a shader with no deferred pass at a queue under 2500 is drawn with the forward opaque things after the deferred light, so its grab holds the lit opaque scene alone, and the sky (drawn only where nothing is) and every transparent thing come after it.
- **The shock wave's Fresnel does nothing at the install's figures**: `_FresnelR0` is 59.9, so `saturate((1 − R0) f + R0)` is whole for any `f` in nought to one; the wave shifts what is behind it evenly by `_BumpAmt` (5) pixels times its alpha.
- **Bevy's own fragment for the standard material is not the only one that lights the range**: the range's grid (`grid.wgsl`, an extended material) has its own fragment, which never called Bevy's decal step, so a clustered decal showed on nothing of the range until it did. Bevy's decals also share one sampler, the first image's ("All decal images in the scene must use the same sampler": the clouds' cookie's here with `CUSTOMS CLOUDS`).
- **A shader patched with a function its own module does not hold**: `pbr.wgsl` patched to call `apply_decals_tagged` (put into `clustered.wgsl` by the same patch) and `mesh_functions::get_tag` needs both imports added to it as lines (`#import bevy_pbr::decal::clustered::apply_decals_tagged`, `#import bevy_pbr::mesh_functions`); `dpdx` must come before the tag's test, which the uniformity analysis takes as a branch on a value that varies.
- **The stencil type a material declares is how the game keeps decals off characters and hands**: the decal shader's pass compares its own `_StencilType` (0, "Static") with the surface's through a read mask of 3 (`MaterialEnum(Static, 0, Characters, 1, Hands, 2)`); its front and back faces' records say `Always` beside its general one's `Equal` (taken as the general one).

## Added 2026-10-20 (the flashbang's effect: 0.86.0)

- **A part of an effect can be switched off in the install and stay so**: the flashbang's `Effect Shockwave` has its `ParticleSystemRenderer` off (`m_Enabled` 0) and its `DistortRenderer` disabled; the adapter still emits into its system, but Unity calls no `Start` or `OnEnable` on a disabled script, so it never reaches `DistortCameraFX`, and nothing draws it. A renderer's `m_Enabled` is read and honoured since 0.86.0.
- **The older emitters' systems are shared**: each `ParticleSystemAdapter` emits into its own object's system in the effects' prefab, one for every effect that names it (the Zarya's `Drop Sparks` among others), and each adapter's ten draws (made once at `Awake`) are gone through by every effect that uses it; the sparks' adapter emits into the very renderer the muzzles' sparks use (`MuzzleEffect.Spark`).
- **`FastRndom`'s tables are Unity's generator's at fixed seeds** (`Random.InitState(14547)` for the floats, 97829 the turns, 567813 the unit ways, 34572 the half sphere, 12578 the circle), each a ring of 1024 read backwards by a shared index; the counts' draws are its own xorshift. Not reproduced (Unity's `insideUnitSphere` and `onUnitSphere` sampling is not published): draws from the same distributions instead.

## Added 2026-10-20 (a launcher's blasts: 0.87.0)

- **A weapon's prefab can hold muzzle objects the game never uses**: the RShG-2 has three `MuzzleParticlePivot`s, and the rule that picks the muzzle's objects (the deepest under the root, and `extractor_smoke`) leaves `ExplosionRPGBackPivot` out; the back blast plays at `extractor_smoke`.
- **The blasts' shaders are of Unity's legacy vertex-lit kind** (`LightMode` `Vertex`): eight lights of every kind handed in `unity_LightPosition` and its fellows. The grenade smoke's measures its lights from a vertex stream (`TEXCOORD1`, the particle's centre) its renderer does not give (no custom streams), so the engine's default is what it gets; and it multiplies each light by its own `w`, so a directional light (the sun) adds nothing to it.
- **A renderer that is off can have no material**: the blasts' roots (`ExplosionRPGFront`, `ExplosionRPGBack`) keep a renderer, off, with an empty material slot; and the bundles keep a renderer's on flag as a boolean (the reader took it as a number until 0.87.0, so an off one read as on: the flashbang's shock wave was saved only by its shader not being ported).

## Added 2026-10-20 (ballistics, its first part: 0.88.0)

- **Unity's own random numbers are read from the engine** (Ghidra on `UnityPlayer.dll` with Unity's public symbols: `Random_CUSTOM_InitState`, `Random_Get_Custom_PropValue`, `Random_CUSTOM_Range`, `RangedRandom`, `RandomUnitVector`, `Random_CUSTOM_get_insideUnitSphere_Injected`; `local\ghidra_random_dec_s105.txt`): a xorshift128 seeded by the 1812433253 multiplier, a number between 0 and 1 from the low 23 bits times the float `0x34000001`, `Range` as a blend `(1 − t)·max + t·min`, `onUnitSphere` from two draws (`trp_sim::unity_random`; ballistics spec, "The random numbers"). Rebuilt, the ballistics calculator's normal table gives a recorded bullet's penetration to 1e-4. **So the notes below (0.86.0, 0.66.0) that `FastRndom`'s tables cannot be made again no longer hold**: they are Unity's generator at fixed seeds and can be made exactly; the flashbang's and the impacts' older emitters still draw from the same distributions instead (to put right).
- **The game's G1 list is looked up as if its places were 0.05 Mach apart** (`floor(speed / 343 / 0.05)`), which they are only to Mach 0.6: above it the place found is not the speed's and the coefficient is drawn on past its pair (at Mach 1, 0.431 for the table's 0.4805). The recorded flights follow the game's lookup (1.4 mm over 2 s), so it is kept.
- **The ballistics' step is the world's own clock, not Unity's frame time** (`GameWorld.DeltaTime`, measured between the world's updates by a stopwatch): on the same frame the recorder's `dt` and a bullet's grown age differ by up to half a millisecond.
