A frame no recorded bullet flew through from the frame before has no record of it, so a replay has to stand Unity's in, and a bullet that hit then may be cut a few centimetres from where the game cut it (16 of 545 hits). **For the recorder (next version)**: write `GameWorld.DeltaTime` with each frame.
- **Automatic fire and a single shot come at different places in the frame**: automatic fire's shots (the first too) are made by the firing operation in the player's update, before the ballistics, so they move on in their own frame; a single shot is made by the weapon animation's event, after them, so its bullet waits a frame (every recorded shot of `courses3` agrees: the AS VAL's bursts in `2-far` 0.008 s out on their own frame, every single shot at its start).
- **Each collider in a level carries its own six figures**, often not its material's preset: on Customs the recorded thick metal had three sets (its preset and its low- and medium-penetration variants), the cardboard a level of 100 where its preset says 0. The hard settings keep 47 presets: one per material by number (0 to 37), then variants (cardboard, fabric, thick metal, pebbles, thick wood at other penetrations) and snow's at 46; the terrain takes `ColliderPresets[material number]`, so a snow terrain would get the medium-penetration cardboard's figures (and material). The recorded terrain hits (soil, asphalt, gravel, tall grass) are their presets exactly.
- **A material without an entry in the effects' list plays nothing at all**: gravel, tile, the grasses, mud, pebbles, chain fence, garbage, shattered glass, tyres and generic hard have none. Recorded: hits on gravel and tall grass were silent where concrete, wood, metal and soil played their banks.
- **A ricochet can be met by its own backward cast at its start**: the cast back from the ricochet's first step's end reaches the face it leaves from, and the game counts it (a stop, `2-surfaces`, frame 14746).
- **Even aimed, the game's shots leave several milliradians off the camera's line**: 2 to 11 for the AS VAL (3 to 9 above), up to 36 below for the MP-443 (`local\shot_vs_cam_s105.pl <trace>`).

## Added 2026-10-20 (what a bullet's hit shows and sounds: 0.89.0)

- **No impact's bank is physical or limited** (`the_impacts_are_read`): every material entry's sound plays plainly at its point, so `LimitedPlay` and its gag (`GagSoundLimiter`: no closer than a radius growing from 1 to 3 m with the listener's distance, for 0.3 of the bank's clip length) never come into a bullet's hit in this install; a knife's hit would take them.
- **The deferred decal renderer's generic decal is the interface's red alert icon** (`notification_icon_alert_red`, 30 by 29, red): a leftover, which no entry of the install ever lays (every entry that lays a deferred decal names only materials with their own sheet).
- **Cardboard and snow play "Generic Soft"**, the list's first entry, though the wood entry names cardboard too and the renderer has a wood sheet for it: the effect's dictionary keeps the first entry naming a material.
- **A grenade's fragments are bullets**: `Grenade.Explosion` fires them through the shared ballistics calculator, so their hits play the same effects (`courses3`'s `1-impact`: concrete, metal, asphalt, gravel, tall grass and fabric, with their emits and sounds recorded). The app's blast hurts by the range's boxes instead (grenades spec): to put right.
- **Bevy 0.18's clustered decals index at most eight pictures at once** (`binding_array<texture_2d<f32>, 8u>` in its view bindings), and its light textures (the clouds' cookie) share them: the bullets' six sheets go into one atlas, each hole's place in it, its cell and its sheet's cells in the decal's 32-bit tag, which the patched decal step reads.
- **Every older emitter's draw is `FastRndom`'s** (`RundomRange.Value` and `FillValues`' turns and seeds; the counts and ways by its whole numbers and tables), but the sparks' adapter's place, which is Unity's own `Random.Range(0, 128)`.

## Added 2026-10-20 (the hits' particle shaders: 0.90.0 to 0.92.0)

- **Unity's built-in `Standard` is in the install with 1,728 programs, its fog variants stripped**: a variant is found by the parsed form's sub-programs (`m_KeywordIndices` against the shader's `m_KeywordNames`, the pass by its `LightMode`), its blob entry being the program's (`probe shaders Standard <folder>`, then `fxc`). The specks' is the forward base pass's 32 and 234 (`_EMISSION _ALPHABLEND_ON DIRECTIONAL LIGHTPROBE_SH`): Unity's `BRDF1` with the gamma-space square root of the specular.
- **Neither `Standard` nor `Legacy Shaders/Transparent/Diffuse` takes a particle's colour** (their programs have no vertex colour): such particles do not fade over their life; only their pictures' alpha and the material's colour count.
- **`TOD_SunCloudColor` is never set** (only its id is made, `TOD_Resources`): a shader blending by it (`Particles/Water Splash`) gets nought, so the splash takes its night tint.
- **A particle's added velocity** (the engine's `ParticleSystemParticles` keeps one apart from its velocity: the velocity module adds into it, the initial module empties it every step, the step moves the particle by both): read with Ghidra (`VelocityModule::Update`, `InitialModule::Update`, `ParticleSystem::SimulateParticles`), so velocity over life does not accumulate where force over life does.
- **A cube's faces lie one after another in its data, each with all its levels** (`m_ImageCount` 6; "dots small", 32 px DXT1: 696 bytes a face).

## Added 2026-10-20 (bullets on characters, armour and damage: 0.93.0)

- **A shot never makes a blunt contusion in SPT**: `Player.ApplyShot` calls `ApplyDamageInfo(…, absorbed: 0)`, and `BluntContusion` wants 10 absorbed on the head; only `ClientPlayer` (online) overrides `ApplyShot`. SPT's player and bots are both `LocalPlayer`, whose only override, `ProceedLocalAbsorbedDamage`, writes the absorbed damage into `DidArmorDamage` (the recorder's `did_armor`).
- **The game's `ArmorDummy`** (a `MonoBehaviour` with its own rule: `136 − 5000 / (36 + durability %)`, `Random.Range` for the chance, a fixed 1 of damage when blocked) is an older test target, not the characters' path: the dummy here is a character with the prefab's colliders, as the user chose.
- **A body collider's figures are set twice**: the prefab gives each `20, 0.3, 0, 0, 0.441, 0.056` and a share of 0.1 (`probe player-scripts BodyPartCollider`, which now prints a ballistic collider's figures), and `InitColliderSettings` puts the globals' level, chance and share over them by collider type; the plates (`ArmorPlateCollider`) override it with nothing and keep the prefab's level 0, chance 1, share 1. The recorded figures are the set ones.
- **One draw decides everything at a body**: the heavy vests' deflection, each armour piece's deflection and block, the ricochet and the deviation all take the number at the bullet's seed from the same 512 floats (the heavy vests' table is a second copy made the same way, `PrecalculatedFloatRandoms(512, 0)`).
- **.NET's `List<T>.Sort` is not stable for three**: for two or three items it swaps by fixed exchanges (`SwapIfGreater` 0–1, 0–2, 1–2), so `[1, 1, −1]` comes out `[−1, 1(second), 1(first)]`; four to sixteen go by an insertion sort, which is. The worn armour's order (`OrderLockedLast`) goes through it.
- **The recorder's armour list is cut short**: `Combat.Armour` walks `Equipment.GetItemComponentsInChildren<ArmorComponent>()` inside one `try`, and something in it throws after the first pieces (Tagilla: the mask alone; a Scav: none; the user's PMC: three), so the wear before and after each hit is not in `courses3`. Wanted of the recorder: each piece of `GetPutOnArmors()` in its own `try`, its template, durability, greatest durability and class; each part's greatest health beside its current; and the world's step (`GameWorld.DeltaTime`).
- **Bots' armour spawns worn and their parts' greatest health varies**: the `3-bot-shots` Scav's Module-3M front insert stood at 5 of 32 (its first blocked hit gives exactly 5.000), the `2-far` Scav's at less than whole; Scavs were met with whole parts of 30/80/70/60/60/60/60, 30/85/70/60/60/65/65 and 30/100/100/70/70/80/80. Over-damage is shared by the parts' greatest health, so a character taken up wounded shares it otherwise unless its greatest is known.
- **A bot heals between hits**: Tagilla's every part rose between two hits and his destroyed left arm came back at 4 (his surgery and medicine, not run in the replay).
- **The replay's step for a shot that hit in its first frame**: the recording's bullet shows its age at the hit (`TimeSinceShot` less the time left), not the step, so it no longer stands for the world's step (0.93.0); where no other bullet gives the step, Unity's frame time stands in, and a bullet that hits within its first 10 ms then arrives at its starting speed (our trajectory's velocity is the step's start's) where the game's has slowed: up to 7 m/s and 0.46 of damage on Tagilla's. The body rules are set beside the game's on the game's own damage and penetration for that reason.

## Added 2026-10-20 (the dummy on the range: 0.94.0)

- **A capture's taps press keys inside the inventory's systems** (`inventory_view::scripted_drags`, in the set `ScreenDrawn`): a system that reads an app's key (`F9`) must run after that set, as `health_view::damage_keys` does, or a `--tap` never reaches it.
- **A body's hit plays the material the hit was only when the character took it**: `PlayHitEffect` takes `PlayerHitInfo.Material` (and no ricochet's whine) for a living character's forward hit; a hit from behind or on the dead plays the collider's own `Body`, and a ricochet off it whines as off anything.
- **Only a bone's own collider takes the corpse's push**: `CorpseRagdoll.FindRigidbodySpawner` compares the hit collider with each spawner's own `Collider`, so a killing hit on a plate or on the head's smaller colliders (`Parietal`, `Jaw`…) pushes nothing.
- **The blood shaders multiply the frame** (`Blend Zero SrcColor`; `DstColor SrcColor`), so on the effects' frame in gamma numbers (`gamma_ui`) they port straight; the blood reads darker on cloth and faint on a dark wall.
- **A transform helper and a mutable transform query in one system** stop the app at its start (Bevy's B0001): a `ParamSet` of the two, as `death::follow` has.

## Added 2026-10-20 (the dummies as characters: 0.95.0)

- **A weapon's handguard carries a hand of its own**: the AKM's wooden handguard holds `Base HumanLPalm` with its fifteen digits (and a second, `LPalm 1`), the grip's finger pose stored as bones (`ArmRig::find`'s uncached grip). Anything that takes the arms' digits by name must take only those under a collarbone.
- **From the hip a shot leaves 0.19 m to the right of the eye**, along the view: at 3.5 m it passes beside a standing character's torso, whose box is 0.31 m wide. With the dummy's arms on its rifle nothing hangs there; in 0.94.0 its hanging left arm took those shots. Turning 3° left (`--yaw -3`) puts them on the chest; the sights put them on the crosshair.
- **What the two dummies cost**: 0.57 ms a frame (the light run, two rounds each, `local\perf_dummies_s107.txt`: 4.48 ms with them, 3.91 with `--feature dummy=off`): two characters' simulation at the player's frame rate, their two bodies and two AKMs drawn.
- **A capture's right button held** brings the sights up slowly against the frame count (they were not up 150 frames in): an aimed capture needs its shots late, or the hip and a turned view.

## Added 2026-10-20 (the workbench and the edit build screen: 0.96.0)

- **Every weapon was its stock preset until now**: a rig of the hands, a picture and a model on the ground were each asked for by the weapon's template and assembled from its stock preset (`weapon.rs`, `icons.rs`, `loose.rs`), so a part moved in a raid (a sight, a magazine) never showed on the gun or changed its handling; the options' list said so (`ADVANCED ATTACHMENT SYSTEM`, not built). Now by the build's key (`trp_data::weapon::build_key`), the stock build still by its template.
- **The hideout is `bunker_2.unity`** (`level161`; its areas `NN_name`, the workbench `10_workbench`, each level an object of props `level0` to `level3`); its props are in no bundle (no `workbench` key in the install's manifest), only in the scene's own files. Level 3: 980 objects, 160 meshes drawn (of 319 before levels of detail and shadow-only renderers were left out), 56 materials, 133 pictures, read in 0.9 to 1.3 s on another thread; none joined into the scene's static batch. Its two triggers: `HideoutInteractionTrigger` on box colliders (a collider's flags each padded to four bytes in the build's layout: trigger, then on).
- **A weapon prefab's object stands 1.47 m up in its container** (the AKM's `_weaponObject`, as the bundle has it, the arms' height), but the game hangs its copy at the container's middle, unturned (`WeaponPrefab.CreateObjectInstance`): the `PreviewPivot`'s pivot (0.134, −0.06, 0.479 for the AKM) is about that.
- **The game's arc-length table of an ellipse is a degree on in its third quarter** (`EllipseCalculator.Ellipse.GetCashLength` reads `CachedLengths[angle − 180]`, where the first quarter reads `[angle − 1]`): a length found from an angle between 180° and 270° comes back a degree further. Kept, as the game places its slots by it (`trp_sim::edit_build`, tested).
- **The screen's slots are sorted along the ellipse before they are spaced** (`UpdatePositions`: `list.Sort` then `Spacer.SpaceOut`, whose merging walks neighbours): spaced unsorted, two came out on one another.
- **The screen's preview** (`CommonUIScene`'s `Weapon Camera`): 22° up and down, planes 0.3 to 1000 m, the preview at a height of 0; lit by eleven point lights of ranges 0.4 to 2 m and two directional fills, all on the preview's own layer (19), which the game moves to the world's middle.
- **What the workbench costs** (the light run, two rounds each, `local\perf_bench_s108.txt`): the whole bench of 0.96.0, 0.24 ms a frame from the lanes (4.56 against 4.32 with `--feature workbench=off`), 0.42 ms standing at it in sight (4.55 against 4.13): its 160 meshes drawn and shadowed; its table alone (0.96.1, `local\perf_table_s108.txt`), 0.04 ms standing at it (4.35 against 4.31), within the runs' own spread.
- **The trigger is kept under any screen** (`controller.rs`: a key let go under a screen is let go, and a press toggles it): under the inventory the weapon is lowered and cannot fire, under the edit build screen it was not, so dragging the preview fired it (the user's, 0.96.0). Since 0.96.1 a press under that screen is not the trigger's.
- **A part chosen at the screen is bare**: `CreateAllModsEver` makes every part's template new, so a magazine chosen comes empty (the AKM's picture then says 0/30, as the game's would).

## Added 2026-10-20 (the edit build screen as the game's: 0.97.0)

- **Holding the pointer moves it** (`cursor.rs`: as a hold begins the pointer is put in the window's middle, so that it is not held outside): a screen that turns something by the pointer's travel while the mouse's own reads none takes that trip for a move. The edit build screen did, and a press on the preview (which began a drag at once) flung the gun by the distance from the click to the middle. The game turns it by the mouse's axes alone, and only once its event system has begun a drag (`DragTrigger.onBeginDrag`, past the drag threshold). A run nobody is at the keys of still turns it by the pointer's travel, which its scripted drags move.
- **Bevy's UI blocks by default only where asked**: a node is `FocusPolicy::Pass` unless it says otherwise, a `Button` `Block`; a node with no policy at all is taken as `Block` (`ui_focus_system`), but every `Node` has one. So a part's view drawn over a slot's box passes clicks to the box, and the box's area, a `Button`, keeps a press from the preview under it. `RelativeCursorPosition::cursor_over` is true over a node whether or not another takes the click, which keeps the dropdown's button shown while the mouse is on it.
- **A line thinner than a pixel can vanish**: Bevy rounds a node's edges to the screen's pixels, so a node 0.83 pixels high (a unit at 900 lines) comes out none where both its edges round to the same pixel. The slots' lines are kept at least a pixel and laid out unrounded.
- **A camera that does not draw keeps its picture**: the preview's camera off (`Camera::is_active`), the image it drew last stays in its target, so the screen shows the gun still while nothing changes.
- **The edit build screen's frame** (release, at the bench, uncapped on Vulkan, three rounds each, `local\perf_eb5_s109.txt`; two each, `perf_eb2_s109.txt`, `perf_eb4_s109.txt`): shut 4.12 ms; open, at rest, 4.33 (0.21 ms for its few hundred nodes); with its camera drawing every frame 4.73; 0.96.1's screen 4.49 (its camera drawing every frame and the slots' bones searched for every frame, with far fewer nodes).
- **The game's views of a thing have their own loader** (`ItemView._iconLoader`: `Loader`, the `spinner_big` sprite, 44 units in the view's middle, off in the prefab and shown while the icon is made); the inventory had drawn the app's spinner in them.

## Added 2026-10-21 (the bench's hands and zoom: 0.98.0)

- **The game keeps its icons on disk** (`ItemIconCache.Path`: `Icon Cache` in the game's temporary folder; `IconCreatorBase`: an `index.json` from each icon's hash to a numbered PNG, written as icons are made, a memory cache over it): nothing prunes it but `ClearIconCache` (the launcher's cache clearing). The app's `local\icon_cache` is the same idea: 1,372 pictures in 27 MB on 2026-10-21, 22 of them builds (and 5 MB of two older cache versions' folders, `v2` and `v3`, which nothing reads now).
- **A spinner shows only while a picture is made**: with every picture cached, none is seen. Run from a folder with an empty `local\` (the install by `EFT_DIR`), the slots' views show the game's loader while their pictures are drawn.
- **The game's only zoom of a preview is its inspect window's** (`ItemInfoWindowLabels.Zoom` on its `ScrollTrigger`); nothing of the edit build or modding screens reads the wheel. A zoom in never brings the camera nearer than shows the whole of the model's bounds as it was put in.
- **A part's view is 64 by 64 wherever a row or grid holds it**: its prefab's `LayoutElement` asks that, and `ItemView`'s own size by cells (`GetCellPixelSize`) applies only where its anchors meet (a grid's cell).
- **The draw that racks is the animator's choice** (the weapon's draw state goes by `AmmoInChamber` and `AmmoInMag` as the draw begins): telling it an empty chamber and a round to feed, and the truth at `AddAmmoInChamber`, racks with no round moved (`Firearm::rack_drawn`).

## Added 2026-10-21 (the inspect window's figures and model: 0.99.0)

- **The window's rows are three prefabs its script points to in its own file** (`ItemSpecificationPanel`: in `sharedassets49.assets`, the bar row 3485, the compact row 3186, the dropdown row 2964, each known by the script on its top; `trp_data::screen::info_rows`). Each row is a `HorizontalLayoutGroup`; its name and value share what is left of it (each `flexible` 1), so a bar's value (left-aligned) begins near the bar's middle and a compact row's (right-aligned) ends at its right.
- **A row's colours follow the pointers they come after**: a compact row's increasing and decreasing colours 12 and 28 bytes after its value's text pointer, a bar row's four after its comparison's bar pointer, then its adding and subtracting sprites (`row_look`).
- **The compact grid is a `FlexibleGridLayoutGroup`** (its cells 311.15 by 23, gaps 12.7 and 4, the vertical axis first, `FixedColumnCount` 2: the probe prints a grid's figures with `PROBE_LAYOUT=1`); it works out its cells' width from its own width, and the script sets its columns to one where a compact row's words do not fit.
- **The window grows past 500**: its `LayoutElement` asks 670 by 500, but Unity takes a thing's least size where it is greater than its preferred, so a weapon's rows (the preview giving up its room to its least, 300) make it about 685 high.
- **A round's calibre is written as its key** ("Caliber762x39"): `AmmoTemplate`'s `Caliber.Localized()`, and the install's locale has no such key (a weapon's drops "Caliber" first, "762x39", which has none either).
- **A row's text needs its own height in the layout**: a value aligned to its top in a row that sizes its children sits in a box as high as the text's preferred height, centred; given none it fell below the middle (`TmpFont::height`).
- **The window's loader is off when it is shown** (`Show`: `_loader.SetActive(false)`), on from the preview's first call until the model is in.
- **`PlaceInClosestPosition`'s cache is static** (`RendererExtensions._cachedPositions`, by template, for the run): a template's camera is fitted once, as first shown.

## Added 2026-10-21 (the inspect window as the game's, the range open: 0.100.0)

- **TextMeshPro takes a text's older alignment first** (its published source, `TMP_Text.LoadDefaultSettings`: where `m_textAlignment` is not `Converted`, 0xFFFF, its low byte becomes the horizontal and the next the vertical): the reader had taken only the two newer fields, so a few older texts were drawn top left where the game centres them on their middle line or puts them at the right (the inspect window's bars' values, its durability and mastering rows, its quest and mastering badges). Read so, the GEAR page is unchanged.
- **An image with no sprite of its own is the engine's plain white**: the app drew it so even where a script puts a sprite on it (a slot's picture, `SetSlotBackImage`); a sprite given now counts as its own.
- **`StretchWindow` is an Odin-serialized script**: its fields begin with Odin's data (900 bytes here), and `_maxSize` comes after; found as the two figures just before its pointer to the window's `LayoutElement`. 1800 by 1024 in this install.
- **A TextMeshPro sprite asset's names are not its sprites' names**: `Sprite Assets/armor_classes` names `armor_classes_1` to `_6` in its character table, each a glyph whose entry points to one of the engine's sprites (named `armor_classes_0` to `_4` and `_6`, in another order); a glyph's entry is 64 bytes (its number, metrics, rectangle, scale, atlas, kind, sprite pointer).
- **Only an armour's own slots are left out of a container's special slots** (`ArmorSlot`, made for a slot whose prototype is `64479fdf9731c8fadc0642c1`): a rig's other slots show.
- **A built-in insert's short name is empty**: its view is captioned with its slot's name (`ItemShortName`: CHEST, BACK and so on).

## Added 2026-10-22 (the documents' lists made one, the version out of the packages, the tests one binary a crate, the hooks, the banners, the crop: 0.100.3)


- **The version was part of every build.** Cargo builds a crate under its package version (the metadata hash that names its artifacts), so raising `[workspace.package] version` with every change rebuilt all four crates from scratch and relinked every binary and test harness (about 37 targets, 24 of them integration tests). Read from `[workspace.metadata.trp]` by `crates/trp-app/build.rs` into `TRP_VERSION`, a raise rebuilds `trp-app` alone (its `rustc-env` is in its fingerprint) and the packages stay at 0.0.0; `Cargo.lock` no longer moves with a raise.
- **The integration tests were twenty-four binaries** (twenty of `trp-sim`, three of `trp-physics`, one of `trp-data`), each linked on its own and each test loading the item database (`Database::load`, forty-two calls over `trp-sim`'s files). As one binary a crate (`tests/sim/main.rs`, `tests/physics/main.rs`, each old file a module) with the database read once into a `OnceLock` (`tests/sim/fixture.rs`), the links are one a crate and the loads one; the tests' names gained their file's prefix (`install::vault…`). On this Linux container without an install the 162 tests of `sim` pass with their note in 0.01 s; the 252 unit tests of `trp-sim` and 54 of `trp-data` pass.
- **`cargo check -p trp-app` on Linux** needs the ALSA headers (`libasound2-dev`: `bevy_audio`) and a winit backend the Windows build never names: `--features bevy/x11`. With those, PhysX from source and all, the app type-checked in 2 m 34 s on four cores (the crop flag and the build script included).
- **`findings.md` itself was out of order**: from 0.53.0 on, each session put its section after the 2026-10-17 ones, newest first, so the file ran 10-10 to 10-17, then 10-21 down to 10-17. Put back in date order (the 48 sections reversed as units); `check.sh` now says when the newest dated section is not the last, and `scaffold.sh` appends.
- **The ledger of rule 5 names files it should not and misses files it should**: nine `x.rs` names in "Placeholders, marked in the code" had no marker in the code, and the bullets named the markers' files irregularly, so a grep could not tell a covered marker from an uncovered one by name. The reconciliation: this day's `history.md` entry.

## Added 2026-10-22 (the placeholders ledger reconciled with the code: 0.100.4)


- The ledger of rule 5 against the code, by the workflow and by hand: eight guesses with no bullet, seven more with no clause, twelve bullets naming no file, nine names with no marker (six marked, three shaders marked, two bullets with no claim), three stale markers lifted; and `placeholders.sh`'s own `grep -q` under `pipefail` reporting named files as unnamed. Detail: `history.md`, 0.100.4.

## Added 2026-10-22 (CLAUDE.md imports AGENTS.md: 0.100.5)


- `/context` on Windows: the fixed cost of a prompt there is about 37k tokens, 23k of it Claude Code's tool definitions, 5.5k skills (2k the Universal modder plugin), 5.7k the system prompt, 0.9k the auto-memory; `AGENTS.md` was not listed under "Memory files" (0.100.5: `CLAUDE.md` imports it).

## Added 2026-10-22 (the agent's own notes folded into decisions.md: 0.100.6)


- The agent's auto-memory on the user's PC held fourteen notes, all duplicates of the documents' rules once `AGENTS.md` loads: deleted by the user; a new note is worth keeping only if it is not in `AGENTS.md` or `decisions.md`.

## Added 2026-10-22 (auto-memory off for the project: 0.100.7)


- The auto-memory switch is a project setting (`autoMemoryEnabled`), so the repository can turn it off for every machine that opens it; the project's own records make it redundant here.

## Added 2026-10-22 (A brief for agents in the public copy: 0.100.8)

- A Perl hook that slurps its input with `local $/;` and then counts a file's lines with `while <$f>` counts one: `local $/` holds until its enclosing scope ends, so the counting loop must set `local $/ = "\n"` in a block of its own. `read_guard.pl` refused fifty-line section reads for a day because of it, found by an agent reading the public copy.
- Claude Code's Read returns a file whole when it is under 2,000 lines, whatever its bytes: `status.md`'s 595 lines are 220 KB, about 55,000 tokens. A line count is no guard against a long document; bytes a line are (what the hook measures).
- An agent told its tool loads no `AGENTS.md` still opened the file by name once it saw it in the listing: in the public copy the brief is read even by tools that do not load it.
- A fan-out that verifies each finding with several agents grows with the finding count, not with the defects: 157 merged findings, most one undefined word each, would have taken 471 verifiers for a brief of 85 lines. Small findings of one kind are judged once, by hand, not one by one.
- Where tests are merged into one binary a crate, every `--test <old file>` in the documents goes stale at once: eighteen in the specs after 0.100.3, found by a newcomer following one (`trp-data`'s `tests/install.rs` is still its own binary, so its commands stand).
- An agent set to read a copy built a 2 GB `target` folder in it: a copy made for reading is made read-only, or the agents are told not to run cargo in it.

## Added 2026-10-22 (the skills' figures: 0.100.12)

- The globals' `config.SkillsSettings` (69 keys in SPT 4.1.6) holds how skills are *earned* (points an action: `MovementAction`, `SprintAction`, `WeaponShotAction` and the like; fatigue and the weight threshold are the `Skill*` keys beside it) and only a dozen *effects* a level (`...PerLevel`, `Elite...`). The per-level buffs of the physical skills (Covert Movement's noise, Endurance's stamina, Strength's weight, jump and throw, Vitality, Health, Stress Resistance, Perception, Attention, Search, Surgery, Mag Drills, Aim Drills, Throwing) are constants of the client's `SkillManager`, not of the file: read them in `local\decomp\src`, as `health.rs`, `firearm.rs` and `magazine_process.rs` already did for theirs. `spec\skills.md` has the file's figures.
- SPT's server source carries the install's `globals.json` whole (`Libraries/SPTushonka.Server.Assets/SPT_Data/database/globals.json`, 1.1 MB, under the release's tag): a published copy of the install's figures, readable where the install is not, by a clone without file contents (`git clone --depth 1 --filter=blob:none --no-checkout`, then `git checkout <tag> -- <path>` for the one file). Not for the repository (rule 3): read in place, figures into the spec in our own words.
- The cloud environment's network policy shuts `escapefromtarkov.fandom.com`, `tarkov.dev` and Wikipedia (a 403 to the connection), and lets `raw.githubusercontent.com` through (not `github.com`'s pages or API). The search tool still reaches the wiki, as a summary of a page, with figures that may be rounded ("roughly 0.4–0.6% per level"): a map of a page, not a source for a constant. To read the wiki itself, the host goes under the environment's Allowed domains.
- By the wiki's 1.2% a level for footsteps on common surfaces, the user's "covert movement lowers footstep sounds by 49 percent" is a Covert Movement level of about 41: the game's skill page shows the buff at the character's level, so a figure read from it carries the level with it.



## Added 2026-10-23 (Either Enter begins again; the regression in parallel: 0.101.1)

**The respawn** (the user: "I cannot respawn once dead"). `death::begin_again` heard `KeyCode::Enter` alone. The engine reports the keypad's Enter as `KeyCode::NumpadEnter`, so a press on the keypad was never heard, while the inventory's window asking how many took both. Everything else it waits for held in every case tried. The death's clock (`Dying`) is set only when `die` builds the corpse. `die` gives up without the corpse, the body's parts and rig, the weapon rig, or the install's ragdoll and physics settings, and then no death screen comes either: a respawn that fails with no screen up would be that, not the key. The screen comes at 1.05 s, and `StartOfRun` is always there. **`TARKOV UI` does not cover the death screen**: nothing it draws goes above `GlobalZIndex(40)` except the effects' tooltip (45), and with every switch as the user's the death screen was captured on top. **A capture run is not a played run.** A run with `--screenshot` reads no `options.json` (`options_view`: `(!options.unattended()).then(options_file)`), so it takes the defaults, and it forces the render thread off. A tap presses `ButtonInput` itself as well as sending the engine's key event. So a scripted run cannot show what a hand's key press does. `local\respawn_keys_s113.ps1` starts the app attended and presses keys through Windows.



## Added 2026-10-23 (HIGH at the start; the 37 against 36.6 traced: 0.102.0)

- **A question shortened past its meaning.** The 0.29.0 note "Your temperature reads 37, the app's 36.6 (your profile's)" became "37 against 36.6?" in the list of questions, and on 2026-10-22 it was asked as the weight. The commit that wrote it (`d61a5b7`, `git log -S"36.6"`) settled it. A question's figures keep what they measure.
- **The health tab's figures** (`HealthParameterPanel.SetParameterValue`): with places, `Math.Round(current, places)` on the float taken as a double and written by the double's own `ToString`, so 37.0 is "37" and 36.6 "36.6" (Rust's `{}` on an f64 writes the same). Without places, floor above half and ceiling below, `F0`.
- **The profile's health** (`SPT_Runtime\user\profiles`, the PMC's `Health`): temperature 37 of 40, hydration 100 of 100, energy 110 of 110, every part full (read 2026-10-23). The scav's temperature is 37.64.
- **The weight's maximum** (`HealthParametersPanel`, on `OnWeightUpdated`): `UpperOverweightLimit` times the skills' `CarryingWeightRelativeModifier` (1 + `StrengthBuffLiftWeightInc`) times the health's, plus the health's absolute modifier; the lower limit, so made, colours it. `StrengthBuffLiftWeightInc.Max(0.3f)`: `Max` divides by 50, so 0.006 a level (`FloatBuff.Max`). The user's Strength progress is 4001.8, level 40: 0.24, and 77 × 1.24 = 95.5, the capture's 95 (`BaseOverweightLimits` 26 to 77 in the globals).



## Added 2026-10-23 (Skills: what each changes and how it is earned: 0.102.1)

- **A skill the client does not list is a skill nobody has.** `SkillManager.GetSkill` searches only `Skills` (the two bots' and the 43 of `DisplayList`), so the profile's progress for Recoil Control, Sniping, Prone Movement, Lockpicking and the rest made but not listed is never read: their buffs stay 0 whatever the profile says. Recoil Control is the one that matters: its buff is the recoil suppression's second part (`WeaponBuffsInfo.RecoilSupression.y`), 0 for everyone in this client.
- **Buffs the skill page shows that nothing reads**: grep each field of `SkillManager` by name outside the file, then by `EBuffId.<name>` and by its string; about twenty have no reader (`StrengthBuffSprintSpeedInc`, `VitalityBuffSurviobilityInc`, `StressTremor`, the Intellect ones, `ThrowingStrengthBuff`, `DrawTremor`, `CovertMovementElite` among them). Some buffs are read through a property of another name (`AttentionLootSpeedValue`, `IsSearchDouble`, `CarryingWeightRelativeModifier`) or inside `SkillManager` (`GetWeaponInfo`, `GetBuffedGrenadeExpense`): look there before calling one unread.
- **The weapon classes' earning figures as the client takes them** (`CreateCombat`): six settings arrays for twelve classes; SMG, LMG and HMG take the Assault's, Launcher, Attached Launcher and Melee the Pistol's. HMG and Attached Launcher are tied to `typeof(int)` and `typeof(float)`, which no item is.
- **A factor is the install's figure times `SkillProgressRate`** (0.4) for every non-weapon action, including the ones whose factor is written in the code (Perception's 2.5 and 0.334, Strength's 0.2s, the vests' 0.05 and 0.025): `SkillAction.Factor` multiplies. The wiki's Stress Resistance figures (0.033, 0.33) are the install's before that multiplication; its Vitality damage figure (0.002 a point) after it.
- **The fed skills** take their share from `SkillProgress`, raised at the top of `Skill.OnTrigger` with the award before the giver's fatigue; `ClientAuthorizedSkill.OnTrigger` never raises it, so Crafting feeds Intellect only on the server.
- **The install's `EliteVitalityDuration`**: 22 under `LightBleeding`, 33 under `HeavyBleeding` (`health.md` had written them the other way round; `trp-sim`'s `health.rs` reads each from its own bleeding, so only the text was wrong).
- **The wiki's host refuses a fetch with HTTP 402** (2026-10-23); its pages came through search summaries only, about 85 days old in the index. The live game changed skill levelling on 2026-10-02 ("up to 60% faster"); the user's SPT client is older.



## Added 2026-10-23 (The skills system: 0.103.0)

- **The game's `Launcher` skill is the underbarrel launcher's.** `WeaponSkill(…, typeof(Launcher), …)`, and `Launcher : GearMod`; the M32A1 is `GrenadeLauncher : Weapon`, matched by no class (and the RShG-2's `RocketLauncher`, and `SpecialWeapon`). `GetWeaponInfo` looks a weapon's buffs up by its exact type (the assault carbine and rifle folded into `BaseAssault`): a weapon of no class takes 1 and 0. The texts call `Launcher` "Grenade Launchers" and `AttachedLauncher` (tied to `typeof(float)`) "Underbarrel Launchers".
- **The weapon's skill is read at the draw, not on a level up.** `SyncWithCharacterSkills` and `UpdateWeaponVariables` run when a weapon is taken in hand or modified; nothing listens to `OnSkillLevelChanged` in the hands. The movement's and stamina's buffs are read as they go (`Physical`'s capacities are functions of the buffs, `OnEnduranceLevelUp` refreshing them; `PhysicalBase` subscribes `OnWeightLimitsUpdated` to Strength's level up, so the weight's limits and the weight, elite sum and all, are worked out again at once: a method group, which a search for `UpdateWeightLimits()` misses).
- **Recoil suppression is one factor on the forces** (`NewRecoilShotEffect.RecalculateRecoilParamsOnChangeWeapon`): `max(0, (1 + modsFactorRecoil) × (1 − x − y × 0.1))` times the template's `RecoilForceUp`/`Back`, the aiming settings' bonus added after; `x` the class skill's (0.003 a level), `y` Recoil Control's (0 here, its skill out of the list).
- **Strength moves the inertia's limits too** (`PhysicalBase.UpdateWeightLimits`): `InertiaLimits + InertiaLimitsStep × SummaryLevel` (0.025 kg a level here) on x and y, besides the overweight limits times the carrying factor. The replay reads a weight off a recorded inertia as an untrained character's, so it keeps the base limits.
- **Covert Movement multiplies six step sounds** (`Player`): the footstep and its blend (`CovertMovementVolumeBySpeed`), the stop, the turn, the crawl and the drop to prone (`CovertMovementVolume`), the kit's noise (`CovertEquipmentNoise`); the soft surfaces are concrete, wood, soil, asphalt, gravel, puddle and thick wood (`ESurfaceSound` 0, 2, 3, 5, 7, 9, 12); how quietly one walks is worked again when the speed moves by a hundredth (`UpdateCovertEfficiency`).
- **Mag Drills' elite check is on a move** (`ItemController`, `MoveOperation`: a magazine whose new owner is the player's inventory), not a sweep: a magazine a shot unknows stays unknown until moved. An unload never takes the elite (`UnloadMagazineProcess(elite: false)`).



## Added 2026-10-23 (The SKILLS tab: 0.104.0)

- **The SKILLS page's scene** (`level44`, `/InventoryScreen/SkillsAndMasteringPanel`, 230 objects): the skills' panel (`BottomPanel/SkillsPanel`) is off in the scene and turned on by its screen; a skill's list view (`Detailed Skill Panel`, 34 objects, `SkillPanel` and `SkillIcon` on one object) and grid view (`Skill Icon`, 15) are templates under `Skill List` (`FlexibleGridLayoutGroup`: two columns, cell 824 x 100, spacing 25 and 34, padding left 6) and `Skill Grid` (`GridLayoutGroup`: cell 96, spacing 24, padding left 7 top 1); the content (`HorizontalLayoutGroup`, padding 20 top and bottom) in a `ScrollRectNoDrag` of step 63. `SkillPanel`'s fifteen pointers end with its two fills' pictures (11th, 12th), its `CanvasGroup` (13th) the view's own object; `SkillIcon`'s `_alphaBlockedSkill` is the icon's `IconContainer`, a `HoverTooltipArea` saying "Not available in alpha" (the install: "Not available in the current Beta version."). `BuffIcon`'s four end with its two grounds.
- **The skill tooltip** is the preloader's (`level49`, `UIContext/Skill Tooltip`): its box a `VerticalLayoutGroup` (padding 15, 15, 52, 15; spacing 13) under a `ContentSizeFitter`, its header ignored by the layout; the description in a row (padding 9, 9, 0, 10) of preferred width 400; `SkillTooltip`'s eighteen pointers hold the base, normal and elite fills (11th to 13th). It lists every buff (hidden ones too), each hidden by `BuffThumb.UpdateVisibility` (which also hides `HidenForPlayers`).
- **A trap: a panel's objects are drawn flat** (`draw_nodes`): a template hidden only by being off in the scene, once turned on so that its copies draw, must be set hidden itself, or what hangs from it shows (a buff's icon on every row at level 0, `skills_tab_s116.png` before the fix).
- **`trp_data::screen` already had a `GridGroup`** (`ScenePanel::grid`, the containers' grids); the SKILLS page's grids wrap it (`CellGrid`, with the flexible kind's widened cells) rather than read it twice.
- **The icons' tables are keyed by saved names** (`StaticIcons.BuffIdSprites`): `EBuffId.CharismaFenceRepPenaltyReduction` is saved as `CharismaFenceReducePenalty` (`JsonEnumName`), the only one of the buffs that differs; the words are keyed by the code's name (`buff.Id.ToString()`).
- **The buffs' figures from the settings** are all the code's own: `WeaponSkillRecoilBonusPerLevel` 0.003, `MountingErgonomicsBonusPerLevel` 0.2, `BipodErgonomicsBonusPerLevel` 0.5, `MalfRepairSpeedBonusPerLevel` 0.005, `RepairPointsCostReduction` 0.004, the vests' (0.006, 0.006, 0.008 light; 0.005, 0.004, 0.01 heavy), Charisma's (0.001 each; elite 0.1, 0.5, 1), Crafting's 0.75 and 0.75 (over 100), Hideout Management's 1 and 0.5 (over 100), Weapon Maintenance's `DurLossReducePerLevel` 0.005 and 0.01. Covert Movement's speed buff is `1 + 0.01` a level, so its line says "by 140%" at 40, as the code writes it.



## Added 2026-10-23 (The SKILLS page beside the game; the drag and the blink: 0.104.1)

- **A sliced sprite with no middle** (the skills' bars' `level_fill_bar`: 8 x 8, border 4 all round): the engine's sliced mesh gives its edges and middle the texture coordinates of the line between the borders, so they are drawn stretched from that line; the app's nine-slice left out pieces with no pixels of their own, which drew only the corners (the bars' dark band missing beside the user's capture). Now a pixel about the line stands for them (`health_view::nine_slice`).
- **A skill's effectiveness at a raid's start is fresh** (`Skill`'s constructor: `GetEffectiveness(0)` = the install's `SkillFreshEffectiveness` 1.3, as `SkillFreshPoints` is 1): every skill below elite shows its fill's arrow up in a raid and its tooltip "Leveling speed increased: 130%" (`(int)(1.3f * 100f)` is 130 in single precision). It falls by points earned (`UseEffectiveness`), and is 1 again after `SkillFatigueReset` (200 s) of none.
- **Why the inventory blinked on a click**: `click(&mut carried, ...)` reaches what is carried through Bevy's `ResMut`, which marks it changed though a plain click changes nothing; the page is then made anew, and new boxes have no `RelativeCursorPosition` until the next frame, so `light_related` found nothing under the mouse and every lit box (the thing pointed at, the orange of what goes with it) went dark for a frame. A trap for any system: a `&mut` taken of a resource to call a function that may change nothing still marks it.
- **The game's drag** (`DraggedItemView`): the dragged picture's pivot is its middle, put at the mouse on each drag (`SetInCenter`), not where it was taken; a grid places it by its corner (`ItemContext.SetPosition`'s second point, `CalculateItemLocation`); `R` turns it whatever its size; the panel under it is clamped to the grid and coloured by the operation (`GetHighlightColor`'s four static colours).
- **The list and grid buttons' `Tab`** (`probe skillspage`): `_normalVersion` and `_targetImage` are `Normal`, `_selectedVersion` `Selected`, as drawn; `option_button_on` is see-through and light, so the chosen button looks lighter only over a dark ground.



## Added 2026-10-23 (The inspect window beside the game; the loot's mark; a drag's cells and spinners cut: 0.104.2)

- **An item's picture may be its prefab's own** (`PreviewPivot.Icon.overrideIcon`; `ItemIconCreator` takes it over the model drawn): the armours' built-in soft inserts (`armor_plates/soft_armor_*.bundle`), whose prefabs draw nothing (one node, no renderer, `probe icon 6571b27a6d84a2b8b6007f92`). Their template's empty `Lower75Prefab` path is not their prefab; an item with an empty `Prefab` path would get `What`, a red question mark (`probe rsprite What`).
- **A slot's name is not its id** (`Slot.Name`): the first `EWeaponModType` (in its order, `mod_mount` first) the id holds; `mod_mount_004` is `mod_mount` ("MOUNT"), and its empty picture is `Slots/mod_mount`. Armour slots (`Soft_armor_front`, `Front_plate`) hold none and keep their ids, which the words have in lower case.
- **TextMeshPro's wrapping and overflow are read now** (`m_enableWordWrapping`, `m_overflowMode`, between the spacings and the linked texts): an item view's caption truncates (3), a part slot's name ellipses (1), a stash slot header's name ellipses; the app cuts them so (`health_view::fitted_words`) where their box is known (a part's caption, an empty part slot's name), not yet in `draw_nodes` at large, where many boxes are drawn at no size.
- **The inspect window's part is a grid view** (`ModSlotView.CreateItemViewKernel` → `ItemUiContext.CreateItemView`: for the inventory a `GridItemView`), so its count is small and it has no mastering and no durability bar; an equipment slot's is a `SlotItemView`.
- **Armour's durability on its view** (`GridItemView.UpdateInfo`): the sum of every `RepairableComponent` in it and its parts over their greatest, where it has an `ArmorComponent` or an `ArmorHolderComponent`, before a magazine's or a resource's.
- **A magazine's top round is its cartridges' last** (`Magazine.FirstRealAmmo` = `Cartridges.Last`), the round a weapon's figures take after the chamber's (`Weapon.CurrentAmmoTemplate`, then `defAmmo`).
- **Bevy cuts a turned UI picture wrongly at a clip's edge** (as if unturned: its quad trimmed and its texture stretched), the spinners' "distortion" at a scrolled panel's foot; an unturned one is cut right.
- **The LOOT header's icon** (`StashHeaderIcons_Off`, 20 by 20) and word lie in rows sized by what their children ask; the app's layout asks nothing of a picture or a word by itself, so they were at no size: `ask_by_content_under` the header's row.



## Added 2026-10-23 (The things' sounds and the vault's: 0.105.0)

- **The things' clips are no bundle's**: a door's, a container's and a trunk's `OpenSound`, `SqueakSound` and `ShutSound` are pointers from the prefab's script in a map's scene (the build's `level<N>`) into the `sharedassets<N>.assets` beside it, their samples in that file's `.resource`. A clip's name is plain in the `.assets` file, so `grep -l -a <name> sharedassets*.assets` says which scene holds it (`metal_door_1_open` in Factory's, `sharedassets2`, and the dorms', `sharedassets10`); the containers' names did not show so (`woodbox_open` and the rest were found by the scene's object names). `probe thingsounds` reads both.
- **A build file's script is read by a bundle's tree of the same class**: `rand_loot_container_twitch.bundle` keeps a `LootableContainer` with its tree (and `AudioClip`s), as `scontainer_crate.bundle` and `suitcase_plastic_lootable.bundle` do; the tree less its header (the object, the flag, the script, the name) reads the scene's script bytes from where `Scene::fields` begins. **A subclass lays its own fields after the base's in their declared order**, private fields without `[SerializeField]` left out: a `Door`'s `HitClip`, `BreachSound`, `_hitSoundRolloff` and `_breachSoundRolloff` follow `_openSoundRolloff` as two pointers and two ints.
- **The metal dorm door's squeak list is its open list** (`metal_door_1_open_01` to `_03` twice): its opening plays two open clips at once, its shutting one of them as the swing begins. Both its breach and its hit are `door_grate_hit`. The plastic door has clips of each kind. No door of the two scenes has a handle or a shut shift; the corpses, the ammunition box and the small suitcase have a shut shift of 1 s.
- **The Customs scenes hold no car trunk**: the `Trunk` scripts there are the safes' doors; `level14`, `20`, `18`, `21`, `6` and `9` hold fifteen things and no trunk.
- **The vault's marks are events of the body's states, not of its clips** (`probe bodystates` prints them with the state): `Vault` 0.10, 0.15, 0.30, 0.55; `Sprint_vault` 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.55; `Climb_on` grab 0.25, start 0.30, slide 0.35, end 0.50. **A state's played length is its blend tree's clip's at the height**, not the state's listed length: the vault's state played 1.35 s at a pace of 1.25 in the install test (listed 1.748), the recording's sprint vault 0.84 to 0.89 s at 1.2.
- **A bank's base volume goes in twice in the vault's path**: `BaseVaultingAudioController.PlaySound` multiplies the range's lerp by the bank's base, and `PlayAtPoint` by the bank's drawn volume (the base less its spread) again; the `courses2` recording has every level so, to the last figure (0.34200725 for `lerp(0.10, 0.60, 0.87) × 0.8 × 0.8`). The grab, slide and end banks keep no clip in their first list with blend points from 0: at a blend value of 0 the pair's first clip is missing and the second plays whole (`SimpleSource.Play`'s last branch), which the recorder writes with an empty first name (its 638 `"clip":""` entries are such pairs, the footsteps' among them).
- **The vault controller's obstacle surface is the obstacle's**: the recorded climb's grab and slide were `grap_climb_wood_thin5` and `slide_climb_wood5` on concrete ground, the top being thin wood.


## Added 2026-10-23 (Sounds of the ground and of the bots: 0.106.0)

- **Every pooled thing is loot** (`AssetsPoolStaticConfig.ComponentsToAdd`: `ObservedLootItem` on ammunition, weapons, magazines, items, parts, offline and online). The AKM's own bundle has no loot part (`probe scripts`), yet a death's `DropItemDead` finds one (`GetComponent<LootItem>`, the subclass) and hands it the weapon's body: so the dead's weapon clatters as a thrown one does. Its kept start (`_startPosition`) is never set for a weapon that was never thrown, so its first landing counts its height from the world's nought; the weapon begins at the palm, inside the falling body, and its first touch is that body at once (15.8 J in the app's run).
- **The audio groups' presets** are 17 `AudioGroupPreset` objects in `resources.assets` (`Audio/AudioGroupPresets/<name>`, `Weaponary` so spelt), read by layout (no type trees) to their last byte (`trp_data::audio_preset`, its test). Each has a `SoundRolloff` curve over a share of the source's farthest; `BetterAudio` sets every source of a group to custom rolloff with it. **This puts in doubt `footsteps.md`'s first-person falloff** (logarithmic from 1 m, read from the prefab `AudioSourceSpatialBuiltinEQReverbPrefab`): the group's factory gives the player's own step sources the `Character` curve over 70 m × the kind's multiplier (at 1.5 m about 0.77 against the logarithmic 0.67). Not changed in this step; to be read once more in the native engine's terms (custom rolloff's use of `minDistance`) and listened to.
- **A bot's steps are the player's rules seen from outside** (`Player.cs`, `PlayStepSound` and the rest, read through a helper agent's report and checked at the lines here): the bank's volume dropped from the footstep, the sprint and the stop; the distance as the blend; 90 m as the rolloffs' base; the kit at 1; `ObservedPlayerMovement` at 0 dB. SPT's `bots/types/*.json` give `BotSound` to 18 of 57 kinds (none to `assault`, `pmcbot`, `usec`, `bear`).
- **The dummies' step asks gathered untaken** since 0.95.0: the puppet's movement ran the rules on every step and nothing took them; a long run kept every one. Taken each frame now (`Dummies::movers`).



## Added 2026-10-23 (Optics read: the spec for scopes: 0.106.1)

- **The zoom's figures are the item's**: `ScopeZoomHandler` takes its range and speed from `items.json`'s `MinMaxFov` (`SightComponent.AdjustableOpticData`), not from the prefab's `ScopeSmoothCameraData` (the VUDU's prefab 24 to 3.2 against the item's 26.5 to 4.4167; the recorded Burris 29.5 to 7.375, the item's). The item's `Zooms` reaches only the HUD's panel.
- **A field of view times the magnification is a constant of the scope** (fixed and variable alike: 21.9 to 29.5 degrees): what `PIP` off's world zoom is worked from (`optics.md`).
- **There is no `TemplateCamera` on `OpticSight`**: one optic camera for all, `resources.assets`' `BaseOpticCamera` (a physical camera: 36 × 24 mm and 50 mm give its stored 26.99 degrees, overwritten per scope), 1024 pixels square, HDR.
- **Two-scope parts name a bone a scope** (`mod_aim_camera_001`, `_002`), and a sight on a part's top rail may take `_002` too; the app's `rig::SightNodes` matches only `mod_aim_camera`, so the G36 and the M60E6 aim at the wrong place now.
- **The app's aiming sensitivity is the weapon's `AimSensitivity` always**; the game's is the sight's for its scope and mode once a sight part is fitted, and a variable scope's moves with its zoom.
- `SightModVisualControllers` is added at run time (`GetOrAddComponent`); `ScopeMaskRenderer` is in no scope bundle.



## Added 2026-10-23 (Scopes, step 1: the sights as the game picks them: 0.107.0)

- **An item's `Name` is its template's id** (`Item.Name` is `Template.NameLocalizationKey`, the id with " Name"): the game's sorts of sights "by name" (`FindAimTransforms`, `GetSightStateAfterChangeMagnification`) are by template id, the walk of the items kept among equals.
- **Every sight's root is given `SightModVisualControllers` when it is made** (`WeaponModPoolObject.OnCreatePoolObject`, `GetOrAddComponent`): no scope bundle carries one, and every sight part switches its prefab's modes.
- **The HKV red dot's bundle has no `CollimatorSight`** (its `ScopePrefabCache`'s one mode names none): `probe sights 623063e994fc3f7b302a9696`.
- **`CycleScopes` sets the scope on the sight listed at the count, not on the item counted**, and counts the items in `AllSlots`' walk (an item's slots in its template's order, then each of those items' the same way: `CompoundItem.AllSlots`, `LinqExtensions.Flatten`); an index that counts past the list throws there, before the eye and the field of view are worked out again. On the M60E6 the rear sight's scope comes first, so the backup on top leaves the Specter on its scope 0.
- **The utilities' `IsZero` and `ApproxEquals` compare against `float.Epsilon`** (1.4e-45), so a zoom handler's edges are exact equality, and `Positive` is above nothing (`Utils.cs`).
- **`GetZoomInput` reads the wheel itself** (`Input.mouseScrollDelta.y`), not the commands: a wheel variant's way is taken as `!positiveAxis` (`positiveAxis` with the inverted up and down) and the turn must be that way, so `ScopeZoomIn`'s variant takes the wheel down and `ScopeZoomOut`'s the wheel up, and either gives the turn as it is: wheel up narrows.
- **The recording's zoom is whole notches on their own frames**: `courses2/4-sights-optic`'s widening from 7.375 to 20.875 is five notches of 0.9 (the Burris's `ZoomSensitivity`), the target moved by the growing velocity (13.5 in all); `bin/sights` fits every frame of it with no input, one notch or the magnification key.
- **The recording's build is gone from the profile**: the Burris is only in the profile's list of things seen (and its AR-P.E.P.R mount), so the mount it stood on is not known.



## Added 2026-10-23 (Scopes, step 2: the picture in the lens: 0.108.0)

- **A scope's lens is geometry, not a screen-space trick** (`CW FX/OpticSight`, disassembled: `local\optics_shaders_s119.md`): the picture is the lens disc scaled by `_Scales.x` (40) on a plane `_Shifts.x` (−0.15 m) ahead of the lens, the shadow a funnel `_Shifts.y` toward the eye; the lens's frame has its axis rescaled to the length of its x, so the weapon's squeeze along the view leaves the planes where they are. Every lens mesh read is one template (centre, 24 white vertices at `r_in`, 24 black at `r_in / 0.647`).
- **The `optic_camera` object's scale is nought** on the Tango (and the PSO): its world transform as a matrix has no turn to read, so the app composes its place and turn from the rig's local transforms.
- **A scope's front glass is often its body's second material** (the Tango's, the Specter's, the S&B's): a lens piece is a material slot, not an object.
- **The lens's shaders cannot be named cheaply**: a material's shader is in the deferred 378 MB `shaders` bundle, and a shader's name is after its subshaders in its tree; the four lens shaders each keep a property no other does (`_MaskTex2` with `_Scales`, `_MarkShift` with `_HDR`, `_SwitchToSightMultiplier`, `_Offset`), which `sight::lens_shader` goes by.
- **The HKV red dot has no `CollimatorSight`**: nothing turns its lens to the zeroing point; the T1 has one, which the game turns each aimed frame.
- **The Tango's reticle is a mesh in the optic camera's picture** (`Custom/Crossfair`, in the scope's own bundle): 3,028 vertices, the first part's 2,756 triangles, black and red vertex colours drawn in order, one blend shape (`tango6t_mark1`); the Burris has none (its reticle is its lens's texture). A mesh's colours are its channel 3; its blend shapes `m_Shapes` (`unity::extract`).
- **The optic picture's chromatic aberration** is `Hidden/ChromaticAberration`'s pass 1 (`globalgamemanagers.assets` 243), on every optic (0.00325 on the Tango and the Burris, 0.007 on the Specter and the G36's 3x).
- **The game draws the live lens twice**: again after the main camera's anti-aliasing, unjittered (`SSAAPropagator`, in `Unity.Postprocessing.Runtime`, decompiled to `local\decomp\pp_s119`).



## Added 2026-10-23 (Scopes: the game's picture beside ours; three recordings: 0.108.1)

- **The recordings keep the sights' state as the items left it**: `courses4`'s Specter starts in mode 1 (1x), the Monstrum in mode 1 (its `Zooms` 2): a replay of them must start the sights from the recording's `sight` fields, not from mode 0.
- **A canted sight turns the weapon about its bore**: the M4A1 aimed through the PK-06 on its 45 degree mount has its weapon bone turned by (0.0017, −0.382, 0.0004, 0.924), 45 degrees about the bone's y (the barrel), the main camera at the setting less 15 (59).



## Added 2026-10-23 (Scopes: the world's effects in the scope's picture: 0.109.0)

- **The gun smoke's trail is the main camera's alone**: `MuzzleSmoke.OnRenderObject` draws (`GL` quads) only when `Camera.current` is `CameraManager.Instance.Camera`, the main one; the optic camera never draws it. Its points are worked once a frame from the camera `LateUpdateMuzzleEffectsValues` is given.
- **The heat haze is the main camera's distortion's alone**: `DistortRenderer.Init` keeps the renderer's material for `DistortCameraFX` and gives the renderer `Hidden/ClipAll`, so no camera's own pass draws it; `DistortCameraFX` (on `Cam2`, not on `BaseOpticCamera`) draws the list into its values with `DrawRenderer` before its image effects, each renderer tested against its own camera's frustum.
- **Every effect's object is on the world's layer**: the effects' bundle's 1,006 game objects are on layer 0 but four (`Effect Smoke Volume`'s `Extrusion` colliders, on 13, `Triggers`); `MuzzleJet` makes the jets' mesh as a new object (layer 0, never set). The optic camera's mask (`0xFDF7FF17`) takes 0, so it draws the jets, the fumes, the sparks (`SimpleSparksRenderer`, a `MeshRenderer`) and every hit's and blast's system. (`probe bundle-layers [bundle]`, new: a bundle's game objects counted by layer, with names.)
- **The frame was left linear for some effects** (the app's, found reading `gamma_ui`): the encode to the game's numbers ran only while a blur, blood, a contusion's, a flash, a trail or PRISM was extracted, though the fumes', sparks', haze's and explosions' specs say they are drawn on the encoded frame. With the lighting switches off, a hit's dust or a blast with no flash or trail in the air was so drawn in linear light; mended (0.109.0). **Still so**: the ambient occlusion (`hbao_view`, "on the frame in the game's numbers") is not in the condition either, so with the lighting off it is put on the encoded frame only while one of those effects shows, and on the linear one otherwise: its darkening changes as a shot is fired. Mended in 0.109.1, the user's choice (next section).
- **Bevy orders nothing between its camera update and a system after the transforms**: `camera_system` (`CameraUpdateSystems`, `PostUpdate`) makes `clip_from_view` from the projection; a system only `.after(TransformSystems::Propagate)` may run before it and take the last frame's. The effects' systems now run after both (the optic camera's field of view moves as a scope zooms).



## Added 2026-10-23 (The ambient occlusion steady, in the game's numbers: 0.109.1)

- **The occlusion-alone display shares the frame's encoding**: `--ssao-display ao` writes the occlusion as the frame's colour, so on the linear frame it shows the occlusion's sRGB curve and on the encoded one the figure itself. A capture with it cannot tell which way the composite multiplied; set the view with the occlusion against the view without it, at the same pixels, before and after (`local\ao_ratio_s120.ps1`): at rest 0.892 then 0.759, as the game's numbers predict (0.892^2.2 = 0.778).
- **The world's passes now keep the frame encoded on every frame they are on** (the occlusion, the sky's scattering, the clouds): two full-screen passes more a frame with the occlusion on and the lighting off, beside the occlusion's own four.



## Added 2026-10-23 (Scopes: a canted sight's turn; the arms on the player's clock: 0.110.0)

- **The weapon motion's time on a frame with no physics step is the player's stopwatch**: `Player.ComplexUpdate` keeps the arms' update time (`_armsTime`), and `ProcessEffectors((_nFixedFrames > 0) ? _fixedTime : _armsTime, ...)` hands it to both halves of the late update (`ApplyTransformations`, `ApplyCameraTransformations`). In `courses4`'s `01-aim` the canted turn's share on such frames solved to 0.0961 where `8 × 1.335 × 0.00833` is 0.0891: a time of 9 ms, the frame's `clock_dt`. The field of view eases by the engine's own time (`SetFovCoroutine`, `Time.deltaTime`).
- **The aim reaches the weapon's motion on its frame**: `ProceduralWeaponAnimation.IsAiming`'s setter is called by the controller as it changes its aim; the simulation took it from the next physics step's input, a frame late on a frame with none (`03-fire-aimed`'s aim at 0.5 s: the game −4.24 degrees on that frame, the simulation nothing).
- **Solving an eased quaternion's share**: from the turn's angles before and after, `t = (s − k c) / (s + k − k c)` with `s`, `c` the sine and cosine of half the angle before and `k` the tangent of half the angle after (a turn toward none); the shares of a recording's frames then tell the time and the rate it ran on (`local\twist_s120.pl` gives the angles).
- **A course's scripts may each carry their own build**: `courses4`'s M4A1 had its 100-round drum in `01-aim` to `04-reload` and a 30-round magazine after (3.375 kg, ergonomics 70.81, aiming speed 2.049 against 4.368, 47.53, 1.335). `replay` takes the first trace's header for the whole folder: a script with another build is replayed from a folder of its own.
- **The 45 degree offset mount** (`5649a2464bdc2d91118b45a8`) turns what is on it 45 degrees whether it sits on the top rail or a side slot: the database's `m4_hera_arms`, `m4_block_2_customized` and the others with it on `mod_scope` are canted builds too.
- **The PK-06 draws an opaque window and no dot** (seen 2026-10-23, the app's): its three modes' lens pieces read as collimators (`probe sights`, `mark_shift (0, −150, 0)`, `hdr 3`), yet the window shows a grey disc, canted or on top; another canted red dot (`5a33b2c9c4a282000c5a9511`) draws its dot through a clear window. Not looked into (`backlog.md`).



## Added 2026-10-23 (The gun smoke ages at the game's frame rate: 0.110.1)

- **The recorder keeps the particle systems, not the gun smoke's trail**: a fired script's `.world.jsonl` lists `GameWorld/Effects/...` systems (the fumes' `Effect Muzzle Smoke`, the hits' `Drop Concrete`, `Effect Dust Splash` and the rest) with every particle emitted and followed; `MuzzleSmoke` is a script's own ribbon (`GL` quads) and is in none. The fumes' lifetimes, by their emits: 0.14, 0.42 to 0.6, and 5 s.
- **A capture run presents with vsync** at the screen's rate (165 here), where the frame limit is not held (`frame_limit::wanted`): `--present off` draws as fast as it can (241), `--present off --fps-limit 60` at 60.



## Added 2026-10-23 (A red dot's window: the PK-06 seen through: 0.110.2)

- **A red dot's window can be a submesh of its body**: the PK-06's `scope_all_belomo_pk_06_LOD0` mesh has two submeshes, the body (`p0/Reflective/Bumped Specular SMap`) and the window (`EFT/CollimatorGlass`, its queue 3000, no picture, white). The four lens shaders the reader knew did not take it, and the app draws every unknown material opaque from `_MainTex` and `_Color`: a grey disc in front of the collimator's dot.
- **The glass shaders' properties** (`probe shaders <name>` with `TRP_PROBE_FIGURES`): `EFT/CollimatorGlass` `_Color _Cube _SpecColor _ReflectColor _GlobalReflectionStrength _Specular _FresPow _FresAdd`; `EFT/Glass` the same and `_MainTex`; `Custom/OpticGlass` adds `_SwitchToSightMultiplier`, `_NormalHideness`, `_MainTex`, `_SpecTex`. A material keeps a former shader's figures (the PK-06's window has `_NormalHideness`, `_SpecPower`), so a test by properties looks for those a shader has, and for a picture it lacks.



## Added 2026-10-23 (Scopes with PIP off: 0.111.0)


- **Where the eye stands behind a scope's lens**: with the sights up, the camera is 14 to 19 cm behind the live lens along its axis in the rig root's frame (the cut's debug line, `make_cut`; the Tango's eyepiece lens is at the back, the Specter's nearer the front). A guess of 5 cm (the eyepiece's eye relief) put the cut's cone on the wrong things and left a dark disc on the Tango.
- **The glass's rim, not its picture ring, bounds what the eye sees through it**: `OpticLens::outer_radius` times the lens node's scale (the Specter's about 1.8 cm). With the inner ring and a triangle cut only when all its corners were seen, a V-shaped piece of the Specter's inside stayed in view; with the rim and any corner seen, nothing does.
- **A mesh drawn once has no corners left in the main world** (Bevy 0.18): a mesh whose `asset_usage` has dropped `MAIN_WORLD` is emptied at extraction, and `Mesh::attribute` then panics ("Mesh has been extracted to RenderWorld"). `try_attribute_option` returns an error instead. A cut made later must keep a copy of the corners from the frame the mesh appears (`keep_sources`, PostUpdate).
- **How other games do scopes without a second picture** (searched 2026-10-23, the user's question): Arma 3's 3D scopes zoom the world and cut a tunnel through each scope's model from the aiming point, by hand, model by model; GAMMA's 3D scopes come in packs whose models are made or edited for them, with a script that hides the lens. Neither has a rule that takes a game's own scopes as they are; the cut from the eye (`optics.md`, "With `PIP` off") is that rule, worked from each lens's figures.

## Added 2026-10-24 (Reloads through a change of model, the round in the chamber, the magazines' pictures at work: 0.116.0)

- **A rig changed under a reload**: the logic of a magazine's reload idles from `MagIn` (`reload_over`) while the animation plays on to `IdleStart`; anything that waits for `can_move_magazine` alone lands mid-clip. `hands_busy` stays true until the animation's rest. The animator's running state (`Animator`'s values, machines, weights) is apart from the bones it poses (`pose`, `scales`, bound to the build's tree at load), so the same controller on another build takes it over (`take_state`).
- **The magazines' points** (`local\mag_scan_s123k.txt`, 225 magazines): 193 with their `patron_` points as direct children, 26 with none (tubes, cylinders, the speedloader, the NSV belt, AGS, MSGL), 6 deeper (the AA-12 drum under an axis, still found breadth first; five belts, whose visual is not built). The PMAG Gen M3 40 (`544a378f4bdc2d30388b4567`) has `patron_001` to `003` and `VisibleAmmoRangesString` "1-3", as the PMAG 30 and the MK16 30.
- **The chamber's point**: `patron_in_weapon`, a child of the weapon bone, empty: the AKM's at (0.00007, −0.45547, 0.00035), the SCAR-L's at (0.00002, −0.43287, 0.00436); the check-chamber clips track it. `SetPatronInWeaponVisible` (`FirearmsAnimator`) is called by nothing in the decompiled code, and the SCAR's animator has no mark for it.
- **`IconsHash.GetItemHash`** for a magazine: its template with `23 + GetMaxVisibleAmmo() << 2`, and for each stack its address's hash (the container's ID and the parent's template) XOR `2879 × n`, times the stack's template's hash: no count. 200 magazines are `1-3`; the UMP's `1-25`, the PMAG window's `1-1;18-30` and the PM's `1-1;4-8` change on every round over a stretch. `ItemIconCreator.GetItemIcon` puts a new holder into its memory index as soon as it is asked, so two views share it while it is still empty.
- **Captures run fast**: about 165 drawn frames a second here; a clip's place on a frame differs with the resolution and the run. `--log-frames <file>` gives each frame's clip and its time in it.




## Added 2026-10-24 (A reload's change of model without a stutter: 0.116.1)

- **The drawing runs behind the simulation** on a short queue of stepped frames (`Controller::stepped`, each with the rig's whole pose); a rig changed for another overwrote every queued pose (`rig_changed`), right at rest and a jump and a stand mid-clip. Each `WeaponRig` knows its bones' paths only through `by_hash` (path hash to entity) beside `bones` (entities in the pose's order): inverted, it matches two builds' bones by path.
- **What of `Hands` is the build's and what runs**: the build's are its settings, its springs' and sway's constants (`zero`, `input_intensity`, `return_speed`, `damping`, `acceleration_max`; `frequency`, `damping`), the strengths from the weight (`sway_strength`, `displacement_strength`, `aim_sway_strength`), `aiming_speed`, `step_frequency`, the hold (`closer`, `folded`), the sights' line and muzzle read off the rig, the mask and switches; everything else runs (`Hands::carry_on_from`).




## Added 2026-10-24 (The eye on the sight's line in automatic fire; a shot felt after the frame's pose: 0.116.2)



- **The camera's target in automatic fire** (`ProceduralWeaponAnimation`, the method that sets `_vCameraTarget`, around `CheckAutoFire`): while aiming, with `_currentAimingPlane` set, the target is the current scope's bone in `WeaponRoot.parent`'s space; then, if the firearm controller's `autoFireOn`, `direction = CurrentScope.Bone.forward * -1` (or `.up * -1` when the bone's name is exactly `aim_camera`), turned into `WeaponRoot.parent`'s space and normalised, and the target moves by it times `num3 = (the aim plane's z + _fovCompensatoryDistance - TurnAway.Position.y + _cameraShiftToLineOfSight.x) - target.z`; otherwise only its z is set to that sum. `_cameraShiftToLineOfSight.y` is added to y after either, then the public `vector` (zero; nothing sets it). `autoFireOn` is set in the automatic fire operation's `Start` and cleared at its end (`Player.cs`: to idle, to a planned action, to a malfunction). Who calls it: `LateTransformations` each aimed frame. The recording agrees: with the game's recoil on the replay, the camera's target backed out of the recorded camera (`c_prev + (c - c_prev) / (dt * aiming speed * camera_smooth)`) lay 4.2 mm under the sim's (then) aim bone over the burst, about a millimetre a degree of climb, which is the eye's 7 cm behind the Specter's aim bone times the climb.
- **A shot is felt after the frame's pose**: `Player.LateUpdate` runs `ProceduralWeaponAnimation.LateTransformations` and then `HandsController.ManualLateUpdate`; the firearm controller's (`Player.FirearmController.ManualLateUpdate`) calls `ProceduralWeaponAnimation.Shoot` when its flag for a shot made in the update is set. `Shoot` drops `CameraSmoothBlender` and calls `NewRecoilShotEffect.AddRecoilForce`, which pushes the processes' forces (felt at the next `FixedUpdate`) and calls `WeaponRecoil.OnShoot` (each curve's clock and value to zero at once). So the frame's pose has the curves as they ran: course 6's M60E6 shows the roll of the weapon (1.0 to 1.4 degrees, its curve's) and of the camera on every shot's frame, gone the frame after; the recorder, run later in the frame, already logs the shot's index and the dropped rate on it.
- **What the burst does to the eye in the game**: through course 6's M60E6 held fire (19 rounds, aimed, the Specter at 4x) the game's eye stays within -0.6 to +5.3 mm of its rest height against the weapon and moves -11 to +20 mm along the barrel (the weapon's push and the camera's follow), and the camera's line turns up to 2.1 degrees from the barrel. The Specter's shadow funnel (inner disc 5.5 mm across at 7 cm toward the eye, the eye about 9 cm behind the lens) slides about a sixth of the window a millimetre off the axis: the game's own picture moves under a burst too, by less than the simulation's had.

## Added 2026-10-24 (Scopes with PIP off: the lens black until the aim settles, its vignette and a black rim: 0.117.0)

- **Where the soft circle reaches** (from the lens figures read in s119, `local\optics_shaders_s119.md`): through the window the picture's uv runs to about 0.38 at the rim on the Specter at its settled eye (14 cm from its 1x lens: the cut's debug line), so its `mask1` (1 to uv 0.27, 0.58 at 0.42) darkens the outer part of the glass; `MaskScope` (1 to 0.42) does not reach the visible glass on the Tango, the G36's 3x or the AUG's 1.5x. On the Specter the funnel's inner disc is met only within about 11 mm of the axis at that eye; past it the annulus (its mask under 0.06), so most of the dark edge with `PIP` on there is the shadow's and the soft circle's together, and the tube's dark lip drawn round the lens.
- **The cut's sliver**: `see_through` drops a triangle when any corner is seen through the glass, so the tube's long inner walls go whole and the world behind them shows in a thin band round the lens's 24-sided disc (2.5% of the glass's radius on the Specter at 1x, 1.3% on the Tango): the outer body's inside faces are culled. The black rim covers it; a tighter cut (clipping triangles at the cone) would be the exact fix.
- **The cut's cost**: on the M60E6 86,157 corners tested and 8 to 10 meshes rebuilt in 0.9 to 1.4 ms (opt-level 1), made three times in one aim as the eye drifted past `RECUT`.



## Added 2026-10-24 (Face shields: the mask, the hinge on the shield, the icon: 0.118.0)

- **An icon's key is every part and each switch** (`IconsHash.GetAllItemHashes`, `HashForItem`): each thing below the one pictured, its slots' and its grids', by its template and place, stopping at one that hides what is in it, each with its switch, its fold and a magazine's shown rounds. The model is a copy with all its parts (`CloneVisibleItem`, `CreateItemAsync`: `AssembleMods` for any `ContainerCollection` but a cylinder magazine, then every `IDress` on the root set up with its item, not animated). A flip refreshes the switched thing's picture and its parents' up the slots (`ToggleResult.RaiseEvents`); an add or a removal below a box's thing refreshes that box (`GridItemView.CheckSlot`).
- **Six face shields carry their own hinge** (`probe scripts` on each bundle: `local\shield_scripts_s126.txt`, `shield_scripts2_s126.txt`; who takes which: `local\shield_helmets_s126.txt`, by `local\shield_helmets_s126.pl` over the database): a `HelmetDress` and its `HysteresisFilter` on the FAST's visor and handgun shield, the EXFIL's shields, the Kiver-M's, the Vulkan-5's and the TOR-2's, each turning an object `axis` of its own model; on the ZSh-1-2M, the Altyn, the Rys-T, the Maska-1SCh, the K1C and the LShZ-2DTM the helmet carries it. The LShZ carries one and takes the FAST's visor, which carries another: both turn.
- **The face shields' masks** (`probe with FaceShieldComponent`, `probe with FaceShieldMask`, `local\faceshields2_s126.txt`, `faceshields_s126.txt`): `FaceShieldMask` is on every armoured thing; on the eighteen face shields it is `Wide` for the Altyn's, the Rys-T's and the three welding masks, `Narrow` for the two Maska-1SCh shields, `NoMask` for the rest (the ZSh-1-2M's among them). `NoMask` is not "no effect": the scratches, blur and distortion are drawn, without a frame.
- **The camera's `VisorEffect`** (`Cam2`, 35224; the whole reading: `local\visor_shader_s126.md`): `Intensity` 0 (the camera's `HysteresisFilter`, 35076, sets it), `ScratcesIntensity` 0.45, `MinLightValue` 0.6 with `IsAffectedByLight` off (no light is ever measured: the scratches' colour is 0.6 of their picture's), a blur of 1.5 over 2 rounds at half size, `DistortIntensity` 0.0075, `VisorMasks` Wide `VisorMask`, Narrow `VisorMask_maska_sch`, NoMask none. The camera's filter: down after 0.6017 s over 0.2 s, up likewise. It sits after `SSAOMask` and before the flash's effect on the camera (`GrenadeFlashScreenEffect`), whose `_BlindnessCoef` it alone reads and which is not put back when the flash ends. `HitSwing` is never started; `VisorPoint` (a 0.2 mm white quad by the eye) is read by nothing. The hideout hides the mask out of first person (`HideoutPlayerOwner`).
- **The glass is dithered, not blended** (`local\visor_glass_s126.md`): `Transparent/DepthZwriteDithered` in the opaque queue, a checkerboard thrown away where its alpha is under a half, and the camera's `Undithering` averaging each marked pixel with the one a row over: a fixed half seen through.
- **A pass that masks the alpha leaves a capture see-through**: the game's mask multiplies all four channels; with the frame's alpha 0 outside the Maska's slit the screenshot's pixels were (0, 0, 0, 0), shown white by a viewer. The app's passes keep the frame's alpha.

Blind fire is a hold (Alt+W over, Alt+S to the side; `BlindShootEnd` on release), accepted from `Idle` only; the left shoulder is `Mouse4`, press.
