## Added 2026-10-25 (Tactical devices: lights, lasers, the head light: 0.120.0)



- **BepInEx's log falls silent at the main menu** in this SPT 4.1.6 set-up (the run of 2026-10-24, both launches): `LogOutput.log` and the console's lines in `Player.log` stop at the bot-difficulty request (line ~586 of an 800,000-line `Player.log`), and the game's own `application.log` at the raid's start, so nothing a plugin logs in a raid is seen. A patch that fails is logged before the recorder's "loaded" line and would be seen; a part left out in a raid would not. Recorder 0.13.1 writes its warnings to `oracle-log.txt` beside it too; until then, a trace is checked for a field that stops early (`local\fields_s128.pl`).
- **The weapon in hand is a scene root of its own** (`HandsController.ControllerGameObject`, placed at the ribcage every frame; the cutscene's code parents it to the player and back to nothing): `GetComponentsInChildren` on the player finds a slung weapon's devices and the head's, never the hands'. Recorder 0.13.0's `lights`, `tactical_models` and `lasers` missed every device of the weapon in hand for this; 0.13.1 looks under the hands' object too.
- **A light is drawn at its distance fade's own figure**: every device's `CullingLightObject` has `_useLightIntensityFromEditor` set, which takes `_maxLightIntensity` over the `Light`'s own (the WF-501B's both 1, their lights 1.2 and 1; the Klesch-2P's both 1.2, its lights 1.4 and 1.2): recorded, the WF-501B's `light_001` at 1 on every lit frame, at nothing on the one frame after it first wakes.
- **A device's switch asked in automatic fire is lost** (course 7, the M60E6's `05-refusals`): the trigger is taken at the press, the transition into `MOD SWITCH` begins as the burst ends and is dropped the next frame, as the fire operation gives way to idling and resets two triggers; no switch, no sound. Our animator does the same driven through the burst.
- **The light shaft's component is a sign of the option**: `VolumetricLight` destroys itself as its object wakes where the graphics option is off, so a recorded light shows it (`lights`' last figure) only until first lit: the user's game has it off.
- **The recorder's `eye` is in the player's own axes** (`root.InverseTransformPoint`), not the world's from the origin as `lights` are: turned by the look's yaw, the camera of course 7's `04-head-light` puts the WF-501B's light where the game's rule does to 1.5 mm.
- **The head light's placement moves only lights that are on**, each along its own turn as last placed (`HeadLightsValidator.Update` checks `activeInHierarchy`): a light lit for the first time moves along its model's turn on that frame (0.37 m off the rule there, once).
- **`weapon_flashlight_cookie`'s red and alpha agree** (BC7, not decoded here: the game's shading drawn with each gave the same capture), so Bevy's light textures, which read red, and the engine's spot cookie, its alpha, light the same.
- **The beam's 3D noise** (`r2`, a `Texture3D` of 64³, `m_Format` 54, a graphics format: one byte a texel) averages 0.48, its cube 0.30; the beam's soft spot (`Untitled 1`) falls from 1 in its middle to 0.38 an eighth of the way out.
- **Bevy picks a light's shadow casters by its render layers** (`check_*_light_mesh_visibility`) and lights every mesh of a camera's view whatever its layers: one's own weapon is left out of its devices' shadows by a layer of its own that the cameras and the sun see and the devices' lights do not (`weapon::HANDS_LAYER`).
- **In the user's first launch of the run the M4A1 was in the second primary slot** and the SPEAR in the first; the holster was empty in both launches, so course 2's pistol draws and course 7's draw of the secondary did nothing.

## Added 2026-10-25 (The range's time of day: 0.121.0)

- **Two exposures, one set of lights.** With `CUSTOMS LIGHT` on the camera is at EV100 0 (`lighting::EXPOSURE_EV100`), and every light the app works out from the game's figures (the sun, the ambient, the devices', the explosion's) is put in Bevy's units for that exposure (`EXPOSURE_SCALE`, 1.2). With it off the camera keeps Bevy's own EV100 9.7 (`Exposure::EV100_BLENDER`) and the range's own sun of 12,000 lux, so a light worked out for EV100 0 comes 2^9.7, about 830, times too dim: a weapon's light barely showed even at night (0.120.0). The devices' lights now take the difference (`tactical_view::exposure_gain`). **Not yet**: the explosion's lights (`explosion_view::place_lights`) are worked out the same way, and are presumably as dim with `CUSTOMS LIGHT` off; not looked at.
- **Customs' night by the game's rules is bright for a fixed exposure.** Carried by Customs' time of day's own ratios, the range's own sun at midnight was about 4,000 lux (Customs' moonlight against its 09:37 light), the ambient about 64 (both twenty times the faded figures below): a grey day. The game's camera adapts (PRISM, the environment manager's outdoor middle grey); the range's own does not. Hence the night's fade by `LerpValue` (`range_day::NIGHT_SHARE`, an `ESTIMATE`).
- **The range with `CUSTOMS LIGHT` too dark** (the user, 2026-10-25: "our range is set up in a way that makes everything way too dark due to exposure"): PRISM holds the frame's kept brightness at the middle grey (0.23, at most 0.9 of exposure: `lighting.md`, "The range's colours for the camera"), so a frame whose brightness is held up by the sky and the bright floor takes everything else down. Not looked into further this session.

## Added 2026-10-25 (Blind fire: 0.123.0)

- **The first-person camera hangs in `CameraContainer`, not from the ribcage.** The player prefab has it beside `Base HumanRibcage` on `Base HumanSpine3`, at the ribcage's rest place and turn, its child `Cam` the camera (`probe tree assets/content/commonprefabs/playersuperior.bundle`, `local\player_tree_s130.txt`; `ProceduralWeaponAnimation.CameraContainer`, `MovementContext` turning it to the view). No clip animates it. Wherever a clip moves the ribcage on its spine the two part: blind fire's layers by its float do (the recorded eye less the ribcage, MDR's `01` at full float: (-0.055, 0.124, 0.097) against the camera's own (0.037, 0.039, 0.053)). Every other recording's eye is the ribcage's (the regression's eye did not move), so only blind fire showed it. `Body::camera_container`, `Movement::camera_frame` (the camera's push), the app's `Shown::camera_apart`.
- **`SmoothLerpAnimCurve`**, the state scripts' own curve, was not read before (`MovementBehaviour::curve`): only blind fire's four states have keys. In 0 to 0.15, straight to 1 at 0.65; out 1 to 0.15, a cubic (slope -2.222) to 0 at 0.6 (`local\blind_curves_s130.txt`).
- **The prefab's blind-fire figures differ from the code's defaults**: the blender's speed 3 (code 5), `SideFireCamera` (-2, -15, 0) (code (-2, -18, 0)), `BlindFireOffset` z 0.16 (code 0.15), `BlindFireRotation` y -40 (code -20) (`probe script ... ProceduralWeaponAnimation`, `local\pwa_player_s130.txt`). The two rotations and offsets reach nothing but the overlap's line: they are multiplied by vectors only ever zero.
- **A press in a refusing state ends blind fire**: the base `MovementState.BlindFire` passes 0 to the gate, not nothing; only the approach's ignores it. The states that take it are standing's and those built on it that do not override it: the step aside, going down to lie, getting up (`IdlePlayerState`); lying, rolled and crawling pass 0.
- **Shared state, `ReEnter` only**: between blind fire's four animator states the movement state is one object, so `ProcessStateEnter` calls `ReEnter` (no `Exit`, no `Enter`); in a blend both states call `OnStateMove` and the one being entered is last (recorded: on the exit's way back in the float stayed 0 until the new state passed 0.15).
- **`SpeedLimiter.DefaultSpeedLimits`** has no line for blind fire (the general cap) and gives `BreachDoor` the standing cap; `Movement::hard_speed_cap` gives the kick the walking one (`State::Breach`). Not changed here: noted for the kick's next look.
- **The M60E6's reload in courses4's `04-reload` was never made by the game**: no spare box within reach, the key found none (`ReloadMagNotFound`). The replay reloaded a full box regardless; it now does nothing where the recorded equipment has no magazine within reach (`r17`).

## Added 2026-10-25 (Weapon mounting and the bipod: 0.125.0)

- **Course 8 of `courses6` holds no mount held** (`commands`, `calls`): both weapons' first `05-mount` answer `WeaponMountingComponent.NoPointFound` three frames after each `cmd:WeaponMounting`; the M60E6's second (`05-mount-233241`) has `OnFoundPoint`, `TrySetPlayerMountingPointData`, `CheckIfTargetPosReachable`, `EnterMountedState`, `ExitMountedState`, `ExitMountedStateHandler(0)` on one frame, twice: the approach's first step leaves at once for a character that cannot walk (`TryApproachTargetPosition`: `!CanWalk` → `ExitMountedState`), the walk's lock (`ObstacleCollision`) being the likely cause (it unlocks only by a turn of the movement direction or of the view from the body, and a character that walked into the sill and stood keeps it). `06-bipod` deploys and folds the M60E6's bipod lying down without mounting; the MDR has no bipod. The `mount` field is written only while mounted or with the bipod out.
- **The search's frames are its batches of rays**: one frame a batch (`RaycastCommand.ScheduleBatch`, then `yield return null` until done): found ahead 2 frames after the key, nothing 3 (recorded both ways). The prone search is answered at once.
- **The pivot**: `MountingPosition` is nought on the M700, the MDR and the M60E6, and the fallback (the left hand's marker's place plus the moved bone's plus (0.05, -0.18, 0.025)) gives the M700 |y| 0.68777, whose yaw limit ±(atan(0.25/0.68777) - 1) = 18.976 is the recording's to 5 places. The M60E6's: (-0.0063, -0.6825, -0.0933); its bipod's `bipod_ground`: (-0.0005, -0.7088, -0.2129).
- **Globals override the classes' defaults**: `MaxVerticalMountAngle` 23 (20), `SensitivityMultiplier` 1 (0.75), `MountingCameraSpeed` 250 (24), `PointHorizontalMountOffset` 0.02 (0.01), `SecondCheckVerticalGridOffset` 0.05 (0.02), `SecondCheckVerticalGridSize` 0.05 (0.15), `VerticalGridSize` 1 (0.5), `MaxFramesForRaycast` 3 (2).
- **While mounted the smoothed lean takes no value** (`MovementContext.SmoothedTilt`'s setter does nothing with `_inMountedState`), so the body's `Tilt` is set only by a side mount, the slow lean's check (`TiltDiff` under 1) reads the frozen value, and the weapon's lean (`PWA.SmoothedTilt`) is the mount's camera lean (0 forward).
- **A frame's motion is scheduled** (`MovementContext.ApplyMotion`: the last asked for in a frame is applied in `AnimatorStatesLateUpdate`), so a mount's approach replaces the standing state's carry-over of the same frame.
- **The bipod's open and close sounds are the hands' clips' events** (`BipodAudioController` consumes the `FirearmsAnimator`'s events): the M60E6's hands have no bipod clips, and its toggle is silent (recorded: no source from 2.50 to 3.40 s or 12.00 to 12.90 s). Its prefab has its own animator for the legs (four clips) and a `BipodAudioController` whose surface sound follows any forward mount's approach (not built).
- **The weapon's mount sound plays at the aiming sound's volume** (`VolumeMultByWeaponErgonomic` is `CalculateAimingVolume`), the approach's time after the mount by `WaitForSeconds` (the mount's frame not counted).
- **The body's weapon bone stands 1.41 m over the feet standing** in our body, so a standing search's band (its height ±0.5 m, held to 0.81 to 1.4 m raised by the pivot's height) stops at 0.91 m: the range's 0.9 m window sill is refused standing and taken crouched, by the game's rule.



## Added 2026-10-25 (The mount icon, the chambered round taken out, recorder 0.14.0, the key's table: 0.126.0)

- **The chambered round's move from the inventory is the hands'** (`Idling.Execute`): a move whose thing is a chamber's round starts `DischargeOperation` (refused with a detachable magazine in, or a magazine of its own not empty), and the inventory's move is its callback, made at the discharge clip's `MagHide` (`OnMagPuttedToRig`). The AKM's `DISCHARGE` is `akm_ammo_out`, 2.600 s, its `MagHide` at 0.695 s, entered on `Discharge` from `IN INVENTORY` (0.25 s) and `IDLE` (`local\akm_discharge_states_s133.txt`; the whole controller's states `local\akm_states_all_s133.txt`, which also lists the malfunction layer's `JAM`, `MISFIRE`, `FEED`, `SOFT_SLIDE`, `HARD_SLIDE`, their `_REPAIR` states and `JAM_LOOK`, for the malfunctions' step).
- **The mount icon flashes for a refused point too**: `WeaponMountingComponent.OnFoundPoint` calls `ClearData` then `NoPointFound` when `TrySetPlayerMountingPointData` refuses, so `OnNothingFound` fires for every refusal but a door being worked under the point (which returns silently). `IsInMountedState` is `MountPointData != null`: set by `SetData` at the mount, cleared by `ClearData` at the exit's end, each raising `OnMountedStateChanged`.

The sim's animator had played every state forward (`abs` of the speed); it now samples such a state's clip mirrored, its events met mirrored too. With one placement (the left shoulder's `Shifted`) the arms' figures came closer to the game's in 13 to 22 scenarios each (`after161` against `after159`).

## Added 2026-10-25 (Durability, malfunctions and overheating: 0.128.0)

- **The game's notices and the malfunction glow live in the preloader's scene** (`level49`, `UI/PreloaderUIScene.unity`), not the battle screen's: `PreloaderUI` holds `NotifierView` (`Preloader UI/BottomPanel/Content/UpperPart/Notifier`, its default template `Content/Notification`) and `_malfunctionGlow` (`Preloader UI/MalfunctionGlow`); `Player` reaches the glow through `PreloaderUI.Instance.MalfunctionGlow`. A notice not shown at once waits in a queue, one shown at a time (`OnNotificationHide`); duplicates are not dropped (reference equality).
- **`BattleUIMalfunctionGlow` is a `SerializedMonoBehaviour`** (`UIElement`'s base): its fields begin with Odin's `serializationData` (40 bytes when empty: format, bytes, objects, string, prefab, the prefab's objects and changes, nodes), then `_glow` and `_glows` (`trp_data::screen::malfunction_glow` reads past it). Its curves use plain Hermite keys (`weightedMode` 0), clamped at both ends.
- **The notice's timing is its animator's**, not a timer (`Notification`'s controller `Item`, `sharedassets49` 113; clips 100 and 99): `AutoComplete` true for `Default` takes the show (2.6667 s) straight into the hide (0.25 s), whose `OnAnimationDone` at 0.0833 s folds the row away over 0.3 s (DOTween's default ease, `OutQuad` in the install's `DOTweenSettings`).
- **A key's name in a notice** is `InputSource.ToString` without its quotes: the keys in their stored order, each by `KeyTools.GetKeyNameAlias` (`LShift`, `Kp 5`, `Num *`, arrows as their signs), " + " between ("T + LShift" for `CheckChamber`), not the options' own words.
- **The heat's spread reads the last shot's heat**, after the shot's own is added (`FirearmController.Shot`); the barrel's deviation by durability multiplies the same term (`GetBarrelDeviation`, its barrel part's figures else the weapon's, `DeviationMax` nought taken as 100).
- **Bevy's view exposure is about 0.001 at its default** (EV100 9.7, physical units): a glow added in the frame's own numbers must not be scaled by it (the first try drew nothing visible).
- **`Copy-Item` keeps a file's modified time**: a shader put back from a copy was not rebuilt into the app (embedded by `include_str!`) until touched.



## Added 2026-10-25 (The repair window: 0.129.0)

- **The repair window's prefab** (`ItemUiContext._repairWindowTemplate`, in `sharedassets49`; `probe repairwindow`): 132 objects laid out by layout groups and fitters at a preferred 700 by 800 (its `LayoutElement`), which the panel's own solver lays out once its self-sizing texts are given their fonts' sizes (`text_size`). Its warnings are five templates (error, warning, information, common and rare buff) copied as needed; the warning's is the error's red with black words.
- **`RepairerParametersPanel`'s fields** after Odin's 40 bytes: 23 pointers (the dropdown, rank, avatar, the standing's object, the texts, the warning panel, the slider, the labels and icons, the kit points' two sprites at the 18th and 22nd, the charge's text), then the sum's critical and default colours; `ConditionCharacteristicsSlider`'s 9 pointers, then its two colours (`trp_data::screen::repair_figures`). The standing's object is `Standing/Standing` (the price rate stays).
- **The window's words**: `"repair speed"` is "repair" in the install (so "Instant repair"), the labels' keys are English (`SELECT REPAIRER`, `DP TO REPAIR: `...) over the prefab's Russian, and the durability's label carries TextMeshPro's named colour `<color=white>`.
- **SPT's server converts only the first kit's points** to durability when several kits pay (`RepairService.RepairItemByKit`), and stops at the first kit it lowers.
- **The slider's knob is the arrow under the bar** (`Knob/Image`, anchored at the handle's lower right), not a handle on it.



## Added 2026-10-25 (Slopes and sliding, and the slide out of a sprint: 0.130.0)

- **The slope's rules are all in the ground check and the base motion** (`MovementContext.UpdateGroundCollision`, `DetectSurfaceNormal`, `ProjectMotionToSurface`, `ApplyGravity`): the surface normal from the ray under the axis (a quarter metre past the check) or the controller's own contact, its five-frame mean, a pull up the slope as long as `0.9 - normal.y` where the mean is under 0.65, added times `G_y` in place of gravity, and downhill motion laid on the ground between 0.5 and 0.995. All of it matched `courses2\6-slope-*` to the sixth place (`movement.md`, "Slopes and sliding").
- **`RollingMedian.Avarage` is a mean, not a median**: the sum of the buffer over how many were added; its first value goes at the buffer's second place, which changes nothing in a sum. `Median` is never called for the ground.
- **On steep ground the ray under the axis misses**: the capsule's rounded foot rests on the slope to one side, so the axis meets the slope further down than the ray's quarter metre reaches (585 frames of the steep recording); the surface is then the controller's contact.
- **The controller's contact normal** (`SimpleCharacterController.surfaceNormalAccordingToCapsule`) is the push-out direction with the largest upward part among those of 0.087 or more, normalised; straight up with no overlap at all, and nought (a zero vector) with walls alone.
- **A climb's or a vault's `InputMotionBeforeLimit` is stale**: those states have their own motion with no `LimitMotionXZ`, so the figure stays what the last walking frame left (the steep recording's `ClimbUp`, 248 frames).
- **`SetPoseLevel(force: true)` puts the smoothed stance there at once**: the sprint's slide drops the body and the camera to a full crouch on the frame. It also skips the push-up skill's credit for standing.
- **A speed limit named `SurfaceNormal`** is in the limits' table from the start at 1 and never changed: no slope slows the character.



## Added 2026-10-25 (Room to move: the refusals of a step aside, a roll, lying down, standing up and the crawl: 0.131.0)

- **Every room check sends its rays and boxes from the capsule's middle** (`PlayerColliderCenter`), not the feet: lying, that is 0.35 m up with a height of 0.3, so the crawl's and the turn's rays reach about a quarter metre under the feet, and the step aside's box starts 0.27 m over them standing.
- **`CanProne` is asked by the prone flag's setter**, which a press in the air reaches too (`MovementState.Prone`; the jump has no handler of its own): the two points it wants ground under are 0.4 m over the feet with 0.6 m rays, so a press more than about 20 cm off the ground is refused there and then. The jump's end asks again only if the flag is up.
- **Getting up asks `CanStandAt` twice for the same box** (at the stance held lying, a full crouch, then `CanSit`), and `UpdatePoseAfterProne` then asks for the remembered stance through `SetPoseLevel`, which asks again: under a ceiling between 1.20 and 1.58 m over the feet the character comes up crouched.
- **`CanProneTilt` is never called** (the lean lying down is not refused by room); `CanRoll` is, by the lying still state's and the roll's `SetStep`.
- **`IsAbleToRotateProne` answers no for no turn at all**: its flag starts false and is worked out only for a turn; nothing is lost by it, the turn being nought.
- **The settings object's three-figure vectors were skipped by the reader** (`hard_settings.rs`: `vec3` fields read as nothing); they are read now, and `probe hardsettings` prints them: six in all, the roll's and the lean lying down's boxes among them.
- **The vault's hands** (`VaultingGameplayRestrictions`): medicine or food in hand refuses a move above its height without hands and, with no move chosen from the last measurement, any move at all; the restriction is asked before the obstacle is measured, so with medicine in hand from the start nothing is ever measured and nothing vaulted. A grenade likewise while its animator's `GrenadeFire` is `Hold` or `Throw`.



## Added 2026-10-25 (The head's push out of walls: 0.132.0)

- **The head's capsules on the prefab** (`probe tree` and `probe script ... PlayerOverlapManager`, `probe dump`): `_headColliders[0]` on `playercollider` (a child of `CameraContainer` at (0, 0.15, 0.15), no turn): radius 0.22, height 0.7, direction y, centre (0, -0.2, -0.1); `[1]` on `ProneCollisionTest` (a child of `Player`, the animated object): radius 0.175, height 1.6, direction z, centre (0, 0.15, 0); `_proneColliderPivot` is `Base HumanSpine2`. The head's `ColliderExtrusion` has `_castMask` 0 in the prefab (set at its `Init`) and `_castHalo` 0.
- **The recorder's `head_capsule` is the capsule's object, not its middle**: `player.Transform.Original.InverseTransformPoint(pom.Collider.transform.position)`. Worked back from the camera, the standing one is the container's (0, 0.15, 0.15) to 0.002 mm on 13,299 frames.
- **`ColliderExtrusion` adds its answers from one place** (`CalculateDepenetration`: the sum of every collider's direction times its depth and a millimetre, all against the capsule where the motion put it), unlike the camera's push, which the picture's camera steps.
- **Walking straight into a wall the head does not reach it**: with the view level the standing capsule's front is about 2 cm behind the body's capsule's (`tests/sim/head.rs`); it is a lean, a look down or a corner that brings it in. In `courses2\6-head-walls` the pushes are all standing, walking, one jump frame and getting up: none lying.
- **The game's meshes are not readable at run time**: 699 of 701 mesh colliders about the sill in `courses7` were written with their names and bounds but no triangles (`readable: false`), so a push cannot be worked out again from the recorder's solids where walls are meshes (most are).
- **`IdleWeaponMountingState.Rotate` compares the prediction with nothing exactly** (`Vector3.Equals`, not the approximate `==`): any push at all refuses the turn.



## Added 2026-10-25 (Speed limits from armour and from being hit: 0.133.0)

- **The recordings' `state_speed_limit` is the armour's**: it falls in steps (1, 0.6699, 0.6664, 0.6520, 0.6493 of a top of 0.6863) whatever the weight between 14 and 40 kg, and our penalty from each recording's kit gives it to the sixth place on 327 of 329 recordings (the two others held at 0.3 by a fractured leg).
- **A plate carrier of class 0 slows nothing by its own figure**: only armour components count (`Player.RecalculateEquipmentParams`), and an item has one only with `MaxDurability` and `armorClass` above 0 (`Vest`, `ArmoredEquipment`); a class-0 carrier gets an `EquipmentPenaltyComponent` instead, which only the backpack's is read from. The Redut-T5's −13.5 % and the Zabralo's −11.5 % are the carriers' own figures; what slows is their inserts and plates.
- **`ApplyHitDebuff` is called with the round's burn times the damage** (`DamageInfo.StaminaBurnRate * Damage`, `Player.ApplyDamageInfo`), the damage as the armour left it; weapon damage is a bullet, a blow, a landmine, a sniper's or a blunt hit (`DamageHelper.WeaponDamage`): a grenade's fragment and its blast are not, and slow nothing.
- **A trap in the app**: `--wear` dresses the body only and puts nothing in the equipment, so it changes no penalty (the armour's limit needs the thing in an armour slot).
- **`courses1\0-death-shot` writes no limit once dead**: the shot kills outright, the trace's alive frames have the limit at 1, the dead ones none.



## Added 2026-10-25 (Stamina from wounds and the quick draw: 0.134.0)

- **The fast weapon switch is the pistol's quick draw**: `OnWeaponSwitchFast` is called twice, by the firearm's `Remove.Start` when the next thing is a weapon in the holster chosen with `FastSlotSelection` (the holster's key's double click, `QuickSecondaryWeapon`) and the character is not lying, and by the holster weapon's `SetAnimatorAndProceduralValues` within a second of that (`QuickdrawTime`). Each charges the arms `WeaponFastSwitchConsumption` (15) over (level / 50 + 1), the level the class skill's of the weapon in question, a whole division: half at 50.
- **A charge to the arms spills whole**: `Consumption.Consume` takes the whole cost from the legs as well when the arms could not pay all of it (`HandsStamina.Consume` returns the shortfall; the base pool is then charged the whole delta again).
- **The quick draw's speeds are the animator's, for that operation**: `SetSpeedParameters(1, mult)` sets `SpeedReload` to 1 and `SpeedDraw` to `min + (level / 50) * (max - min)` (`WeaponFastDrawSettings`: 1.5 and 2 for a pistol and for anything else in this install), over 1.5 for the weapon put away; the next taking in hand puts the skill's speeds back.
- **A wound's burn keeps no overuse** (`Stamina.Consume(..., fromDamage)`), and lets the pool refill at once (`AllowsRestoration`).
- **The install binds `QuickSecondaryWeapon` to `1` as a double click** (`Control.ini`, `DoubleClickTimeout` 0.3): the holster's single press waits for it to fail, in the game and in the app's input model.



## Added 2026-10-25 (Stimulants' and food's buffs: 0.135.0)

- **A weight buff is never absolute in the game**: the `WeightLimit` buff adds an `Endurance` effect without calling its `Init(absolute)`, so `BaseHealthController.UpdateWeightModifier` always multiplies; Obdolbos's 0.1 marked `AbsoluteValue: true` raises the limits by a tenth, though the game's screen writes it in kilograms.
- **The blood loss's stop blocks damage's bleedings only**: `HasBloodLossBlockers` is read in `ApplyDamage` and in the wound's relapse; a bleeding put on otherwise (`DoBleed`) still opens. Its activation ends every bleeding there is.
- **A buff's kind is cut to thirteen letters in the game's enum for some** (`QuantumTunnel`, `RemoveAllBloo`, `DamageModifie`, ...), while the install writes the full names; the reader takes either.
- **The skills' summary level caps at 60 with a buff above nought** (51 otherwise) and every rule reads it, so Propital's +20 Vitality on a level-45 character makes Vitality elite while it works.
- **The medical table already holds every item that names a set**: 42 of them (the stimulators, the drinks, the MRE and the other buffed food), as it puts in every medical item, food and drink of the install.



## Added 2026-10-26 (The voice and a bone's crack: 0.136.0)

- **The voice's draw holds the clip said last out of the next round**: `TagBank.Match` clears the others' marks as it takes a round's last clip, so after the first round of 13 the rounds are 12 long, and no clip comes twice running.
- **The last part hit starts as the head** (`LastDamagedBodyPart`, an `EBodyPart` whose first is the head, set only by `ApplyDamageInfo`): a death with no hit before it (a fall, a bleeding never hit) is silent, as a head's kill is.
- **Dead, the app's controller no longer takes the health's events**: the killing hit's damage and the death are taken by the voice itself.
- **The calm breath is very quiet**: `bear2_breath_ok` is at -50.4 dBFS RMS, the out of breath's -41.6, the hurt's -31.1, the dying's -31.7; the hurt grunts -17 to -31.
- **A helmet's hearing cut shows in the recorder's mixer figures**: in `anything-212919` the ZSh-1-2M (`DeafStrength` High) put on takes `GunsVolume` and `AmbientVolume` from 0 to -50 dB over 83 frames (0.70 s of the recording's clock), and back as it comes off; its face shield (`DeafStrength` None) changes nothing. Not built (`backlog.md`, "muffled hearing under headgear").
- **The range's starts and what is ahead of them**: a test casts a body's sphere from each (`range.rs`); the yard's `open` start has 300 m clear facing +x; facing away from the row it had a box 28.6 m out.



## Added 2026-10-26 (Desaturation, tunnel vision and the camera's sharpening: 0.137.0)

- **The raid's camera is the level's, not `Cam2`**: `Cam2` has `CC_Sharpen` but no `DesaturateEffect`; Customs' camera (`sharedassets15.assets`) has both, its ramp in another file of the build (256 by 1, RGB24). The recorded camera's component list (`cam_fx`: `TOD_Scattering`, `MBOIT_Scattering`, `DesaturateEffect` at 16) is the level camera's.
- **The sharpening's strength is the player's** (`Sharpen` in `Graphics.ini`, `CameraManager.SetSharpen`; 0.6 here); the prefab's is not used in play.
- **A medkit's desaturation does nothing on the screen**: the accumulator drives `HealthDesaturate` to -0.15, but `CC_Sharpen` hands it to the shader held to 0 to 1 (`Mathf.Clamp01`). Exhaustion's 0.15 shows; pain's 0.3 is in the table but `IsValidEffect` never takes pain.
- **The mask's fading waits a frame**: `UpdateAmount` reads the radius before moving it, so after a painkiller the desaturation moves only from the frame that begins with the radius open (`0-fall-leg`, `0-medical`: exact once so).
- **The sharpening's desaturating mask is the sharpening's own** (`_desatMask` from `CC_Sharpen.MaskDesaturate`, the rest from the `DesaturateEffect`): `0-medical` begins with the sharpening's at 1 and the provider's at 0, the radius open (left from before).



## Added 2026-10-26 (A blunt hit's concussion: 0.138.0)

- **What a helmet takes concusses**: `BluntContusion` runs after every `ApplyDamageInfo` with the armour's absorbed damage; the head only, 10 or more, a contusion of a quarter of it at half strength after 0.1 s. Tagilla's welding mask, blocking a 60-damage round at penetration 20, lets 2.4 through and takes 57.6 (14.4 s).



## Added 2026-10-26 (The mount: the arms with the weapon, the pivot's turn, the drawing: 0.138.1)

- **The mounted pivot is turned by the frame before's turn** (`MovementContext.MountingRotationFunction`: `WeaponMountingPos` is the controller's place plus the point less `Weapon_root.TransformPoint(MountingRotationCenter)`, read before `SetPositionAndRotation` turns the controller to this frame's `HandsRotation`). Recorded in `courses7\10-anything` (recorder 0.14.0, the M4A1): with our pivot on the recorded `weapon_root_anim` bone, the gap to the recorded point grows 10.3 mm (`-212814`) and 9.9 mm (`-212842`) a degree of the frame's turn, the lever 10.7, and is gone on the first still frame. The recorder's bones and its `pos`/`point` stand in frames some 2 m apart (a constant: the gap's change is what tells).
- **The game's arms go with the mounted weapon whole**: its upper arms' reach to the palm (left 0.280, right 0.195 m) and their distance to the weapon (0.255 m) do not change on a mount, while the upper arms move up to 0.53 m from the ribcage (`ribcage_p`, `left_upperarm_p`).
- **The game's weapon against the view on a mount** (`eye`, `camera_world_rot`, `weapon_root_anim_p`): at 120 frames a second it changes every other frame (the physics step's springs), by under 1 mm, while the view turns at the mount's 1 degree cap.
- **Trap, ours**: `see_collarbones` saw the animated collarbones from the weapon rig's root, which stands for the ribcage until a mount moves it (`Shown::rig_apart`); the first-person arms' mesh is skinned to those kept collarbones (`spawn_body`), so the sleeves stretched by the root's move.



## Added 2026-10-26 (The magazine's model in a reload from the inventory: 0.138.2)

- **The game makes the magazine's model at `MagShow`**: `InsertMagOperation.OnMagAppeared` (a magazine put in from the inventory) and `ReloadExternalMagOperation.OnMagAppeared` (a reload) call `Firearms.SetupMod(slot, Singleton<ObjectsFactory>.Instance.CreateItem(magazine, isAnimated: true))` once (`_magAppeared`), then, for a belt, `_weaponPrefab.UpdateAnimatorHierarchy`. The part is changed on the weapon's own model; nothing else of the weapon is made again.
- **Trap, ours**: a build's rig was asked for only once the item changed, and a rig takes about half a second on its thread (`local\run_mag.txt`: the drum's build 0.47 s); the change of rig then waited for rest unless it came before `MagShow`.



## Added 2026-10-26 (Medicine's, food's and drink's figures in the inspect window: 0.139.0)

- **The Resource `Static Icons` holds four tables of a figure's icons**, each an enum dictionary: the attributes' (`ItemAttributeSprites`, 100-odd keys), the health factors' (None, Health, Hydration, Energy, Radiation, Temperature, Poisoning, Effect), the damage effects' (HeavyBleeding, LightBleeding, Fracture, Contusion, Intoxication, RadExposure, Pain, DestroyedPart) and the buffs' (25, **their names cut to thirteen letters**: `StomachBloodl`, `QuantumTunnel`, `RemoveAllBloo`...). `probe staticicons`.
- **The rows' rules** (`local\med_inspect_research_s137.md`, with each class cited): the discard limit is `Item`'s own and so first for every thing; a buff's colour goes by `IsBuff`, not the sign (blue 84,193,255, red 196,0,0, read off the captures' pixels); .NET's `Math.Round` halves to even (SJ1's -0.25 is written -0.2); the bleedings' words end in a line break in the English texts.



## Added 2026-10-26 (The edit build screen's characteristics, and a pick made safe: 0.140.0)

- **The edit build screen makes its whole preview again at every pick** (`ItemObserveScreen.WeaponUpdate`, `RefreshWeapon`, `WeaponPreview.SetupItemPreview` → `CreateCleanLootPrefab` of the whole item after `WaitForAllBundles`); it takes every slot's view away first (`ClearModSlots`) and makes them again in the preview's callback (`CreateModSlotViews`), and a new call cancels the wait of the one before (`_unsubscribe.Cancel`). Only a reload in the hands changes one part on a model (`Firearms.SetupMod`).
- **The characteristics panel's rows go in a grid** (`CharsPanel`, a `FlexibleGridLayoutGroup`; `CompactPanel` beside it is left empty), the bars and the compact rows in one list each, made in that order into the same container (`CharacteristicsPanel.InstantiateItemFields`); the weight's bar runs 0 to 15 kg (`Range`), less better; the durability is compared with nothing (`CompareWith(null)`).
- **The comparison's piece pulses**: `CharacteristicPanel.Update` adds 0.8 a second to a counter and sets the alpha 0.2 plus its ping-pong over 0.4 (0.2 to 0.6 in half a second and back). The value's "(+N)" is the compared build's change less this one's, whole, with no space before it.
- **Ours**: the loose models' reader was let go of whenever its queue emptied (`loose::read_models`), and made again took 0.43 s of a pick's 0.70 s.



## Added 2026-10-26 (Models that flash, and the frame's cost: 0.140.1)

- **Models that flash.** Bevy 0.18 lets go of a changed material's prepared copy before it readies the new one (`bevy_render` 0.18.1, `erased_render_asset.rs`, `prepare_erased_assets`: "any users will not see the old asset after a new asset is extracted, even if the new asset is not yet ready"), and a material naming a picture the renderer does not have is not ready (`AsBindGroupError::RetryNextUpdate`, `bevy_render_macros` `as_bind_group.rs`) and is tried again next frame: until then nothing drawn with it is drawn. Since 0.80.0 the app sends at most 8 MB up a frame after the warm-up (`RenderAssetBytesPerFrame`, `main::UPLOAD_BUDGET`), so a burst of raised copies (some 10 in a frame as one walks up to the dummies) left materials unready for frames: the dummies' suits and AKMs, the build screen's receiver. Fixed by pointing a material at its copy only once the renderer has the copy (`streaming::see_on_card`).
- **The order of the stores' word to the renderer**: the stores tell the renderer what changed in `PostUpdate` (`AssetEventSystems`); the renderer takes a material told of with what the material holds at the end of the frame. A material changed earlier in the frame and pointed in `Last` at a picture added in `Last` went across naming a picture the renderer had not been told of (seen by `UNHEARD`). The copies are now taken before `AssetEventSystems`.
- **The engine leaves open** whether a frame's pictures are readied before its materials (`MaterialPlugin` adds `ErasedRenderAssetPlugin::<MeshMaterial3d<M>>` with no `AFTER`). Ordering them (a system after `prepare_assets::<GpuImage>` and before the materials') was tried and measured: with the two fixes above it changed nothing (0 lost either way), and was taken out.
- **What did not flash**: culling (the skinned pieces are `NoFrustumCulling`), the levels of detail (none drawn), parking (`park.rs` touches what comes back).
- **What cost a walking frame**: the body animator's sampling walked a clip's keys from the start for each sample (`Clip::sample`), and the drawn pose worked out root motion nobody reads. 0.62 ms of a walking frame before, 0.18 after (debug).
- **The F1 figure and `pace.ps1` agree** when measured the same way: release, 2560x1440, the user's settings, the panel open: 352 by `pace.ps1`, the panel's own 355. The debug build (our crates at `opt-level` 1) draws some 40% fewer.
- **The dummies do not stop the player**: the walker passes through the camera on the lane walk (`--start lanes --tap 400:w:450`). Not changed here.



## Added 2026-10-26 (The debug panel's commands: 0.141.0)

- **Bevy's `Interaction` can come back to `Pressed` within one press** (a synthetic click, `--drag x:y:x:y`, sent a button's command two or three times when it was sent on every change to `Pressed`): a command is sent only on the frame the left button goes down (`ButtonInput::just_pressed`), as the edit build screen's picture was already handled.



## Added 2026-10-26 (Reactive targets, and the range redrawn: 0.142.0)

- **A bot of the single-player game does not flinch**: `Player.Create` switches the character's `HitReaction` off, and only `ObservedPlayerView` (the online game's view of another player) switches it on; an offset modifier switched off adds nothing (`OffsetModifier.ModifyOffset`). The simplified skeleton's animator `Hit` is the infected's. The recorder writes the bones as the camera draws (`OnPreCull`), after the IK, so its frames would show a flinch.
- **`ArmorHitSoundPlayer` is never heard**: it waits on `BulletSoundsUtils.HitArmor`, which nothing calls.
- **DOTween's rules** (its published source, not the game's): `DOLocalRotateQuaternion` blends by `Quaternion.Slerp` with the eased value; an `AnimationCurve` ease is read at the share of the tween times the curve's last key's time (`EaseCurve.Evaluate`). A killed tween's `AsTask` (the game's `TweenExtensions`) is cancelled, which ends an awaiting chain.
- **An `AudioClip` of a scene's shared file** reads with the effects' bundle's tree (`range_targets::popper_clips`), its data from the `.resource` beside the file: the poppers' four PCM clips, one at 29,329 Hz.
- **The range's map**: `--view map:80 --hud never --resolution 2560x1440` is 9.4 pixels a metre, the start at (998, 581.6) of a 2000-wide display; the user's drawing over it is read so.



## Added 2026-10-26 (The handheld rangefinder: 0.143.0)

- **The rangefinder's container prefab holds its hands' rig as a second root** (`item_ranger_1500_container` with only the muzzle's combined mesh, and `item_ranger_1500.generated` beside it with the arms, the item's meshes, the lens and the readout's canvas): its picture drawn from the container alone had nothing in it. It is a `WeaponPrefab` as a grenade's or a knife's is, and its picture is drawn from the prefab's tree as theirs are (`icons.rs`).
- **The aim's stamina with the rangefinder** (`Physical`'s aim consumption): `AimRangeFinderDrainRate` in place of `AimDrainRate` while `HandsController is PortableRangeFinderController`, with an aim weight of 1 (`UsableItemController.IsAiming`: `Physical.Aim(_isAiming ? 1 : 0)`), and the walk slowed by `SetAimingSlowdown(IsAiming, 0.33)` as the sights slow it.
- **Bevy's text takes a font's own style**: `get_attrs` asks for the family and the style of the face the handle was loaded from, so the build's Bender Italic file draws italic with no style asked (the weight is still the text's: `FontWeight`, finding of 0.29.0).
- **A UI aimed at the optic camera draws into its picture**: `UiTargetCamera` on a camera rendering to a 1024 image lays the UI out in that image's pixels, which is the prefab's canvas (scaled to 1024 by 1024); the UI is drawn over the scene, not at the canvas's plane 2 m out.



## Added 2026-10-26 (Shadow meshes drawn as shadow alone: 0.143.1)

- **Items carry meshes that cast their shadow alone**: the repair kits' `shadow_LOD0` and `shadow_lod1` (`m_CastShadows` 3, a material `24 - Default` on the plain standard shader with no texture). The engine draws no pixel of them; a reader that keeps only `m_Enabled` draws them white. `probe objects <bundle>`, then `probe dump <bundle> <renderer id>` shows `m_CastShadows`; the scene props' reader had kept it already (`scene_props::Renderer::shadow_only`).
